The armour's lines (Impenetrability, Bludgeon Bane) stay item spells.
- *The current skill.* The rule is `SkillRules` (`Session/Contracts/SkillRules.cs`, M3 2.2 a2), which follows ACE's `CreatureSkill.Current`: `TryBase` (retail `InqSkill` raw: the formula over the raw attributes, 0 below the skill's `_min_level`, plus `Init` and ranks), `TryCurrent` (the formula over the enchanted attributes, the multiplicative skill enchantments, the vitae, then the additive ones each truncated to int before summing, `EnchantmentManager.GetSkillMod_Additives`, `EnchantmentManager.cs` 759-765; review 1.4-R10), `Compose`, `Modifiers` (allocation-free), `CostToRaise` and `CostToRaise10` (the skills page's buttons, docs/retail-ui.md section 23.2). `GearCasts.TryCurrentSkill` calls it with the magic formula (Focus + Self) / 4 and `GearCasts.SkillValue` calls `Compose`; behaviour unchanged (the existing tests). `TODO(g4-enchantment-affects-skills)` is closed: `EnchantmentRules.AffectsKey` is the cull's key match, with the attack and defence skill groups (`Enchantment::AffectsAttackSkills` 0x005CBD10, `AffectsDefenseSkills` 0x005CBD60); the Defender aura (BuffStatMods 1599 to 1605, 4400) is a float stat (type 0x9008, key 169), not a skill, so it does not lift Melee Defense, while "Aura of Defense" (2245) does, by key 6. The augmentation bonuses (`GetAugBonus_Base`, `GetAugBonus_Current`, `CreatureSkill.cs` 169 and 184; retail's `InqSkill` adds the credit augmentation PropertyInt 365, the +10 specialisation augmentations 300 to 302, Jack of All Trades 326 and the luminance augmentation 344) are not modelled (`TODO(m3-skill-aug-bonus)`, review 1.4-R12; BSD Mage has none). ACE's chance is `GearCasts.Chance` (`SkillCheck.cs` 7-22, factor 0.07f). The first casts use the level rule at chance 0.999 from this skill (`GearCasts.Pick`); the buff cycle of 2.1 uses `--buff-cast-chance` (0.9) and the retry one level down.
- *Mana.* `GearCasts.ManaCeiling` compares the most a cast group can cost with the read model's current mana before it starts. ACE's `Creature.CalculateManaUsage` (`Creature_Magic.cs` 14-60) takes the base mana, plus `ManaMod` for each worn clothing item for an armour-category Item Enchantment on a player (SpellCategory 160 to 189); Mana Conversion (`GetManaCost`, lines 62-92) only lowers it, to 1 at least; a cast that fizzles at the pre-check costs 5 (`Player_Magic.cs` 566-572). When short, a named precondition wait for regeneration runs with a bound (the scenario 120 s, `CastLiveTests` 180 s; nothing has been cast, so it is no retry), logged with the numbers; if the mana never comes, or the maximum is below the cost, the scenario reports `cast=failed` with a warning and `CastLiveTests` fails with the numbers. A UseDone 0x0401 is named "NOT ENOUGH MANA" with the numbers. Every cast failure carries what the server said (`GearCasts.Explain`): kind and code (0x0400 components, 0x0401 mana, 0x0402 fizzle, any other UseDone code), the outcome's own words, and the chat lines that arrived during the cast.
- *`CastLiveTests`* (Loopback, BSD Mage, run by the lead): the gear first, `SetCombatMode(Magic)`, then (a) a Creature Enchantment and a Life Magic self buff, each the highest level of the first profile line at chance >= 0.999 (no retry), Confirmed with the enchantment in `PlayerQualities.Enchantments` with the SpellTable's duration; (b) an Item Enchantment spell of the wand lines, cast by its form (a self cast, above); for a spell that is cast at an item, ACE sends no 0x02C2 to the player (`WorldObject_Magic.cs` 430-452), so the proof is UseDone 0 (`Sent`) plus the chat line "You cast <spell> on <item>"; (c) a certain fizzle (the cheapest spell the read model's spellbook holds whose Power exceeds the current skill by more than 50: `Player_Magic.cs` 534-535, `GearCasts.PickFizzle`, review 1.4-R11) as a ServerError 0x0402; (d) mana falls over the successful casts; (e) component burn (review 1.4-R3): P(no burn) is computed from the SpellTable (`GearCasts.NoBurnProbability`: ACE `Spell.TryBurnComponents`, `Spell.cs` 157-189, `ComponentLoss x CDM x min(1, Power / skill)` over the account's formula slots, multiplied over the casts made, the fizzle included, since ACE burns on a fizzle, `Player_Magic.cs` 868); `burned >= 1` is asserted only when P(no burn) < 1e-3, else report-only, and the test never casts again until something burns; the burn code is `Player_Magic.cs` 1183-1215. (f) After the burn the gear scenario runs again (cast off) and must not fail, the main pack's slot count must equal the count before the casts, each component weenie must be one stack (the merge, section 9), and a further rerun has `topup=0 gear_steps=0`. Then `SetCombatMode(NonCombat)`. Summary line: `cast_live creature=<id>:<outcome>:self life=... item=<id>:<outcome>:<self|item> fizzle=<id>:0x0402 mana_needed=<n> mana_before=<n> mana_after=<n> p_no_burn=<p> burned=<n> burn_check=asserted|report-only merges=<n> slots=<before>-><after>`. The fizzle and the Magic mode's other facts: an untargeted war spell is refused unless its NonComponentTargetType is None (`Player_Magic.cs` 1379-1383), so War Magic's first live cast is item 3.1's (Flame Bolt at a tusker; decision D21), together with the live check of `TODO(m3-cast-projectile-attribution)`. `GearLiveTests` (Loopback) judges `gear=wielded` and, on a second run with `cast: false`, `gear_steps=0`; a third run with the cast on is report-only (review 1.4-R1).
- *The mage-build template and a cast.* A cast changes nothing the mage-build template reads: `MageTemplate.IsMet` reads the level (>=), the attribute Start values, the skill advancement class and the ranks (>=), all base values that buffs never touch; ACE's `Proficiency.OnSuccessUse` (`Entity/Proficiency.cs` 15-110) only ever raises skill ranks and the level (it grants the experience and spends it on the skill), and the checks compare with at-least.

**Open TODOs of M3 1.4.** `TODO(m3-inventory-delete-alone)` (closed in 3.2, section 32), `TODO(m3-standin-weenies)` (answered for the kit's weenies only), `TODO(m3-standin-wield-rules)`, `TODO(m3-standin-magic-skill)`, `TODO(m3-standin-spell-statmods)`, `TODO(m3-standin-cast-mana)`, `TODO(m3-standin-cast-components)`, `TODO(m3-kit-foci)`, `TODO(m3-skill-aug-bonus)`, `TODO(m3-cast-mana-unknown)`, `TODO(item-creature)`; the plan's section 9 places each. The login-check `cast=` field is item 2.1's `buff` leg on BSD Mage (decision D20); the gear scenario's verdict carries `cast=` in 1.4.

**The buff cycle (M3 2.1 s0b, s1, fix B; `BuffCycle`, `Session/Mage/BuffCycle.cs`, `BuffOptions`, `BuffCycleTests`).** Engine-agnostic: it uses the Action API and the read models only (not `PlayerTools`), allocates nothing per tick, and needs the player already in Magic mode (else it fails loudly). `new BuffCycle(session | (actions, state), spells, BuffOptions, log)`; `Start(now)` runs a cycle, `StartRebuff(now, forced)` a re-buff pass; `Step(now)` runs every frame until `Done` or `Failed`; `Stage`, `Result` (`.Fields` is the verdict text), `RebuffDue(now, margin)`, `MinRemainingSeconds(now)`, `LineOrder`. The counters (fizzles, transfers, revitalizes, mana_min) are cumulative over the object's life.
- *The ordered pass.* The owner's order: Focus and Willpower, then Creature Enchantment Mastery, then the other masteries, then the rest. The level per line is the highest one `GearCasts.Chance` gives at `--buff-cast-chance` from the skill the earlier casts left (recomputed from the read model after every cast); a fizzle is retried once one level down, then the line is a miss. A self line's expiry comes from the registry entry; an armour line's from the cast's UseDone plus the SpellTable duration for each worn piece the chat lines name (the armour lines are one cast at the caster, D23, and the cycle reads the chat lines of section 7's Item Enchantment rule).
- *The upgrade pass (s1).* A `Start` pass ends with a sweep of the 24 lines: each line holding a level below the one the rule gives from the current skill is cast once more at that level (Focus and Willpower, VI to VII), never retried (a fizzle is the miss `Short:upgrade-fizzled` and the line stays at its level). A second cycle on a buffed mage casts nothing. This closes `TODO(m3-buff-upgrade-skip)`. In the scripted chain the skills end at 376 (Creature, Item, Life) and 405 (War): the 373 and 402 of the pins plus 3 from the two upgrades.
- *The forced pass (D25).* `StartRebuff(now, forced: true)` recasts only the line closest to expiry, with a margin of its remaining time plus one second, so one cast proves a recast while the buff is active; with no active line every line is due. `BuffResult.Rebuffed` is per pass (`RecastWhileActive` above 0), and `RecastWhileActive` counts the pass's casts that landed on a line still active when the cast began; the leg's `forced=yes` needs the forced pass to have cast at least one line and recast at least one while it was active (review 2.1-R5). `RebuffDue` and `StartRebuff(forced: false)` are called by the re-buff driver (M4 4.1; the driver never forces a pass, below). The gap `m3-buff-rebuff-driver` (review 2.1-R10) is closed in M4 4.1.
- *The mana loop (D24).* Before a cast the cycle compares `GearCasts.ManaCeiling` with the current mana; when short it casts Stamina to Mana by chance and affordability, Revitalize Self when the transfer cannot cover the shortfall (or stamina is short), and a named, bounded wait when neither can run (`--buff-transfer-tries`, `--buff-mana-wait-s`). A "reserve" rule (keep a transfer's cost spare) was tried and fails, because the boost loop never reaches it; it is left out. The regeneration wait goes to NonCombat: after `BuffCycle.NonCombatAfterSeconds` (5 s) of a mana wait the cycle sets NonCombat (`IActionApi.SetCombatMode`, awaited) and returns to Magic (awaited) before the next cast, because ACE halves stamina and health regeneration in a combat mode (`Creature_Vitals.cs` `GetStanceMod`, about 220); a wait shorter than 5 s stays in Magic (a switch at every wait made 126 mode changes for waits of about a second). The cycle reads the buffed skill from the read model and looks the `BuffStatMods` rows up once (`TransferOf` allocates; `GearCasts.TryCurrentSkill` allocates while the registry holds enchantments, so the wait reuses the skill its first look read).
- *The result.* `BuffResult` carries `buffs levels rebuffed forced min_remaining_s fizzles transfers revitalizes mana_min cycle_s mode_changes misses` (`CycleSeconds`, `ModeChanges`, `Upgraded`, `ArmourCast`; `Fields` reads `buffs=a/w levels=.. rebuffed=.. min_remaining_s=.. fizzles=.. transfers=f/t revitalizes=.. mana_min=.. cycle_s=.. mode_changes=.. misses=..` and ` forced=yes` for the forced pass). Until M4 4.1 the armour lines' expiry lived in the cycle object, so a new object recasts both armour lines (`m3-buff-armour-memory`, closed in M4 4.1: the armour ledger below keeps it per worn piece for the session, and an appraisal seeds it at a login). A surpassed armour piece line is counted active for that piece (`BuffCycle.ArmourLanded` reads ACE's "but it is surpassed by" line, and the `surpassed=` count shows them; `TODO(m3-buff-armour-surpassed)` is closed in code, M4 4.1 doc pass 2).
- *Options.* The group "Buff cycle" in `OptionCatalog.Buff.cs`, read by the main scene and the headless host: `--buff-cast-chance` (0.9, D10), `--buff-rebuff-margin-s` (300), `--buff-transfer-tries` (8) and `--buff-mana-wait-s` (180), and from M4 4.1 `--buff-auto on|off` (off: the re-buff driver, below) and `--buff-auto-check-s` (5 s, at least 1). `BuffOptions.ToString()` ends `auto=off auto_check_s=5`. The game's buff leg and the play host read them (section 30; `m3-buff-leg-options` is closed in M4 4.1). The README's option list is not edited by this section.

**The mana economy (M3 2.1 s0b, fix B).** There are two sides. The cycle's `need` before a cast is `GearCasts.ManaCeiling` (the base mana plus ACE's armour surcharge, `Creature_Magic.cs` `CalculateManaUsage` 14-56): a conservative need. What ACE charges is lower: Mana Conversion rolls twice against the spell's power (`GetManaCost` 58-100; chance `SkillCheck.GetSkillChance`, factor 0.03; the second roll is skipped at cost 1), and the cost is at least 1. Live (`BuffLoopLiveTests`, 2026-10-16): 26 lines (24 plus the two upgrades) in 56 s of session time, transfers 3/3, revitalizes 1, fizzles 0, mana_min 63, every line VII, maximum mana 466 with the buffs. The scripted ACE (`ScriptedCaster`) models that cost: Mana Conversion is (Focus + Self) / 6 + 169 ranks (the SkillTable's formula, pinned by `The_scripted_mages_mana_conversion_formula_is_the_dats`), 258 unbuffed and 311 after the cycle, with a seeded roll. At the live economy (0.2 mana a second, 1.85 s a cast) the scripted cycle takes 59 to 61 s over sixteen seeds (live 56 s) with 0 to 1 mana casts (live 4): the time matches, the mana casts do not. The live mage paid about the full price for its first five casts (mana 63 after five casts of up to 70), more than the model's reduction gives; the live Mana Conversion current skill was not recorded (the read model has no read of skill 16), so the gap is not explained. The ceiling economy (`ScriptedCaster.CeilingCost`) is kept as the worst case: 1,325 s, 48 mana casts (24 transfers, 24 revitalizes), 58 mode changes, 74 casts. With stamina regeneration 0 the worst case takes over 3,300 s and the first buffs (VI, 2,700 s) expire before it ends. `GearKit.ManaCastsPerCycle` stays 28 as the kit's worst-case figure, kept on purpose (the live count is 4). The smoke step `login-buff` gives the Godot run 900 s (login, gear and a cycle of about 60 s).

**The stand-in's time base for the cycle (M3 2.1 s2a).** The stand-in's cast timeline is the buff cycle's offline time base: a self buff with three or more scarabs takes (the sum of the wind-ups plus the gesture) seconds to take effect, plus 1 s of recoil, not the 1 s of M3 1.2. No profile spell has two scarabs and every profile buff is FastCast (a gesture and the target effect), so the profile's own casts take the gesture and the recoil only.

**BuffScenario** (`Session/Scenarios/BuffScenario.cs`, M3 2.1 s1; `BuffScenarioTests`). The stages are start, gear, mode-magic, cycle, rebuff, mode-peace, logging-off, quitting, done. `endSession: false` ends at done in the world (for the game's leg and the tests); the optional `cycle:` argument runs the scenario again on a cycle object that already ran (that run's ordered pass casts nothing; a new object recasts the two armour lines). Success: the gear is on, both passes ended, 24/24 lines active, no miss, and the forced pass recast a line while it was active. `CycleCast` and `CycleArmourCast` count the ordered pass only (the forced pass always casts one line). `new BuffScenario(spells, account, BuffOptions, log, arrivalSeconds, endSession, cycle)` or over a `GearKit`; `Step(session, now)` every frame; `Cycle`, `CycleResult`, `ForcedResult`, `VerdictFields()`, `Gear`. `ScriptedCaster` answers `ChangeCombatMode` and wears the kit's own weenies, so the gear scenario sees a ready mage. Live tests, written by s1 and run by the lead: `BuffLoopLiveTests` (Loopback; asserts the gear, 24/24, no miss, each cast at the rule's level from the skill its log line read, every line VII or better, `rebuffed` and `forced` yes, `min_remaining_s` above 0, prints `buff_live <fields>`; a second run casts no self line in the ordered pass and at most the two armour lines, prints `buff_live_second self_casts=<n> armour_casts=<n> <fields>`) and `EnchantmentExpiryLiveTests` (Loopback and Tier=Soak; D26: ACE's expiry path, `EnchantmentManager.HeartBeat` 1220 subtracts the interval from every entry and `Remove` 312-352 sends 0x02C3 and an F750 with Sound.SpellExpire 0x96 for every layer, a surpassed one too; it casts the cheapest 1,800 s self spell the mage knows, waits at most the duration plus 120 s, asserts the registry no longer holds it, the `EnchantmentChanged` Removed event named it and an `ObjectSoundPlayed` for the player with sound 0x96 followed; prints `expiry_live spell=<id> duration_s=<n> removed_after_s=<n> f750=<n>`).

**The re-buff driver (M4 4.1 a and c; `RebuffDriver`, `Session/Mage/RebuffDriver.cs`; closes `m3-buff-rebuff-driver`).** `RebuffDriver` is the caller of `BuffCycle.RebuffDue` and of `StartRebuff(forced: false)`. Every `--buff-auto-check-s` of the session clock it lists the due lines (with the margin `--buff-rebuff-margin-s`) and, when a line is due and no guard holds, starts one unforced pass. It enters the magic combat mode itself, returns to NonCombat afterwards only when it changed the mode, and ticks the cycle to its end. It never forces a pass. Its states are Off, Idle, Waiting (the name of the guard), Running and BackingOff; `Passes`, `LastResult`, `LastFailure` and `NextCheckAt` are read by its host and its tests. It is `IDisposable` (it unsubscribes the damage notices). The constants (the 10 s damage window, the 60 s back-off and the 120 s warning) are named on `RebuffDriver`; `RebuffDue` allocates one list per check and nothing between checks.
- *The guards, in order.* "not in the world yet", "in portal space", "dead" (current health 0), "the buff cycle is running another pass", "another action holds a lane" (the Action API has an action queued or in flight) and "in a fight" (combat mode melee or missile, or a defender notice 0x01B2 within 10 s). A wait is logged once per change as `[buff] re-buff driver: a line is due and the pass waits: <guard>`; a guard that holds 120 s with a line due logs a WARN naming the first due line and the least time left, again every 120 s.
- *A failed pass.* A pass that fails (the cycle Failed, the combat mode refused, lines missed, or nothing cast although a line was due) backs off 60 s (`LastFailure`, `NextCheckAt`). A pass that casts nothing because nothing was due is Idle, not a failure. The driver's cycle is seeded first: it appraises and restores the armour ledger before any armour line counts as due or any combat mode is entered (`Phase.Seed`; `Passes` does not move).
- *The play host (M4 4.1 c; `RebuffDriverHost` in `scripts/Session`, over the engine-free `Session/Mage/RebuffHostLogic.cs`).* A thin Godot node: it loads the SpellTable on a worker when asked, ticks the driver once a frame and saves the armour ledger at exit. The toggle is the Pause key (`LifestoneHotkeys.AutoBuffKey`, DirectInput 0xC5, action 0x7F000003; retail's game map does not bind Pause), and `--buff-auto on` turns the driver on at entry; either switch turns it on and off. Only BSD Mage has a buff profile (`BuffProfiles`); another character gets "[Lifestone] no buff profile for this character". The notices are `ChatKind.Notice` lines starting "[Lifestone] ": auto-buff on and off, re-buffing (with the lines), re-buffed (with the count) and re-buff failed (with the reason). The host is installed by one line in `PlayMode.Install` when a character enters, and not for `--login-check` runs. A press of Pause before the host is wired logs `TODO(m4-autobuff-key-unwired)`.
- *The ledger's per-login seeding and saving* is in the armour ledger paragraph below. `TODO(m4-buff-driver-lane-holds)`: the lane guard sees queued and in-flight actions only, not a lane that is merely held (a cast awaiting its UseDone, a heal's Ready hold); the pass's first action then waits in its own lane queue, which is safe.

**The armour ledger (M4 4.1 b and fix; `ArmourBuffLedger`, `Session/Mage/ArmourBuffLedger.cs`; `ArmourLedgerSeeder`, `ArmourLedgerFileStore`; closes `m3-buff-armour-memory`).** The armour lines' expiry is kept per worn piece in the session's `ArmourBuffLedger`, one per login (`ArmourBuffLedger.For(state)`). `BuffCycle` takes it by an optional constructor parameter; without one the cycle keeps a private ledger and seeds nothing. `BuffScenario` hands every cycle it makes the session's ledger, so a second cycle object casts no known armour line, and the driver's cycles share it. `Remaining` is 0 for none or an ended line, NaN for a line present with an unknown time left. `Forget` drops a piece or a line, and `Prune` drops the pieces no longer worn; the cycle prunes at each collection.

- *The appraisal seed.* When a cycle holds a shared ledger that is empty and unseeded (a login), `Start` (and `StartRebuff`) first appraises each worn Armor and Clothing piece once through `IActionApi.Identify` (stage `seed`, `BuffCycle.StageSeed`). A line whose spell, or a spell of the same SpellTable category with at least its Power, is in the piece's `Appraisal.ItemEnchantments` is recorded present with an unknown time left; the cycle counts it active and due at once (0 s left), and `min_remaining_s` is 0 while it is unknown. An appraisal that does not answer costs 5 s (the tracker's timeout) once per login, is logged 'gave no tables', and its piece is cast as before.
- *The clock across a log-off.* ACE counts an item enchantment down by its owner's heartbeat only (`EnchantmentManager.cs` 1217-1233, `PropertiesEnchantmentRegistryExtensions.cs` 239-259: `StartTime -= heartbeatInterval`, expired at `-Duration`). The worn pieces are ticked from `Creature_Tick.cs` 15-27, the pack from `Container_Tick.cs` 38-51 and `WorldObject_Tick.cs` 98-101, with no catch-up at login, so the clock stops while the owner is offline. `ArmourBuffLedger.Export(guid, now)` keeps the known seconds left as JSON keyed by the character's guid (`IArmourLedgerStore`).
- *The one-shot store (fix, lane c).* `ArmourLedgerFileStore` (Client, no I/O) reads and writes through `IArmourLedgerFiles`; the game's adapter `UserDataFiles` (`RebuffDriverHost.cs`) keeps the file `user://armour/armour-<guid 8 hex>.json` with no name in it. It is saved when the phase leaves the world and at the host's exit, and only by a session that ran the ledger: a save mid-play would overstate the time left, because ACE's clock runs while the character is online, so a hard kill loses the save (the conservative rule then). The store is read and cleared once at the login of a profile character (`RebuffHostLogic.Bind`), and the seeder writes an empty snapshot right after reading one, so a second login cannot use the same seconds. `ArmourLedgerSeeder` restores a saved entry only when the appraisal shows the same spell id on the same piece; the seconds played since the login (`ArmourBuffLedger.LoggedInAt`) are counted, and a line they use up is present with an unknown time (due at once). Under `-IsolateUserData` the folder is per isolated slot, so one smoke run does not carry the file to the next. The one gap of 4.1 b, the missing host store, is closed here.
- *With no armour worn* the armour lines are not wanted: `RebuffDue` and a re-buff pass (forced too) skip them with no miss, logged once (`NoteNoArmour`, cleared when a piece is worn). The ordered cycle (`Start`) is unchanged and still records `no-worn-piece`.

**Since M5 item 3 (section 53).** The mage's kit is the ground's supplies, not The Healer's Heart and the classic armour: `GearKit.Build(spells, account, heroRing, ground)` is the vendor Wand 2472 wielded (never weenie 8552), the vendor robe worn, the four foci (15268 to 15271), each stock line to its keep and the purse, granted once by the build (`BuildVerifyStation.MageBuildOf`, `GearKit.KitPlan.For`). The build also ties the ground's portal (`PortalTieLeg`), and the buff cycle skips a level whose foci the pack does not hold. The account name no longer feeds the foci formula. Where this section names the Healer's Heart, the component ids or the decoys as the kit, that is M3's and M4's kit.

## 30. The buff leg: `--login-check=buff` (M3 2.1 s3, fix A; D20, D22, D25, D27, D28; DoD 5, 6 and 13; M4 4.1 c, DoD 14)

A kind of its own, in the style of section 28: never part of `--login-check=all`, hooked by one line (`BuffLegRunner.Install`, ahead of the legs' construction) in `LoginCheckRunner.Install`, as the throwaway leg is. The game enters BSD Mage on the test account (absent: it fails loudly, "run --scenario=mage-build"), waits for the arrival (Travel Present, 3 s settle), re-seats the wand, runs `BuffScenario` (section 29) with `endSession: false` in the game's session (the gear, Magic mode, the ordered cycle with its upgrade pass, then the forced re-buff pass, or with `--buff-auto on` the re-buff driver's pass (below), NonCombat), logs off and quits. A failure at any stage logs off before it is reported. `BuffLegRunner` is in the Game project; `BuffLegVerdict` and `BuffLegPhases` are in Client, because the Game project may not name `Lifestone.Net`.

**The loading worker.** The runner starts a worker at install that reads the SpellTable and the login name and builds the `GearKit` there (review 2.1-R8, fix A): the name is only hashed into the kit's component formulas, never stored, logged or printed, appears in no field or result, and the log line "the gear kit was built on the loading worker, off the game thread" carries `build_ms`. The runner hands the kit to `BuffScenario`. Per frame the runner reads counters only (no handler, no LINQ, no allocation); nothing new is JIT-warmed for it (section 27's warm-up note covers the avatar adapter's cast path).

**The re-seat.** ACE sends no F750 for a cast; it sends one on a wield or unwield (`Player_Inventory.cs` 306, 405; `Creature_Equipment.cs` 360, 436), on an enchantment's expiry (`EnchantmentManager.cs` 331) and on an item received. On a ready, buffed mage none of these is certain, so before Magic mode the leg moves the wielded wand to the pack (`MoveToPack`) and wields it again in its Held location (`Wield`), both through the Action API. That is the leg's fixture for D27's `sounds_server>=1`, not a gear step; the log says so (`buff: re-seating the wielded wand ...`). A wand in the pack is just wielded; no wand at all is left to the gear step. Whether the wield's F750 sounds (a wave exists) is first seen live: with `sounds_server=0` the leg fails with the D27 sentence.

**The driver leg (M4 4.1 c, DoD 14; `--buff-auto=on`).** With `--buff-auto on` the leg runs no forced pass. After the ordered cycle the scenario (stage `driver`) makes a second `BuffCycle` with the driver's margin (the shortest line's remaining time less 30 s, at least 1 s; a cycle's own re-buff pass uses the margin of the options it was made with, which is why the driver gets a second cycle) and ticks a `RebuffDriver` until one pass ends. The verdict then prints the driver pass's `rebuffed=` and pass fields with `forced=no`, and ends `driver=on rebuff_lines=<n> rebuff_s=<s>` (report-only; `ok=` judges `rebuffed=yes` from the driver). The review frames are three of the driver pass's casts and a fourth (`<stem>-4.png`) of the chat with the "[Lifestone] re-buffed" notice. The leg reads the `--buff-*` options (`BuffLegRunner`; a bad value fails at the first frame). A mage that arrives buffed with an empty store can read `rebuffed=no` on the first driver run, because `min_remaining_s` is 0 for an appraised armour line of unknown time (the margin floors at 1 s and the pass recasts those lines); the first live run writes the store. The smoke step `login-buff` passes `--buff-auto=on`.

**The 0x0D login wait (M4 4.1 fix, D18 extended; `LoginRetryPolicy`).** A Lifestone addition; retail has no wait. An EnterWorld refused with CharacterError 0x0D (ACE still holds the character online) is retried every 5 s while the total waited stays within 75 s (`LoginRetryPolicy.CharacterInWorldWait` and `CharacterInWorldDelay`, decided by `DecideEnterWorldRetry`); the attempt count does not apply to it. Each retry logs the attempt and `waited X of at most 75 s`, and the final refusal logs `after waiting N s`. The request's 30 s cap restarts at each 0x0D resend, and the action's backstop `ActionPolicy.EnterWorldSeconds` is 110 s (30 + 75 + 5 s slack; it was 105 before M4 4.1 fix2b). The other codes keep D18's attempts rule (0x01, 0x15 and a lost ConnectRequest: 3 retries, 3 s apart). `--retail-net` (`LoginRetryPolicy.Retail`) waits for nothing. The 60 s are ACE's (commit 95ef426d), and the 75 s are `LoginRetryPolicy.BundledSessionTimeoutSeconds` (60, the bundled config's `DefaultSessionTimeout` in `server/templates/Config.template.js`; a test fails on drift) plus `LogoutAllowanceSeconds` (15, Lifestone's estimate of the logout); the EnterWorld backstop is 30 + 75 + 5 s slack (`ActionPolicy.EnterWorldSlackSeconds`) = 110 s (R12, R13). ACE's sources: `CharacterHandler.cs` 230-234 refuses 0x0D while the character is still online; `NetworkSession.cs` 332-338 and `Session.cs` 141-145 and 319-321 time a session out 60 s after its last packet (the bundled config's `DefaultSessionTimeout`); `Player.cs` 499-627 runs the logout; `PlayerManager.cs` 92-99 and 132-141 force a log-off after 15 minutes, the ceiling.

**The verdict.** `[logincheck] buff done ok=<..> gear= worn= cast=ok|fizzled|failed|n/a buffs=<active>/<wanted> rebuffed= forced= min_remaining_s= levels= cycle_s= cast_scripts= cast_sounds= cast_windows= cast_frame_max_ms= sounds_server= fizzles= misses=` (`BuffLegVerdict.Compose`, offline-tested by `BuffLegVerdictTests`). `buffs=` prints the lines whose buff is active at the end (from the registry and, for the armour lines, the cast's per-piece chat lines) over the lines wanted, as the scenario does. Judged (`ok=`, D28): the scenario ended without a failure, `gear=wielded`, every wanted line active (`buffs=24/24`), `rebuffed=yes`, and `sounds_server>=1` (D27). Report-only in this gate: `cast=` (D20: `ok` when every cast that ended was confirmed or a fizzle its retry recovered, `fizzled` when a line was missed by fizzles (`fizzled`, `upgrade-fizzled`), `failed` for any other failure or miss, `n/a` when nothing was cast), `cast_scripts`, `cast_sounds`, `cast_windows` (deltas of `AvatarStats.CastScripts`, `CastSounds` and `CastWindows` around the scenario, `docs/avatars.md` section 17), `cast_frame_max_ms` (the worst frame while a cast window is open; the frames of the review captures are left out), `forced`, `min_remaining_s`, `levels`, `cycle_s`, `fizzles`, `misses`. `sounds_server` is the F750 voices started between the Enter and the scenario's end (`SoundLegTally`, one leg, `buff-leg`).

**The frames (review 2.1-R9, fix A).** With `--play-screenshot=PATH` and no `--play-screenshot-after`, the leg saves three 800x600 frames of the forced pass's cast (the last self buff of the cycle; a fully buffed mage casts only the forced line; with `--buff-auto on` the frames are the driver pass's, and a fourth, `<stem>-4.png`, is the chat), with the developer HUD hidden: at the cast's effect time (the first script played on the player in the forced pass) plus 0.1, 0.4 and 0.8 s, as `<stem>-1.png`, `-2.png` and `-3.png`. Each path is logged with its delay (`buff: review frame N of 3 at the cast effect + D s: PATH`). The scenario is held up to 3 s while they are taken, so `cycle_s` includes the hold, about 1 s. A forced pass with no cast logs "the forced pass cast no self buff, so no frame was taken". The detail line's field is `review_frames` (it was `mid_cast_frame`). `PlayOptions.Parse` moves the path to `BuffShotPath` for this kind, so play mode's own capture (a fixed wait, then quit) does not fire; `PlayMode.PlayShotDeveloperHudNames` is `internal` for the leg. With `--play-screenshot-after` the old behaviour stays. The frames are derived from the retail dats' art and stay under `cache/`.

**Limits and run.** The runner's limit is `BuffLegRunner.WholeCheckSeconds`, 600 s, from the live measure (a cycle took 56 s of session time for 26 casts; the scripted worst case, 1,325 s, no longer sets it), and a log off after a failure may take 120 s more. The smoke step gives the Godot run 900 s (login, gear and a cycle of about 60 s). Run it live (the lead only, backend running, BSD Mage built): `pwsh -File tools\smoke.ps1 -Backend require -Only login-buff`, or `pwsh -File tools\godot.ps1 -Run -Offscreen -IsolateUserData -TimeoutSeconds 900 -Resolution 800x600 -GameArgs:'--account=test --login-check=buff --play-screenshot=cache/m3/2-1/buff-cast.png'`. The step is skipped under `-Backend auto` and `start` (it changes the character: buffs on, components burned) and slice-check never runs it. The `--login-check` help in `OptionCatalog.Play.cs` names the `buff` kind and the walking legs' judge; `--play-screenshot` names the three frames.

**Offline.** The verdict is tested without Godot (`BuffLegVerdictTests`); the smoke judge `Test-RecordedSources` is run for real by `BuffLegJudgesTests`; the runner's stage machine is not tested offline (no stand-in serves BSD Mage and a cast on the player's body is the game's own, `docs/avatars.md` section 17): the lead's live leg is its proof.

**The options.** The leg reads the `--buff-*` options (M4 4.1 c; `m3-buff-leg-options` is closed): with none given, the defaults are the ones it used before.

**The verdict and the cast counts (M3 4.2 x3, x2).** The leg's line ends `surpassed= cast_scripts_expected=<casts of both passes> mana_tries= ... proved=[ frame_over_50=yes]`. `cast_frame_max_ms` over 50 ms prints the flag and passes; over 75 ms it is not judged (`TODO(m3-buff-cast-frame-judge)`, M4). `cast_scripts=2` for a 26-cast cycle was a bug, not a quiet character (corrected 2026-10-31, `m5-buff-effects-drawn`). A live pass with every line due counted the F755s on the player: the 20 creature and life casts brought none, and the two that came were the armour lines' (EnchantUpWhite and EnchantUpPurple, which ACE plays on the player for a cast at the caster). The client sent a SelfTargeted spell untargeted (0x0048), and ACE plays a cast's target effect only when the cast names a target (`WorldObject_Magic.cs` `DoSpellEffects` 355-367: the untargeted path's `SetCastParams` passes a null target), so every creature and life self buff went without its orbs or shell. Retail's client sends such a spell at the player's own id (`ClientMagicSystem::CastSpell`, 11.6096 0x00568DE0: `SpellFlags.SelfTargeted` goes through `FreeHandsAndCastSpell` 0x00567C90 with the player's id, and only a spell with no target type goes out untargeted); `CastTracker.SendTarget` now does the same, and the same pass counts `cast_scripts=28` for 26 casts (some casts play two). The 24 buff spells have no CasterEffect, only a TargetEffect. `BuffCastScriptsTests` runs a cycle's casts through the avatar set (every self script played in its window; the armour ones counted `ServerScriptsMissing`).


**A hand run of a buff or death leg on a profile character (M4 4.1 doc pass 2; review R14).** A hand run of any leg that plays a profile character online (`bsd-fight`, `bsd-death`) must pass `-IsolateUserData`, or the next play login restores overstated armour seconds (review R14). The gap is documented in the remarks of `RebuffHostLogic` as `TODO(m4-buff-ledger-hand-legs)`; it is not built.

## 31. The fight loop (M3 3.1; D10, D12, D21; DoD 3, 7, 8, 9, 11)

Item 3.1 puts BSD Mage in the Black Spawn Den and has it kill a Tusker Slave with Flame Bolt, by the Action API and `PlayerTools` only. The pieces: the den's data (`BlackSpawnDen`, the table queries), the counters (`FightTally`), the scenario (`FightScenario`, with its walk), the headless `--scenario=fight`, the game's leg `--login-check=bsd-fight` (`FightLegRunner`), the offline stand-in's fight (section 16) and the projectile on screen (`docs/avatars.md` section 17). The slice table is `docs/milestones/m3-plan.md` section 4.7.

**The den by generator, and the floor rule (D12; m3-plan section 4.7).** The den is landblock 0x0105. Three generators (wcid 3955) populate it: 0x70105022 in cell 0x0154 (Drudge Stalkers, Drudge Raveners, Tusker Guards), and 0x7010507A and 0x7010507B in cell 0x0382 (Virindi, Virindi Masters, Tusker Slaves, Tusker Guards). The arrival's floor (z 0, the cell 0x0375 of the Places row) holds only Tusker Slaves (wcid 1628): 17 spawns in 12 cells. The drudges and most of the virindi live in the north wing below it (z -24 to -30), with two virindi outliers among the tusker guards' cells. **The floor rule:** the loop's waypoints are that floor's cells, and its target filter takes wcid 1628 or 1629 whose cell is on that floor, never 23, 237, 1609 or 1610. The map is `cache/m3/3-1/den-map.md` (a read-only SELECT of `landblock_instance` and `landblock_instance_link`; `cache/` is gitignored). **Superseded by M5 item 2 (section 52):** the walk and the target filter no longer stop at this z 0 floor. The den is the profile `black-spawn-den-aluvian`'s six storeys (z 0 to -30, 499 walkable cells), the loop takes the hunted slots of every storey (78 of the 117), and the three generators (900 s delay, 60 s tick) are unchanged. The floor rule above is M3's.

**`BlackSpawnDen` and the Places row (`Session/Mage/BlackSpawnDen.cs`, `Interiors/InteriorLocations.cs`).** Retired by M5 item 2 (section 52): the class is gone. Its facts are the profile `black-spawn-den-aluvian`'s, read through `GroundRules`, `GroundRooms` and `HuntingGroundTests`, and the Places row `InteriorLocations.BlackSpawnDenAluvian` stays. What follows is M3's record: `BlackSpawnDen.Landblock` (0x0105), `FloorLowWords` (the floor's cell low words: the 13 cells of s0 and, since lane fa, the connected z 0 floor read from the dat's cell graph, 158 cells in all, minus the exit portal's room 0x0341-0x0343 and 0x035C, which `IsExcluded` holds), `Arrival` (a `CellFrame`), `IsExcluded(lowWord)`, `IsFloorCell(fullCell)` (a bit-mask lookup; exclusions win), `IsTarget(wcid, fullCell)` and `LeftFloor(cell)` (landblock 0x0105 and not a floor cell: the walks fail with "the walker left the den's floor" the moment it is true, review 3.1-R4; a settle off the floor fails with "settle: the player settled in cell ...", D12). The Places row is `InteriorLocations.BlackSpawnDenAluvian` (key `black-spawn-den-aluvian`, cell 0x01050375, (100, -210, 0), the portal's quaternion; in `All`, so `TeleportCatalog` and `PlayerTools.Place` reach it); `Dungeon(...)` takes an optional `source` string, and `CatalogWithInteriorsTests` pins 7 dungeons.

**`NearestCreature` and `HealthOf` (`Contracts/WorldObjectTable.cs`).** `NearestCreature(wcids, centre, maxMetres, cellFilter, out guid)` returns the nearest creature (`ItemType & Creature`, as `AvatarWire` reads it) of the listed weenie classes, by the 3D straight line in global metres (`CellFrame.ToGlobal`, so landblocks compare correctly). It skips a creature whose health is zero or below (an unknown health is kept), a creature whose forward motion is the Dead motion (`WorldObjectTable.DeadMotionLowWord`) and every guid in its `excluded` list (the scenario excludes a target whose kill notice named it, fix A). The filter is a cached delegate (the ground's cell filter, a method group in a field; section 52), so the call allocates nothing. `HealthOf(guid, out fraction)` returns the last UpdateHealth fraction (false until one came). Retail's attackability and target choice (`ClientCombatSystem::ObjectIsAttackable` 11.6096 0x0056B340, `GetAttackTarget` 0x0056B630, `Handle_Combat__QueryHealthResponse` 0x0056B2A0) are cited, not read: the server stays the judge of attackability.

**`FightTally` (`Session/Scenarios/FightTally.cs`).** A bus subscriber (`Start(view)`, `Dispose`, `Reset`; `NoteCast`, `NoteMiss`) that counts, with no allocation per line (a hand parser over spans): `Casts`, `Misses`, `DamageDealt` and `DamageLinesDealt`, `CriticalsDealt`, `DrainDealt`, `DamageTaken` (the spell damage taken plus the 0x01B2 melee damage), `SpellDamageTaken`, `DrainTaken`, `HitsTaken` (0x01B2 only), `Evades` (0x01B4), `Kills` (0x01AD), `LastKillText`, `XpGained` (the 0x02CF total-experience delta from the baseline taken at `Start`) and `UnparsedMagicLines` (report-only, expected to be non-zero). **The damage-line forms** are F7E0 lines of chat type Magic (0x07) from ACE `SpellProjectile.cs` 810-845, each with the optional prefixes "Critical hit! ", "Overpower! ", "Sneak Attack! " in that order: "You <verb> <target> for <n> points with <spell>." (dealt), "With <spell> you drain <n> points of <vital> from <target>." (dealt, `DrainDealt`, not damage), "<source> <verbs> you for <n> points with <spell>." (taken) and "<source> casts <spell> and drains <n> points of your <vital>." (taken, `DrainTaken`). Any other Magic line (a resist, a cast notice, an expiry) is `UnparsedMagicLines`, never guessed. **The killing blow has no line:** ACE calls `OnDeath` instead of writing it (`SpellProjectile.cs` 857-861), so `damage_dealt` is the blows before the kill, and the kill is the 0x01AD notice. `TargetKills` and `LastKillNamedTarget` (review 3.1-R7, report-only) count a 0x01AD whose text contains the target's name from its CreateObject (no name: `TODO(m3-fight-target-name)`).

**`FightScenario` (`Session/Scenarios/FightScenario.cs`; `--scenario=fight`).** Stages (`FightStages` flags Gear, Buffs, Place, Mode; All for the headless run): start, gear (`GearScenario`'s steps), buffs, place (`PlayerTools.Place` to the ground's `ArrivalDestination()`, a `place loc` request; section 52), teleporting, settle (3 s), mode-magic, fight, mode-peace, leave-den (the way out of the den before the end, `GroundExit`, section 52; not in the arena-here run), logging-off, quitting, done. The buff top-up runs `BuffCycle` itself with a 600 s margin (`TopUpSeconds`; not `BuffScenario`, which would run the gear twice and cast the forced pass), and is skipped and logged when all 24 lines have 600 s or more. The fight's phases: choose, mana, cast, hit, dead-wait, walk.
- *The target* is `WorldObjectTable.NearestCreature` over the ground's target wcids on its walkable cells (`GroundRules`; section 52), minus the dying and the named-dead.
- *The range* is ACE's (`Player_Magic.cs` 495-505): `min(BaseRangeConstant + (Init + Ranks) * BaseRangeMod, 75)` of the spell (`FightScenario.CastRange`, `MaxRange` 75 m), from the skill's start level plus ranks, NOT the buffed value (the plan said buffed; ACE wins, and an over-estimate costs only an out-of-range answer, 0x0550).
- *The Flame Bolt level (D10)* is the highest of Flame Bolt I-VII (ids 27, 81, 82, 83, 84, 85 and 2128) that the spellbook knows and `GearCasts.Chance` puts at or above `--buff-cast-chance` (0.9) from the buffed War Magic (`FightScenario.ChooseLevel`; the offline arena run takes it from a fixed skill, below). One fizzle drops one level, once. Flame Bolt is a regular line, so `TODO(m3-volley-blast-levels)` does not apply to the fight.
- *The miss rule.* A cast is a named miss unless it was Confirmed (the projectile's CreateObject, section 7's `Cast` row) and a damage line or the kill follows within 3 s (`HitWaitSeconds`). The marks for hit detection are taken when the cast is sent, because the damage line can arrive in the frame of the confirmation. Three misses in a row (`MissesBeforeWalking`), `MissileOutOfRange` (0x0550) or no target in range walk one leg. A dead target waits up to 2 s (`KillNoticeSeconds`) for the 0x01AD. Since M5 item 1 a mana short is answered by the fight's mana order, not a bare wait: the bolt is sent from its try floor, then the dance, the potion or the rest for stamina; a wait that remains counts against `--fight-seconds` (section 45).
- *The limits.* The fight ends when `kills >= --fight-kills` AND `damage_dealt > 0` (fix A, DoD 8): a first hit that kills leaves `damage_dealt` 0, so the loop logs "fighting on until a damage line" once and goes on, bounded by `--fight-seconds`; the leg's `kills` can then exceed `--fight-kills`. The timeout reads `kills=K of N and damage_dealt=D`. The player's death, a walk that fails or stops without arriving, a SpellTable without the level, a read model without War Magic and a leg with no walker (`TODO(m3-fight-no-walker)`) fail loudly.
- *Self-defence (M5 7 F4; `Session/Scenarios/FightScenario.SelfDefence.cs`, `--fight-defend-forget-s`, default 20 s, 0 is off, Lifestone's own bound).* A living creature that attacks the player becomes the fight's target at once, before the profile's targets, whatever the profile says of it (avoided, no-go, outside the level band), until it is dead, gone from the object table, or has not attacked for the bound; then the profile's targets resume. The fight hears the attack on the session bus: GameEvent 0x01B2 DefenderNotification and 0x01B4 EvasionDefenderNotification (the `CombatEvent`s `DefenseHit` and `DefenseEvaded`) and the Magic-typed chat lines a creature's spell writes to the player ("<source> <verb> you for <n> points with <spell>.", "<source> casts <spell> and drains ...", "You resist the spell cast by <source>", "<source> casts <spell> on you"; ACE @95ef426d `SpellProjectile.cs` 810-845, `WorldObject_Magic.cs` 199 and 1429). Every notice names the attacker, none gives its guid (ACE's CurrentAttacker private update carries it and is not decoded, `TODO(m3-autotarget-attacker-id)`), so the attacker is the nearest living creature of that name in the object table (`NearestLivingCreatureNamed`); a name with none is logged once as `TODO(m5-defend-attacker-unseen)`. The pick (`Choose`) asks `DefenceStep` first: the nearest attacker in cast range with a clear line is the target, wherever it stands (a no-go cell included); with none, the nearest attacker decides: in a no-go cell the fight holds (nothing is walked into a no-go cell; logged once, "stands in the no-go cell ... holding"), else it walks (`StartWalk`, why "self-defence: no attacker is in range with a clear line"), and the walk phase drops that walk the frame an attacker is in range with a clear line. The walk has no leg toward a given creature, so the walk is the tour's next leg (`TODO(m5-defend-walk-to)`); an attacker that fights the player in melee is already in line. Logged once per attacker: "self-defence: <name> 0x<guid> attacked (<how>); fighting it before the targets" (how: damage notice, evasion notice, spell damage, spell drain, spell resisted, spell cast on the player). Its kill is any kill: it counts in `kills=` and `target_kills=` (the 0x01AD names the target the tally was given), reaches the respawn schedule (`GroundTour.Killed`: paired by spawn or by cell, else counted in `kills_unpaired=`), and the verdict line gains `defended=<n>` after `target_kills=` (report-only: the kills of creatures fought as self-defence). Not covered: the loot step runs before the pick, so an attacker is fought after the corpse in hand is done; a bolt in flight and the mana wait finish first. The option `--fight-defend-forget-s S` (0 to 600, default 20; 0 turns self-defence off) is registered in `Options/OptionCatalog.Fight.cs`.
- *The walk.* The stock `CellRoutePlanner.IsWalkable` refuses the den's plaza doorways, whose planes lean 11 degrees (normal Z 0.196), so it reaches 1 spawn cell of 12 (`TODO(m3-fight-planner-lean)`). M5 item 2 retired that walk (section 52): the walk is `GroundWalk` (headless: an `OfflineWalker` over the ground's collision, on a `NavRunner` over the ground's room graph, its waypoints from the tour) and `GameFightWalk` (the game's, below). `DenPlanner` (a BFS over the floor cells) and `DenWalk.Tour` are gone. `IFightWalk` is the seam (`Prepare`, `BeginLeg`, `Tick`, `Abandon`, `Failure`).
- *Verdict fields* (`FightScenario.VerdictFields()`, printed by the headless host as `fight <fields>` before `headless done`): the leg's fields below, and `target_kills=` last.

**Headless `--scenario=fight`.** `HeadlessScenario.Fight` (BSD Mage on the test account only, as gear and buff: `--character` and `--create-character` are ignored with a warning and any other `--account` is refused). The run builds the ground's collision and room graph for `GroundWalk` (`--fight-ground`, section 52), then runs the scenario with `FightStages.All`. Options (`OptionCatalog.Fight.cs`, group "Fight loop", read by the main scene and the headless host): `--fight-kills` (default 1; 0 is no kill limit with `--fight-minutes`, section 45) and `--fight-seconds` (default 300, session-clock seconds from the first cast stage); a bad value fails the run at once. The default time limit is 3,600 s (`Program.FightQuitAfterSeconds`: the gear, a top-up of up to 1,300 s, the fight, the log off); since M5 item 1 a fight limit above the 300 s default raises it (`Program.FightQuitAfterSecondsFor`, section 45). The `--buff-*` options of section 29 reach the headless run's top-up; the game's buff leg reads them too, since M4 4.1 c (`TODO(m3-buff-leg-options)` is closed; section 30). A failure at any stage logs off first.

**`FightLiveTests` (`tests/Lifestone.Loopback.Tests/Session/FightLiveTests.cs`, `[LoopbackFact]`; the lead only).** Runs `FightScenario` against the real ACE (the mod ready, BSD Mage built): the scenario ends without a failure at stage "done"; `kills >= 1` and `damage_dealt > 0`; projectiles seen and logged with their create position, velocity and Setup; not every cast a miss; the player did not die; the level is I-VII. It is what proved the projectile attribution rule live (below).

**The leg: `--login-check=bsd-fight`.** A kind of its own, in the style of section 30: never part of `--login-check=all`, hooked by one line (`FightLegRunner.Install`) in `LoginCheckRunner.Install`. The game enters BSD Mage on the test account (absent: it fails loudly, "run --scenario=mage-build"), waits for the arrival (Travel Present, 3 s settle) and runs `FightScenario` in the game's session with `FightStages.All` (the gear, the buff top-up, the place, Magic mode, Flame Bolt at the tuskers of the arrival's floor until `--fight-kills` kills or `--fight-seconds`, non-combat), then logs off and quits. A failure at any stage logs off before it is reported. A bad `--fight-kills` or `--fight-seconds` fails the leg at its first frame ("bad fight option: ...", review 3.1-R11); the options help says the game leg's buff top-up ignores the `--buff-*` options.
- *The game's player is the walker.* `GameFightWalk` (`scripts/Session/`) is the game's `IFightWalk`: it plans each leg over the ground's room graph (built on the loading worker) from the player's position and drives the player's body with a `NavRunner` (the session's keys, a `RouteConfirmer` on the player's collision, the session's creature feed with the dats' radii; section 52). Movement goes out through the host's sink (the presence gate), unaudited. A leg that enters a no-go cell stops the walk, named (`GroundWalk.NoGoReason`; the 'left the floor' rule of review 3.1-R4 is retired by D4). `TODO(m3-fight-no-walker)` is closed for the game.
- *The loading worker* reads the SpellTable, builds the `GearKit` (the login name is hashed inside the worker and stays in its locals), loads the ground's room graph (`GroundRooms.Load`) and calls `RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod` over every method of the `Lifestone.Client.Session.Scenarios` and `.Mage` namespaces and of `GameFightWalk` and `FightLegRunner` (the log line "the fight's methods were prepared on the loading worker" carries the count and the milliseconds), so none of the fight's code is compiled on the game thread in the den.
- *The verdict.* `[logincheck] bsd-fight done ok= kills= damage_dealt= spells_cast= misses= xp_gained= damage_taken= hits_taken= evades= projectiles= cast_frame_max_ms= fight_frame_max_ms= bsd_min_fps= fight_s= bolt_light= dyn_lights_max= viewer_light=` (`FightLegVerdict.Compose`, `FightLegVerdictTests`). The three light fields (M3 4.1 lane c; omitted when no frame was sampled): `bolt_light=yes|no` (every projectile that flew at least 3 frames had its own entry in `DynamicLightFrame` for at least 90% of its frames, `LightFrameSampler`; judged in the offline fight loop, report-only live), `dyn_lights_max=<n>`, `viewer_light=on|off`. **Judged:** no failure, `kills >= 1`, `damage_dealt > 0`. **Report-only** (a new judge is report-only in its first gate; 3.2 judges the worst frame against 50 ms, worst of three, and `damage_taken` and `hits_taken`, DoD 9): everything else. `damage_dealt` excludes the killing blow. `projectiles` is the delta of `AvatarStats.ProjectilesShown` over the leg (a Flame Bolt's one GfxObj degrades to nothing: "shown" is what is counted, and the bolt on screen is its particle trail; review 3.1-R6 renamed the counter from `ProjectilesDrawn`, the field keeps its name). A detail line follows: `bsd-fight detail seconds= level= range_m= walks= projectiles_created= projectile_explodes= projectile_windows= cast_windows= frames= den_frames= fps_windows= worst_fight_frame_at_s= worst_fight_frame_ms= worst_session_tick_ms= worst_pump_messages= worst_pump_ms= worst_retail_ui_step_ms= worst_play_mode_step_ms= worst_play_mode_work_ms= worst_play_mode_adapter_ms= worst_play_mode_events_applied= review_frames= scenario_verdict=` (the frame attribution of the slowest fight frame).
- *The frame measures* (`FightFrameSampler`, Client; one call per frame, no allocation; the engine's delta is the previous frame's whole time). `cast_frame_max_ms`: the worst frame while the cast window is open (the buff top-up's casts included). `fight_frame_max_ms`: the worst frame of the fight stage while a cast window or a projectile window (`AvatarSet.ProjectileWindowOpen`) is open or the loop has a target engaged (`FightScenario.Target != 0`). `bsd_min_fps`: the lowest one-second window of frames in the den (the player present, landblock 0x0105; the window in progress at the end, a window a skipped frame fell in and a window the player left are not counted). The frame after a saved review frame is left out of all three (the read-back stalls it).
- *The review frames.* With `--play-screenshot=PATH` and no `--play-screenshot-after` (`PlayOptions.Parse` moves the path to `BuffShotPath` for `bsd-fight` as for `buff`), the leg saves four 800x600 frames with the developer HUD hidden from the fight stage's start. `<stem>-bolt.png`: the first projectile created after the first damage line (the target in sight), plus 0.15 s. `<stem>-hit.png`: the Explode of a projectile whose damage line comes within 1 s of it (either order, paired by time), plus 0.1 s from that Explode; an Explode on a wall with no damage line gives no hit frame (review 3.1-R3). `<stem>-kill.png`: the 0x01AD kill plus 0.5 s, the kill line in the chat. `<stem>-viewer.png` (M3 4.1 lane c): the player in the den with no projectile alive for 1 s (logged as "no projectile alive for 1 s in the den"), the viewer light round it; it can fire before the first cast and holds the scenario a few frames. Flights under 3 frames are counted, not judged (`TODO(m3-bolt-light-short-flight)`). `FightShotPlan.Begin/Step(created, explodes, damageLines, kills)` is the pure plan: at most one frame a frame, two hidden frames before a save, each slot triggered once; the scenario is held while a frame is owed, at most 3 s after its trigger (past it the frame is given up and named). The log carries `fight: review frame <name> at its trigger + D s: PATH` for each and `fight: review frames: bolt=taken hit=taken kill=taken` at the end (a slot whose trigger never came reads `trigger never came`; it is never faked). The frames are derived from the retail dats and stay under `cache/`. With `--fight-storeys-min` 2 or more, away from the arena and with a tour that has an arrival storey, the first, bolt, hit and kill slots wait for a storey other than the arrival's: `FightShotPlan.WaitsForOtherStorey` re-bases them on every frame the body stands on the arrival's storey (`FightScenario.ReviewFramesWaitForOtherStorey`, `FightScenario.OffArrivalStorey`), so each triggers on an event of another storey. The viewer slot is unchanged, and the default is off. A slot whose trigger never came then reads `trigger never came (waited for a storey other than the arrival's)`.
- *Limits.* `FightLegRunner.WholeCheckSecondsFor(fightSeconds)` = login 60 + gear 120 + buff top-up 240 + teleport and settle 30 + `--fight-seconds` (default 300) = 750 s; a log off after a failure may take 120 s more (`RecoverySeconds`); the smoke step gives the Godot run 1200 s (a test pins 1200 >= 750 + 120 + 300).
- *Run it live* (the lead only, backend running, BSD Mage built): `pwsh -File tools\smoke.ps1 -Backend require -Only login-bsd`, or with the frames `pwsh -File tools\godot.ps1 -Run -Offscreen -IsolateUserData -TimeoutSeconds 1200 -Resolution 800x600 -GameArgs:'--account=test --login-check=bsd-fight --play-screenshot=cache/m3/3-1/fight.png'`.

**The smoke step `login-bsd`** (`tools/smoke.ps1`): `--account=test --login-check=bsd-fight`, 1200 s, a backend step run ONLY under `-Backend require` (SKIP under `auto` and `start`: it changes the character, with experience gained and components burned). Its judge is `bsd-fight done ok=true` and exit code 0, and it prints the verdict line. It sits in `$backendSteps` after `login-buff`; slice-check never runs it.

**The attribution rule, live (`TODO(m3-cast-projectile-attribution)`, D21).** Live (`FightLiveTests` and the `bsd-fight` leg, 2026-10-17): every one of the player's Flame Bolts was created at the player (about 1.2 m up, within the 5 m rule) with Flame Bolt's Setup 0x0200040D and a velocity of about 15 m/s along the aim, and each was taken; no other Missile object came near the player in the den, so the rule held. The wire still names no caster, so the TODO stays open for a crowd of casters (section 7, `Cast`).

**The whole loop offline** (retired: `PlayChecks.Fight.cs` was removed in M4 4.1 t1 with its rows, and the stand-in tusker it fought is gone since M4 4.1 t2; the fight is proved by the live legs and section 35's tests; the text below is the removed check's account, kept for history; after fb's own check, section 16). `FightScenario` with `FightOptions { ArenaHere = true }` runs against a second stand-in tusker (`StandInTusker.Make`, guid 0x80000B02, placed ahead of the player inside the range of level I). "Arena here" masks the stages to Mode (no gear, no top-up, no teleport), turns the cell filter off (any cell is a target) and takes the Flame Bolt level and the range from `FightOptions.ArenaSkill` (default 300, the stand-in's fixed skill, `TODO(m3-standin-magic-skill)`) through `FightScenario.ChooseLevel(uint skill, ...)`, with no spellbook: the stand-in's spell learn sends no magic update, so its book never holds Flame Bolt II to VII (`TODO(m3-standin-spellbook-updates)`; a first run chose level I, 1 point a blow). It is not on the command line (an init property). Three plain `Check`s and one `fight loop done` log line: the setup; the leg's own judge (`FightLegVerdict.Compose`: `kills >= 1`, `damage_dealt > 0`); and `projectiles_shown >= 1 projectile_explodes >= 1` over the fight (D28). The sampler's cast and fight maxima are reported.

**The offline play-checks of the item (retired, as above).** fb's check (`PlayChecks.Fight.cs`) was six plain `Check`s on the stand-in's tusker (casts, confirmed, kills, `damage_dealt`, damage lines, `xp_gained`, and the per-cast lines, hits, misses and kills) and a `fight done` log line. fc's check (`PlayChecks.Projectile.cs`), `a-stand-in-flame-bolt-is-shown-and-flies`, judges `projectiles_created>=1`, `projectiles_shown>=1`, that it moved and that its window opened and closed; it reports `wait_frames`, launches, explodes and pipelines, and a report-only line `projectile with meshes` (Shock Wave I). A shown Flame Bolt is not checked for its particles (the renderer has no per-object count, review 3.1-R6). The play-checks ran 172 checks at fd's last count (max frame 33.5 ms, offscreen); `a-stand-in-flame-bolt-...` and the Shock Wave line are in `docs/avatars.md` section 17.

**Option `--retail-sphereless-missile`** (`OptionCatalog.Play.cs`, `PlayOptions.RetailSphereless`, passed to `AvatarSet.RetailSpherelessMissile` where `PlayMode` sets `RetailUnhideFlash`): see `docs/avatars.md` section 17. It closes `TODO(m3-sphereless-missile)`.

**Tests.** `BlackSpawnDenTests` (retired by M5 item 2: `GroundRulesTests` and `HuntingGroundTests` hold its facts; section 52), `WorldObjectTableQueryTests`, `FightTallyTests` (s0); `FightScenarioTests` (scripted runs, the dat-backed planner and level tests, the arena-here run, `ChooseLevel(skill)`); `HeadlessFightOptionsTests`; `StandInFightTests` (fb); `ProjectileFlightTests` (fc, `docs/avatars.md` section 16); `FightLegRunnerTests` (sampler, plan, verdict wiring, option routing, the smoke step and its time) and `FightLegVerdictTests`; `GameChatTests` gained two (`docs/retail-ui.md` section 21.2). The runner's stage machine is not tested offline (no stand-in serves BSD Mage and the player body is the game's own), and `GameFightWalk` has no offline test (the Game project has none): the lead's live leg is its proof.

**Open TODO names of 3.1.** `m3-fight-walk-audit` (the legs are not audited against ACE), `m3-fight-planner-lean`, `m3-fight-left-floor` (named in the "left the floor" failure, for a planner or physics gap), `m3-fight-target-name`, `m3-standin-magic-skill`, `m3-standin-spellbook-updates`, `m3-cast-projectile-attribution` (above), the stand-in's own (section 16) and the projectile's (`m3-bolt-cell-tracking`, `m3-bolt-warm-up`; `docs/avatars.md` section 17). Closed: `m3-fight-leg`, `m3-fight-no-walker`, `m3-sphereless-missile` (the flag), `m3-standin-projectile-model`, `m3-standin-cast-plan-lazy`. Not applying: `m3-volley-blast-levels`. All in `docs/milestones/m3-plan.md` section 9.

**The clear-line pick and the wall shots (M3 4.2 p4, fix A).** The bolt goes at the nearest tusker with a clear line (`IFightWalk.HasClearLine`; `GameFightWalk`: `PhysicsWorld.SweepViewer`, a 0.3 m sphere, both ends at 2/3 of a 1.8 m body). None clear walks at once. The headless ground walk (`GroundWalk`) answers the same sweep on its own physics world, `GameFightWalk.ClearLine` (M5 item 2 F3): it answered the interface's default `true` before, so the headless fight bolted into rock (a 30-minute read of the den made 17 kills with `wall_hits=94` of 144 casts). A bolt that explodes short (velocity zero 0.6 s with no damage line, stopped before 80% of its flight) is a wall hit, and the walk moves on to the first clear one; `wall_hits=` is report-only beside `misses=`, and `bolt_light=no` and `viewer_light=off` fail every bsd-fight leg (`bolt_light` reads n/a when no flight had three frames). A wall hit is judged by distance: the bolt's stop point more than `FightScenario.WallStopShortMetres` (4 m) short of the target (`TODO(m3-fight-target-radius)`); a resist line since the cast is never a wall hit. `--fight-ignore-line` restores the old pick. The cause: the arrival cell 0x0375 and the tuskers' 0x0383 are 44.2 m apart through rock, and ACE and the client check no line (`ClientMagicSystem::CastSpell` 0x00568DE0). The sweeps cost 0.03 to 0.15 ms each for the den's longest blocked picks (fix A), so no cache is needed. Named: `TODO(m3-fight-los-radius)`, `m3-fight-los-target-height`, `m3-fight-los-approximation`, `m3-fight-wall-impact-point` (not built: the impact point is not logged), `m3-fight-approach-target` (not built), `m3-standin-wall-clip` (not built: the stand-in still flies bolts through walls), `m3-den-room-statics`. Tests: `FightLineOfSightTests` (the arrival is blocked to 0x0383; the 13-cell table pinned), five scenario tests, three verdict tests and one frame-plan test. The review frames are `-first`, `-bolt`, `-hit`, `-kill` and `-viewer`; `-first` is 0.15 s after the first projectile is created.


## 32. The death loop (M3 3.2; D17, D33-D36; DoD 9, 10, 11)

Item 3.2 has BSD Mage bound at Holtburg's lifestone, unbuffed in the Black Spawn Den, hit and killed by the Tusker Slaves, respawned at the lifestone with vitae and 75% vitals, and re-geared, by the Action API and `PlayerTools` only. The pieces: the two new actions (section 7), `NearestWithFlags`, the counters (`DeathTally`), the verdict (`DeathLegVerdict`), the scenario (`DeathScenario` with its approach walk), the headless `--scenario=death-and-regear`, the loopback tests, the game's leg `--login-check=bsd-death` (`DeathLegRunner`, with `DeathFrameSampler` and `DeathShotPlan`), the offline stand-in's death (section 16) and the three combat windows (`docs/retail-ui.md` section 23.8). The slice table is `docs/milestones/m3-plan.md` section 4.8.

**What ACE does at a player's death** (`Player_Death.cs` 34-292; the plan's section 4.8 preamble has the line numbers). In this order: `OnDeath` sends the VictimNotification 0x01AC to the player (and 0x019E to the others); `Die` sends the Dead motion (F74C, stance NonCombat), 0x02E9 health 0, 0x02CD NumDeaths, DeathLevel and VitaeCpPool, the vitae 0x02C2 (spell 666, 5% a death, at most 40%: `EnchantmentManager.cs` 391-443) and then **0x02C6 with every enchantment removed** (211-216; BSD Mage has no `AugmentationSpellsRemainPastDeath`). After the Dead animation plus 1 s: the lost items, then the corpse (`CreateCorpse`, `Creature_Death.cs` 428: a level-126 player drops level/10 plus 0 to 2 items by value, wielded ones included, 347-540; a wielded item leaves by `TryDequipObjectWithNetworking(ToCorpseOnDeath)` and the client sees 02DA Wielder 0, 02CE 0, F74A, the unwield sound and F747, with **no** 0x0024), and in the same tick the teleport through portal space (F751) to `Sanctuary ?? Instantiation`, then NonCombat, the lifestone protection (60 s, `SetLifestoneProtection`) and, 3 s after the arrival, 0x02E9 for health, stamina and mana at 75% of the maximum (262-282). So the corpse's CreateObject and the F751 come in one tick. BSD Mage's sanctuary is its creation spot until it uses a lifestone (`Lifestone.cs` 44-75: the Sanctuary motion, the attune line "You have attuned your spirit to this Lifestone. You will resurrect here after you die.", half the stamina). ACE answers a Use of the lifestone with UseDone before that chain ends, and moves an out-of-range player (`CreateMoveToChain`) only while the client follows its MoveToObject. **The protection is dispelled by any teleport** (`Player_Location.cs` 702-703, `Player_Death.cs` 937-942: "Your actions have dispelled the Lifestone's magic!"; it also ends by itself after 60 s with "You're no longer protected by the Lifestone's magic!", 934), so a loop that reaches the den by a teleport never meets the protected blow (0x028A 0x0502, `Player_Combat.cs` 299, 416-418, 480-482; M3 3.2 review R2): `protected=` stays 0 live and the protected blow is proved offline only (below).

**The suicide (D33).** ACE has a player-side suicide: GameAction 0x0279 (`GameActionDie`; `Player_Death.cs` 299-345): five HearSpeech lines 3 s apart (15 s), then `Die` without 0x01AC and 0x019E and with WeenieError YouKilledYourself after the NumDeaths update; WeenieError YoureTooBusy while the player is dead or teleporting; a second request while one is in progress is dropped without a word; `HandleSuicide` stops when NumDeaths changed meanwhile (the tuskers killed the player first). `IActionApi.Suicide` and `AddSpellFavorite` are in section 7. A death removes every enchantment, so the loop takes one suicide in the den first when any enchantment but the vitae is active: the purge makes the mage unbuffed, the corpse stays in the den (never in Holtburg, where the baseline's laps run), and a buffed run dies twice (two vitae steps).

**`NearestWithFlags` (`Contracts/WorldObjectTable.cs`).** `NearestWithFlags(descriptionFlags, in centre, maxMetres, out guid)` returns the nearest object with a position whose CreateObject description flags contain any of the bits, in `NearestCreature`'s frame (global metres, no allocation): `WorldObjectTable.LifestoneFlag` 0x4000 (`BF_LIFESTONE`) and `CorpseFlag` 0x2000 (`BF_CORPSE`), as `WorldContentCensus` reads them.

**`DeathTally` (`Session/Scenarios/DeathTally.cs`).** A bus subscriber like `FightTally` (`Start(view)`, `Dispose`, `Reset`; no allocation per message) that reads ACE's order: `VictimNotices` (PlayerDied from 0x01AC only), `DeadMotions` (the player's F74C with forward Dead), `Deaths` (the NumDeaths delta, a suicide included), `VitaePercent` ((1 - the multiplier of spell 666) x 100, rounded), `Purges` (EnchantmentChanged Purged or PurgedBad: only a purge that removed something, because the reducer publishes nothing for an empty one, `TODO(m3-purge-empty)`; so the tuskers' death of an unbuffed mage reads `purged=0` while ACE did send 0x02C6, and DoD 10's "0x02C6 seen" is read at the suicide's purge of a buffed mage), `DeathTeleports` (the first F751 after a death signal), `HasArrival`, `ArrivalCell` and `ArrivalFrame` (the self-teleport position after it), `ProtectedHits` (WeenieError 0x0502 `LifestoneMagicProtectsYou`), the shares of the vitals (`HealthAfterArrivalPercent` and the stamina and mana ones, the **first** 0x02E9 of each vital after the arrival: ACE's three at +3 s, so the regeneration of the next minute does not move them, review R8; `VitalsAfterArrivalPercent` the lowest of the three once all three landed, NaN before; the share is the current over the maximum as the state computes it, so the vitae's reduction of the maximum is inside the denominator, 21/27 at 5%), `HealthMinPercent` and the embedded `Fight` (`HitsTaken`, `DamageTaken`, `Evades`). The killing blow has no 0x01B2, so a blow that kills is not a hit taken.
- *The corpse window (review R1).* `CorpsesSeen` and `LastCorpseGuid` count a CreateObject with the corpse flag whose name is exactly "Corpse of " + the player's name (ordinal) **only between a death signal and that death's arrival** (`_afterDeath || _awaitingArrival`); `DeathFrameSampler` uses the same rule (`_awaitingPortal || _awaitingArrival`). ACE keeps the earlier runs' corpses in the den and re-creates them for the client at every arrival and whenever one comes into view, so the live `corpse=7` and `corpse=9` of the first rounds counted old ones; the others are `StaleCorpses`, printed as `corpses_stale=` (report-only).
- *The protection line (review R2).* `ProtectionDispels` and `ProtectionExpiries` count the two Magic chat lines above; `Protection` is `dispelled`, `expired` or `none` (the last line wins) and prints as `protection=` (report-only). A live buffed run shows `dispelled` (the den teleport after the respawn); an unbuffed one with no teleport after its respawn shows `none` or `expired`.

**`DeathLegVerdict` (`Session/Scenarios/DeathLegVerdict.cs`).** `[logincheck] bsd-death done ok= died= suicides= vitae= respawn=lifestone|elsewhere|none corpse= purged= protected= vitals_pct= gear_restored=yes|no worn= hits_taken= damage_taken= evades= health_min_pct= death_s= death_frame_max_ms= fight_frame_max_ms= deaths= corpses_stale= protection=dispelled|expired|none corpse_looted=yes|partial|no|skipped items_recovered=<n> trip_s=<x|n/a> trip_abort=<reason|none> rebuffed=yes|no|skipped den_kills=<n> decoys=<n|n/a> decoy_safe=yes|no|n/a decoys_dropped=<n|n/a> gear_dropped=<n|n/a> decoys_recovered=<n|n/a> pretrip_heals=<n|n/a> gear_left=<n|n/a> mode_mismatch=<n>[ frame_over_50=yes]` (no name, no account; the fields are additive, a new one goes last). **Judged:** no failure, `died == 1` (exactly one death by the tuskers: the victim notices; the suicide sends none), `respawn == lifestone` (`ClassifyRespawn`: the arrival within `LifestoneRadiusMetres` = 15 m of a `BF_LIFESTONE` object in the table), `gear_restored == yes`, `damage_taken > 0`, `hits_taken >= 1` (DoD 9, D36: judged in the unbuffed leg; report-only in `bsd-fight`). **Judged** (M3 4.1 lane c): `death_frame_max_ms` over 75 ms fails; over 50 ms the line ends with `frame_over_50=yes`. **Report-only:** everything else, `vitae` (D35) included. The M4 2.2 s1 fields `corpse_looted=`, `items_recovered=`, `trip_s=` and `trip_abort=` are report-only too: `corpse_looted=yes` = every entry of the opened corpse came (no refusal, timeout or unresolved skip), the corpse was closed and nothing aborted; `partial` = some but not all came (an abort, such as the 40% health guard among the tuskers, included: the pick-up in flight at an abort is drained and counted); `no` = a trip began and none came; `skipped` = no trip (the option or the stage is off, or a precondition failed).  The verdict's `vitals_pct` is each vital's value read at the END of the respawn's 5 s wait (after ACE's 3 s restore, `Player_Death.cs` 262-282; the first 0x02E9 after the arrival can be an earlier regeneration, 2.2 review R1), never the trip's. `DeathScenario.VerdictFields()` carries them before ` bars= unbuff= bound=`. `DeathLegVerdict.Measure(tally, objects, suicides, gearRestored, worn, deathSeconds, deathFrameMaxMs, fightFrameMaxMs)` reads a tally and the object table. `FightLegVerdict` judges the frames since 3.2 s0: `cast_frame_max_ms` or `fight_frame_max_ms` over 75 ms fails, over 50 ms the line ends ` frame_over_50=yes` and the run passes (the lead reads the worst of three); an unmeasured frame is not judged. `damage_taken` and `hits_taken` stay report-only there.

**The death leg's new fields (M4 3.1 d1, d2; report-only in their first gate).** `rebuffed=` is yes when the inner scenario's buff stage ended with every profile line up (its `buffs=` note `skipped`, or `topped-up:<casts>` with `BuffCycle.Result.Active` equal to `Wanted`: `IFightLeg.BuffsAllUp`), no when it failed or ended with a line down, and skipped when the stage is off or never ran; `den_kills=` the inner fight's kills (0 when it did not run); `decoys=` the decoys the pack holds when the wait among the tuskers begins (`DecoyKit.IsDecoy` by weenie class; the unbuff suicide is a death too and can have dropped some, so the line is logged with a second `DecoyKit.Check` and a warning when it is not safe); `decoy_safe=` the gear stage's `DecoyCheck.Field` (`n/a` with decoys off or with no gear stage); `decoys_dropped=`, `gear_dropped=` and `decoys_recovered=` the listing's counts (above); `pretrip_heals=` the casts of the pre-trip heal. `n/a` stands for "not measured", never 0. The line carries `mode_mismatch=<n>` before the optional `frame_over_50=yes` (M4 3.1 g's `CombatModeWatch`, started with the scenario and stopped at its end or its failure; report-only; its first violation is logged as a warning and the detail line carries `mode_mismatch_unchecked=`).

**`DeathScenario` (`Session/Scenarios/DeathScenario.cs`, `DeathOptions.cs`; `--scenario=death-and-regear`).** `DeathStages` flags Gear, Bind, Den, Unbuff, Death, Regear, Vitae, Corpse, Rebuff = 256 and Fight = 512 (All). Each stage is logged once as `[death] stage <name>`: gear, bars, bind-place, bind-teleporting, bind-settle, bind-use, bind-line, den-place, den-teleporting, den-settle, unbuff, unbuff-dying, unbuff-arrival, unbuff-vitals, death-mode, death-wait, respawn, respawn-vitals, corpse-teleporting, corpse-settle, corpse-seek, corpse-opening, corpse-looting, corpse-closing, corpse-return, corpse-return-settle, regear, regear-settle, logging-off, quitting, done. M4 3.1 d1 and d2 add `unbuff-decoys` (after `unbuff-vitals`, only after a suicide with decoys on) and `rebuff`, `rebuff-peace`, `fight` and `pretrip-heal` (only when the health share is the one failing trip precondition). The order after `respawn-vitals` (`DeathScenario.Order.cs`): with the fight on (the default) `regear` (the plain kit, `kit.WithDecoys([])`, so the decoys on the corpse are not spawned a second time), `regear-settle`, `rebuff`, `rebuff-peace` (when the rebuff runs alone), `fight`, `pretrip-heal`, `corpse-teleporting` ... `corpse-return-settle`, `done`; with the fight off, M4 2.2's order (`corpse-*`, `regear`, then `rebuff` alone when it is on); both off is the 2.2 loop. `DeathLegRunner` runs `DeathStages.All`, so the order is chosen by `--death-rebuff on|off` and `--death-fight on|off` (default on; bad values are errors). Rebuff and fight are ONE composed inner `FightScenario` (an `IFightLeg`, from the `FightLegFactory` seam; the default `DeathScenario.DefaultFightLeg` wraps the real scenario), stepped as the regear's `GearScenario` is: `FightStages.Buffs` for the rebuff (the death purged every enchantment, so it is the full cycle), `Place | Mode` and the kill loop for the fight (`--death-fight-kills`, default 4; `--death-fight-seconds`, default 180); the rebuff alone drops the inner scenario at its own stage `place` and the mage leaves the magic mode (`rebuff-peace`). `DeathOptions.Rebuff`, `Fight`, `FightKills` and `FightSeconds`; `--death-stages` also takes `rebuff` and `fight`.
- *Gear and the spell bars.* The gear stage is `GearScenario`'s steps. The bars are inside the gear flag: `DeathScenario.PlanBars(Spellbook)` puts on bar `ProfileBar` the highest of its first seven spells the spellbook knows for each of the 31 arena profile lines (a line with none is left off and warned), and on bar `BoltBar` Flame Bolt I-VII (ids 27, 81-85 and 2128), each appended after what the bar already holds and only when absent, so a second login plans nothing (`bars=set:<n>` or `bars=ok`). **The bars are 0-based, as ACE's bar id (0 to 7): `ProfileBar` = 1 and `BoltBar` = 2 are the second and third tabs (II and III) of the spellcasting window (review R7); the first tab keeps whatever the character had.** The plan's "bar 1" and the highest-of-I-VII choice are the lane's reading; the owner changes either with one constant. ACE answers `AddSpellFavorite` with nothing and the read model's `SpellBars` changes only with the next PlayerDescription, so each is sent once and no echo is waited for.
- *The bind (D34).* `PlayerTools.Place` to Holtburg's point of interest (`TeleportCatalog.FromAce` of `AcePointsOfInterest` "Holtburg", sent as `place poi`), a 3 s settle, `NearestWithFlags(LifestoneFlag, player, 60 m)` (remembered: `LifestoneGuid`, `LifestonePosition`), then **the approach walk**, then `IActionApi.Use`. The walk is `IApproachWalk` (`Session/Scenarios/DeathApproach.cs`: `Prepare`, `Begin(target, stopWithin)`, `Tick`, `Abandon`), passed as the scenario's last constructor parameter `approach:`: the stop radius is the lifestone CreateObject's `UseRadius` (centre to centre, at least 0.8 m), else the named placeholder 1.5 m (`LifestoneApproachMetres`, `TODO(m3-lifestone-radius)`); nothing walks when the player is already within it; the walk is bounded by `ApproachSeconds` 30; a walk that ends stuck, failed or late still sends the Use (ACE's range check judges, loudly); with no walker the Use goes out with a warning (`TODO(m3-bind-approach)`). Headless it is `HoltburgApproach.Create(dats, log)` (an `OfflineWalker` over Holtburg's landblock and its eight neighbours with a `RouteDriver` over a one-goal route; it places a still-unplaced body from `Self.Position` as a fallback), in the game `GameApproachWalk` (below). The scenario ticks a prepared approach walker every frame from the Holtburg place until the bind takes, as `DenWalk` is ticked. **The attune line:** ACE answers UseDone before its chain sets the sanctuary, so the bind is done on the lifestone's Magic chat line (any Magic line after the Use within `BindLineSeconds` 15) plus the UseDone, logged as `bind: the lifestone's chat line: ...` (the text is the world database's, never guessed); a WeenieError of `BindFailureCodes` (0x001D YoureTooBusy, 0x0036 ActionCancelled, 0x0037 ObjectGone, 0x0498 YouHaveMovedTooFar, 0x049C YouWanderedTooFarToLinkWithLifestone, 0x058D YouCannotUseThatItem) or the timeout fails "the bind did not take" with the distance; the other WeenieErrors (0x051C chat channel joins) are logged as ignored. Distances are logged before the walk, after it and at the Use.
- *The den and the unbuff (D33).* `PlayerTools.Place` to the ground's arrival (section 31; section 52), a 3 s settle.
