When `ActiveEnchantments` holds any spell that a death's purge removes (not 666, not an item's lasting spell with duration -1, not a cooldown with a spell id above 0x7FFF: what ACE's `RemoveAllEnchantments` keeps, `EnchantmentManager.cs` 358-365, and the reducer keeps on 0x02C6) the scenario logs "unbuff: the mage is buffed (<n> enchantments); a suicide purges them as every death does", sends `Suicide()`, waits for the death, the death's teleport and the arrival (+5 s for ACE's 3 s of vitals), checks that none of those remains (else a loud failure) and runs the den stages again; `unbuff=suicide|none|killed|n/a`. **The race:** the tuskers hit the buffed mage during the suicide's 15 s countdown; if they kill it first (a 0x01AC before the suicide completes) their death purges as well, it is the run's death (`unbuff=killed`, `died=1`, `suicides=0`, a warning is logged) and the scenario goes straight to the respawn stage. The tally is not reset after the suicide: `deaths` counts both, `purged` includes the suicide's, `corpse` the death's own corpse of each, `died` only the tuskers'.
- *The death wait.* Non-combat (a mode change only if needed), `IFightWalk.BeginLeg` to the nearest tusker cell (the first leg of the ground's tour, section 52), then the wait: the health share is logged on each change of 10%; no 0x01B2, 0x01B4 or protection notice in `IdleLegSeconds` 60 starts the next leg; the death is a 0x01AC (or NumDeaths above the suicides); `--death-seconds` bounds it, and the timeout names hits, evades, protected, damage_taken, health_min_pct and legs (D17). A null walker fails the stage (`TODO(m3-fight-no-walker)`). `DeathOptions.StopAfterHits` (an init property, not on the command line; DoD 9's short mode) stops `StopAfterHitsSeconds` = **3 s** after the first 0x01B2 with the health share under 100% (the plan's and DoD 9's "one minute" was amended by the live fix, review R6), with no re-gear and a clean log off; a death first is reported (`DiedBeforeStop`), its respawn is report-only, and the mage logs off un-geared (the next run's gear stage repairs it).
- *The respawn, the classification frozen in the world.* The scenario waits for the death's F751 and the position (60 s, else `respawn=none`), the player present, then 5 s, and classifies with `DeathLegVerdict.ClassifyRespawn`; `elsewhere` is a failure naming the arrival cell. **The scenario freezes its measures once, in the world, when it concludes or fails** (`DeathScenario.FreezeMeasures`, called by `Conclude`, by `Fail` and by the game runner's `Fail`); `Outcome(deathFrameMaxMs, fightFrameMaxMs)` reads the frozen measures and adds the game's frame figures, and `VerdictFields()` is `DeathLegVerdict`'s fields plus ` bars= unbuff= bound=`. This is the fix for a false alarm of the live rounds. The first rounds read `respawn=elsewhere` at the very end of a run whose respawn stage had read `lifestone`, and a WARN said the lifestone object "leaves the table after the respawn". **It never did:** the late re-measure ran after the log off, and `LogOffTracker` confirms the log off only at CharacterSelect with `Self.Guid == 0`, which is after `GameStateReducer.LeaveWorld` ran `objects.Clear()`, so `ClassifyRespawn` found an empty table (review R3). Our object table forgets nothing; nobody should "fix" `WorldObjectTable` or the reducer's delete gate for this. The WARN `verdict: a re-measure now would read ...` can fire only in the world (the tally's arrival overwritten, or the lifestone really gone) and is skipped out of the world.
- *The re-gear and `ReleaseDeletedHeld`.* A second `GearScenario` (`endSession: false`, no cast) spawns again what the death dropped (the mod's `item` verb: the top-up of the kit) and wields the wand and the five pieces; `gear_restored=yes` when it reads `gear=wielded worn=5`; since M4 2.2 s1 the corpse stage goes back for the mage's own corpse first (the corpse trip, below), and the re-gear spawns only what the death destroyed; `--death-recover-corpse off` is M3's run (D8). The re-gear needed no code change: `PlayerTools.SpawnGear` counts a kit item in the pack by weenie class as held and `GearScenario.Wield` wields a carried one (pinned by `GearScenarioTests.A_kit_item_already_in_the_pack_is_wielded_and_not_spawned_again_...`). The first live re-gear failed with "inventory-unsettled" (5 held items with no world object): ACE drops a wielded item with F747 and no 0x0024, and the reducer used to remove the world object but leave the guid in the equipped list. `GameStateReducer.OnDeleteObject` now calls `ReleaseDeletedHeld` (`GameStateReducer.Inventory.cs`, one line in `GameStateReducer.World.cs`), which releases a held guid with `InventoryChanged.Removed`; this closes `TODO(m3-inventory-delete-alone)`. Retail: the client takes an F747 through `CM_Physics::DispatchSB_DeleteObject` (11.6096 0x006AD600) to `SmartBox::HandleDeleteObject` (0x00451EE0), then `ACCObjectMaint::DeleteObject` (0x00558330) and `CObjectMaint::DeleteObject` (0x00508FA0), which destroys the physics object and the weenie object whatever container or slot held it; **`TODO(m3-inventory-delete-retail)`** (logged once, with the first release): whether the weenie's destruction hook also takes the item out of retail's placement list was not read. The scenario's `regear-settle` stage logs held items with no world object (guid and held kind, container, placement, location) and, after `RegearSettleSeconds` 5, fails naming their guids.
- *The decoys (M4 3.1 d0, D20 and D22).* The death loop's kit is `GearKit.ForDeathLoop(spells, account, options)`: the plain `GearKit.Build` kit plus the eight `DecoyKit` lines (`GearKind.Decoy`: one item each, no valid location, never wielded), which `--death-decoys on|off` (default on; off is M3's kit exactly) switches. ACE drops the highest-valued items of a death (`Player_Death.cs` 481-603 and 629-673, `DeathItem.cs`: level 126 drops 6 to 8 items), so the decoys are ordinary, light items worth 8,000 or more, each the first of its death category (Massive Mana Charge 27329, Intricate Carving Tool 9295, Handle Mold 41396, Tusker Island Shirt 22565, Skull Cap 25522, Virindi Scalpel 11007, Healing Chocolate Olthoi Cake 11129, Entrancing Adjanite Gem 27771; 452 burden; four sold by vendors in the pinned world database), and they outrank what the loops need to kill the tuskers: the wand (The Healer's Heart 8552, 5,400), the Olthoi Helm, the Platemail Gauntlets and the Sollerets, and every spell component but the Mana Scarab (level VIII spells only; carried, 15,000 each, one more item above the wand). Bonded gear (the Greater Amuli Shadow Coat and Leggings, the Platinum Scarab) is never a candidate. `DeathDropModel` is ACE's rule as a pure function (category by ItemType, candidates, per-unit stack value, the halving of every item after the first of its category, the variance as bounds) and `Survives` says whether the needed items can be among the dropped: at least N_max not-needed candidates must have a minimum final value above the best needed item's maximum (unhalved, +10%). `DecoyKit.Check(state, level)` runs it over the live inventory once per gear stage and returns the one log line (`decoy check: N items above the wand ...`) and the `decoy_safe` field's value (`DecoyCheck.Field`). The count is `CountFor(level) = min(8, MaxDrops(level))`: the decoys alone cover N_max and the Mana Scarab is the spare. The gear scenario spawns a missing decoy by the same `item` top-up (counted by weenie class, one each; `GearScenario.DecoyGrants`), never wields one, and for a decoy kit `kit=<held>/<count>` counts them (`gear_steps` stays the wand's and the armour's). Tests: `DecoyKitTests`, `DeathDropModelTests` (an independent simulation of ACE's draw agrees), `DeathDecoyOptionsTests`, and the decoy rows of `GearKitTests` and `GearScenarioTests`. TODOs: `TODO(m4-decoy-bonded-flag)` (the weenie description carries no bonded flag; the check takes the kit's three bonded weenies, `DeathDropModel.BondedKitWcids`, deleted in M5 3 F1 with the retired decoy check, as bonded and every other item as not bonded) and `TODO(m4-decoy-value-unknown)` (an item whose description sends no Value counts as 0, as ACE's `wo.Value ?? 0`). The decoy check's line ends with `TODO(m4-decoy-bonded-flag): the bonded gate rests on the kit's three known bonded weenies, any other bonded item counts as a candidate` every time, and with `; TODO(m4-decoy-value-unknown): N held object(s) sent no Value and count as 0` when a description sent no Value (counted as 0, as ACE's `wo.Value ?? 0`). Full line: `decoy check: 9 items above the wand (best case 5940, the weakest of the 9 at least 7200); 6 to 8 drop at level 126 of 15 candidates: safe; TODO(m4-decoy-bonded-flag): ...`. After the decoy check the gear stage logs the live load: `load: <burden> of <capacity> burden, <p>% of the capacity` (the inventory page's `EncumbranceSystem.Load`, as `LootRules` reads it), and warns over 95% (`DecoyKit.LoadWarnPercent`): 'the decoy kit and duplicates leave the mage at N% of its capacity'. No behaviour changes. The regear before the trip spawns every kit item a death dropped and the trip returns the other, so duplicates are carried; letting the regear skip what the corpse will return needs the listing before the regear and is a design question left open.
- *The bars on the screen.* The panel shots open the first tab that holds spells (`docs/retail-ui.md` section 23.8); play keeps retail's first tab.
- *Headless.* `HeadlessScenario.DeathAndRegear` (section 20): summary line `death <fields>` before `headless done`, the default time limit 2,310 s with the defaults (900 + 630 + `--death-fight-seconds` + `--death-seconds`, section 20).
- *Tests.* `DeathScenarioTests` (scripted ACE in ACE's order, the stand-in for the gear and re-gear; the unbuff race, a respawn elsewhere, the missing-object failure and the freeze), `DeathTallyTests`, `DeathLegVerdictTests`, `DeathTrackerTests`, `DeathActionCodecTests`, `DeathSendTests`, `WorldObjectTableFlagQueryTests`, `HeadlessDeathOptionsTests`, `LoginCompleteWaitTests` (the closed F747 gap). M4 2.2 s1 adds the corpse trip's tests: `DeathScenarioTests` (the partial `DeathScenarioTests.Corpse.cs`: the happy path, each skip, each abort, a death on the trip, the frozen fields), `DeathLegVerdictTests`, `DeathCorpseOptionsTests`, `DeathLegRunnerTests` (the fifth slot) and `GearScenarioTests` (a kit item in the pack).

**The loopback tests (the lead only; `[LoopbackFact]`, `DeathLive.Run` in `DeathLiveTests.cs`).** `IncomingDamageLiveTests.The_unbuffed_mage_among_the_tuskers_takes_a_blow_and_its_health_share_falls` (DoD 9): `StopAfterHits`; `hits_taken >= 1`, `damage_taken > 0`, `health_min_pct < 100`. `DeathLiveTests.The_mage_dies_to_the_tuskers_respawns_at_Holtburgs_lifestone_with_vitae_and_is_regeared` (DoD 10): `died == 1`, `vitae > 0`, `respawn == lifestone`, the death's own corpse (`corpse >= 1`, review R1), `gear_restored`, `purged >= 1` when it suicided, the vitals between 60% and 90%, hits and damage above 0. What each proves is the DoD line it names; neither is part of `slice-check`. `DeathLiveTests` runs `DeathStages.All`, so it includes the trip since M4 2.2 s1; it prints `death_live_corpse ...` and asserts nothing about the corpse fields (a skipped or aborted trip is a legitimate live outcome). M4 3.1 d2 adds the decoy loop's assertions to the same test: the old DoD 10 assertions read the FROZEN verdict fields where the fight and the trip could move a live tally (`died`, `vitals_pct`, `hits_taken`, `damage_taken`), and `decoy_safe=yes`, `gear_dropped=0`, the corpse's listing at least 6 entries (level/20) with `decoys_dropped >= listed - 1` (a flat `decoys_dropped >= 6` fails one run in three by design: the unneeded Mana Scarab, 15,000, ranks among the top drops and N is 6, 7 or 8), `decoys_recovered == decoys_dropped`, `rebuffed=yes` and `den_kills >= 1`; a field that is n/a fails the test naming it and the trip's state. It prints `death_live_decoys ...`. The ceiling for the headless run is 2,310 s (900 + 630 + 180 + 600); expected 10 to 15 minutes.

**The corpse trip (M4 2.2 s1, D5).** `--death-recover-corpse on|off` (default on) and `--death-recover-min-health PCT` (default 60). Preconditions (each failing one is `corpse_looted=skipped` and a Warn line "corpse: skipped (corpse_looted=skipped): <reason>"): respawn classified `lifestone`; a corpse remembered (`DeathTally.LastCorpseGuid`, `LastCorpsePosition`, `LastCorpseAt`: the F745's cell, position and session clock, kept because the table forgets the corpse after the teleport away); under `PlayerCorpseMinRotSeconds` = 3600 s since the F745 (`W/Corpse.cs:103-118`: `max(3600, level x 300)` s with items, 15 s empty); and a health share at least the minimum. The trip: `PlayerTools.Place` (`place loc`) to the remembered cell and position, `CorpseSettleSeconds` = 1 s, the den's floor rule, the corpse by guid within 15 s, `OpenContainer`, `CorpseLooter` with `LootRules.TakeAll`, `CloseContainer`, then `Place` back to Holtburg's point (the bind's) and a 3 s settle. Each abort is named and counted as `trip_abort=`: `health-under-40`, `trip-over-90s`, `corpse-not-found`, `open-refused` (an open view is closed fire-and-forget; an open that lands late is closed too), `off-floor`, `place-refused` (nothing placed back: the mage never left). A death on the trip (a 0x01AC, the Dead motion or a NumDeaths rise) fails the leg with the corpse's state named (`trip_abort=died`). **The protection does not survive the trip:** ACE dispels the lifestone protection on every teleport of the player (`Player_Location.cs` 702-703), so the trip's departure ends it and the arrival among the tuskers is unprotected. The trip is kept short instead: with the fight on it comes after the regear, the rebuff and the fight (the tuskers are dead when it arrives; M4 3.1 d1); with the fight off it starts at the end of the respawn's vitals wait. The settle is 1 s, and the 40% abort applies. The first trip also makes `protection=dispelled` true in the tally, so M3's judged fields are frozen: the tally-derived measures (`died deaths vitae corpse purged protected hits_taken damage_taken evades health_min_pct corpses_stale protection`) are taken once at the end of `respawn-vitals` (`_atRespawn`, now for every run, not only one with a trip), and the regear, the rebuff, the fight and the trip never move them; a death during the rebuff or the fight fails the leg naming the stage (`rebuff: the mage died during the rebuff ...`, `fight: ...`, never a second `died=1` count); the health precondition (60%) now meets the mage after the fight, so a fight that leaves it under 60% skips the trip (`TODO(m4-death-trip-health)`) unless the pre-trip heal below brings it back; the trip's blows, teleports and re-created old corpses never move them.

**The corpse's listing (M4 3.1 d1).** The opened corpse's own entries are kept (guid, then weenie class as the F745s arrive) and classified when the trip turns back: `decoys_dropped=` (listed `DecoyKit.IsDecoy` entries), `gear_dropped=` (listed entries `DeathDropModel.IsNeeded` says the loops need), `decoys_recovered=` (the listed decoys that are in the pack after the trip: a taken entry keeps its guid). With the fight on, the regear ran BEFORE the trip, so a needed item that was listed and not taken is a Warn line, never a failure (`corpse: N item(s) the loops need were on the corpse and were not taken`; report-only in its first gate, as every new judge is, CLAUDE.md); the leg line's `gear_left=<n|n/a>` counts the listed items the loops need that the trip did not take from the corpse (n/a when no listing was read). In M4 2.2's order the regear after the trip covers it and the run only warns. Because the regear runs BEFORE the trip, what is left behind is a duplicate on the corpse, not a missing item. With `--death-decoys off` every decoy field is `n/a` and nothing is classified.

**The pre-trip heal (M4 3.1 d1 F1; `DeathScenario.Heal.cs`).** With the fight on, when the health share is the ONLY failing trip precondition the stage `pretrip-heal` heals first: Heal Self VII (2073, `ArenaProfile.FightExtras`) through `IActionApi.Cast`, at most 4 casts (each logged: the share before, the outcome, the share after; a cast is skipped when the mana is under `GearCasts.ManaCeiling` or the spell is not in the SpellTable, and an outcome other than a success or a fizzle ends the casting), then up to 60 s of regeneration on the session clock (logged on each 10%). A mage still under `--death-recover-min-health` skips the trip as before (`TODO(m4-death-trip-health)`). The fight scenario leaves the mage in non-combat and a cast needs the magic combat mode (the client guard, D40), so the stage enters the magic mode before its first cast and leaves it after. Not in M4 2.2's order. The verdict line gains `pretrip_heals=<n>` (report-only).

**The unbuff top-up (M4 3.1 d1 F2; `DeathScenario.TopUp.cs`).** The unbuff's suicide is a death too and drops items by the same rule, and the new order ends every run buffed, so after the suicide's respawn (`unbuff-vitals`) the stage `unbuff-decoys` spawns the decoys the pack lacks with the `item` verb (`PlayerTools.SpawnGear` over a kit of the decoys alone: counted by weenie class, nothing wielded, no cast), waits for them as the gear stage does, and runs `DecoyKit.Check` again, so `decoy_safe=` is read after the top-up and `decoys=` (counted when the wait among the tuskers begins) after it too. The suicide's own corpse stays in the den holding the old decoys and is not gone back for (the trip targets the death's own corpse by the tally's window); one log line says so. Skipped without a suicide, with `--death-decoys off` and for a kit without decoys; the `unbuff=killed` race has no top-up (the tuskers' kill is the run's death). Open: `m4-death-trip-heal-resume` (a heal skipped for mana does not resume when the mana regenerates during the wait).

**The leg: `--login-check=bsd-death`.** A kind of its own, in the style of section 31: never part of `--login-check=all`, hooked by one line (`DeathLegRunner.Install`) in `LoginCheckRunner.Install`. The game enters BSD Mage on the test account (absent: it fails loudly, "run --scenario=mage-build"), waits for the arrival (Travel Present, 3 s settle) and runs `DeathScenario` in the game's session with `DeathStages.All` and `endSession: false`, then logs off and quits. A failure at any stage logs off first. A bad `--death-seconds` fails the leg at its first frame ("bad death option: ..."). The runner parses `DeathLegOptions` (Client, `Session/Scenarios/DeathLegOptions.cs`): `DeathOptions` (the five options `--death-decoys`, `--death-rebuff`, `--death-fight`, `--death-fight-kills`, `--death-fight-seconds` and the older ones), `BuffOptions.FromArguments` (the `--buff-*` options: the rebuff is a buff cycle) and a `FightOptions` whose `Kills` and `Seconds` are the death options' (the fight leg's own `--fight-*` are not the death loop's); a bad value in any of them fails the leg at its first frame, as the others do. The kit is `GearKit.ForDeathLoop` (the decoys with `--death-decoys on`, M3's plain kit with off).
- *The game's player is both walkers.* `GameFightWalk` (the den, section 31) and `GameApproachWalk` (`scripts/Session/GameApproachWalk.cs`: the walk from the Holtburg place to within the lifestone's use radius before the Use) share one `RoutePlayHost`; `GameApproachWalk` drives the player controller with a `RouteDriver` over a one-goal `ScriptedRoute`, as `HoltburgApproach` does headless, and refuses an unplaced body.
- *The loading worker* reads the SpellTable, builds the gear kit (the login name hashed inside the worker), loads the den's floor graph and prepares the methods of the `Scenarios` and `Mage` namespaces, `GameFightWalk`, `GameApproachWalk`, `DeathLegRunner` and `RouteDriver` (the log line "the death loop's methods were prepared on the loading worker"). The session's own handlers for the death's messages (0x01AC, the vitae, 0x02C6, the corpse) are prepared by `SessionJitWarmUp` at start-up.
- *The frame measures.* `DeathFrameSampler` (Client; no allocation): `death_frame_max_ms`, the worst frame from the player's own Dead motion (F74C) to the first frame after the player is present again at the death's arrival, **portal space and the arrival's landblock load included**: the frame the motion was seen in counts from the next call's delta; the arrival (the self teleport after the death's F751) is counted, not waited for, since ACE sends the F751 and that position in one call (`Player_Location.cs` 686-696); the call that saw `SelfTravel.Present` counts (its delta is the last portal-space frame) and two calls after it (the frame the player materialised in and the first frame after it) close the window (package 2 review, finding 1: a window closed three calls after the position missed the tunnel and the materialise burst); each death (the suicide's, then the tuskers') opens one; the frame after a saved review frame is left out. It also keeps `CorpseBeforePortal` (the corpse's CreateObject between the Dead motion and the F751) and the arrivals, for the review frames, with the corpse window of R1. Report-only (a new judge, plan section 5; the lead decides when it is judged against 50 ms). `fight_frame_max_ms` is `FightFrameSampler`'s with the den stages (`den-settle`, `unbuff`, `unbuff-dying`, `death-mode`, `death-wait`) as "fighting" and the target always engaged; report-only here.
- *The detail line.* `bsd-death detail seconds= stage= stage_timings= unbuff= bars= bound= legs= dead_motions= death_portals= death_arrivals= corpses_seen= corpse_before_portal= death_windows= death_frames= worst_death_frame_at_s= worst_death_frame_ms= worst_death_frame_stage= worst_death_session_tick_ms= ... worst_death_play_mode_events_applied= fight_frames= worst_fight_frame_at_s= worst_fight_frame_ms= ... review_frames= scenario_verdict=` (the frame attribution of the slowest death frame and of the slowest den frame, the stage timings as `stage@run-seconds`, the review frames). The leg line is `DeathLegVerdict`'s, with d1's fields after `trip_abort=` in the order above, then `gear_left=<n|n/a>` after `pretrip_heals=`, then `mode_mismatch=<n>` (report-only; printed before the optional `frame_over_50=yes`, as on the hunt leg line: `... pretrip_heals=<n> gear_left=<n|n/a> mode_mismatch=<n>[ frame_over_50=yes]`; `DeathLegVerdict.WithField`, `DeathLegRunner.LegLine`). The stage timings include the new stages by name, as `NoteStage` appends every stage the scenario enters.
- *The review frames.* With `--play-screenshot=PATH` and no `--play-screenshot-after` (`PlayOptions.Parse` moves the path to `BuffShotPath` for `bsd-death`), six 800x600 frames with the developer HUD hidden, `DeathShotPlan` (Client, next to `FightShotPlan`): `<stem>-hit.png` (a 0x01B2 with the health under 100%, plus 0.2 s: the vitals down), `<stem>-dead.png` (the player's Dead motion plus 1.5 s: the death line in the chat), `<stem>-corpse.png` (the corpse's F745 plus 0.3 s) and `<stem>-lifestone.png` (the death's arrival plus 3.5 s, taken at the first frame with the player present: the lifestone and the 75% vitals). `<stem>-corpse-open.png` (M4 2.2 s1, `DeathShotPlan` slot 4 `corpse-open`): the mage's own corpse open, 0.5 s after the scenario lists it, with the scenario held (at most 3 s) so nothing is taken yet and the container window (lane s2) shows the contents. It is not named `corpse`, because that slot (the death-time corpse, always given up live by ACE's one-tick corpse and teleport) already owns `<stem>-corpse.png`. A run with no trip reads `corpse-open=trigger never came`. The plan watches from the moment the scenario waits among the tuskers (so the unbuff suicide's Dead motion, corpse and arrival are not the frames; on `unbuff=killed` the plan is armed on the victim notice, usually after the Dead motion in the same pump, and the dead, corpse and lifestone frames read `trigger never came`); each slot is triggered once; the scenario is held at most 3 s after a trigger while a frame is owed (the lifestone's 3.5 s runs past the hold). A slot whose trigger never came reads `trigger never came`. **The corpse frame is given up by name and never faked:** ACE creates the corpse and sends the teleport in one tick, so portal space is up 0.3 s after the corpse's F745 (`not taken (portal space was already up 0.3 s after the corpse's F745)`; the log says which came first), and the world is not drawn in portal space. The corpse is proved by the tally (R1's window) and, drawn in the Dead pose, by the offline loop. The frames are derived from the retail dats and stay under `cache/`. In the live frames the vitae page is open by itself in the dead and lifestone frames: that is **retail** (`gmVitaeUI::RecvNotice_VitaeChanged`, 11.6096 0x004A7AA0, opens page 15 when the multiplier falls while the page is hidden; `docs/retail-ui.md` section 23.3), and the red clipped "In" at the vitals' left is the spew box's "In Portal Space - Please Wait..." row under the dats' vitals window, 0.1 s after the tunnel ended (whether retail clears it at the tunnel's end is open). M4 3.1 d2 adds `<stem>-decoys.png` (`DeathShotPlan` slot 5 `decoys`): the mage's inventory page open with the decoys listed. It is watched BEFORE the plan is armed for the tuskers' death (the slot's trigger is read even before `Begin`): once the gear stage's decoy check has been made (`DeathScenario.DecoyCheckResult`; the scenario is then in its `bars` stage), the runner opens the inventory page with the panel host's own toggle (`GamePanelHost.Toggle(GameInventoryPage.Panel)`, what the toolbar button does; never a session action), reads the decoys the main pack holds, and the frame is due 0.3 s after the open (two hidden frames at least, the scenario held at most 3 s) and only while every one of those decoys has its cell in the page's pack list (the list scrolled with `ScrollToShow` to the first of them, and three frames drawn). The slot is given up by name after 3 s (`the inventory page did not list the decoys within 3.0 s`), and the page is closed again when the frame is taken or given up. A shell with no inventory page within 8 s of the check, or `--death-decoys off` (no check), leaves the slot reading `decoys=trigger never came`: the frame is never faked. `corpse-open` keeps slot 4 and its trigger; the trip is now last, after the fight (the plan is armed at the wait among the tuskers, as before).
- *Limits.* `DeathLegRunner.WholeCheckSecondsFor(deathSeconds, fightSeconds = 180)` = login 60 + gear 120 + spell bars 30 + bind 90 + suicide and respawn 90 + the decoy top-up 30 + the den 30 + `--death-seconds` (default 600) + respawn 65 + re-gear 120 + the rebuff 240 (the fight leg's buff top-up limit; a live cycle of 26 casts took 56 s) + the teleport back to the den 30 + the walk to the tuskers 60 + `--death-fight-seconds` (default 180, counted from the first cast) + the pre-trip heal 120 (four casts at 15 s and 60 s of regeneration) + the corpse trip 90 + the return to Holtburg 60 = 2,015 s (M3's 1,205 s plus 630 s of new stages plus the fight's 180 s); the limit is always the whole loop, a stage that is off only finishes early; a log off after a failure may take 120 s more (`RecoverySeconds`). The smoke step gives the Godot run 2500 s (a test pins `2500 >= 2015 + 120 + 300` and each limit against the scenario's own stage limits).
- *Run it live* (the lead only, backend running, BSD Mage built): `pwsh -File tools\smoke.ps1 -Backend require -Only login-death`, or with the frames `pwsh -File tools\godot.ps1 -Run -Offscreen -IsolateUserData -TimeoutSeconds 2500 -Resolution 800x600 -GameArgs:'--account=test --login-check=bsd-death --play-screenshot=cache/m3/3-2/death.png'`. It changes the character: vitae, dropped items, a corpse in the den.

**The smoke step `login-death`** (`tools/smoke.ps1`): `--account=test --login-check=bsd-death`, 2500 s, a backend step run ONLY under `-Backend require` (SKIP under `auto` and `start`: it changes the character), after `login-bsd` in `$backendSteps`. Its judge is `bsd-death done ok=true` and exit code 0, and it prints the verdict line. It copies into its summary, report-only (`n/a` when a field is absent): `corpse_looted items_recovered trip_s trip_abort rebuffed den_kills decoys decoy_safe decoys_dropped gear_dropped decoys_recovered pretrip_heals mode_mismatch`; its judge is unchanged. The step's help text lists the fields and the six frames. `slice-check` never runs it.

**The whole loop offline** (`PlayChecks.Death.cs`, after db's own check, section 16). `DeathScenario` runs against db's stand-in tusker (a second one, guid 0x80000C04, attacking, placed when the scenario waits) and a second stand-in lifestone (0x80000C03, Used by the check as db's is). "Arena here" (no teleports): the `Gear`, `Bind` and `Den` stages do not run (the bind is the check's own Use); `Death` (a still walk, `StandInStillWalk`: the tusker is in reach) and `Regear` run, plus `Unbuff` only when the stand-in's player holds no enchantment (it holds one in the current visit, so it is left out: the stage subscribes its tally only in a place or death stage, so the suicide path needs the teleports). Judged by `DeathScenario.Outcome` = `DeathLegVerdict.Compose` (`died=1 respawn=lifestone gear_restored=yes hits_taken>=1 damage_taken>0`) and five more checks of the death on screen; reported: `death_frame_max_ms`, `fight_frame_max_ms`. **The protected blow and the corpse drawn are offline only** (the protected-blow check and the corpse check of `PlayChecks.Death.cs`), because ACE dispels the protection on the teleport; DoD 10's audit row names which evidence is live and which is offline. M4 3.1 d2: the arena-here run names its stages and its options (`Decoys = false, Rebuff = false, Fight = false`; the stages `Death | Regear` plus `Unbuff` as before, no `Gear`, `Bind`, `Den`, `Corpse`, `Rebuff` or `Fight`): the new order's stages have no teleports, no inventory to spawn into and no real den offline (D23: no creature is added to the stand-in), so they are proved by `DeathScenarioTests` over scripted rigs and live by the lead.
- *The death on screen, checked on the stand-in with no fix needed.* The player's Dead motion (the server's F74C, stance NonCombat, forward Dead) reaches the body through `PhysicsPlayerBody.ApplyServerMovement` (`CPhysics::SetObjectMovement`, 11.6096 0x0050A160) and plays (`Substate = Dead 0x40000011` within a frame; the Ready of the respawn stands it up, `Ready 0x41000003`; `PlayerIsDead`, 11.6096 0x006B4CB0, already stops the player's own keys while the forward command is Dead). The corpse is an ordinary server object (`BF_CORPSE`, "Corpse of <name>", the player's Setup and motion table, forward Dead): its own avatar, drawn by the server-objects layer in the Dead pose; the player's body does nothing with its CreateObject (the ObjDesc is the stand-in's placeholder, `TODO(m3-standin-corpse-weenie)`; whether ACE's corpse wears the player's clothing is first seen live). Portal space plays on the death's F751 like any teleport's. `GameVitals` follows the 0x02E9 updates: 0.00 at the death, 0.75 and 0.74 after the arrival. The test `the-player-plays-the-dead-motion-on-its-own-body-and-stands-up-after-the-respawn` pins the first (`docs/avatars.md` section 17 has the avatar side).

**Live facts (the lead's rounds, 2026-10-17; the bundled ACE).** The bind took with the attune line seen (one round in 5.7 s), the approach walk bringing the player from 5.4 to 6.6 m away to 0.8 to 2.5 m of the lifestone. The unbuff suicide purged (`purged=1`). Unbuffed among the Tusker Slaves the mage was hit (3 to 7 hits, 56 to 135 damage; evades too) and **died in 11 to 22 s** (`death_s`; its maximum health was 174), the protection having been dispelled by the teleport (`protected=0` in every round). The respawn was at Holtburg's lifestone through portal space (`respawn=lifestone`), the vitals 75.6 to 76.5% three seconds after the arrival, `gear_restored=yes worn=5` with the kit 59/59. **The vitae climbed 5% a death and reached ACE's 40% cap** (D35; at the cap a 1% step costs about 35,000 XP, a kill gives 30,000): BSD Mage is at the cap and stays near it. The leg's last live run: `bsd-death ok=true died=1 vitae=40 respawn=lifestone vitals_pct=75.6 gear_restored=yes worn=5 hits_taken=3 damage_taken=78 death_s=21 death_frame_max_ms=11.0 fight_frame_max_ms=13.4` (an earlier run read 16.7 ms); the frames hit, dead and lifestone were taken and the corpse frame was given up by name. Every earlier `respawn=elsewhere`, "inventory-unsettled" and no-UseDone failure is one of the fixes above (the attune line, the approach walk, `ReleaseDeletedHeld`, the freeze). The live gate (the worst of three, the full smoke) is the lead's and not recorded here.

**Open TODO names of 3.2.** Opened: `m3-purge-empty` (an empty purge publishes nothing: the reducer, the lead's call), `m3-lifestone-radius`, `m3-bind-approach`, `m3-death-walk-replace` (only if the walker is not re-placed after the suicide's teleport; not seen live), `m3-inventory-delete-retail`, `m3-standin-bars-live`, the stand-in's own (`m3-standin-tusker-attack`, `m3-standin-vitae-spell`, `m3-standin-instantiation`, `m3-standin-lifestone-weenie`, `m3-standin-corpse-weenie`, `m3-standin-death-items`) and the windows' (`docs/retail-ui.md` section 23.8). Closed: `m3-death-leg` (dd), `m3-standin-death` (db), `m3-standin-corpse` for the player (db; a creature's still makes none), `m3-inventory-delete-alone` (da fix 4) and `retailui-runtime` for the three combat windows. All in `docs/milestones/m3-plan.md` section 9.

**Open TODO names of 3.1 (the death loop, M4 3.1 d0 to d2).** `TODO(m4-decoy-bonded-flag)` and `TODO(m4-decoy-value-unknown)` (the decoys); `TODO(m4-death-trip-health)` (mostly answered by the pre-trip heal: a mage still under the minimum skips the trip) and `TODO(m4-death-trip-heal-resume)`. Closed in 3.1 d1 F2: `m4-death-unbuff-decoys`. Named, not TODO: the headless host's `Program.DeathQuitAfterOverheadSeconds` (900) and its test `HeadlessDeathOptionsTests` still say the 2.2 loop's overhead (a CLI default only: the live test passes its own limit from `DeathLive.QuitAfterSecondsFor`), and whether the fight's frames join the death leg's report-only `fight_frame_max_ms` (`InDenStage` lists only the unbuffed den stages) is the lead's call.

**Since M5 item 3 (section 53).** The death loop is the death handler (`Session/Mage/DeathHandler.cs`, D51). After the respawn it re-buffs what the mage holds, heals when the health is low, takes the corpse trip by its route (Primary Portal Recall where the tie is known, else the runner), puts the items back, re-casts the item lines and goes back to the fight. The regear, the vitae stage, the decoy top-up and the decoys are removed from every host: `--death-decoys` and `--death-stages` with `vitae` or `regear` are refused by name. The place-based corpse trip, the unbuff top-up and the regear paragraphs above are M4's and describe code that no longer runs. The trip's aborts and its fields are the handler's (`trip_abort=`, `corpse_recovered=`, `route=`, `items_back=`, `vitae_grants=0`); section 53 has the order, the options and the tests.

## 33. The M3 4.2 polish pass (D39-D43)

The end of M3: the owner's polish items P1 to P5 and the 4.2 audit fixes, each a slice (`cache/m3/4-2/`). The prose here is the
one place for the slices' notes; the sections above name the behaviour where it lives.

- **P1, the cast bar (p1).** The gear stage of the death loop binds BSD Mage's bars: tab I (ACE bar 0) is Focus, Willpower and
  Creature Enchantment Mastery at VI, then the 24 buff lines at VII in `BuffCycle.OrderedLines` order; tab II (bar 1) is Fire
  Vulnerability Other VII (2170), Flame Bolt VII (2128), Revitalize Self VII (2083), Stamina to Mana Self VII (2345) and Heal Self
  VII (2073); tab III keeps whatever ACE holds. The build learns 2170 and 2073 too (`ArenaProfile.FightExtras`; 249 spells).
  `login-buff` runs the bars step after the gear (`MageSpellBars`; log only).
- **P2, the target panel (p2).** Section 21.7 has the blank-panel rule and the auto-target rule.
- **P3, the stance rules (p3).** Section 23.8 has the combat-mode refusals and the held-item rule. The cast guard is in §7.
- **P4, the wall shots (p4).** Section 31 has the pick, the wall-hit rule and the frames.
- **P5, the testing loop (p5a, p5b).** The default filter is `Category!=Loopback&Tier!=Soak&Tier!=Exhaustive&Tier!=Timing&Status!=KnownFailure` (M4 1.1 s3; README.md,
  tests/Shared/README.md, CLAUDE.md's own copy is the owner's). `slice-check`'s `-MinTests` defaults to the baseline file `tests/Shared/test-count-baseline.json`,
  and the exhaustive stage is floored by that file's `exhaustive` count. `slice-check -Exhaustive` (or `-Stages exhaustive`) runs the
  `Tier=Exhaustive` tests (76, the dat sweeps: `RenderSurface`, `SceneryOracle`, `SceneryWorldSweep` with the constant test,
  `CellVisibilityExhaustive`, `ModelsExhaustive`). The Exhaustive tier of `Lifestone.Data.Tests` takes 69 s, not 92 s. The
  script-side cuts: G-A is `slice-check -Smoke -MinTests <baseline>`, which implies `-Exhaustive`, `-SoloFrames` and `-FullScan`
  and no longer runs `dotnet test` twice; `smoke.ps1 -Backend require` implies `-SoloFrames` and `-FullScan` and prints
  `frames: solo (-SoloFrames implied by -Backend require)` on its first line (the probe and the hero takes still need
  `-SoloFrames` named); a `--play-frames` run is `--play-checks --play-frames` (`--play-skip-scenarios` was removed in M4 4.1 fix2b; the option catalog no longer has it). The test-side cuts: `IncomingDamageLiveTests` is dropped (its one unique assert,
  `health_min_pct < 100`, is in `DeathLiveTests`); the eight mod-verb loopback tests are three (`The_status_places_and_refusal_verbs_...`,
  `The_place_verbs_refuse_busy_...`, `The_spell_learn_and_item_verbs_...`; a failure message starts `[verb]`); the idle test is
  `Idling_in_the_character_list_past_the_silence_watchdog_does_not_trip_it` and idles 30 s (the 20 s watchdog plus 10 s), not 45 s.
  The death wait of about 53 s in `DeathScenario` is not the tusker's rhythm: the player arrives under ACE's lifestone protection
  (`Player_Death.cs` 907-933; dispelled only by the player's own actions, 935-940), and the stand-in absorbs the blows; unprotected,
  the same tusker killed the player in 8.9 s. `TODO(q5-death-wait-protection)` names it. The stand-in's tusker rhythm stays
  `TODO(m3-standin-tusker-attack)`; the live per-hit damage is 35 to 40 against the placeholder's 8 to 16.
- **The death loop's record (p5b).** `DeathOptions.StopAfterHits` has no caller after the cut and is a candidate for removal
  (review R11).
- **The stand-in's humans (p6).** The stand-in sends a ModelData for every human-Setup object and the player (`StandInVisit.HumanModel`,
  `HumanLooks`); the corpse copies the player's model (ACE `Creature_Death.cs` 474-480). The host composes the looks through
  `CharGenAppearance.Compose` (`PlayChecks.HumanLooks.cs`); every garment is a placeholder (`TODO(m3-standin-human-look)`, logged
  once per role). The live path applies ObjDescs correctly (a replay of both recordings, no appearance problems); only the stand-in
  sent none. The stand-in sends no F625 on a wield, an unwield or the death's lost items (`TODO(m3-standin-objdesc-on-wield)`; ACE
  `Creature_Equipment.cs` 365, 438). The report-only play-check field `humans_dressed=n/m` counts humans with at least one
  texture-changed part over the humans the stand-in made.
- **The texture fallback (fix A).** A texture change whose new texture cannot load keeps the surface's own texture (retail
  `CSurface::UseTextureMap` 11.6096 0x00537020 not applied; `CPartArray::SetTextureMap` 0x00519110 reports the failure and the part
  draws); counted in `TextureLoader.OverrideFallbacks` and told once per texture (the "textures" warning, `override-unloadable`).
  Before this, the loader threw and the whole part's mesh was dropped. It is retail's rule, not a glitch fix, so no flag.
- **Smoke, judges and the record (x3).** A step with `frame_over_50=yes` reads `PASS (frame over 50 ms)`; its detail ends
  `; frame_over_50=<yes|no> <window>_frame_max_ms=<x> ...`. The record-audio levels line ends `windows=<n> clipped_samples=<n>[ muted=...]`
  (report-only). The soak log prints `soak counts: client CRC failures N, client duplicate server packets dropped N, link duplicate
  encrypted deliveries C2S N S2C N` beside the ACE-side counts (`LivePhaseBTests`). The pinned NotInM3 set (`GamePanelsTests`) is panel
  ids 1, 2, 8, 9, 12, 14, 16 and 25.
- **The shell's first paint (fix B).** A step already over 2 ms while the shell holds the world skips its paint, and a step over 6 ms
  after its paint skips the upload (`RetailUiLayer.ShellBusyMilliseconds` and `ShellCopySkipMilliseconds`). The one remaining spike was a single
  gen1 collection of 4 to 8 ms inside one element's construction in the first (long) shell build (`GamePlayScreen`'s staged build). M4 1.1 s4 holds
  the collector off over that build with `GC.TryStartNoGCRegion(240 MiB)` (now `NoGcWindow`, ended in `Complete`, `Dispose` or a throwing build, by its owner only;
  off under the test host through an internal seam, not a command-line option). The build allocates about 11 MiB on the game thread. 24 MiB was too small
  under server GC (the window ended early and the gen1 still ran: the budget is per heap); 240 MiB held. The build's log line gained `staged unit gc <ms>
  (<gen0, gen1, gen2>)`, `wiring unit gc <ms>`, `build gc <ms> gen0/1/2 a/b/c` and `no-gc window held|ended early|refused|off (start <ms>, allocated <KiB>)`;
  the play-check `no-ui-step-after-enter-is-over-12-ms` detail gained `shell_gc_pause_ms_gen012=<ms>/<gen0>/<gen1>/<gen2>` (report-only).
  Before (master): longest unit 8.5 ms, worst_ui_step 9.9 to 12.4 ms (one run failed the 12 ms rule). After: longest unit 2.4 ms, worst_ui_step 7.6 ms, GC 0.
  Live probe (the lead, solo, `smoke.ps1 -Only login-check,login -Backend require -SoloFrames`, one sample each): before, login-check's shell longest frame
  4.5 ms and longest unit 2.1 ms, login's longest frame 9.8 ms and longest unit 8.9 ms with a GC in it; after, login-check 4.6 ms and 2.2 ms, login 4.3 ms and
  2.4 ms, build gc 0/0/0, the no-GC window held (10,310 and 14,436 KiB allocated). `TODO(m3-ui-step-gc-margin)` stays open: its closure waits on the post-window number (the first collection after the window ends: pause, generation and frames after `Complete`, in the log line "first collection after the no-gc window" and the play-check detail `shell_post_window_gc`); it closes only if that collection is also under the 12 ms margin. The window is sized from the GC heap count (`NoGcWindow`: 32 MiB a heap, 240 MiB at least, 512 MiB at most, so it holds up to 16 heaps and says UNDERSIZED beyond that). **M4 P5b (`TODO(m4-throwaway-teleport-frame)`):** `GC.EndNoGCRegion` leaves gen 0 the region's budget, so after a held window the next collection waited for up to 512 MiB of allocation and promoted everything since the build: the 57-95 ms pause at the next arrival (the throwaway's Holtburg teleport, every journey's first). The owner of a held window now leaves one gen-0 collection pending at its end, which `RetailUiLayer.Step` runs before the next step's clock (the window keeps collections out of the UI steps, its own closing one included; `closed by a gen0 collection of <ms>` on the first-collection line), and the post-window watch counts from after it, logs "none in 900 frames (still watching)" and goes on to the first collection, with its gen-0 size and promoted bytes.
- **Engine notes (fix B).** `RenderingServer.GlobalShaderParameterGetList()` logs an engine ERROR outside the editor: never call it.
  `DynamicLightTable.Declare` adds the sampler without asking.
- **The hero video's live takes (h).** The capture, edit and measure scripts for the M3 hero video are under `cache/m3/4-2/hero`; see
  [hero-video.md](hero-video.md) §8.
- **The loop's log lines (h).** The legs' log lines the hero video uses as beat anchors: the cast landed, the cast confirmed (the
  projectile), the fight is won, the death (the tuskers killed the player) and the regear done.

## 34. The fighter (M4 1.1; D1, D9, D13, D14; DoD 2)

M4's fights need a character that fights in melee and at range and heals itself: `Holtburg Hunter`, an Aluvian on the test account, built to level 40 by a
scenario. The mage (section 29) is the pattern; the code is the reference and this section says where things are.

**The template** (`Session/Fighter/FighterTemplate.cs`, with `FighterSpendingPlanner`; `FighterTemplateDatTests`, which pins the numbers against the dats and fails when
the rule changes). The creation intent (`CreationIntent(name, retailClasses)`): Aluvian male; Strength, Endurance and Coordination 100, Quickness, Focus and Self 10 (330
of the attribute points). Four skills are trained at creation: Heavy Weapons (44) **specialised** (12 credits), Missile Weapons (47) 6, Healing (21) 6 and Melee Defense (6) 10:
34 of the heritage's 52 credits, 18 carried forward unspent. Level 40 is 20,502,261 XP (`XpTable.XpForLevel(40)`) and 20 skill credits through level 40
(`SkillCreditsThroughLevel(40, 1)`), so 38 credits are left unspent. No skill is trained later (`TrainedLater` is empty).

The plan's targets (`Targets`: ranks above the creation value, in plan order; each takes its share of the level-40 XP and the leftover rolls forward):

| Stat | Share | Ranks | Final |
|---|---|---|---|
| Heavy Weapons (specialised) | 30% | 145 | |
| Melee Defense | 15% | 112 | |
| Missile Weapons | 10% | 107 | |
| Healing | 5% | 97 | |
| Strength | 10% | 94 | 194 |
| Endurance | 10% | 93 | 193 |
| Coordination | 10% | 94 | 194 |
| Max Health | 5% | 91 | |
| Max Stamina | 5% | 90 | |

Spent 20,487,694, remainder 14,567 (planned from zero: live, the four trained skills start at 5 ranks / 526 XP each, so the live remainder is never compared). Quickness,
Focus and Self stay 10. Capacity is 150 x 194 = 29,100 (15,000 at creation). `IsMet(PlayerQualities)` gives `TemplateCheck` (`AllMet` is `template=ok`), as the mage's.

**The kit** (`FighterKit.Items`: a `GearItem` and a `FighterRole`; `FighterKitTests`). Total burden 4,860 (`FighterKit.Burden`, tested against the line sum).

| wcid | Item | Count | Burden each | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 331 | Mace (Heavy Weapons) | 1 | 675 | wielded (MeleeWeapon) |
| 45 / 47 / 81 / 56 / 115 | Leather Cap / Coat / Leggings / Gauntlets / Boots | 1 each | 100 / 810 / 960 / 270 / 420 | worn |
| 307 | Shortbow (Missile Weapons) | 1 | 450 | pack |
| 300 | Arrow | 200 (max 1000) | 5 | pack |
| 628 | Handy Healing Kit | 2 separate items | 50 | pack |
| 377 | Potion of Healing | 5 (max 100) | 15 | pack |

Kits do not stack, so two are granted. The bow and the arrows are not wielded by the kit (the combat slices wield them). `GearItem.Kind` is only the nearest of the mage
GearKit's kinds: use `FighterItem.Role`, and do not give `FighterKit` to the mage's `GearScenario`.

**The three templates and `--fighter`** (M4 P4 tpl). `FighterTemplate` holds each fighter's character, kit, hunt
ground and band. Three test accounts, one fighter each: `test` is Holtburg Hunter (melee on the belt, the Mace, the 1.1
fighter); `test-melee` is Holtburg Brawler (level 40, Heavy Weapons specialised, the Mace; the Low B belt, levels 8 to 20);
`test-archer` is Holtburg Archer (level 40, Missile Weapons specialised, the Shortbow in the pack and 500 Arrows, the low
north forest). `--fighter=hunter|melee|archer` (headless; it follows `--account`, and a mismatch fails with a message that
starts `--fighter:` and never shows the account value) selects the template. `fighter-build` and the hunt accept the three
template accounts; every other scenario stays on `test`. The hunt's `--hunt-weapon` and `--hunt-ground` default to the
template's (Hunter and Brawler: melee and the belt; Archer: missile and the forest). The Archer rests with nothing wielded,
so its build verdict reads `wielded=0`.

**The per-template band and the avoided targets** (`FighterTemplate.TargetBand`, `FighterTemplate.AvoidedTargets`, set only on
the Archer; they apply only on the template's own ground). The Archer's band is the forest's levels 20 to 30: a first live run
shot 104 arrows at a tusker behind trunks with no hit, and a level-40 creature beat it, so the forest needs a band below
the fighter's own level. `AvoidedTargets` names the forest's Skeletons, Shadows and Elaniwood Golem, which resist pierce in
ACE's data (a Shadow took about 5 an arrow); the hunt does not pick them. With the band, the avoided targets and the seek, a live run read
`kills=2 shots=17 hits=14 seeks=1`.

**`--scenario=fighter-build`** (`Session/Scenarios/FighterBuildScenario.cs`; `HeadlessScenario.FighterBuild`, wired in `Lifestone.Headless/Program.cs`, quit after 600 s;
`--character` and `--create-character` are ignored with a warning: the fighter is always the template's character). It finds `Holtburg Hunter` in the character list or creates it
with the template's creation intent, enters it alone, reads the status (Player access: the server mod must report `xp` and `item`), then builds, each step only if the read model
still needs it: (a) `xp` up to `XpForLevel(40)`; (b) `TrainSkill` for a template skill shown untrained (credits Melee Defense 10, Missile Weapons 6, Healing 6); (c) the raises of
`Targets` in plan order; (d) the kit, one `item` grant at a time (a stack line is topped up by the missing count, never merged; the healing kits count separately); (e) the Mace
wielded at its `ValidLocations` and the five leather pieces worn (the mage's wield pattern; a non-kit piece in the way goes to the pack first); (f) `PlayerTools.Place` to the
Holtburg point of interest when the character is not in its landblock (no bind: the hunt of 1.2 binds). Then it logs off and quits. The entered character's name is checked against
the template's before every building step (`NameCheck`): a null name waits, a different name fails and sends nothing.

The scenario is **idempotent**: a ready fighter sends nothing. The verdict is `fighter=ready|built|failed level=<n> template=ok|short [template_short=...] kit=ok|<Name:held/needed,...>
wielded=<wcid|0> worn=<n>` (logged once as `[fighter] verdict`, printed as `fighter-build <fields>`). The first live build gave `fighter=built level>=40 template=ok kit=ok wielded=331
worn=5`, and a second run `fighter=ready`.

Tests. `FighterBuildScenarioTests` (5, `Client.Tests/Session/Fighter/`) run the whole headless scenario on the scripted ACE (`ScriptedAceSession` with `ScriptedFighter`, a model of
ACE's `xp`, stat, `item`, wield and place answers; call `Pump()` each step): create from nothing, ready, partial, other characters untouched with a full account and a refused
grant, and the name check. The stand-in cannot run a build (no Login or EnterWorld), as for the mage. `FighterBuildLoopbackTests` (`Loopback.Tests/Session/`, the lead runs it)
asserts `fighter=ready level=40 template=ok`, a second run that sends nothing, and the Advancement chat (type 0x0D): "You are now level N!" after the xp step, "Your base ... is now N!"
per raise, none on the second run.

**`login-check`'s `fighter=`** (M4 1.1 s5). The verdict line gains ` fighter=<present|absent|pending-delete>` after `mage_char=` (`LoginCheckScenario.ReportFields`, `FighterOf`): whether
the account's character list (`GameState.Characters.Entries`) holds `Holtburg Hunter` (`FighterTemplate.CharacterName`), compared case-insensitively; `pending-delete` is listed but
marked for deletion, `n/a` is a run that never read the list. Report-only, like `level=` and `mage_char=` (section 27): it never fails the check.

**The protected name** (M4 D1). `Holtburg Hunter` is a graduated test character: `CharPurgeRules.Graduated` (the server mod) and `StandInCharPurge.Graduated` (the stand-in) hold it
after `BSD Mage`, a test pins the two lists equal, and `char purge` refuses it with `graduated`, in any case. It is levelled only by its build scenario and the loops' kills, and is
never reset or purged.

**M4 3.1 fix2: which item a step acts on (the lookup defect of the live gate).** The hunt's steps (the swap, the restore, the heals) choose the item by `FighterItems.Find` (`Session/Fighter/FighterItems.cs`): an EQUIPPED instance of the weenie class wins over a pack copy, otherwise a pack instance (the Mace the swap put aside is preferred for the restore). The held list (`GearInventory.Collect`) names the main pack and the side packs before the equipped items, and the hunt's loot adds duplicates of the kit's classes (a looted Shortbow, Mace, arrows), so the earlier first-match lookup read "not wielded" for the pack copy of a Shortbow ACE had just wielded and waited for ever (`still waiting after 10 s: a step the server confirmed is not in the read model yet: wield the Shortbow`); ACE then refused the Mace because a launcher was wielded (WeenieError 0, `CheckWeaponCollision`) and every later hunter test failed on it (the combat-mode guard of lane g refusing the melee mode with a Shortbow wielded, correctly). Each swap and restore step logs the object's guid. The restore reads the END STATE (the Mace wielded, no launcher, no ammunition) and repairs a half-done swap: wielded ammunition first, then the wielded launcher by its own guid, each waited for, then the Mace. `FighterBuildScenario` clears the weapon slots the same way (`ClearWeaponSlots`: the ammunition, then the launcher, into the pack) before it wields the Mace, so it is the repair tool for a hunter a half-done swap left holding the bow (it repaired the live hunter at the gate). `FighterItemsTests`, four `HuntScenarioTests` rows and two `FighterBuildScenarioTests` rows (names ending `_3_1_fix2`) pin it; `ScriptedFighter` refuses a melee wield while a launcher is wielded and the reverse, as ACE does. A melee hunt that STARTS with a launcher wielded still fails loudly at the guard; only its wind-down repairs the state.

**Open TODOs.**
- `TODO(m4-healing-minlevel)`: Healing has MinLevel 2 in the SkillTable; the live build created the character with Healing trained at level 1 and the template check passed, so ACE's
  creation (`PlayerFactory.Create`, ACE @95ef426d, reference only) accepted it. The name stays in the code, as the failure text, until a retail-side check closes it.
- `TODO(m4-specialize-later)`: a specialised skill that shows untrained cannot be trained later (a specialise costs credits `TrainSkill` does not spend); the build fails and names it.
  Not reached: Heavy Weapons is specialised at creation.
- `TODO(m4-kit-itemtype)` is closed in 3.1 s0: the Handy Healing Kit's and the Potion of Healing's ItemType is 0x80 (Misc), and the kit's BoostValue (PropertyInt 90) is 50 (the earlier "boost 16" was wrong): `FighterKit.ItemTypeMisc`.
- `TODO(m4-template-skill)` and `TODO(m4-template-attribute)`: a skill or attribute id the template has no name for throws.

## 35. The hunt loop and melee and missile (M4 1.2; D10, D12; DoD 3, 4)

M4's fights need the attack sends on the wire, a read of the attack state, the actions that wrap the sends, a stand-in
that answers them offline, and a scenario that hunts the Holtburg belt with the melee weapon or the bow. The code is the
reference; this section says where things are.

**The wire** (`src/Lifestone.Net`). Three client sends: GameAction 0x0008 TargetedMeleeAttack {target, height, power},
0x000A TargetedMissileAttack {target, height, accuracy} (both start a run) and 0x01B7 CancelAttack (ends it). The codecs
are `Messages/Actions/CombatActionCodecs.cs`, a port of OpenAC's `AttackTargetRequest.cs` at the f22b048 pin
(section 9.1 of [protocol.md](protocol.md); [third-party/net.md](third-party/net.md), "The M4 combat wire"). `IAcSession`
sends them in the world as `SendMeleeAttack`, `SendMissileAttack` and `SendCancelAttack` (`SessionMachine.World.cs`). A
NaN or infinite power or accuracy throws ArgumentException before any sequence is used. ACE clamps the values to 0..1
itself (`W/Player_Melee.cs` 90, `W/Player_Missile.cs` 82); OpenAC does not, so the codecs send the value given. The
inbound events are GameEvent 0x01A7 AttackDone {WeenieError} and 0x01B8 CombatCommenceAttack (no payload), in
`Messages/Combat/AttackEventCodecs.cs`, dispatched through `IInboundMessageHandler.OnAttackDone` and `OnCommenceAttack`,
which the reducer overrides (`Session/State/GameStateReducer.Combat.cs`). Retail's senders are
`CM_Combat::Event_TargetedMeleeAttack` (11.6096 0x006AAB70, 11.4186 0x006A9C10), `Event_TargetedMissileAttack`
(0x006AACC0, 0x006A9D60) and `Event_CancelAttack` (0x006AA940, 0x006A99E0). The retail handlers of 0x01A7 and 0x01B8 are
not cited yet: `TODO(m4-attack-done-retail-cite)`. The stand-in side is in section 16.

**The attack state** (`Session/Contracts/AttackSequence.cs`; `IGameSessionView.Attack`, `IGameSessionView.Ammo`).
One send gives a whole run: with AutoRepeatAttacks on for the character, ACE walks the player to the target and
repeats the swing (or the shot) while the target lives, and the client sends nothing between swings
(`cache/m4/1-2/capture.md` C1 and C3). `AttackSequence` is the reducer's record of that run. Its `State` is `Idle`;
`Started` on the player's own attack-motion F74C, an arrow's launch or 0x01B8; `SwingOver` on 01A7 {0}, one swing over
with the next pending; and `Done` on 01A7 with a non-zero code (0x0036 at a kill, a cancel, a refusal, or a target gone).
It also holds `Target`, `TargetName`, `Swings` (own attack-motion F74C events, melee), `Shots` (falls of the wielded
ammunition's 0x0197 stack, missile), `LastWeenieError` and `Number` (the run's count). A TurnToObject F74C that follows a
send is not a swing. The reducer publishes `AttackChanged` on every 01A7, changed or not, so a tracker sees a refusal
that follows a Done at once (`TODO(m4-attack-done-event)`, closed in s4b). `Ammo` is `HeldAmmo` (wcid, guid, stack), null
when no ammunition is wielded. `NearestCreature` (`Session/Contracts/WorldObjectTable.cs`) finds the nearest creature
by a name list and a range. The creature's level is not on the wire, so a creature of unknown level is skipped, never
guessed in (`TODO(m4-creature-level)`); the hunt supplies the level from its ground's table by wcid.

**The actions and the trackers** (`Session/Actions/ActionApi.cs`, `Session/Actions/Trackers/CombatTrackers.cs`;
`AttackTrackerBase`, `MeleeAttackTracker`, `MissileAttackTracker`, `CancelAttackTracker`). `IActionApi` has `Attack`,
`MissileAttack` and `CancelAttack`. They run in lane `ActionLane.Attack`: one in flight at a time, in call order, no
rate limit. Timeouts run from the send: `ActionPolicy.AttackSeconds` 20 s (`UseSeconds`), `MissileAttackSeconds` 10 s,
`CancelAttackSeconds` 5 s. The completion rules:

- *Melee.* Confirmed at the first sign ACE took the send: the player's own attack-motion F74C, a TurnToObject F74C naming
  the target (the approach's MoveToObject is also an F74C, 4 ms after the send; a refusal sends no motion), or 01B8. The
  run's end is the reducer's, not the tracker's.
- *Missile.* Confirmed at the first sign of the launch: the run's start, the wielded ammunition's 0x0197 stack falling, or
  the arrow's CreateObject F745 (taken for ours by the war-spell projectile rule). The last arrow may take the stack with
  no 0x0197, so its F745 is the sign (`TODO(m4-last-arrow-launch)`: `AttackSequence.Shots` does not count it).
- *Queued.* An attack sent while a run is under way gets no answer from ACE (it queues the power, `E/AttackQueue.cs`), so
  it is Confirmed at once.
- *Refusals.* A refusal is a WeenieError (0x028A, or 0x028B with a string) or the transient 02EB, then 01A7. Melee is
  refused by WeenieError 0x04EB or 0x04CC, by a too-busy code, and by a 02EB that says "cannot attack". A missile is
  refused by the transient 02EB texts only (R9, 1.3: "You cannot attack X" and "You are out of ammunition!"), and by 0x0550 (out of range), 0x03F9, 0x001D, 0x04EB or 0x04CC. The tracker settles the refusal at
  the end of the tick in which the error was seen, so the 01A7 need not be observable. These WeenieError names come from
  the spec, not from a read of ACE's enum in this slice. A silent refusal (a 01A7 with a code and no start) is
  `ServerError` with the code kept: there is no Failed kind.
- *Cancel.* Confirmed on the run's Done; `Sent` at once when none runs.

**The stand-in** (`Session/StandIn/StandInWorld.Attack.cs`; section 16). It answers the three sends in capture.md's
order. Its placeholders all log once per run as `TODO(m4-standin-...)`: `attack-timing`, `approach-walk`,
`moveto-params`, `swing-pick`, `player-damage`, `creature-corpse`, `aim-motions`, `weapon-rows`, `weapon-kinds`,
`launch-encumbrance`, `arrow-model`. The launcher has no weapon row, so its range uses ACE's default launcher velocity of
20 m/s: 40.8 m. Unmodelled, with no name yet: the 02CF and 02DD skill-use pair in the hit tick, and the 02EB stance switch
of a bow wielded with no ammunition. `MeleeAttackTrackerTests` and `MissileAttackTrackerTests` cover the sends, and the live `MeleeLiveTests`, `HuntLiveTests` and `MissileLiveTests` prove them (the stand-in's `StandInMeleeTests` was deleted in the polish pass, cut C; `StandInMissileTests` keeps the wield row).

**The heal stand-in and the recovery rule (M4 3.1 s2, s3).** With `StandInVisit.Heals` (or `HealItems`) the hunt's potions drink for real in the stand-in: 25 health, the stack message and 1.47 s of busy (the hunt's own tests keep the old fixture, a potion that heals nothing, by leaving the flag off; the recovery tests set it). `StandInWorld.Heal.cs` answers the kit and the potion as section 16 says. With no potion to drink, a kit whose estimated chance is under `--heal-chance` is no longer waited out: when its chance is at least `--heal-floor-chance` (default 0.3, the constant `RecoveryRules.KitFloorChance`) and fewer than two rolls were lost at this health, the kit is used as the last resort before the regeneration wait (`RecoveryDecision.KitLastResort`; the log line says "last resort"). Under the floor the rule is as before: wait up to 300 s, fail at once under the 30% retreat share.

**The recovery rule (M4 3.1 s3; `Session/Fighter/RecoveryRules.cs`, `RecoveryOptions.cs`; closes `TODO(m4-hunt-recovery)`).** Pure and allocation-free per decision. `HealChance.Estimate(healing, boost, specialised, missing, combat)` is ACE's formula (`Healer.DoSkillCheck`): eff = round((Healing + BoostValue) x 1.5 specialised else 1.1), diff = round(missing x 2 x 1.1 in a combat mode, x 1.0 otherwise), chance = 1/(1+e^(-0.03 (eff - diff))); pinned against the live capture (eff 264: 145 missing in combat 0.16, 72 missing 0.96). A recovery starts under `--heal-below` (default 75% of the maximum health) and, once started, goes on to `--heal-until` (90%). At each look: the **kit** while it has uses, fewer than two rolls were lost in a row at this health, and its estimated chance is at least `--heal-chance` (0.7: at 188 health that is 81 or more health in combat, 71 or more in peace); else a **potion** (25 points, no roll) while the missing health is at least 15 (a potion at a full vital is spent for nothing, capture C3); else, with the share at or over the trigger, nothing; under the retreat share (30%) a loud failure at once; else **wait** for regeneration, then a loud failure after 300 s. A lost roll does not change the health, so two in a row set the kit aside for that health (the driver clears the count when the health moves). The Healing skill is read from the read model (`RecoveryRules.ReadHealingSkill`: skill 21, (Focus + Coordination) / 3 plus init and ranks through `SkillRules.TryCurrent`; the formula is pinned by `RecoveryRulesDatTests`), every 5 s; an untrained skill sets the kit aside (ACE's 0x04FC), an unreadable one falls back to 190 (the capture's value) loudly once (`TODO(m4-heal-skill-read)`: the stand-in's description lists no skill, so offline it always falls back). A kit or potion refused twice in a row (Healing untrained at once) is set aside for the run. More than 40 heals in one recovery fails the run (a safety net, never a rule). `--heal on|off` (default on; off runs no recovery at all, the retreat still protects), `--heal-below PCT`, `--heal-until PCT` (not under below), `--heal-chance P` (0 to 1), parsed in `HuntOptions.FromArguments` (a bad value is an error), registered in `OptionCatalog.Hunt.cs` for the main scene and the headless host. **Not built:** a heal during a fight; the retreat at 30% stays the in-fight rule (`TODO(m4-hunt-infight-heal)`, logged once in the run's first lines). Tests: `RecoveryRulesTests` (the decisions at their boundaries, the capture's numbers, two lost rolls, the wait and the failure, the options), `HuntScenarioTests` over the stand-in's heal, `HuntOptionsTests`.

**The hunt ground** (`Session/Fighter/HuntGround.cs`, `HuntGroundDatTests`). A `HuntGroundRow` is data: name, level band,
creature names, cells and `InUse`. `HuntGround.LowB` is the Low B belt, band 8 to 20. Its names are those of the
level-8 row (Banderling Scout, Blue Phyntos Wasp, Outcast Monouga, Field Ursuin, Small Shadow Child, Red Phyntos Drone),
the level-15 row and the level-20 row. Its cells are the ten south-west cells nearest Holtburg, in the world database's
order, the first `0xA5B00032`. A cell's centre is `(x*24+12, y*24+12)` with `x=(i-1)/8` and `y=(i-1)%8`; `PlacePoint`
puts the player at the terrain height there (`HuntGround.TerrainHeight`) plus 0.5 m. `HuntGroundDatTests` closes
`TODO(m4-belt-cell-terrain)`: water is a corner vertex of terrain type 16 to 20 (ACE's table), and slope is the lowest
surface normal over a 5 x 5 grid against `PhysicsGlobals.FloorZ` (0.664). The finding: all ten cells are dry and flat
(lowest normal z 0.993). What stands on a cell is not read (`TODO(m4-belt-cell-objects)`). `HuntGround.LowNorthForestMix` is the second ground (M4 3.1 s0; `--hunt-ground=belt|forest`, default belt): band 30 to 40 (the hunter is level 40; spec 5.9's 30 to 60 narrowed), `LowNorthForestTable` of 38 creatures by wcid with level (the lead's SELECT of generator 21183; the NPC Fisana the Jeweler and the Tusker Shrine Statue are left out), and 33 cells, the 0x9FB* strip 7.7 to 8.6 map units west, nearest first, first `0x9FB50039`; `HuntGroundDatTests` finds all 33 dry and flat (lowest normal z 0.973 or more) and pins the ten nearest as Dry, which closes `TODO(m4-forest-cells)`. A scenario that names a row whose `InUse` is false still fails with `TODO(m4-hunt-ground-unused)`.

**The hunt scenario** (`Session/Scenarios/HuntScenario.cs`; `--scenario=hunt`, `HeadlessScenario.Hunt`). It acts only
through `IActionApi` and `PlayerTools`. Its stages, in order:

1. *Name.* The entered character must be Holtburg Hunter; `fighter=wrong` fails before anything is sent.
2. *Bind.* `place poi` to Holtburg when the character is not in its landblock, a 3 s settle, the nearest lifestone (an
   object with the lifestone description flag), then `Use`. Once. A failed bind is logged and the run goes on unbound
   (`TODO(m4-hunt-bind)`).
3. *Health* (the driver of the recovery rule, M4 3.1 s3). Before the first fight (from the bind) and between kills (after the corpse sequence ends and the attack's Done is in, before the next acquire; never during a fight), `HealthStep` asks `RecoveryRules.Decide` and acts: the kit (`UseWithTarget(kit, self)`), a potion (`Drink`), `heal-wait` (regeneration, up to 300 s), a loud failure, or nothing (the stage returns to `swap` or `ground` at the first look, to `acquire` between kills). Each decision logs one line (`heal: kit 0x... (N use(s) left) at 43% (108 missing), estimated chance 0.69 ...`, `heal: potion ...: the kit's chance 0.31 is under 0.7`), and each outcome one line with the amount (`heal: the kit restored 83 Health (19 left)`, `heal: the kit's roll was lost ...`). The old floor (`StartFloor`, `MaxPotions`, `PotionRiseSeconds`) is gone. The Heal lane (s1) holds the busy chain, so the driver waits for each handle's Outcome and adds no wait of its own, with one exception: a heal answered 0x001D (the server's busy: a pick-up or the corpse's close just before is another lane's) is sent again after 1 s, logged, at most 10 times per recovery (`HealBusyRetrySeconds`, `MaxHealBusyRetries`); the stand-in showed it right after a loot. 
4. *Swap* (missile only), in capture.md C2's order: NonCombat; the Mace to the pack (0x0019); the Shortbow wielded (0x001A,
   refused while the Mace is wielded: GE 00A0 with WeenieError None, `Player_Inventory.cs` 1663-1668); then the arrows
   wielded (0x800000). Missile mode comes after the arrows: a bow wielded in a combat mode with no ammunition brings a
   02EB "out of ammunition" stance switch (`Player_Combat.cs` 843-856).
5. *Ground.* `place loc` to the first usable cell.
6. *Acquire.* `NearestCreature` within 60 m (35 m under the bow) of the creatures whose weenie class id is in the
   ground's table (`HuntGround.LowBTable`: 37 creatures of generators 1999 and 2001 with their levels, from a read-only
   SELECT on release v0.9.296) and whose table level is in the ground's band, 8 to 20. The level is not on the wire
   (`TODO(m4-creature-level)`), so it is the table's, keyed by wcid; the level-30 Tumerok Warrior is listed and the band
   filters it. A creature whose wcid is not in the table is never a target: there is no fallback
   (`TODO(m4-hunt-unlisted-creature)` is closed). None in range: the next cell, at most four cells in all, then the run
   fails. A refused target is excluded apart from its level.
7. *Query* (`QueryHealth`), then *mode* (SetCombatMode Melee or Missile).
8. *Attack.* One `Attack` or `MissileAttack` (Medium height, power or accuracy 0.5) per run. ACE walks the player in and
   repeats the run; the next send waits for the run's Done and a living target. A refusal (any non-Confirmed outcome,
   the 02EB "You cannot attack" included) excludes the target. A kill waits for the Done or 3 s. A melee
   approach is cancelled by ACE once the player is 15 m from where it began (`FailDistance`, `Player_Move.cs` 167 and
   241; 028A `YouChargedTooFar` 0x003D, then 01A7 0x0036 with no swing). The hunt reads that 01A7 at once (an
   `AttackChanged` whose number is still the send's) and retries the Attack from where the player stopped, as long as each
   retry closed at least 3 m, at most 6 (`charge_retries=`): the walk to a far target is ACE's own approach in 15 m legs,
   not a client walker (`MeleeAttackTracker` stays Confirmed: the approach did start). Any other code before a swing
   excludes the target. A sequence still
   Active at the next send waits at most 13 s (`KillDoneSeconds` + `WaitSeconds`); then the hunt logs it, sends
   `CancelAttack` and excludes the target (`stalls=` in the detail line). The reducer resets the attack sequence
   when the world is left (log off, enter world).

Every ending winds down the same way: CancelAttack if a run is under way; NonCombat; the weapon restored (the arrows and
the bow to the pack, the Mace wielded, so that the fighter-build scenario still reads `wielded=331`); `place poi` to
Holtburg; log off; quit. A health fall under 30% or the `--hunt-seconds` limit ends the fight through this wind-down and
fails the run, with `returned=yes`. A run that cannot get back reports `returned=no`. The `endSession` flag (default
true) makes the run end at the return for a host that logs off itself: the offline stand-in refuses the log off and the
quit.

*The fields.* `HuntTally` (`Session/Scenarios/HuntTally.cs`; not `FightTally`, which reads a mage's damage from chat) gives
`fighter=ok|wrong kills swings shots hits misses blocked seeks damage_dealt damage_taken hits_taken evades xp_gained returned=yes|no`
(`VerdictFields()`; `blocked` and `seeks` are new in M4 P4 and report-only in their first gate, see "The P4 hunt rules" below). A blow is the combat notice 0x01B1 (hits, damage_dealt); a dodge 0x01B3 and the "hit the environment"
lines are misses; the target's blows 0x01B2 and 0x01B4 are hits_taken, damage_taken and evades; swings and shots are the
runs' counts summed; kills and xp are `FightTally`'s. `damage_taken` also sums a creature's spell lines (the Magic-channel
"X bashes you for 32 points with Shock Wave II.", as `FightTally` does); `hits_taken` counts the 0x01B2 notices only (the
live melee run of 2026-10-20: 32 points of 01B2 plus a 32-point spell line made the 64 the health lost, and the share's
maximum read 188 as it should). `xp_gained` is the total-experience delta, not a kill count: the same run gained 487 with
no kill, in three steps on a first evade, a first landed hit and a spell resist (ACE's skill-use experience; the exact rule
is not read). All are report-only in 1.2. `DetailFields()` adds `weapon bound potions cells attacks refused restored stalls
health_start_pct health_end_pct` (the last two report-only: the health share read at the first Step of the health stage and
at the last Step).

*The P4 hunt rules (M4 P4: bow, sfix, seek).*

- **The missile retreat.** A missile hunt retreats at health under 50% (`HuntScenario.RetreatShareFor(weapon)`: 0.5 for
  missile, `MissileRetreatShare`; melee keeps 30%). Two blows took a 199-health Archer at level 30 to 32%, so the share is
  the weapon's, and a Hunter's missile hunt also retreats at 50%. `TODO(m4-archer-retreat)` stays open until a run shows 50%
  is enough (read `health_end_pct`).
- **The environment misses.** An arrow that hits the environment (a trunk, a tree, scenery) counts in `blocked=` (report-only,
  right after `misses=`, which still includes it). Each shot logs one INFO line: `[hunt] shot <n>: hit for <d> (0x01B1) |
  evaded (0x01B3) | environment (chat: <text>); <k> in a row`. Three environment misses in a row with no hit or evade
  between cancel the attack and walk on (`fight: 3 environment misses in a row at '<name>' 0x..; cancelling the attack and
  walking on`): the target is skipped in its cell, then the next target in the cell or the next cell is taken. A fixed
  count with no option (`HuntScenario.BlockedBeforeWalking`). The missile pick has no clear-line check yet:
  `TODO(m4-missile-clear-line)`, logged once per missile hunt at the first acquire.
- **The seek rule.** With no band creature within the acquire range (35 m for the bow, 60 m for melee), the hunt picks the
  nearest creature of its table and band that the client holds, outdoors, within 250 m of the ground's current cell, not
  yet sought, excluded or blocked. It `place loc`s to a dry point 20 m (bow) or 3 m (melee) from that creature, on the
  player's side first and then in 45-degree steps round it, facing the creature. Each creature is sought once, eight
  times per run at most. The point must be dry and walkable by the dats (a water corner, or a normal under
  `PhysicsGlobals.FloorZ`, rejects it); the fixed cells remain the fallback. Log: `[hunt] seek: '<name>' level <n> at <m> m;
  place loc to <x,y> in <cell> (<d> m from it)`. The seek reads terrain only: a building, a tree, another static or a
  creature can stand on the point. `TODO(m4-seek-terrain-only)` names that, logged once per hunt (M4 P4 rfix-b).
- **`seeks=<n>`** counts the times the hunt placed itself beside a band creature the client already held beyond the acquire
  range. It follows `blocked=` on the `hunt done` line and in the tally. Both are report-only in their first gate.

*Options* (`Session/Scenarios/HuntOptions.cs`, `OptionCatalog.Hunt.cs`): `--hunt-kills` (1 to 1000, default 1),
`--hunt-seconds` (default 300) and `--hunt-weapon=melee|missile` (default melee) and `--hunt-ground=belt|forest` (default belt; the `name=value` form only) chooses the ground row: `HuntOptions.GroundRow` is passed to `HuntScenario` by the headless host and by `HuntLegRunner`, whose worker reads the terrain heights of the chosen row's usable cells; the scenario's first hunt line names the ground (`ground Low North Forest Mix: 33 usable cell(s) of 33`), and the target rule is the belt's, with the ground's table and band. The default quit-after is 600 s plus
`--hunt-seconds`. The account rule: the hunt accepts the three template accounts (`--fighter` picks the template, section 34); no other scenario leaves `test`. The summary line `hunt <fields>` is
printed before `headless done`.

*Tests.* `HuntScenarioTests` (`tests/Lifestone.Client.Tests/Session/Fighter/`, 10) run the scenario on the offline
stand-in: melee kill and return, missile with the swap and the restore, a refused target excluded, the next cell, the
unlisted fallback, nothing near, potions and the regeneration wait, the retreat under 30%, the wrong character, the forest
row refused, and the options.

**The leg: `--login-check=hunt`** (`src/Lifestone.Game/scripts/Session/HuntLegRunner.cs`; `LoginCheckKind.Hunt`, hooked
by `HuntLegRunner.Install` in `LoginCheckRunner.Install`). It enters Holtburg Hunter on the test account (absent: it fails
and says to run `--scenario=fighter-build`), settles for 3 s, runs `HuntScenario(endSession: false)`, logs off and quits.
The options pass through; a bad value fails the leg at its first frame. The terrain heights of the ten belt cells are
read on a worker before the hunt. Limits: login 60 s, prepare (bind, belt, swap) 300 s, `--hunt-seconds` (default 300),
wind-down 120 s: 780 s without the shop visits, and 1,200 s with `--sell on` or `--buy-kits` above 0 (`HuntScenario.VendorTripsSeconds`, 420 s added); a recovery log off of up to 120 s more. Run it with `-TimeoutSeconds 1200`, and smoke's `login-hunt` Godot timeout is 1,500 s.

The verdict line is `[logincheck] hunt done ok=<true|false> kills damage_dealt damage_taken hits_taken evades xp_gained
fighter swings shots hits misses blocked=<n> seeks=<n> returned=<yes|no> attack_frame_max_ms=<x|n/a>` (M3's six in M3's order, then the five new, then 1.3's; `blocked` and `seeks` are M4 P4's, after `misses`). `HuntLegVerdict`
(`Session/Scenarios/HuntLegVerdict.cs`) judges `ok` on three things: no failure (the run reached done, wind-down
included), no death notice, `kills>=1` with `damage_dealt>0`, and from 1.3 `swings>=1` (melee) or `shots>=1` with `hits>=1` (missile). The rest are report-only, `attack_frame_max_ms` among them (judged from 2.1). A `hunt detail` line
follows with the seconds, the options, the scenario's `DetailFields` and the frame note.

**The shops (M4 3.2 s4a).** Back in Holtburg (`Home`, after `wind-restore` and `wind-home`, so the Mace is wielded and the bow is in the pack) the hunt visits Renald the Elder with `--sell on` (default) and Thelnoth Cort with `--buy-kits N` (default 2, 0 off) before it logs off: `place loc` into the shop at the vendor's own position (cells 0xA9B40141 and 0xA9B40111; `TODO(m4-vendor-approach)` names the walk through the door that is not built), the vendor looked up by weenie class (718, 715), `OpenVendor`, then `Sell` in batches of 20 (`SellRules`) or `Buy` of the missing Handy Healing Kits (the 0062 row for wcid 628, cut to what the pyreals cover), then `place poi` Holtburg again. A failure there never fails the hunt: `sell=failed:<reason>` or `buy=failed:<reason>` on the detail line. No visit is made after a failed hunt or when nothing is to be sold or bought (`sell=none`, `buy=enough`).
