The leg line carries `sold=<n> bought=<n> pyreals=<delta> appraised=<n> vendor_frame_max_ms=<x|n/a>` beside `mode_mismatch=<n>` (all report-only in 3.2; `sold>=1` and `vendor_frame_max_ms` are judged at 4.2), and the scenario's detail line ends `sold= bought= pyreals= appraised= sell= buy= buy_pyreals=`. `pyreals` is the sale's coin delta only; the purchase's price is `buy_pyreals`. `--appraise-loot` (default on) identifies each item the corpse loop picks up once (the scenario diffs the held guids against a snapshot taken when the loop began; `CorpseLooter` is unchanged). The vendor review frame is `<stem>-vendor.png`, 1 s after Renald's 0062, either weapon, taken whatever shows; its log line says `vendor panel visible: <class name>|no`.

*Review frames* (`--play-screenshot=PATH`, no `--play-screenshot-after`; 800x600, the developer HUD hidden). The frame
is the one the weapon gives (`HuntShotPlan`). Melee: `<stem>-swing.png`, 0.3 s after the first own swing of a run (the
run's Swings reaches 1), with the target within 6 m, the distance re-checked at the frame's moment. Missile:
`<stem>-arrow.png`, 0.1 s after the first arrow's F745 (wcid 300, in the world), only if the arrow still exists then. A
failed condition drops the trigger and waits for the next one; the scenario is held at most 3 s. The log names the frame,
its trigger, the target and its distance, the wielded weapon's wcid and the HUD state; the end note names a frame whose
trigger never came. `HuntLegVerdictTests` (11) and `HuntLegRunnerTests` (6) cover the verdict and the leg.

**The live tests** (the lead runs them; `tests/Lifestone.Loopback.Tests/Session/`): `MeleeLiveTests`, `MissileLiveTests` and
`HuntLiveTests` (with `HuntLiveRun`, the shared harness). Their recordings go to `cache/m4/1-2/live/`. `AttackCaptureLiveTests`
was the research capture and is removed; `cache/m4/1-2/capture.md` is its note.

**The smoke step `login-hunt`** (`tools/smoke.ps1`): `--account=test-melee --login-check=hunt --hunt-weapon=melee --hunt-kills=3` (the Brawler; it was `test` until M4 P4),
with the Godot run given 1,500 s (1,200 s until 3.2 added the shop visits). Since M4 3.1 s4 the step runs `--hunt-kills=3`, so `looted>=1` is judged over two or more kills (`HuntLegVerdict.LootJudgeMinKills`); the leg stops itself at `--hunt-seconds` (default 300) of hunting whatever the count, so the 1,500 s covers three kills with the recoveries between them (checked against the leg's own limits, not by a run). It runs only under `-Backend require`; under `auto` and `start` it is SKIP, with the
message `login-bsd` uses. Its judge is the leg's `hunt done ok=true` line (from 1.3: `kills>=1`, `damage_dealt>0`, and `swings>=1` for melee or `shots>=1` with `hits>=1` for missile). `swings`,
`shots`, `hits`, `misses`, `returned` and `fighter` are copied into the summary report-only (`n/a` when the line lacks
one). `frame_over_50` is read as for the other judged live legs (worst of three, the contended rule of D42).
`attack_frame_max_ms` is copied into the summary report-only (`n/a` when the line lacks it) and is not judged until 2.1 (D11). Since 3.1 s4 the summary copies, report-only, `heals kit_uses potions mode_mismatch` from the verdict line and `heal_failed_rolls heal_points heal_wasted` from the `[logincheck] hunt detail` line. The step is in `$backendSteps` and in
`$frameJudgedSteps` after `login-death`; `slice-check` never runs it.

**The smoke step `login-hunt-missile`** (M4 P4): `--account=test-archer --login-check=hunt --hunt-weapon=missile --hunt-kills=2`,
the Archer on the forest ground, with the review frames (including the arrow frame) and `--play-screenshot` at its
`frames/` folder. It shares its body with `login-hunt` (`$huntSteps`) and its judges: `ok`, `kills>=2`, `looted>=1` (over two
or more kills), and `shots>=1` with `hits>=1`. In the gate's parallel phase its frame fields are reported, not judged (see the gate below).

**The automation accounts by step (`smoke.ps1`'s `$stepAccounts`, M4 P4).** Every backend step names its account, and the
readiness probe (`Test-BackendReady -Accounts`) checks the key of each account the wanted steps need (key names only):

| Steps | Account |
|---|---|
| `login-check`, `login`, `login-throwaway`, `login-buff`, `login-bsd`, `login-death` | `test` |
| `login-hunt` | `test-melee` (Holtburg Brawler) |
| `login-hunt-missile` | `test-archer` (Holtburg Archer, `--hunt-kills=2`) |

**The P4 live gate and its three-run groups (`tools\live-gate.ps1`, M4 P4).** The gate runs the parallel phase by account
(`test`, `test-melee` and `test-archer` at once, each lane in order: its loopback classes, then its smoke steps with
`-ContendedFrames`, which report frame times instead of judging them) and then a solo phase that judges. The solo phase runs
the frame judge three times (`login-check,login`), one melee hunt (`login-hunt`, with its frames), the movement-precision
classes (`Lane=solo`), the throwaway's frame runs three times (`login-throwaway`) and the leak scan (`stray-output -FullScan`,
over the whole gate folder). Frame rule for the three-run groups (the frame judge and the throwaway): over 75 ms fails, over
50 ms is flagged; two of three runs over 50 ms fail the group, and a run whose only failure is a frame window counts as one
flagged run. The gate's `-Serial` order is the old one, with `LIFESTONE_LOOPBACK_SESSION=per-test`. The command lines are in
`tools\live-gate.ps1 -Plan` and in m4-plan.md section 5.

**The audio check (M4 P4; M2 D8).** Every automated Godot run must log `audio service installed ... muted=true`. A run without that line, or with `muted=false`, fails its step with `audio check: <reason>` (`Get-AudioMuteVerdict`
in `smoke.ps1`). `record-audio` is held to it too and keeps its own `muted=true` judge. `tools\play.ps1 -Audio` is refused
with `-Offscreen` before the backend is looked for. The audio check is judged in its first gate as a named exception to the
report-only rule (m4-plan.md section 5 item 3): it checks a fact, not a threshold.

**The live order** (`cache/m4/1-2/capture.md`, 2026-10-20, ACE 95ef426d, Holtburg Hunter at belt cell `0xA5B00032`). With
AutoRepeatAttacks on, one 0x0008 gave five swings and one 0x000A gave five arrows. Each repeat opens with 01B8. A 01C0
comes in the same tick as every 01B1 and 01AD; the spec (`docs/milestones/m4-fights-and-loot.md` 5.3) says no 01C0 follows
the player's own hit, and the capture shows one. A kill is 01AD with no 01B1, then the corpse, then 01A7 {0x0036}, which
can trail a teleport by about a second. The bow wield is refused (GE 00A0) while a melee weapon is wielded, so the swap
goes in the order above.

**Live round 3 fixes (fix3).**
- *The client walks ACE's approach itself (R1).* ACE's attack answer is the player's MoveToObject F74C and no F748 for the player; the client's physics carries the MoveTo out and reports its autonomous
  positions (retail `CPhysics::SetObjectMovement`, 11.4186 0x00509690, 11.6096 0x0050A160, runs the server's MoveTo in the physics object's update; the report cadence is the controller's, otherwise unknown).
  Round 3's headless hunt had no physics player, so its position never moved and the retry rule excluded targets 28-59 m away. `HuntFollow` (Scenarios/HuntFollow.cs) is the headless hunt's body: an
  `OfflineWalker` as `RoutePlay` and the ground walks make it (section 52), in a physics world that attaches the collision of each landblock the server puts the player in (and the eight around it) on first need, placed by the server's
  teleports and given the server's positions and MoveTos. `HuntScenario` ticks it each frame and measures every approach distance from it (the log says `own body`), at each send and in the retry line; the retry
  rule is unchanged (at most 6, each at least 3 m closer). Game: the player controller's walker is already the body (`AvatarSet.SetMovement` gives the self MoveTo to `PhysicsPlayerBody.ApplyServerMovement`),
  and `HuntLegRunner.PlayerBodyFollow` only reports its position to the hunt. Offline: `StandInWorld.ApproachSendsPositions = false` (and `ClientPosition`) models "MoveToObject, no F748"; the test sink hands the body's
  positions to the stand-in. `TODO(m4-hunt-follow-collision)` and `TODO(m4-hunt-follow-unplaced)` are the loud paths.
- *Two Arrow stacks (R2).* The swap merges every Arrow stack into the largest (`MergeStack`, waited for), wields that stack by its guid and checks it by that guid; the restore puts the wielded stack back. The restore
  now reads the end state (the Mace wielded, no bow, no arrows) whatever the run did, and `restored=` comes from that state, not from whether the swap ran.

**Open TODOs.**

- The stand-in placeholders named in the stand-in paragraph above (`TODO(m4-standin-...)`).

Closed in 1.2: `m4-hunt-unlisted-creature` (no fallback: an unlisted creature is never a target), `m4-standin-attack` (the stand-in answers the sends), `m4-attack-wire-ctor` (the reducer's constructor
calls `WireAttack`), `m4-attack-done-event` (every 01A7 is published), `m4-belt-cell-terrain`, and the two stand-in limits
of s4a (`m4-standin-swing-motion`, `m4-standin-approach`), replaced by `m4-standin-swing-pick` and `m4-standin-approach-walk`.

**M4 1.3: targeting and the combat UI (D11; DoD 3 and 4).** `AttackRequested` (§5) is the event of each send that reaches the wire. `attack_frame_max_ms` is the worst frame from an attack's send to its run's Done, over every run of the leg (`AttackFrameSampler`). It is on the hunt line, on the attack play-check rows (`PlayChecks.Attack.cs`) and in smoke's summary. It is report-only in 1.3 and judged from 2.1 (D11). The leg's judge is as the leg paragraph above says: melee `kills>=1 damage_dealt>0 swings>=1`, missile `shots>=1 hits>=1`. The melee review frames are four. `swing` is 0.3 s after the first own swing (the 1.2 frame, `HuntShotPlan`). `c1-combat` is the combat window in melee. `c1-powerbar` is the combat window's meter mid-charge, from half the run's power to 0.05 short of it (`HuntUiShotPlan.MidCharge`; review 1.3-R2: at the first level above 0 the frame read as empty). `meter` is the toolbar's health meter of the selected target, lower than at its selection after a swing. `HuntUiShotPlan` (`Session/Scenarios/HuntUiShotPlan.cs`) holds the last three and the framing. Each needs the target within 6 m and an unblocked camera, and is held as the 1.2 frame is. The review hunt's chase camera is framed for the run (`m4-hunt-frame-camera`, closed in code; the lead's live frames are its proof). Missile keeps `arrow` (M4 polish pass P3: the missile hunt now frames its camera as the melee one does, scale 1.2 turned -40 degrees so the bow is seen from its side, `HuntUiShotPlan.MissileFramedScale`, `MissileSideDegrees`; the arrow frame is taken only from the settled, unblocked framed camera and its line logs `camera_scale camera_pivot_m heading_offset player_box player_above_window combat_window_top`, `player_above_window` report-only, `HuntUiShotPlan.PlayerFramed`). The live frames and the live hunt are the lead's to run. `TODO(m4-last-arrow-launch)` is logged once per run when the last arrow's F745 is taken as the launch with no 0x0197 (`MissileAttackTracker`). **M4 3.1 p: the projection rule runs live.** The live hunt frames of 1.3 and 2.2 printed `player_box=n/a`, because `WorldPicking.TryProjectIncludingPlayer` required the server objects layer's record of a draw (`ServerObjectsLayer.IsDrawn`), which the layer never makes for the player (its `_Process` draws the avatars that are not `IsPlayer`; the player view draws the player). `SelectionProjectionRules.Refusal` (Lifestone.Client/Play) keeps the three tests (body drawn, not held, drawn by the layer) and exempts the player from the layer test; the thresholds (30% overlap, the 35-degree side turn, the combat window's top) and `HuntUiShotPlan.TargetVisible` are unchanged. If a player box is missing again the first review frame logs `hunt: the player's box did not project at the first review frame` with a `why=` naming the input (`WorldPicking.WhyNotProjected`). **The heal and potion frames (M4 3.1 p):** slots 4 and 5 of `HuntUiShotPlan` (`<stem>-heal.png`, `<stem>-potion.png`), taken 1 s after a kit use whose roll won (`HealTally.Heals` rose) and after a drink that restored points (`Potions` rose, `Wasted` did not, `Points` rose), each triggered once and framed as `c1-combat` is (the framed camera; no target is asked for, the hunt heals between kills), held at most 3 s; a trigger that fails at its moment is dropped and waits for the next heal; the leg's end note (`hunt: retail-UI review frames: ...`) names a frame whose trigger never came. The runner reads `HuntScenario.HealTally`, never the scenario's private state.

m4-plan.md section 9 rows R8 and R9 are closed. R8: the melee and missile rows judge the session (`IGameSessionView.Attack` State Done, Swings and Shots, `LastWeenieError` 0x36, the 01AD kill notice, the target gone, and the `Ammo` stack falling by the shots); the stand-in's counters are diagnostic text. R9: `MissileAttackTracker` refuses on "You cannot attack X" and "You are out of ammunition!" only. Any other 02EB during a missile attack is logged once as `TODO(m4-missile-transient-text)` and does not refuse; this supersedes the "any 02EB" refusal in the wire paragraph above.

**M4 2.1: the hunt opens its kill's corpse (s4a; `--hunt-open-corpse`, default on; off restores 1.2).**
`HuntScenario.EnterKillWait` snapshots the corpses already in the object table and the target's last position. `KillWait`
then runs `StepCorpse` beside the wait for the attack's last 01A7 {0x0036}: Seek (the first F745 named "Corpse of <target
name>" within `CorpseRangeMetres` (6 m) of the target's last position, not in the snapshot, waited for at most
`CorpseAppearSeconds` (5 s) from the kill notice; capture C2 saw 0.54 s) -> `IActionApi.OpenContainer` (in the combat
stance, which ACE accepted) -> Confirmed: the viewed container's entries are logged (name and wcid each) -> once the attack
is over, `CloseContainer`. There is no loot here (2.2's). A corpse that does not appear is counted (`CorpsesMissed`, a
warning); an open that is not Confirmed (a ServerError, TimedOut or Refused) is counted (`CorpsesRefused`); neither fails
the hunt. The retreat rule (health under 30%) and the hunt's seconds abandon the sequence at once (an open view is closed
fire-and-forget), so the sequence never delays the retreat. The detail line gains `corpses_missed=` and `corpses_refused=`.
Verdict fields, appended after `attack_frame_max_ms`: `corpses_opened=<n>` (opens Confirmed) and `corpse_items=<n>` (entries
seen, summed). They are report-only; the lead judges `corpses_opened>=1` at the item's gate by reading the line.
`attack_frame_max_ms` is judged from 2.1 (D11): over 75 ms fails the leg (`ok=false`, the problem
`attack_frame_max_ms=<x> (at most 75 expected)`), and over 50 ms ends the line with ` frame_over_50=yes`, the same flag as
the bsd-fight, buff and death legs. The window is `AttackFrameSampler`'s (an attack's send to the 01A7 that ends its run);
an unmeasured value (n/a) is not judged. The line is now `... returned= attack_frame_max_ms= corpses_opened= corpse_items=[
frame_over_50=yes]`.

**M4 2.2: the hunt loots its kill (s0; `--loot`, default on; off restores 2.1).** After `ListCorpse` the hunt enters `CorpsePhase.Looting` and runs a `CorpseLooter` (`Session/Fighter/CorpseLooter.cs`) over the viewed corpse: one `IActionApi.PickUp(item, player, 0, ActionSource.Host)` at a time in `ContainerView.Entries` order, a new one only when the previous handle has an Outcome (the Inventory lane holds the busy chain; the loop adds no wait; the capture of 2026-10-21: about 2.8 s an item from a corpse). The rule is `LootRules` (below). The corpse is closed once the loot is Done and the attack's last 01A7 is in; the retreat rule and the hunt's seconds still abandon the sequence (the open view is closed fire-and-forget, and the looter says `stopped_by=abandoned`). A monster corpse rots after 300 s, so the loot has no other limit. The tally counts `looted` (PickUps Confirmed) and `loot_skipped` (entries the rule left, for every reason); `DetailFields` prints both, and the leg prints `looted=` and `loot_skipped=` after `corpse_items=`.

**The loot rule** (`LootRules`, DoD 8): take an item when its description's `Value` is at least 1 (`--loot-min-value`) and at most 1,000,000, its `ItemType` has a bit of the vendor mask (`--loot-types`, default `shop` = 0x480467A7, Renald the Elder 718; `all`; or a hex mask), and the burden stays at or under 100% of the capacity (`--loot-max-burden`, `EncumbranceSystem.Load` as the inventory page computes it, the item's own burden from the weenie description). The checks run value, type, burden; the skip reasons are `not_resolved already_held value type burden`. The mask is the shard's weenie 718 (the lead's SELECT), not a wire value: `TODO(m4-loot-rules-shop-mask)` (3.2's 0062 carries it live). The vendor's accept rule is ACE's `W/Vendor.cs`; retail's client-side address is unknown.

**The loop's rules** (`CorpseLooter`): an unresolved entry (its F745 not in) is left and judged when it arrives, for 3 s from the first step, then counted `not_resolved`; a ServerError or Refused move is counted `Refused` and the loop goes on, except WeenieError 0x002A / "You are too encumbered to carry that!" (028A, or the 02EB text), which ends it (`stopped_by=burden`); a 25 s timeout is counted `TimedOut`. There is no merge case: ACE never merges at pick-up (the capture of 2026-10-21: arrows and coins), and a looted stack is its own stack with a 0022. `TODO(m4-loot-merge-stacks)`: a looted stackable with a held stack of its class stays a separate stack; the hunt logs it once per run and does not call `MergeStack` in 2.2.

**`loot_frame_max_ms`** (`LootFrameSampler`, report-only, judged from 3.1): the slowest frame from the corpse's open being sent to its close being settled or the sequence abandoned (`HuntScenario.CorpseWindow`); a frame over 50 ms prints `loot_frame_over_50=yes` (a note, never a failure); a review frame saved inside the window is skipped, as the attack sampler skips its own. The leg line now ends `... corpses_opened= corpse_items= looted= loot_frame_max_ms=<x|n/a> loot_skipped= [frame_over_50=yes] [loot_frame_over_50=yes]`.

**The heal actions (M4 3.1 s1).** `IActionApi.UseWithTarget(kit, self)` and `Drink(potion)` return a `HealHandle`; both complete on the UseDone (a kit's and a potion's lines and updates all come before it), so a handle holds the typed `HealResult` (`Healed`, `FailedRoll`, `Drank`, `Wasted`; the amount, the vital, the uses or the stack left, the critical flag) read from the F7E0 line by `HealResult.TryParse` (three shapes: "You [expertly ]heal yourself for N Health points. Your Handy Healing Kit has M uses left.", "You fail to heal yourself. Your ... has M uses left.", "The Potion of Healing restores N points of your Health."; "has 1 use left" and "is used up" are read; nothing allocates but the result). `HealTally.Record(handle)` is the one line the hunt's driver calls: `KitUses`, `Heals`, `FailedRolls`, `Potions`, `Wasted`, `Points`; only a Confirmed handle counts. **A lost roll is a use spent, not an error**, so a kit at low health burns its 20 uses (the live capture: five failed rolls in six at 22% health): the recovery rule heals early or drinks a potion. The trackers are `HealTracker` and `PotionTracker`; the tests are `HealTrackerTests`, `PotionTrackerTests`, `HealLaneTests`, `HealResultTests`, `HealTallyTests` and `HealActionApiTests`.

**The hunt's leg and detail (M4 3.1 s3, s4, p).** The leg line ends `... loot_skipped= heals= kit_uses= potions= ground=belt|forest mode_mismatch=<n>` and then the frame flags (`loot_frame_over_50=yes frame_over_50=yes`). `heals` is the kit uses whose roll won plus the potions that restored something (`heals>=1` is judged from 3.2's gate when `--heal on` and the leg started under `--heal-below`: `HuntLegMeasures.HealthStartShare`, the scenario's first health read, printed as `health_start_pct` on the detail line; the reason is "no heal although the hunt started at N% under the T% trigger"; a leg that started at or above the trigger is not judged on heals); `ground` is the chosen row; `mode_mismatch` is lane g's `CombatModeWatch`, started by `HuntLegRunner.BeginScenario` and read at `Finish` (`HuntLegMeasures.ModeMismatch`), report-only, and nothing judges it. The detail line adds `heal_failed_rolls= heal_points= heal_wasted=` (beside `potions=`, now the confirmed drinks) and `mode_mismatch_first=<the first disagreement or none>`. `looted>=1` is judged over a leg of two or more kills (never on one, and not with `--loot off` or `--hunt-open-corpse off`); `loot_frame_max_ms` over 75 ms fails and over 50 ms ends the line with `loot_frame_over_50=yes` (and `frame_over_50=yes`).

**The live tests (M4 3.1 s4; the lead runs them).** `HuntLiveRun.Run` honours `--hunt-ground` (the options' ground row is passed to the scenario; before s4 the headless hunt always went to the belt) and takes the command-line options (`Run(output, name, quitAfterSeconds, params string[] args)`: `--hunt-kills`, `--hunt-seconds`, `--hunt-ground`, `--heal*`, `--loot*`); the recovery options always travelled inside `HuntOptions`. `HuntLiveTests` gains the loop test (the belt, `--hunt-kills=3 --hunt-seconds=300`, 2400 s limit; prints `hunt_loop kills= looted= loot_skipped= heals= kit_uses= potions= returned=`; asserts `returned=yes`, the verdict composer's `ok` and `kills>=2`). `HealLiveTests` (Loopback; it replaces the capture test, which is removed) has two tests. Test one is Holtburg Hunter in one session through `IActionApi`: a kit at the full vital (the outcome is recorded as `heal_full=`, not asserted), the forest hunt for damage (recovery off, 150 s), at Holtburg a kit use (Confirmed, Healed or FailedRoll, Structure down by one, a Healed amount at most the missing health) and a potion while under the maximum (Confirmed, Drank 1 to 25, the stack down by one), and a second kit use while under 90%; it prints `heal_live kit_uses= healed= failed_rolls= potion_points= wasted= health_before= health_after= heal_full=`. Test two is BSD Mage: a kit from the `item` verb is refused 0x04FC and the kit is dropped, the pack as before; it prints `heal_mage ...`.

**The play-check rows (M4 3.1 s4).** Three rows, in the run's second session, on `PlayChecks.ReplayVisit` (the visit after the reconnect: `HealItems` announces a Handy Healing Kit and five Potions of Healing; the player STARTS with health 5 from the synthetic description, `Recorded = null`, `VitalUpdates` empty; `TODO(m4-standin-start-vitals)`): `heal-potion` (a drink at a partial vital: Confirmed, Drank 1 to 25 and at most the missing health, the vital up by the line's amount, the stack down by one), `heal-kit` (two kit uses queued at once: the first Confirmed as Healed or FailedRoll with the Structure down by one and, for a won roll, the vital up by the amount; the second still pending when the first completed, sent after its UseDone, then Confirmed or refused as a full vital), `heal-potion-at-full-vital` (the vital topped up by kit uses, then a drink: Confirmed, Wasted, the stack down by one, the vital unchanged). No creature, monster or corpse is in any of it (D23): the hunt's heal-between-kills logic is proved by `HuntScenarioTests` and live by the lead. The same rows run without Godot in `HealPlayCheckRowsTests`. The replay visit's stand-in start is logged once as `TODO(m4-standin-start-vitals)` (`StandInVisit.StartVitalsPlaceholder`); a row that needs the recorded character or a full vital belongs in the first session (the seam note for 3.2's vendor and appraisal rows).

**The scripted hunt rig (M4 4.1 fix2a; tests only).** `HuntScenarioTests` runs the hunt over `HuntScriptRig` (`tests/Lifestone.Client.Tests/Session/Fighter/HuntScriptRig.cs`): the real headless runner and a `GameSession` over `ScriptedAceSession` and `ScriptedFighter`, plus a model of what the hunt meets, answered in the captures' order (the melee attack with its approach, the blow, the kill, the corpse and its removal; the missile shot and its flight; the refused attack; StackableMerge; the corpse's open, pick-ups and close; the heal by kit, potion, busy and untrained refusals). It draws nothing and places nothing in any stand-in world; it is internal test code, reachable from no play-check or frame, and the stand-in's no-creature test (`NoStandInCreatureInDrawnRunsTests`) stays green over it. `ScriptedAceSession` gained the additive `UseHook` (the script answers a Use itself, no UseDone; null by default). Of the 55 hunt rows deleted with the creature (M4 4.1 t2), 44 are back in four groups: the recovery rule 12, the weapon swap 5, the corpse and loot 14, the target choice, approach and ground 13. The 11 shop rows (3.2 s4a: the sale at Renald's, the purchase at Thelnoth's, `--appraise-loot`, the refusals) were returned by fix2c (a fifth group, Shops 11), so all 55 are back and `TODO(m4-hunt-scripted-shop-rows)` is closed. They run over the rig's scripted vendors (`HuntScriptRig.Shops.cs`, `Shops = true`: Thelnoth 715 and Renald 718 at their real cells, 10,000 pyreals, Leather Bracers): the open (UseDone 0.825 s and 0.956 s after the Use, the 0062 1.043 s and 0.650 s after that; captures C1 and C2), the buy (the coin's 0197 or 0024 and 02CD, the 0022 of each new item, 3 ms later the refreshed 0062 and the UseDone, 3 ms after that the F745s), the sale (the 0022 into the vendor, the payout stack, the UseDone, the 0062 after it for Renald, the F745 5 ms later), the refusals (the 02EB line, 00A0, UseDone 0 or 0x001D busy) and the Identify (0x00C9, 5 ms later). No vendor emote (GE 0x02BD, `TODO(m4-ge-02bd)`), no burden 02CD and no pick-up sound are scripted; nothing is drawn. `ScriptedAceSession.TradeSends` (additive) records the items of each Buy and Sell, in order. The other 30 rows of the seven stand-in classes tested the removed creature's own protocol and stay deleted. Health numbers are the scripted fighter's (188 at full).

## 36. Containers and item moves (M4 2.1; DoD 6, 7)

M4 2.1 gives the client its containers (a side pack's contents, a corpse, a chest) and the item moves on them: pick up,
drop, split and give. The wire is in docs/protocol.md §5.1, §5.3 and §5.4 (GE 0x0196 and 0x0052; GA 0x0195, 0x001B,
0x0055, 0x0056, 0x019B and 0x00CD). This section says where the code is.

**The container model (s1).** `GameStateReducer.Containers.cs` keeps a `ContainerView` per 0196 (its order, each entry's
ContainerType, the foci slot) in `GameState.Containers` (§6). The viewed ground container is the one the player opened: a
second open closes the first (0052 first), and 0052 clears it. The stale objects stay in the object table, as ACE keeps
them. An entry resolves on its F745 after its 0196 (the ground-resolution rule); the 0022-before-F745 and a foreign
container's 0022 (a give to an NPC) are tolerated. `PlayerInventory.SidePacks` is filled from the 0196 at login and on a
pack's arrival. The placement inserts and shifts the other entries (`W/Container.cs` 558-570). `LoginCompleteTracker` waits
for every entry of every side pack that its 0196 names, inside the 3 s cap (§15). The events are `ContainerOpened`,
`ContainerClosed` and `ContainerContentsChanged` (§5). The reducer logs the 0196 once as `TODO(m4-retail-handler-0196)`
(§29's list): no retail handler is cited for it yet.

A 0022 out of the viewed container, and back into it, changes that view's entries in place. The capture's orders (C1 to C4)
are in §16.

**The actions and the trackers (s2).** The kinds, lanes, policy and the busy rule are in §7; the trackers are in §17.
The locked chest's refusal is a `ServerError` with the sound 0x94 as its text (DoD 6).

**The stand-in (s3).** The answers are in §16, in the capture's order; the placeholders are listed in
docs/milestones/m4-plan.md §4.4.

**The hunt's corpse (s4a).** §35 above.

**Play-checks (s4a).** `PlayChecks.Containers.cs` adds three offline rows after the attack rows. Each judges the session's
state (the Action API outcome, `GameState.Containers`, `PlayerInventory` and the object table), not the stand-in's counters:
`container-corpse-opened` (a melee kill of a stand-in tusker leaves a corpse; `OpenContainer` Confirmed; the viewed
container is that corpse and its entries are the Bowl and the Smock), `container-pickup` (`PickUp` of the Bowl Confirmed; the
item is in the main pack and gone from the corpse's view) and `item-drop` (`Drop` Confirmed; no longer held; in the object
table with a position). The rows close the corpse, return to peace and stow the mace. Open:
`TODO(m4-standin-corpse-contents)`. (M4 2.2 s0: the corpse's Bowl and Smock carry a Value (10 and 25), a Burden (15 and 30) and an ItemType the vendor mask covers (Misc 0x80, Clothing 0x4): placeholders, labelled, so the offline hunt loots both under the default rule.)

**The live tests (s4b; the lead runs them).** `ContainerLiveTests` (three tests: a side pack at login on Lifestone
Throwaway, its contents from the 0196; the hunter's own kill's corpse opened and closed, its contents read; Chest 143 opened
and closed, and the locked chest refused with its text) and `ItemMovesLiveTests` (one test on Lifestone Throwaway: a dropped
item picked up; a drop; an Arrow 300 stack split to the pack, to 3D and to wield; a Bag of Rye Seed 30488 given to Alfrin
and accepted; a give refused with 0x046A at Alfrin and at Gawain Cooper). Both are loopback tests on the live backend, all
through `IActionApi`, each printing one verdict line (`containers ...`, `moves ...`). They replace
`ContainerCaptureLiveTests`, which is removed; its research is in cache/m4/2-1/capture.md. The shared helpers are in
`tests/Lifestone.Loopback.Tests/Session/LiveLegs.cs` (`LiveRun.Run` records a headless session to cache/m4/2-1/live;
`LiveLeg` is the step-script base). Run them with `dotnet test tests/Lifestone.Loopback.Tests --filter
"FullyQualifiedName~ContainerLiveTests|FullyQualifiedName~ItemMovesLiveTests" --logger "console;verbosity=detailed"`, about 6
to 9 minutes with the backend up; they are skipped without one.

**Consumers (M4 2.2 s2).** The inventory page (`GameInventoryPage`) reads a side pack's contents through `ContainerViews.TryGet(pack)` and redraws on `ContainerContentsChanged`; the container window (`GameContainerPanel`) reads `ContainerViews.Viewed`, follows `ContainerOpened`, `ContainerClosed` and `ContainerContentsChanged`, and acts only through `IActionApi.CloseContainer` and `IActionApi.PickUp` (`ActionSource.Ui`). Both skip an entry until `ContainerViewEntry.Resolved`. The drops of the minimal drag (`retail-ui.md` section 23.10) go through `MoveToPack`, `PickUp`, `Wield`, and `Drop` in the same way.

**Panel shots of the main pack (M4 3.1 p).** `--play-panel-shots` takes the inventory page twice: `7-inventory-backpack-and-paperdoll.png` with the first side pack selected (unchanged), then `7-inventory-backpack-and-paperdoll-mainpack.png` with the main pack selected (`GameInventoryPage.ShowContents` of the player's own id, as a click on the player's cell does) once `PanelFramesPlan.MainPackListed` holds (the pack list has one cell for each held item whose object landed), or after 60 frames, said by name. The page is left on the main pack, its put-back state. A character with no side pack gets two shots of the same page.

## 37. Vendors (M4 3.2 s1; the close rule and the price model)

**The model.** `GameState.Vendor` / `IGameSessionView.Vendor` is the open vendor's `VendorStock` (null when none): the vendor guid, `Mask` (the ItemType mask it buys; the hunt's sell rule reads it, and `m4-loot-rules-shop-mask` closed on the wire: Renald's 0062 carries 0x480467A7), min and max value, `DealMagicalItems`, `BuyPrice` (what it pays), `SellPrice` (what it charges), the alternate currency, `Entries` (stack -1 unlimited, a listed 0 kept as 0 and not buyable; guid; name, wcid, Value, ItemType, icon) and a `Revision` that rises with every 0062. `PriceToBuy(entry, amount)` = per created stack `max(1, ceil(SellPrice x Value - 0.1))` in float arithmetic; a stackable is chunked by MaxStackSize at the unit Value times the stack size. `PayoutFor(item)` = `max(1, floor(BuyPrice x Value + 0.1))` on the whole stack's Value. `BuysType(itemType)` = `(ItemType & Mask) != 0`. A promissory note (ItemType 0x40000) is charged 1.15 and paid 1.0 (ACE `Vendor.GetSellCost`, `GetBuyCost`, W/Vendor.cs 573-609).

**The trackers follow the capture's order** (`cache/m4/3-2/capture.md`, gitignored). A buy's refreshed 0062 comes before its UseDone; a sale's refreshed 0062 comes before the coins at Thelnoth (C1) and after the UseDone at Renald (C2). The trackers never wait for the refreshed 0062, and the payout's new stack is a new entry (never merged into a held stack).

**The close rule (`TODO(m4-vendor-close-rule)`: the range rule is unread).** ACE's `Vendor.CheckClose` (every 1.5 s) only plays the Close emote when the player's cylinder distance exceeds the UseRadius, and the vendor's close sends no message. Retail's client close is the object-range exit: `gmVendorUI::OnObjectRangeExit` 0x004C0F70 (11.6096), with `gmVendorUI::RecvNotice_CloseVendor` 0x004C0F40, `CM_Vendor::SendNotice_CloseVendor` 0x006AB1D0 and `VendorItemsUI::CloseVendor` 0x004C1210 (the addresses named by the review, 3.2-R4; the range itself is not re-read). The model closes the open vendor when an own position update puts the centres more than UseRadius (0.6 m when the vendor sent none) plus `VendorCloseMarginMetres` (1.0 m, standing for the two cylinder radii) apart, when another vendor's 0062 arrives, when the vendor is deleted (F747) and when the world is left (`LeaveWorld`). A vendor whose object is unknown is never closed by distance. The vendors' UseRadius (3 m for Thelnoth, 5 m for Renald) is read in section 5.9 of `docs/milestones/m4-fights-and-loot.md`.

**Retail's price and open code (not read).** `VendorBuyUI::UpdateTransactionValue` 0x004C3D40, `VendorSellUI::UpdateTransactionValue` 0x004C41A0 and `gmVendorUI::UpdateTotalValue` 0x004C54D0 (11.6096; the review's addresses, 3.2-R4) hold the totals; the prices above are ACE's rule until they are read (`TODO(m4-retail-vendor-price)`). The 0062's consumer is `gmVendorUI::OpenVendor` 0x004C5790, and its sender is `CM_Vendor::SendNotice_OpenVendor` 0x006AB220.

**The shop entry, the loot rule and the sale (M4 3.2 fix).** The hunt enters a shop 1.5 m on the vendor's +X side; the -X side is tried once when that spot is outside the cell or the use radius (`HuntScenario.Vendor.cs`). The loot rule reads Renald's (wcid 718) last 0062 mask this session, else the shard's value, and logs it once as `loot: type mask ... from ...`; an open Thelnoth never changes it. The vendor window's close also closes the model's open vendor (`IGameSessionView.CloseVendorWindow`, which publishes `VendorClosed`), so the window and the model agree. A sale's predicted payout counts only the items that left the pack, and the detail names the items the vendor did not take.

**OpenAC #218 (m4-plan D16), `VendorStockTests.OpenAC_218_*`:** every CreateList row the 0062 carries is listed in the server's order; the packed 0xFFFFFF reads unlimited (-1) and is buyable at any amount; a listed 0 stays 0 and is not buyable.

## 38. The appraisal model (M4 3.2 s2)

`GameState.Appraisals` (`AppraisalCache`, bound 256 guids, the one answered longest ago dropped; counters `Received`, `Appraised`, `Failed`, `NotFound`, `Sequence`) holds each answer. `Appraisal` has the six tables by id, `SpellBook` raw plus `Spells` and `ItemEnchantments` split by the 0x80000000 bit, the profiles, the typed readers and `AppraisalIds`. The reducer is `GameStateReducer.Appraisal.cs`; `IGameSessionView.TryGetAppraisal` reads it. `AppraisalTally` (`Session/Fighter`, fed by `Record(IdentifyHandle)`) counts them: the hunt's `appraised=` is its `Appraised`. The tracker finds its answer by polling the cache (`Sequence` taken at the send), so `ActionTracker` gained no hook.

**Retail.** `ClientUISystem::ExamineObject` is at 0x005657B0 (11.6096; `Opcodes.cs` cites the sender `CM_Item::Event_Appraise` 0x006A94A0). The handler of 00C9 is not read: the candidate is `CM_Item::DispatchUI_AppraiseDone` 0x006A9400 (3.2-R4; `TODO(m4-retail-handler-00c9)`). The unpacker is `AppraisalProfile::UnPack` 0x005B4A30, and the consumer is `gmExaminationUI::RecvNotice_SetAppraiseInfo` 0x004B6E20.

**The examine panel** reads `IGameSessionView.TryGetAppraisal(guid, out Appraisal)` after `IActionApi.Identify(guid, ActionSource.Ui)` completes (`IdentifyHandle.Result`). A Failed result still has the tables (the panel shows them) and no profiles. A failed creature roll was not seen live (capture C3: the hunter's Assess beat Thelnoth's Deception; `TODO(m4-appraise-fail-live)`), so the 5 s cooldown is read from ACE's source only.

## 39. The shortcut bar (M4 3.2 h1)

`SelfState.Shortcuts` is the `ShortcutBar`: 18 slots, `IsMissing(slot)` live from the inventory. The description fills it (`GameStateReducer.Shortcuts.cs`, one call after the inventory) and leaving the world clears it. `ShortcutsChanged` is published per slot changed (section 5). The description's list is `0:0x80001097/0, 1:0x80000AF4/0` after the two adds of capture C4 and empty after their removes (`cache/m4/3-2/capture.md`).

**OpenAC #219.** The reducer's `Leave` (0024, 019A, a 0022 into a foreign container, an F747 of a held object) removes the row and publishes `ItemLeft`. The Action API sends 019D once per update (`ShortcutEvictions`). A move inside the player's packs and a wield keep the row. ACE never removes a row itself (`Player_Character.cs` 245-262). The retail behaviour is the same: `gmToolbarUI::RecvNotice_ServerSaysMoveItem` 0x004BFF40 (11.6096) removes the slot when the item moved and the player no longer owns it (`IsOwnedByPlayer`), with the broadcast on, so #219 is retail's behaviour and not an addition. `UpdateFromPlayerDesc` 0x004C04A0 adds only rows with an object id, and the shortcut number is the slot index.

**The rules still open.** `m4-shortcut-missing-at-login`: a row for an object not held stays and reads missing (retail's `UpdateFromPlayerDesc` adds every row with an object, with no check). `m4-shortcut-eligible-flags`: `IsShortcutEligible` 0x004BDE60 also tests the weenie's stuck bit 0x4 and a virtual answer the decompile does not name; only held and not-pyreal are built. `m4-shortcut-classify`: the client has no WeenieType or BoosterEnum, so a kit and a potion are told by wcid, item type and name until the appraisal model exists. `m4-shortcut-spell`: a spell-only row is kept, and `UseShortcut` answers NotImplemented.

**`UseShortcut`** hands the object to `ItemHolder::UseObject` in retail (or runs the pending target mode first). The kit's self target is a Lifestone choice until `gmToolbarUI::UseShortcut` 0x004BDFE0 is read (review 3.2-R8).

## 40. The wayfinder's keys, the fighters' magic, the vitals and harm-heal (M4 P5a; D33-D42)

**Move is bound in both hosts.** `IActionApi.Move` (`MoveIntents`) is bound to the rig's controller in both hosts: the Godot
play host binds in `PlayMode` right after the rig is built (`Bind(controller, RunByDefaultOf(controller))`) and unbinds in
`TearDown`; the headless scenarios (`RoutePlay` without a host, `HuntFollow`, `FightWalk`, `DeathApproach`) bind right after
`OfflineWalker.Create` and unbind on dispose (`Unbind(expected)` removes only the controller that scenario bound). Unbound,
every intent is NotImplemented with an ERROR line, as before. `Bind` requires the option; `SetRun(run, source)` asks for run
or walk, and the binding applies the option (`HoldRun(run != RunByDefault)`). `INavBody` and `ControllerNavBody` give the
wayfinder the body (position, cell, on ground, `ServerDriving`, the controller's clock) and nothing else.

**The human's key (R6).** The play host raises `MovementBindings.HumanKey` for every movement-map press (not releases, not
camera keys, not automation). `PlayerController.HumanKey` forwards it, and `PlayMode` wires it to `MoveIntents.ReportHumanKey`.
The runner subscribes to `Move.HumanKey` and ends Interrupted, releasing its own keys but not the one the person pressed. A
headless run has no human, so the event never fires. The run key is requested through one path (R2): the runner asks for "run"
or "walk", and the binding applies the option. `TODO(g5-run-by-default)` stays open for its other half: a later toggle does not
reach the rig, and the offline player has no description. The scripted-walk inversion is settled.

**The fighters' magic (D33, D38).** The Holtburg Brawler and Holtburg Archer train three schools to level 55. Creature
Enchantment (skill 31, 8 credits), Item Enchantment (32, 8) and Life Magic (33, 12) take 28 of their 44 unspent credits
(`FighterTemplate.TrainedLater`; pinned by `FighterMagicDatTests`, `FighterTemplatesDatTests` and `FighterBuildScenario.TrainCredits`).
The rest of the experience is a broad spread (`FighterTemplate.MeleeSpread` and `ArcherSpread`: attributes, trained skills, vitals,
and the three schools with Focus and Self inside it), bought on from the level-40 plan's ranks by `FighterSpendingPlanner.ComputeLevel`.
The target level is the smallest that gives the level IV self-buffs and Lifestone Recall at the 0.9 chance: `FighterSpendingPlanner.SmallestLevel`
over Creature Enchantment and Life Magic at least 182 (level IV, power 150) and Item Enchantment at least 132 (Lifestone Recall, power
100). That is level 55 (`XpForLevel(55)` = 86,399,136), with the skills at 183, 183 and 161; the remainder is 22,827 experience for the
Brawler and 175,370 for the Archer. The run rate rises from 1.19 (4.77 m/s; Quickness 10, Run 5 ranks) to 2.47 (9.9 m/s; Quickness
115 and 117, Run 113 and 114). The spread is Lifestone's own build, not retail data. `FighterBuildScenario` is idempotent and only adds:
a rerun trains 31, 32 and 33 in plan order, raises every target below its rank, learns the profile's spells and grants any experience
shortfall. Its verdict gains `magic=trained:creature+item+life` (or `short:<schools>`), and `RequiredVerbs` gains `spell learn`.

**`FighterProfile` and the buff leg.** `IBuffProfile` (`Session/Mage/IBuffProfile.cs`) describes a character's lines: the buff order
(leading lines, then the other "Mastery Self" lines, then the rest), the spells to learn, the line named, the mana transfer line and
the stamina restore line. `BuffProfile` is the data class. `ArenaProfile` is the mage's, and `FighterProfile.Brawler` and `Archer` are
the fighters' (12 buff lines and 4 utility lines). `BuffCycle` and `RebuffDriver` take a profile (null is the mage's); a profile with no
armour line creates no armour-ledger seeder. `BuffProfiles.For(name)` knows the BSD Mage and the two fighters; the Hunter has none.
`ProfileRole` gains HarmSelf, HealSelf and Travel. The mage's extras (Harm Self I to VIII, Lifestone Recall 1635, Primary Portal Tie 47,
Primary Portal Recall 48) are in `ArenaProfile.Extras`, so `MageBuildScenario` learns them. Missile Weapon Mastery Self VII has three dat
rows (2207, 2219 and 2313, one per old missile skill); the profile pins 2207, the VII after the VI of 467 to 472.

**The caster and ACE's rule (D33, C1b).** A Brawler or an Archer wields a weapon and no caster, and ACE enters a player into the magic
combat mode only with a wielded caster: `Player_Combat.cs:872-880` (no caster, back to NonCombat), the caster being the Held-slot item
(`Creature_Equipment.cs:141-144`, `GetEquippedWand`). A Held item also needs the main hand free: `CheckWeaponCollision` in
`Player_Inventory.cs` (the Held case) refuses it while a melee weapon or a launcher is wielded, and the reverse (GameEvent 0x00A0,
WeenieError 0), at ACE @95ef426d. The client's `CombatModeGuard` (`MagicWithoutCaster`, D23) refuses the mode first; the guard is
unchanged. So the two fighters' kits keep a caster in the pack: weenie 8552 "The Healer's Heart" (the mage's wand, `GearKit.Wand`) with
`FighterRole.Caster`, burden 50, granted by the build once (a rerun adds it to the existing characters; `kit=` names it when short).
`FighterBuildScenario.Caster` reads pack, hand, none or n/a, and the build fails if the caster ends in the hand.

`CasterSwap` (`Session/Fighter/CasterSwap.cs`) moves the weapons. The In half unwields the ammunition, then the weapon or launcher, wields
the caster (Held) and enters the magic mode; the Out half returns to peace, unwields the caster, wields the weapon and then the ammunition.
Each step is one `IActionApi` action, waited for by its tracker, and then the read model is read again. Bounds: 12 steps and 60 s a half,
and 3 s for the model to catch up. A failure is named `swap:<step>` and never retried. A failed In half runs the Out half as a restore and
then reports the first failure. The fields are `swapped=yes|no swap_s=<n.n>`, and `swap_why=` after a failure. `RebuffDriver` takes an
optional swap: a pass that finds no caster in the hand swaps in first and out after the pass, and a refused swap ends the pass with
`LastFailure = swap:<step>` and a back-off. `RebuffHostLogic` passes `CasterSwap.ForCharacter(name)` (null for the mage, the Hunter and
others). `TODO(m4-caster-value)`: the caster's Value is not read.

**The fighters' spell components (M4 P5a fix F-abil).** The live round's Harm Self was refused with 0x0400 (`YouDontHaveAllTheComponents`): the fighters' kit had no
components. `FighterComponents.For(profile, spells, account)` derives them as `GearKit.Build` does for the mage: the covered spells' formulas as the account casts them
(ACE rewrites the taper slots from the stored account name, `SpellFormula.cs:224` at ACE 95ef426d), one `FighterRole.Component` line per component. The covered
spells are those whose cast chance reaches 0.9 at the school's planned skill plus 20 (Creature Enchantment 183, Item Enchantment 161, Life Magic 183): levels I to IV of the
buffs, Heal Self, Stamina to Mana and Revitalize, Harm Self I to III, Lifestone Recall. A stack is 3 times the expected burn of one gate (3 buff cycles, 8 casts of each mana line
in the 3 cycles and 3 harm-heal legs, 12 Harm Self and 6 Heal Self casts a leg, 6 recalls), at least 5, at most the weenie's maximum (100). `FighterKit.WithComponents`
adds them to the template's kit, and `FighterBuildScenario` (given the SpellTable and the account name) grants only the missing count, so a rerun tops up what casts
burned and `kit=` names a short component as `Name:held/needed`. For the test accounts the Brawler's kit is 40 component lines (612 pieces, burden 2,448) and the Archer's 41
(617, 2,468): 49 and 51 of the pack's 102 slots, burden 5,908 and 6,603 against 15,000 at Strength 100. The sale rule keeps every spell component and the caster.

**Lifestone Recall home (D38, C1c).** `RecallHome` (`Session/Fighter/RecallHome.cs`) casts Lifestone Recall (1635; Item Enchantment,
power 100, base mana 50, no duration) from wherever the character is back to its lifestone. `RecallHome.ForCharacter(view, name, log,
homeLandblock = 0xA9B4)` returns one for the Brawler and the Archer (over the `CasterSwap`) and for the BSD Mage (alone), else null.
`Begin(now)` is refused while a recall runs and fails at once for a character that is not present; then `Step(now)` runs every frame
until Done or Failed. A failure is `recall:<present|swap-in|spell|mode|cast|teleport|arrival|swap-out>`, with `Detail`, `Recalls`,
`Seconds` and the fields `recalls=N recall_s=S recall_fizzles=F [recall_why=...]`. A fizzled cast (0x0402) is counted and the cast retried, at most
`RecallHome.MaxFizzles` (5) times in one recall, then `recall:cast` (M4 P5a fix F-abil, P5a-R4: Item Enchantment is built to the 0.9 chance rule, so one cast in ten
fizzles). The ACE refusals are named from `WorldObject_Magic.cs:1045-1066` and
`:1087-1095`, and by `WeenieError` 0x049E, 0x049F, 0x04A3, 0x04AD and 0x04CC (the PK timer). Under D38 the melee and archer plans'
`run home` stations are to become `recall home`; P5a's builds do not make that station change.

**The vitals keeper (C2).** `VitalsKeeper` (`Session/Vitals/VitalsKeeper.cs`) is one policy for the three characters, stepped like
`RebuffDriver.Tick` with no blocking waits. It acts only through `IActionApi` (UseWithTarget, Drink, Cast, SetCombatMode and `Move.Command`)
and reads only the server-confirmed read model (vitals, spellbook, pack, skills, combat mode, the vitae enchantment). Its health rule is
`RecoveryRules.Decide`, the hunt's own rule (called, not copied), with the thresholds `--heal-below` 75 and `--heal-until` 90, raised to a
station's `MinHealthPercent`. The order is: the kit when the rule says Kit (Healing trained, chance at least `--heal-chance`); else Heal Self
(the highest known level at `--vitals-cast-chance` 0.9, with the mana for its cost in hand); else the rule's potion, the kit as the last
resort, or a rest (Wait and Fail are both a rest). The rule's tail holds: a recovery with no kit or potion that helps ends at the start
trigger, so a rest regenerates to 75 percent, and only Heal Self goes on to 90. Two lost kit rolls at one health set the kit aside until the
health moves (the rule's `MaxLostRolls`). A kit refused 0x04FC, or Healing untrained, means no kit. Busy 0x001D waits 1 s, at most three
times, then `Blocked why=busy`.

Stamina under `--vitals-stamina-below` 30 percent (or a station's minimum) gets Revitalize Self where it is known and castable, else a rest,
each on to `--vitals-stamina-until` 80 percent; `MayAttack(out why)` refuses under `--vitals-attack-stamina` 15 percent. When the station's
`NextCastMana` (or `MinManaPercent`) exceeds the mana, Stamina to Mana Self is used where known, castable and giving something
(`BuffStatMods.Transfer`); else a rest, unless the station sets `NoManaRest` (`Blocked why=mana:<m>/<need>`); a need over the maximum is
`Blocked why=mana-max:<need>/<max>`. The vitae is reported (`VitaePercent`) and never acted on. Casts need the magic mode, entered when
`CombatModeGuard` allows it with what is wielded, and peace is restored before Ready; a refused mode sets the spells aside for the keeper's
life, logged once. Three fizzles in a row set a line aside until the next `NeedsFor`.

The bounds: each rest is capped by `--vitals-rest-cap-s` (600; over it `Blocked why=rest-cap:health|stamina|mana`), 40 actions a pass
(`Blocked why=actions-cap`), and vitals unknown for 10 s (`Blocked why=vitals-unknown`). No retry is silent. `NeedsFor(VitalsNeeds)` returns
true when met (Ready), else Working; `Step(now)` returns Working, Resting, Ready or Blocked. `TryInFightPotion(targetInReach)` is off unless
`--vitals-infight-potion on`; it drinks under `--vitals-infight-potion-below` 40 percent only with the target out of reach and nothing in
flight. The keeper does not swap the caster in: a fighter with the caster in the pack casts nothing and reports `cast=needs-swap` (kits,
potions and rest remain). `TODO(m4-keeper-swap)`: P5b runs the keeper's casts inside a swap.

The verdict (report-only in its first gate) is `vitals_min=h/s/m heals= kits= potions= rests= rest_s= transfers=`: the minimum shares seen,
floored (`-` when unknown), heals (kits, potions and Heal Self casts), and the seconds of ended rests. The eight `--vitals-*` options are in
the group "Vitals keeper" (`OptionCatalog.Vitals.cs`).

**The rest posture (C2).** `--vitals-rest-posture stand|sit|lie` (default stand). Rests are in peace and still: the keeper enters NonCombat
first. Sit and lie press and release the emote key through `IActionApi.Move.Command` (MotionCommand Sitting 0x41000013 and Sleeping
0x41000014; the human's emote keys are `MovementBindings.EmoteCommandOf`). The keeper stands up with the stop key (Ready 0x41000003) before
any other action and when it stops. With no controller bound, the intent is NotImplemented and the keeper warns that the posture was not
taken and rests standing. ACE honours both on the MoveToState path (`GameActionMoveToState.cs:19-30`, then `Player_Tick.cs:190-193` and
`:272-288`, `RawMotionState.cs:117-125`). Regeneration (`Creature_Vitals.cs:78-168`, ticked every 5 s from `Creature_Tick.cs:33`) takes the
base rates health 0.3, stamina 3 and mana 1 a tick, multiplied by the stance factor (`:210-236`): 0.5 in a combat mode or running forward,
crouch 2.0, sit 2.5, sleep 3.0; health and stamina only, not mana. Lying down therefore triples health and stamina regeneration in peace.
The client's `MotionInterpreter` refuses the postures in combat stance (`:246-256`). Not verified live: the animation, and whether the
client clears Sleeping on the next key (the lead tries `lie` in the live run).

**Harm-heal (C3).** `HarmSelfPlan` chooses the Harm Self level for a station that must lose health. The levels are ACE's world data
(`ace_world.spell`, ACE-World-Database v0.9.296, the database the bundled backend loads; `damage_Type` 128, health, on every row). The dat's
SpellTable carries power and cost but not these numbers, so they are pinned in `HarmSelfPlan.Levels`:

| Level | Spell id | Boost | Boost variance | Harm range (points) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| I | 8 | -4 | -2 | 4 to 6 |
| II | 1167 | -7 | -5 | 7 to 12 |
| III | 1168 | -10 | -8 | 10 to 18 |
| IV | 1169 | -16 | -14 | 16 to 30 |
| V | 1170 | -23 | -22 | 23 to 45 |
| VI | 1171 | -31 | -29 | 31 to 60 |
| VII | 2071 | -40 | -35 | 40 to 75 |

The roll is `HandleCastSpell_Boost` (`WorldObject_Magic.cs:485-491`, ACE @95ef426d): a random value between the boost and the maximum
boost, times the resistance modifier; `MaxBoost = Boost + BoostVariance` (`SpellProperties.cs:393`). The vital is clamped at 0
(`Creature_Vitals.cs:50-55`, `Player_Vitals.cs:130-139`), and a self-cast that reaches 0 kills the caster (`WorldObject_Magic.cs:600`,
`:678-691`); the cast gate (`:270-274`) refuses only a dead target. So no cast may be allowed to reach 0. `HarmSelfPlan.Choose` does nothing
at or under the target share (50 percent). Otherwise, of the castable levels allowed by `health > MaxHarm + MarginPoints` (`MarginPoints` 10,
Lifestone's own), it picks the level whose mean harm leaves the health closest to the target (a tie goes to the lower level). A table test
runs every health, level and maximum. A health over the target with no allowed level stops the round (`HarmStop.Margin`, logged).
`TODO(m4-harm-heal-resist)`: a drain-resistance enchantment above 1.0 would scale a harm past its table range (`GetResistanceMod`,
`WorldObject_Magic.cs:491`); the margin covers a small one. The script logs it where it bites: a WARN once per leg when a landed harm took more health than its
level's maximum, and `resist=` in the verdict (M4 P5a fix F-abil, P5a-R11).

`HarmHealScript` (`Session/Vitals/HarmHealScript.cs`) is a step machine like the keeper and `CasterSwap`. `Step(now)` sends at most one action
and judges every claim by the read model (the vital moved), never by the outcome alone. The fighter path (a `CasterSwap` is given) goes:
peace; swap in once; harm to 50 percent with Harm Self; a Handy Healing Kit (the health must rise; a lost roll is counted `kit_lost` and the
use retried, at most three times); harm again; a Potion of Healing (the health must rise); the keeper restores the health to its until
share; swap out once. The mage's path has no swap (it wields its wand): harm, Heal Self (the highest known level at the 0.9 chance: VII,
`DeathScenario.Heal.cs:26`), the keeper, and no kit (Healing untrained) or potion. A failure after the swap in still runs the swap out, and
the script ends Failed with the first reason: `harm-cap` (a harm phase over 60 s), `heal-cap`, `keeper-cap`, `harm-nospell`, `harm-short`
(under 15 points missing after the harm: no heal could show), `harm-no-effect`, `harm-refused:*`, `kit-no-rise`, `no-kit`, `potion-no-rise`,
`no-potion`, `heal-no-rise`, `busy`, `keeper:<why>`, `swap:<step>` or `timeout:<phase>`. A fizzle (`GearCasts.FizzleCode`) is counted
`fizzles=`, and the next look decides again inside the 60 s cap: counted and capped, never a silent retry. Not retail: the 60 s cap, the 2 s
settle for the server's vital update, the three kit tries and the 15-point room.

When the mana pays for no Harm Self level, or not for the level the plan wants, the harm phase casts Stamina to Mana Self itself (the highest known level at the
chance whose cost the mana pays and that gives more mana than it costs), at most `HarmHealOptions.MaxManaTopUps` (6) times a harm round, instead of waiting out the
60 s cap; with none castable it logs why once, casts the levels the mana pays and the cap decides (P5a-R7). The script also keeps the checks the old Brawler heal
fact made live (P5a-R6, D28): each kit use's Healed amount over 0 and at most the health missing and its Structure down by one; the potion's Drank amount 1 to 25
and its stack down by one. A break is recorded in `Anomalies` and printed `checks=`; the facts assert it empty.

The verdict is `harm-heal done ok=true|false [why=] heals= kits= potions= harms= fizzles= health_min_pct= swapped=yes|no kit_lost= busy=
health_end_pct= s= kit_points= potion_points= potion_stack=<before>-><after> transfers= resist= checks=ok|<n>` (the host adds `granted=`; the last six fields are
new and report-only in their first gate except `checks`). `heals` counts the kits, potions and Heal Self casts that raised the health, both the
script's own and the keeper's. The live proof is the `harm-heal` station (`HarmHealStation`) of each journey: mage (test), melee (test-melee), archer (test-archer); its verdict
fields `heals potions swapped checks` are the judges (the loopback classes were deleted in P5b J6). The belt hunt's heal leg (`HunterLeg`) is retired from
the heal facts.

**The fighters' buff leg (C4).** `--scenario=buff` runs on `test` (BSD Mage, unchanged: gear, bars, cycle, forced pass) and on `test-melee` and `test-archer`
(Holtburg Brawler and Archer). The account rule: buff accepts the default account or the account of a fighter template whose kit holds a caster;
`--fighter=hunter` on buff is an error ("no caster"). The headless host's mage-only warning is per character ("ignored ... the character is the
account's template"). The fighter's leg (`BuffScenario(FighterTemplate, ...)`) is: start; login (skipped in the world; the character must be the template's, else
it fails at once with nothing sent); swap in (`CasterSwap` In once: the arrows and the weapon to the pack, The Healer's Heart wielded, the magic mode); the cycle
(the fighter's twelve buff lines through `FighterProfile`); the forced re-buff pass (or the driver with `--buff-auto on`, handed the same swap); swap out (Out once:
peace, the caster to the pack, the weapon and the arrows back); log off; quit. There is no gear step (the inner gear scenario is inert), and a failure after the swap
in runs the Out half first, then logs off. The verdict keeps the mage's fields (`gear=n/a worn=n/a` for a fighter); `buffs=12/12` counts the profile's buff lines;
the line ends `swapped=yes|no swap_s=<s>`, with `swap_why=swap:<step>` after a failure (a refused caster reads `swapped=yes` with the reason, the weapon having moved and
come back). `IBuffProfile.TransfersAhead` (true for `FighterProfile`, false for the mage) lets the cycle cast Stamina to Mana Self before a buff when the mana left
after it would be under the cost of the strongest transfer the chance rule allows; the mage's rule would reach 25 and stall under the transfer's cost. The live proof is the `buff` station
of the melee and archer journeys (`buffs=12/12 rebuffed=yes forced=yes`); the loopback classes were deleted in P5b J6. A pass first took about 700 s of waits for mana; with the
mana order below it takes 18 s (round 6).

**The fighters' creation, the spending floor and the re-creation (D40, C5).** Each template has its own creation attributes (Aluvian, 330 points, each 10 to 100;
"high" is 100): the Hunter is unchanged (Strength, Endurance, Coordination 100); the Brawler has Strength, Coordination, Focus 100 and Endurance, Quickness, Self 10; the
Archer Coordination, Quickness, Focus 100 and Strength, Endurance, Self 10. Coordination feeds every weapon skill; Strength is the Mace's other half and the burden
(150 times Strength); Quickness is Melee Defense and Run (the run rate); Focus is Healing, the three schools ((Focus + Self) / 4) and Arcane Lore (Focus / 3); an
Endurance of 10 costs health and stamina, which the spend buys back. Both fighters (and the Hunter) train Melee Defense at creation and the three schools afterwards. Arcane Lore is
skill 14, a free trained skill of the heritage (the design brief's "23" is Lockpick, a 6-credit skill, and is not trained). **The floor rule** (Brawler and Archer only):
every spendable stat (the six attributes, the three vitals, every trained or specialised skill including the heritage's free ones: Arcane Lore 14, Magic Defense 15, Jump 22, Run
24, Loyalty 36, Salvaging 40) first gets ranks until its next rank costs at least 0.1% of the level's total experience (taken at level 40 for the base plan, at 55 for the whole);
the shares then spend the rest. Arcane Lore ends at 164 at level 55 (136 at 40). Numbers at level 55: the Brawler Strength 202, Endurance 126, Quickness 114, Coordination
206, Focus 196, Self 106, Heavy Weapons 287, Melee Defense 225, Run 229 (rate 2.468), Creature Enchantment 184, Item Enchantment 181, Life Magic 184, Health 188; the Archer
Strength 97, Endurance 126, Quickness 206, Coordination 209, Focus 196, Self 105, Missile Weapons 256, Melee Defense 256, Run 322 (rate 2.696), the schools 183, 179 and 183,
Health 189 (before Mana Conversion, below). **The mismatch guard:** a character whose creation attributes are not its template's fails the build before anything is bought
(`template=mismatch:<attribute> <have>/<want>`), which is how the old fighters (Endurance 100 at creation) were found. **`--fighter-recreate`** (fighter-build on `test-melee`
and `test-archer` only): log off, send retail's character delete (the character goes to pending deletion and can be restored; never the mod's purge), wait for the list to show it
pending, create the template's character, build it; the verdict reads `recreated=yes old=pending-deletion`. ACE freed the name at once. The re-creation was the owner's one-time
exception (D40); the old characters stay pending deletion.

**Magic gear, side packs and ACE's difficulty rule (D41, C6).** `Session/Fighter/FighterGear.cs` pins, per fighter, 13 real `ace_world` weenies: a weapon, seven armour pieces
covering the head, hands, feet, chest, abdomen, upper and lower arms and legs once, a necklace, two rings and two bracelets, each with its name, locations, ItemDifficulty,
spells by id and name, level requirement, burden, value and item mana and rate. The Brawler wields a Staff of All Aspects (45456) with a Leikotha, Twilight and Fiazhat mix (the
highest difficulty 100); the Archer keeps Serpent's Flight (30303) in the pack, wielded by the hunt's swap, with a mixed set (the highest difficulty 110). The selection is a
seeded SHA-256 order over the filtered candidates (the seed and filter are in `FighterGearSet`'s remarks). The plain Mace, Longbow and leather set left the two kits (a built
character keeps them; the build takes a worn plain piece off when the gear needs its place; the Hunter's plain kit is unchanged). **ACE's rule** (95ef426d): an item's spells
activate on equip when ItemCurMana is above 0 (`Player_Inventory.cs:314`) and the wielder's current Arcane Lore is at least the ItemDifficulty (`WorldObject_Use.cs:205-209`);
the item equips either way. Creature and Life spells go on the wearer, Item spells on the item (`Creature_Magic.cs:98-134`); the wield requirements are in
`Player_Inventory.cs:2080-2200`. The item's mana burns every 5 s while its spells are active, and at 0 the spells go (`Player_Tick.cs:620-690`). Arcane Lore in the SkillTable is
Focus / 3 (`FighterGearDatTests` pins it). A piece whose ItemDifficulty is above the character's Arcane Lore fails the build by name. The verdict gains, after `worn=` and `magic=`,
`gear=n/n gear_magic=on|off|n/a spells_active=a/w pack=n/102` (report-only in its first gate; `gear_magic` is named so as not to clash with `magic=trained:...`): `gear` counts the
pieces worn, wielded or (the bow) held; `spells_active` counts the worn pieces' wearer spells present in the enchantment registry or surpassed by a stronger spell of their category.
Live (round 3): the Brawler `gear=13/13 spells_active=38/38`, the Archer `30/30`. A worn piece with no active wearer spell is logged by name; the journey's build-verify
recharges the gear (section 41, the gear's mana; `TODO(m4-gear-mana)` closed). **The side packs:** ACE refuses any pick-up or unwield into a full main pack (`Container.cs:527`, the main pack's `ItemCapacity` 102; side packs do not count), and
the caster swap's unwield of the weapon needs one free main-pack slot. The round 2 gate found it: the 40 component stacks of F-abil on an old character with about 55 loot items. So
the kits carry two "Pack" (136); the build moves the component stacks into them (`PackStep`) and fails `pack-full` when the main pack holds more than 72 of 102 after the kit; the
hunt's loot stops after two consecutive "Unable to put X into container" refusals (`why=pack-full`, `TODO(m4-hunt-pack-full)`).

**Mana Conversion, the potions and the fighters' mana order (D40, C7).** Round 5's Archer heal leg ended with its mana at 60 of 291 on the keeper's 40-action cap. Between "Stamina to
Mana Self IV at mana 199" and the next at 69, the keeper had cast Revitalize Self IV three times (stamina 19%, 50%, 73%): Revitalize costs mana itself (base 50), so each cast ate about
43 of what the transfer gained, and that is the 130-point drop. The cause was the order, not a lost message. **Mana Conversion** (skill 16) is trained for 6 credits (as the mage),
with a 2% share in both spreads (taken from Max Health, 27% to 25%); it first gets the floor's ranks (108 here), so it is 158 at level 55 ((Focus 196 + Self 106) / 6 = 50, plus
108 ranks), and 166 with the Healer's Heart in the hand in a combat mode (ACE's ManaConversionMod 1.05, `WorldObject_Weapon.cs:235-262`). ACE charges an untrained caster the full
base cost (`Creature_Magic.cs:41-51`); at 166 a level IV buff (base 50) is expected to cost 12.4 and Harm Self V (base 60) 28.3 (the exact model gives 24.0, section 45), from two skill checks against power / 2 and power
(sigmoid factor 0.03, one shared luck roll). Before M5 item 1 the keeper and the cycle planned with the ceiling (the base cost), so the real spend was a quarter to a half of the plan; since then they send from the try floor, and a refused try costs nothing (section 45). The
fighters keep 13 unspent credits each. A rerun on an existing character trains and raises it (only adds). **The potions** are real retail weenies read from the pinned world SQL
(the database was down): Mana Potion 379 and Stamina Potion 378 (Food, +25, burden 15, stacks to 100, value 170 and 75); the Draughts (+10), Elixirs (+65, burden 75) and Tonics
(+85 and +100) were not taken, and 31196 and 31197 are ACE's own copies. The stacks: Brawler 100 Mana and 50 Stamina, Archer 60 Mana and 10 Stamina, in the side packs
(`FighterRole.VitalPotion`, so they take no main-pack slot; a rerun grants only the missing count and merges); the Archer's capacity (14,550) leaves 1,138 after the kit and the
loot room, which `FighterComponentsDatTests` asserts. The kits' burdens are 6,730 (Brawler) and 6,775 (Archer). **The fighters' order** (`VitalsOptions.FighterOrder`, set by
`BuffScenario` for a fighter and by the harm-heal station for a caster swap): for mana, a Mana Potion first (under the station's next need); then Stamina to Mana Self only when stamina is at
`--vitals-stamina-until` (80%) or more, with no potion left, at most `VitalsKeeper.FighterTransferCap` (2) casts a pass, named in the log; then a rest in peace. Revitalize Self serves
stamina alone, never mana; stamina takes a Stamina Potion first (under `--vitals-stamina-below`, on to `--vitals-stamina-until`), then Revitalize Self only when none is left, then a rest.
ACE gives mana no stance factor (`Creature_Vitals.cs`, `GetStanceMod`), so sitting or lying does not speed it: a fighter regenerates about 1 mana a 5 s tick, so 115 mana is about
575 s against the rest cap of 600, which is why the potions come first. Each drink waits for the vital's update (`PotionVitalWaitSeconds` 3 s; ACE sends it 0.74 s into the 1.48 s use);
a vital that has not changed sets that potion aside with a warning. The verdict counts `mana_potions= stamina_potions=` (report-only). `BuffCycle` (a profile that `TransfersAhead`) drinks a
Mana Potion whenever the next buff needs more mana than is held, never casts Revitalize for mana, and transfers only at a high stamina, with no potion held and within the cap;
`FutileManaCasts` stays the safety net. The mage's order is unchanged. `TODO(m4-mana-potion-capture)`: no packet capture of a Mana Potion drink exists; the wait on the vital
update is the guard. Live (round 6): both heal legs `transfers=0`, both buff legs 18 s.

**The gate's lane budgets, the release wait, the login retry and D42 (F-gate).** `tools/live-gate.ps1` `ExpectedSeconds` (limit twice that): `loopback:test-melee` 800 (1,600) and
`loopback:test-archer` 750 (1,500), because the buff leg adds about 700 s (the archer lane: 253 s done, about 60 s for Appraise, about 700 s for the buff leg). **The release wait:** a
step killed by its timeout stamps `killed-<lane>-<step>.stamp` in the gate folder (the account, the UTC kill time, 80 s); the next step on the same account (or an unknown account)
logs `WAIT n s` in `gate.log` and sleeps the remainder; accounts `none` (build, leak-scan) never wait. ACE's online state is not in the database, so the wait is the fixed one (80 s: the
60 s session timeout, the log-off and 5). **The login retry** (since 4.1): the reducer holds a retryable `CharacterError` (0x01, 0x0D, 0x15) that arrives while the phase is
`EnteringWorld` until the session's queued events say retry (`RetryScheduled`: counted, logged `login_retries=n`, nothing published) or give up (`CharacterErrorReceived`, published once);
`EnterWorldTracker` now sees only the final error, so `LoginRetryPolicy`'s 75 s wait works for the game and the headless host (before, the first 0x0D ended it). **D42:** the death
loop's vitae grant may cross a level (the owner); it is logged `vitae_level_crossed=yes level=a->b` and is still exactly the clearing amount; the stage still fails when the
`XpTable` cannot name the level. The test that refused a crossing was replaced by one that grants across a level, logs the crossing and never grants more than clears.

## 41. Journeys and stations (M4 P5b; D36, D37, D43)

**The journey.** A journey is one character's whole day in the world, as a list of stations run in order: `JourneyScenario` over a `JourneyPlan`
(`JourneyPlans.Mage`, `Melee`, `Archer`; the three gate journeys, and `JourneyPlans.Hero` for the hero hook, which is not in the gate). Its stages are
logged once each: login (skipped in the world), known-state (the character is present, in Holtburg, in peace; a place here is `recovery_place=1` and is
never budgeted), then per station `travel:<name>`, `vitals:<name>`, `station:<name>`, `recover:<name>` after a failure, and `logoff`. The gate runs
`smoke -Only journey-mage` (on `test`), `journey-melee` (`test-melee`) and `journey-archer` (`test-archer`); each step is judged by its done line (section 5, README).

**Stations.** `IStation` is one stop: it knows its needs (`StationNeeds`), runs, and returns its verdict fields. `ScenarioStation` wraps an existing
scenario (Gear, Buff, Hunt, Vendor, the den fight, the death) with its options set by the plan; `ScriptLeg` runs a script station (the harm-heal
script, the cast facts, the kit-refused check) through the client's `LiveLeg`. `JourneyStations.Create` builds one station per kind of `StationKinds`
(18 kinds: build-verify, gear, buff, cast-facts, kit-refused, harm-heal, bind, den-travel, den-fight, death, recall-home, hunt, vendor, vendor-facts,
appraise-items, appraise-vendor, chests, shortcuts). Stations per plan, with Lifestone's own expected seconds (the lead tunes them): mage build-verify 30,
gear 60, buff 200, cast-facts 120, kit-refused 20, harm-heal 90, bind 60, den-travel 60, den-fight 215 (kills 3 and a tour of two storeys, section 52), death 280, recall-home 30 (1,165 in all); melee
build-verify 30, buff 120, bind 60, hunt 300, harm-heal 90, recall-home 30, vendor 180, vendor-facts 90, chests 90 (990 in all); archer build-verify 30, buff 120, bind 60, hunt 400,
appraise-items 40, harm-heal 90, recall-home 30, appraise-vendor 90, vendor 150, shortcuts 70 (1,080). The journey's limit in the headless host is the plan's
expected seconds plus 300 s, plus `JourneyLimits.WholeExtraSeconds` (the archer's hunt extra of 800 s; section 52): mage 1,465, melee 1,290, archer 2,180; the Godot host's limit is login plus 1.5 times the expected, plus 300 s and the same extra. Every limit in the gate is twice its step.

**The travel seam (`IJourneyTravel`).** Every trip a station makes goes through the seam, with a purpose: `Bind` (the lifestone), `Ground` (the belt,
forest or the den), `Corpse`, `Home` (Holtburg), `Spot` (a fight's place), `Seek`, `Cell`, `Vendor`. `PlaceTravel` is today's place (a teleport to a
point, checked by the landblock). `JourneyTravel` decides the means: Home is Lifestone Recall (1635; the fighters cast it over the `CasterSwap`);
Ground to the den (the journey's dungeon ground, `JourneyContext.Dungeon`, from `--fight-ground`; section 52) is Primary Portal Recall (48) when the mage's portal is known tied, else the budgeted place to that ground's arrival; Ground to the belt
or the forest is the budgeted place; Seek, Cell, Vendor, Corpse, Spot and Bind use the host's runner (none: `travel:run:no-runner`). Over the budget a
place fails `travel:place:over-budget` with a WARN line (not an ERROR). The tolerance is 5 m (Lifestone's own). The **arrival contract**: a place keeps
its teleport and landblock check; a run, a recall or a nothing-to-do is authoritative (the caller trusts it); a leg on foot is bounded at 900 s
(`HuntScenario.TravelLegSeconds`). The hunt, the death and the fight (`GroundExit` since M5 item 2, section 52) go through the seam (`HuntOptions.Travel`, `DeathOptions.Travel`,
`FightOptions.Travel`), so the same rules hold in every station.

**Facts the journeys rest on (W0, live on all three characters).** The ties are not in the player description, so a journey cannot read a link from
it. `Lifestone Recall` (1635) reads the character's linked lifestone (ACE's LinkedLifestone), which only the spell **Lifestone Tie** (2644) sets at a
lifestone (`WorldObject_Magic.cs:966-976`); without it the cast is refused (0x049E). The builds therefore learn 2644 and the kits carry its components
(J3b). Bind is Use on the lifestone (the respawn point). The mage's **Primary Portal Tie** (47) links at 3 m in front of the den's portal (the portal's
guid 0x71B90000, cell 0x1B90002C; the portal's facts are the ground profile's `portal` section, section 52); after **Primary Portal Recall** (48) the mage arrives inside the den (0x01050375), so a tied journey reaches the den by
recall and its den-travel station walks no portal (`teleports=0 recalls>=2`). The untied answer (0x04A3) was not seen live, because the mage was tied
in the probe. Lying down (the death's undress and lie-down, D37) needed its own cause: see below.

**Off the lifestone (M4 4.2 FS).** A Lifestone Recall arrives at the linked lifestone's own location (`WorldObject_Magic.cs:1087-1095`), inside its
cylinder, and Lifestone's player does not collide with server objects (`TODO(d25-server-object-host)`, docs/physics.md "Other objects"), so the client
stays there where retail's would place itself out. The mage's journey ended so and logged off; on the Soak tier's next login ACE created the mage there
(`Player_Networking.cs:224`, `SendSelf`) and only then placed it out of the lifestone (`WorldObject.cs:209-220`: `enter_world`, `SyncLocation`), 2.22 m
north, with no message to the client: the where-am-I check failed (server (81.33, 14.02), client (81.33, 11.80)). With the lifestone as a physics object,
Lifestone's own placement lands on ACE's spot to the centimetre (`LifestonePlacementTests`). Until a host keeps physics objects for server objects, the
recall-home station walks to the lifestone's known point (84, 7.7) when it arrives within 2.5 m of a lifestone (`stepped_off=yes`, after the recall's
fields), so no journey logs off inside one; elsewhere than Holtburg it fails, named. Since M5 item 5 (D45, D73; docs/session.md section 55) the lifestone collides as one sphere (Setup 0x020002EE), so the arrival lands 2.22 m out where ACE puts it and the recall-home station expects `stepped_off=no`; the step-off stays the named fallback for a body still inside the stone's collision (`stepped_off=yes`), and other towns use the host's stone object, a host without one failing named.

**The three plans and the hero plans.** The mage (BSD Mage on `test`), the melee (Holtburg Brawler on `test-melee`) and the archer (Holtburg Archer on
`test-archer`) each run the station list above. The hero plans (`--journey=hero-archer|hero-brawler|hero-mage`) are the same stations with the showpieces
and the camera (J7): `--journey-shots` takes the station frames (`JourneyLegRunner.StationShots.cs`: the hunt's combat and power-bar frames, the fight's
bolt, hit and kill, the death's frames its trigger allows (the corpse and decoy frames need UI the journey does not drive), the buff's mid-cast frame, named `<stem>-<station>-<slot>.png`) and the showpiece bursts
(`ShowpieceWatch`: frost arrow, mace hit, portal recall and arrival, Cassius' Ring of Fire 1785, which the mage learns for the hero fight). The camera is
retail's chase camera only, with the rotation a hero shot uses. Hero runs are not judged by the gate. `--journey-film` (the M4 hero video's takes, docs/hero-video.md §9) frames the same camera and saves no frame, so a screen capture records the run at full rate (a 1080p frame's save stalls the game about 0.5 s); den-travel's camera turns 35° with the shots or the film, and the hero mage's den-travel steps off a lifestone within 4 m before Primary Portal Recall (`stepped_off=`, the recall home's walk), so the cast is not filmed through the crystal. The hero plans also fill the lower-right bar first (`hotbar`, `HotbarPicks`), the Brawler's ends at the store, and the mage's clears its leftover den corpses (`corpses`: `@lifestone corpses rot`, then a wait for the dropped contents to rot) and runs the den death after the ring fight; with `--journey-film` the character page's attributes are open during the death's re-buff and the inventory with the paper doll while a hunt loots (`HeroPanels`).

**One death (D37).** `DeathStages.All` is Gear, Bind, Den, Death, Regear, Vitae, Corpse, Rebuff, Fight: the mage dies once (`deaths=1`, was two) and the
journey's death station never uses an unbuff suicide (`suicides=0`). The `death-mode` stage undresses (the equipment to the pack) and `death-wait` lies
down (`MotionCommand` Sleeping through `IActionApi.Move`) once the walk stops; the report-only fields are `undressed= lie_down= lie_echo=`. The lie-down
was not sent on the wire at first: the walker had been in the Magic stance (a cast's TurnTo carried it), so the retail motion refused Sleeping
