(`CantLieDownInCombat`). The walker's stance sync is a named gap to close (not yet a TODO in the code). The death's composed fight after the respawn is
the den walk, which both journey hosts now offer.

**The two hosts and one leg planner.** `--scenario=journey --journey=mage|melee|archer` runs in the headless host (the account is the journey's; another
account is refused); `--login-check=journey` runs it in the Godot play (`GodotJourneyRunnerHost`, the player's body as the walker, sources by cell). The
hosts share one planner and one leg: `JourneyLegPlanner` (the goal, the source with the indoor exit route, arrival, the failure name, moved) and
`JourneyRunLeg` over `IJourneyLegBody`. They differ only in the body (`HeadlessBody` or the player) and where collision and tiles come from. Before
the Godot host's first leg it waits for the settle gate (`JourneySettleGate`, 120 s; `docs/wayfinding.md`, the journeys' planner).

**Verdict lines.** A station prints `journey station=<name> ok=<bool> s=<seconds> <fields> [why=<reason>] [skipped=precondition:<x>] [caused_by=<station>]`;
a skipped station after an earlier failure reads `skipped=`, so `ok` means every station was ok or skipped after a failure, all were reported, and the
teleports are within the budget. The done line is `journey done ok= name= stations=n/m failed=<names|none> teleports= recalls= budget= route_m= run_s=
vitals_passes= recovery_places= granted= recovery_place= recovery=`. `Get-JourneyVerdict` in `tools/smoke.ps1` judges the done line (`ok=true`,
`failed=none`, `teleports` within the budget, no `ok=false` station without `skipped=`). The fields `route_m`, `rungs`, `teleports`, `recalls`, `granted`,
`recovery_places`, `vitals_passes`, `run_s` and the keeper's fields are report-only in the first gate. `recovery=suicide` appears only in the mage's den
when the way home fails; the other characters use one recovery place (outside the budget, judged 0 from the second gate).

**The gear's mana (closes `TODO(m4-gear-mana)`).** The fighters keep their magic gear over many gates, and each piece's own mana burns while its spells are
active (ACE `Player_Tick.cs:623-659`; at 0 the spells are removed). Retail's remedy is a mana stone, so the Brawler's and the Archer's kits carry one Titan
Mana Charge (ace_world 9060: 5,000 mana, burden 50, Value 9,000, sold by 19 vendors), granted by the mod's `item` verb and topped up by the build as the
potions are. The fighter's build-verify (and only it: `FighterBuildScenario(rechargeGear: true)`, step (e3), after the kit is worn and packed) runs
`GearRecharge`: it appraises every worn piece of the set, the Archer's bow in the pack and the held charges (`IActionApi.Identify`; ItemCurMana and
ItemMaxMana, PropertyInt 107 and 108, are assessment properties, and ACE sends nothing while the mana burns). When a piece is under half its maximum it uses
a charge (`IActionApi.UseManaStone`, GameAction 0x0035 {stone, target}): on the player when a worn piece is low (ACE pours it over every equipped item below
its maximum, rationed evenly, `ManaStone.cs:121-190`), on the bow when only the bow is; then it appraises again, and uses another while a piece is low and
the kit's charges last. Only a stone with mana is used (an empty stone used on an item destroys the item). ACE casts an item's spells only on wield
(`Player_Inventory.cs:313-315`), so a piece whose spells were removed at 0 is taken off and worn again in the same place after its charge
(`TODO(m4-gear-mana-reactivate)`: whether retail re-cast them by itself is unread). The step never fails the build; the verdict adds
`gear_mana_min=<lowest piece's percent after the step>|unread recharged=<charges used>[:no-charge|:refused|:unread]` after `spells_active=`, report-only in its
first gate. One charge covers about three hours of the gear's burn (the trigger fires about 4,200 short for the Brawler and 4,600 for the Archer). Tests: `GearRechargeTests` (a scripted ACE: the burn, the ration, the re-wear),
`ManaStoneTrackerTests`, and `FighterBuildScenarioTests`' recharge cases.

**Limits.** The live gate's ExpectedSeconds and the projection are in section 5 of `docs/milestones/m4-plan.md`. Open TODOs from the journeys are
section 9 there (`m4-journey-buffed`, `m4-journey-ground-height`, `j4-kit-account`, `j4-station-kind`, `j4-hunt-character`, `chest-use-range`,
`renald-front-room-point`, `m4-vendor-issellable-wire`, `m4-death-walkless-wait`, `m4-lie-down-controller`, `m4-travel-failure-unnamed`).

**Since M5 item 3 (section 53).** The journeys make no place to a dungeon. The build ties the ground's portal, one tie state per ground (`JourneyFacts.PortalTieOf`: Unknown, Tied or Untied), and the mage plan's budget is `JourneyPlans.MageTeleportBudget` = 0. A dungeon travel to an untied ground fails `travel:ground:untied` (`TODO(m6-first-tie-walk)`); the Ground purpose never places to a dungeon. The death handler casts Primary Portal Recall itself. The travel stations' lifestone and point come from the ground's home (`GroundHome`), not Holtburg's. The sentences above that name the den's budgeted place, `PortalTied` or Holtburg's point for every journey are M4's; the logistics station and the done line's `granted=` and `places=` are in section 53.

## 42. Fresh characters each live gate (M4, after P5b; D44-D46)

**The re-create.** Leftover state broke several of P5b's gate rounds with no bug behind it (a fighter left in a back room or a cellar, a full
pack, used-up kits and potions, the mage's den-portal tie already set), so every live gate now starts its journeys from new characters (D44).
`--fresh-character` on the builds (`--scenario=fighter-build --account=test-melee|test-archer`, `--scenario=mage-build` on `test`; refused for
the Hunter, any other account or scenario, and with `--fighter-recreate`) logs in, deletes the journey character from the character list
through retail's delete without entering it, waits for the list to show it pending, creates the template's character, enters it and builds
it as the idempotent build always does. Only after the build has ended met does the new copy purge the old ones (`Session/Scenarios/FreshPurge.cs`):
`char purge "<its own name>"` until the verb answers `unknown-character`, so the copy just deleted and any older pending or expired rows go,
and a failed build leaves the old copy restorable. The mage is then placed in Holtburg (a fresh character starts outside it); the fighter's
build already places. The verdict gains `fresh=recreated|created purged=<n> refill=...`, and a re-created character whose old copy was not purged fails.

**The refill (`Session/Scenarios/FreshRefill.cs`, gate round 1's finding).** ACE refills the vitals only on a level-up (@95ef426d
`Player_Xp.cs` `CheckForLevelup` calls `SetMaxVitals`), and the build's raises come after its one level-up, so a fresh character
used to start its journey with its current vitals at the creation values (the mage at health 6/205). In fresh mode the build's last step,
before the purge, grants exactly the experience to the next level when any vital is below its maximum, and waits (10 s) for the level and
every vital at its maximum (`refill=level-up:<a>-><b>`, or `none`). The gate characters therefore end one level above their templates
(the mage 127, the fighters 56); the extra experience stays unspent.

**The swap's busy wait.** The fighters' caster swap waits while `IActionApi.BusyReason` names a busy chain (a potion's Ready on the
Heal lane, a move's chain) before each step, at most 15 s (`swap:busy-wait`), as the recall does (P5b FC-a1): the fresh Brawler's
unwield right after the vitals keeper's Mana Potions had been refused.

**The purge rule (D45).** For `BSD Mage`, `Holtburg Brawler` and `Holtburg Archer` only (`CharPurgeRules.Recreated`), the mod's purge refuses
as graduated while the account holds no live copy of the name, so the last copy never goes; it decides `own-character` by id rather than by
name, so the new copy may name itself; and it takes only the pending copies. The other graduated names stay refused by name, even pending.
The stand-in (`StandInCharPurge`) applies the same rule, and one table runs through both.

**The gate.** `tools\live-gate.ps1` puts a `recreate` step at the head of each journey lane (in `-Serial`, `recreate-<name>` before each
headless journey), judged by `GateJudge.psm1`'s `Get-RecreateVerdict`: exit 0, `headless done scenario=<s> ok=1`, and the fresh and purged
fields. A journey whose re-create failed is not run (one named FAIL row). `-KeepCharacters` skips the step and says so once.

**G-A (D46).** Before a live gate, `slice-check -Gate`: the build, the default tests and the Exhaustive tier, no Godot step, and one line
naming the offline steps the diff selects; the offline smoke runs once per item at its end (`-Changed`, or `-Smoke` at a wave's last item
and the milestone end).

## 43. The client-side object cull (M5 item 0 c1; D16; DoD 1)

**The retail rule.** Retail's client drops an object the server has stopped sending by itself. `CObjectMaint::AddObjectToBeDestroyed`
(11.6096 0x00509A40) stamps the object for destruction 25 seconds later and queues it; `CObjectMaint::UseTime` (0x00509480) deletes each
due object whose stamp is still the queued one; `CObjectMaint::RemoveObjectToBeDestroyed` (0x00508F10) cancels. An object is queued when
its cell is released from the client's loaded cells (`CObjectMaint::ReleaseObjCell`, 0x005086E0: every object there that is not static
and has no parent, its children with it), when it is created or moved into a cell the client has not loaded (the lost cell,
`CObjectMaint::GotoLostCell`, 0x00508CE0), or when it moves into a container that is neither the player's, the open ground container nor
the trade partner's. It is cancelled when the object enters the world again or is deleted. `CObjectMaint::DestroyObjects` (0x00509700)
is the log-off clear. D16 (m5-plan.md section 7): retail's time is 25 s, the same as ACE's assumption.

**The ACE side.** ACE relies on the same 25 s (`Physics/Common/ObjectMaint.cs:21,41` @95ef426d) and sends no delete message (F747) for an
object that left a player's view. Without the cull, a reloaded den filled with frozen ghosts, the light table filled at 256 and, after ACE
recycled guids (6 hours), new objects merged into stale entries.

**Lifestone's cull.** `ObjectCull` (`src/Lifestone.Client/Session/State/ObjectCull.cs`) is engine-free and owned by the reducer.
`GameSession.Tick` ticks it (`GameStateReducer.CullObjects`) right after the parked messages expire, so the Godot game and the headless
host share it. Times are on the session clock.

**What counts as visible.** The reducer knows the player's cell, not the renderer's loaded cells. Outdoors, and in a building cell (one
seen from outside), an object counts as visible while its cell's landblock is the player's or one of the eight around it, which is ACE's
own visible set. In a dungeon cell (an EnvCell not seen from outside), an object is visible while its cell is the player's cell or in
that cell's visible-cell list from the dat (ACE's `envCell.VisibleCells`, `ObjectMaint.cs:345-348, 365-373` @95ef426d). The set therefore
moves with the player from cell to cell, and an object outside it for 25 s is culled, as retail's `ReleaseObjCell` does. The player's cell
is the server's last position for the player (ACE echoes the player's own position to it), which is ACE's own view of that cell.

The lists come from the host through `ObjectCull.Cells` (an `IWorldAttachments`: cheap reads of data the host has already loaded, never a
load on the frame path). The Godot game gives it the portal view's `WorldAttachments` (the interior registry the streamer loads) in
`PlayMode.AttachPortal`; the headless host gives it its `DataOnlyAttachments` in the `HeadlessRunner` constructor (its `Focus` decodes the
player's cell and its visible cells as the player moves). While a list is not known yet (or reads empty), the landblock rule holds for
that scan.

**The scan.** The visible set is re-read once a second (retail's `CObjectMaint::UpdateVisibleObjectList`, 0x00508E60, has the same period),
and a due object goes on the first tick at or after its time. Any message the reducer applies for a pending object (a create, a position,
a motion, any update through the instance gate) cancels its entry. The next scan queues it again with a fresh 25 s if it is still outside,
as retail re-stamps an object moved into a lost cell.

**Never culled.** The player; an object with no world position of its own (contained or wielded: it leaves with its holder, holders before
what they hold); a static object (retail keeps them); what the player holds or wields; the ground container the player has open and its
contents.

**The removal** is the delete message's own path (`GameStateReducer.RemoveObject`: the table, the inventory, the container views, the
vendor, then `ObjectDeleted`), so the avatar set, the fight's tables and the wayfinder's creature feed follow. The object light table's
rows fall with the avatars: the server objects layer's sweep gives a row back once its avatar is no longer drawn.

**A recreated guid.** A culled guid that the server creates again is a fresh entry (`ObjectCreated` with `Recreated` false): its positions
apply from its own stamps, not the ghost's.

**Counts and the login check.** `GameSession.Cull.Pending` (waiting now) and `GameSession.Cull.Culled` (since the session started) feed
the soak line (section 44). One INFO line goes to the log per cull batch, category `objects` ("culled objects the server stopped sending",
with count, first, pending, culled_total, objects). The login check's `[logincheck] done` line ends with the report-only
` objects=<n|n/a> drawn_in_cell=<n|n/a> light_rows=<n|n/a>` (`LoginCheckScenario.ObjectFields`): the most objects the session's table held,
the most avatars DRAWN in interior cells at once, and the most object light-table rows in use at once. The soak line's `in_cell` is
different: every avatar whose body is in a cell, drawn or not.

**Tests.** `ObjectCullTests` (the cull's rules) and `ObjectCullAllocationTests` (its allocation); `ObjectCullCellsTests` (two dungeon cells
that do not see each other; a step into a cell that sees the object; a building cell; a list not known yet; no lists, the whole-landblock
rule and the TODO once); `ObjectCullDenTests` (in the same file: the Black Spawn Den, 0x0105, with the headless host's provider; it skips
without the dats).

**Open.** The m5-plan.md section 9 row `m5-object-cull` is closed. `TODO(m5-object-cull-cells)` is closed for both hosts; a host that supplies
no lists (the Godot game without a portal view, and the tests) still logs it once while the player stands in an EnvCell.

## 44. The soak line, one log and Godot's errors (M5 item 0 c3, c5; DoD 1, 13)

**The soak line.** One INFO line, `[soak] ws_mb=... log_bytes=...`, every `--soak-line-s` seconds (default 60; 0 = none; a bad value is
reported by name in the "bad argument" warning and the default is kept). It is written by `SoakLine`
(`src/Lifestone.Game/scripts/Diagnostics/SoakLine.cs`), a node that `ObjectsLayer.Install` adds beside `ObjectsSystem`. Play mode binds the
session, the avatar set (a function, since a log-off builds a new one) and the light table with `SoakLine.Bind`. The text is built by
`SoakLineFormat` (Client, engine-free) from a reused `SoakSnapshot`, into one reused `StringBuilder`. The frame path does one
`SoakFrameWindow.Add` (a ring of 16,384 doubles, with exact max, over-50 and over-75 counters) and one clock read, with no allocation
(`SoakLineTests`). Everything else happens once per interval. An exception in the line is logged once as an error and never reaches the
frame.

**Fields (43, in this order).** `ws_mb private_mb` (process working set and private bytes) `managed_mb` (`GC.GetTotalMemory(false)`)
`committed_mb frag_mb loh_mb` (`GC.GetGCMemoryInfo`; LOH = generation 3) `gen0 gen1 gen2 gc_pause_ms` (cumulative since the process began)
`static_mb video_mb tex_mb` (Godot's static, video and texture memory) `nodes orphans resources` (Godot's object monitors) `objects` (the
session's object table) `avatars` (the avatar set) `in_cell` (avatars whose body is in a cell, drawn or not) `light_rows`
(`ObjectLightTable.InUse`) `cull_pending culled` (section 43: the cull's waiting entries, and its cumulative count) `frame_max_ms frame_p99_ms
over_50 over_75` (the frames since the previous line; the p99 reads the last 16,384) `tick_ms pump_msgs` (`GameSession.LastTickMs`,
`LastPumpMessages`) `sent recv resends naks dup gaps loss_pct` (the link's totals; `naks` = NAKs the client sent) `silence_max_s` (the longest
time without a server datagram, sampled four times a second; n/a before any) `phase reconnects` `kills deaths cycles` `godot_errors`
`log_bytes` (the length of lifestone.log).

**n/a.** A field with no source reads `n/a`: `kills deaths cycles` always, until the item 4 driver reports them (`TODO(m5-soak-tallies)`,
logged once in the "soak line on" line); the session fields (`objects phase reconnects tick_ms pump_msgs` and the link) in a scene without
a session; `avatars in_cell light_rows cull_pending culled` before play mode binds. `godot_errors` is n/a only if Godot's logger could not be
registered (`TODO(m5-soak-godot-errors)`).

**The options.** `--soak-line-s=S` sets the interval (0 turns the line off). `--log-copies=console|file|all` sets where log lines go besides
lifestone.log (below). `--log-max-mb=N` caps lifestone.log (above). `--soak-frame-s=S` (0 = none, else 60 to 86400) and `--soak-frame-dir=DIR` (default cache/soak/frames) save the soak frames (below).

**godot_errors.** `GodotErrorLogger` (Godot 4.7's `Logger`, registered with `OS.AddLogger` in `Log.Initialize`, so start-up errors count)
counts every error, script error and shader error Godot logs, on any thread (interlocked); warnings do not count. Lifestone's own `Log.Error`
lines are not counted: they reach Godot through `GD.PushError`, and `GodotErrorTally.Echo` skips them, so the count holds Godot's own errors
(an uncaught C# exception counts once). Verified live: one `GD.PushError` moved the count from 0 to 1 and printed as `ERROR:` on the console.
The smoke suite counts the same way (`godot_errors=<n>` in each Godot step's row, judged 0; see the README's smoke suite section). The tests
are `GodotErrorTallyTests` and the soak line's `SoakLineTests`.

**The watcher.** `tools/soak-watch.ps1` is read-only, and the lead starts it in the background beside a run: every `-IntervalSeconds` (300)
it writes one CSV row to `-OutFile` (default `cache\soak\watch-<UTC>.csv`) and one `[soak-watch]` console line: the working set, private bytes,
handles and threads of the Godot (`Godot*`), `ACE.Server` and `mariadbd` processes (n/a when absent; the largest working set when there are
several), the MiB of the Godot user-data logs folder (default the isolated one), `server\runtime\logs`, `server\runtime\mariadb-data`, and
the free GiB of the repository's drive. `-Once` takes one sample; `-DurationSeconds` ends the loop. It starts, stops and kills nothing, and
reads no file contents. Run it from the main checkout (the server folders live there); `-GodotLogsDirectory`, `-ServerLogsDirectory` and
`-DatabaseDirectory` override the defaults. `tools/soak.ps1` (the item's lead only, from the item worktree; it starts no backend) is the soak's one command: it refuses unless `cache\backend-owner.txt` (the lead's lock) exists in the main checkout, the backend is up with the mod ready and no Godot process runs; it wakes and holds the display for the run (GateHost, ended in a finally), starts `soak-watch.ps1` (300 s, CSV in the run folder, the server folders of the main checkout, the Godot logs of this checkout), runs `play.ps1 -Offscreen -IsolateUserData -KeepServer -Character "BSD Mage"` with `--autopilot-start --autopilot-minutes=M --max-fps=60 --soak-line-s=60 --log-copies=file --log-max-mb=256 --soak-frame-s=F --soak-frame-dir=cache/soak/<stamp>/frames` (recording off), then copies `lifestone.log` and `lifestone.1.log` into `cache\soak\<UTC stamp>[-rehearsal]\`, runs the report and writes one `[soak] summary` line; `-Until HH:mm` (UTC) or `-Minutes M`, `-Resolution`, `-FrameSeconds`, `-Rehearsal`, `-Plan` (prints both roots, the run folder, the preconditions and the commands, starts nothing).

**The report.** `tools/soak-report.ps1 -RunFolder <dir>` is read-only and report-only (D54): from the run folder's log it reads the `[soak]` lines by field name, the `autopilot cycle` and `autopilot done` lines, the `reconnect: the session left the world` lines (each with its phase, state and cycle) and the `autopilot host` hand-off lines, and from `watch.csv` the largest working sets, and writes `report.txt` plus the same lines to the console: `done_ok`, `cycles` with `cycle_lines` and `accounted`, `ws_slope_mb_per_h` (least squares over the lines after the first hour), `objects_max`, `in_cell_max`, `light_rows_max` with `light_rows_ok` (under 256), `godot_errors` (the last line), `reconnects` with causes, `p99_first_ms` and `p99_last_ms` (medians of `frame_p99_ms` over the first and the last hour), `kills`, `deaths`, `interrupted`, `resumed`, `log_mb`, `minutes`; a value with no data reads `n/a`.

**The soak frames (M5 item 7).** In play mode a frame is saved every S seconds: the first one falls S seconds after the player's body is first placed in the world, and another S seconds after a new login's body. `SoakFrames` (`src/Lifestone.Game/scripts/Diagnostics/SoakFrames.cs`, stepped by `PlayMode._Process`) reads the window back on the main thread, and `ScreenshotWriter.SaveInBackgroundQuietly` encodes and writes the PNG on a worker, to `soak-<UTC yyyyMMddTHHmmssZ>-<n>.png` at the window's own size, with one INFO line `[soak] soak frame saved n=<n> path=<file name>`. The clock rule is `SoakFrameSchedule` (Client, engine-free): a stall of several periods owes one frame, not a burst. The folder follows `OutputPaths` (never src/ or tests/). The frames are screenshots of the retail render: gitignored under cache/, never committed.

**One log file (`m5-log-triple`).**

**One log file (`m5-log-triple`).** `lifestone.log` has every line in every mode. `--log-copies=console|file|all` (default `console`):
`console` = lifestone.log and the console, with Godot's `godot.log` off (`project.godot`: `debug/file_logging/enable_file_logging=false`,
and the `.pc` variant, which would otherwise turn it on); `file` = lifestone.log only, with no console copy of Debug, Info and Warning lines
(errors still go through `GD.PushError`, so the console and the error count see them); `all` = the three copies, since `tools\godot.ps1` adds
Godot's `--log-file` under the user data folder (verified: the file is written although the project setting is off). Every check reads the
console, because smoke redirects stdout, so the default keeps it. Nothing under `tools/` reads `godot.log` or `lifestone.log`. Rotation by size is done (M5 item 7, `m5-log-rotation`): `--log-max-mb=N` (default 256; 0 = no cap). When lifestone.log passes N MiB (bytes counted as written by `LogRoller`, `src/Lifestone.Client/Diagnostics/LogRoller.cs`; no file stat per line) `Log` closes it, renames it to lifestone.1.log (replacing an older one) and begins a new lifestone.log whose first line is `INFO [log] log rolled rolls=<n> previous=lifestone.1.log max_mb=<N>`, so the logs on disk stay under 2N MiB. If the rename fails (another program holds the file) the file is reopened for append, one warning is pushed, and the next try is another N MiB on (the bound is then 3N). The soak line's `log_bytes` keeps reading the current file.

**The leg guard (`Session/Hosting/LegFaultGuard.cs`, M5 item 0 c2).** Every leg runner's `_Process` (Journey, Fight, Hunt, Death, Buff,
Throwaway and the login check's) is `try { StepFrame } catch (Exception e) when (Guards(e))`. The first fault is reported once, as a Lifestone
error in the runner's category (`<runner>: <stage> threw <ExceptionType>: <message>`), followed by one more error entry holding the whole
exception (type, message, stack). The guard trips and the runner starts its own fail path with that reason: log off if logged in (Journey,
Fight, Hunt, Death, Buff: `Fail`), the scenario's abort recovery (Throwaway: `Abort`), the character sent back and logged off (the login
check, in the playing stage: `StartRecovery`), else `Finish(false, reason)`. The verdict is ok=false naming the exception, and the process
quits non-zero. After the first fault a runner steps only its fail path: observers and samplers no longer run, and the next frames do
nothing. A fault in those frames is counted, never reported, and the runner finishes at once. The login check, when it is outside `playing`
with the character in the world, sends a LogOff before its verdict. Out-of-memory is not caught. The play-mode re-buff driver
(`RebuffDriverHost`) uses the same guard but has no leg to fail: on a fault it reports once, switches the driver off and disposes it, and the
game keeps running; the Pause key then writes one log line ("the driver is off for this run after an error") and does nothing else. The
headless host (`HeadlessRunner.Step`) already ends the run on a frame's exception, once: one error line (`the headless run stopped:
<Type>: <message>`, no stack) and a finish with ok=false. It is not wired to the guard; the stack is the only thing it lacks.

**The soak run's switches (M5 item 7; `tools/soak.ps1`, `Options/OptionCatalog.Play.cs`, `OptionCatalog.Autopilot.cs`).** `tools\soak.ps1 -Minutes N` (or `-Until HH:mm`), run by the item's lead from the item worktree, plays BSD Mage with `--autopilot-start --autopilot-minutes=N --fight-loot=on --max-fps=60 --soak-line-s=60 --log-copies=file --log-max-mb=16 --log-keep=0 --log-vitals-s=10 --log-objects-m=60 --clean-hud --mute`, then `--soak-frame-s` and `--soak-frame-dir` and `--autopilot-stop-file`. `--log-max-mb N` caps `lifestone.log` (default 256; 0 = no cap): a file past N MiB is closed and rolled to `lifestone.1.log` and a new one begins with a line naming the roll; `--log-keep N` keeps N rolled files (default 1; 0 keeps every roll), so the long run's 16 MB cap with `--log-keep=0` wrote 3 files and 44.32 MB. `--soak-frame-s S` (0 = none, else 60 to 86400) saves one PNG of the window every S seconds, the first S seconds after entering the world, into `--soak-frame-dir` (default `cache/soak/frames`; the script passes the run folder's `frames`); the script's `-FrameSeconds` sets S (default 1800; the long run used 900). The frames are screenshots of the user's own render and are never committed. `--autopilot-start` starts the dial once at login, as `/auto` does (BSD Mage only; section 54), and a run it started logs off and quits the game at its own end, exit 0 for ok=yes and 1 otherwise; `--autopilot-stop-file PATH` is the run's clean stop from outside (the host looks every 5 s; the script's `-Stop` writes it). The script's own switches: `-Record` (ffmpeg's desktop grab of the window, 30 fps, hour-long `.ts` files under the run folder's `record`, with `--video-sync-marker`; D97), `-ControlDrill` (the take-over drill, section 54), `-TripAfterKills N` (one forced trip after N kills, a rehearsal's), `-ExamineWornFrames` (`--examine-worn-frames`, the item examination's colour proof, D142), `-Rehearsal`, `-Resolution` (default 1280x720), `-Plan`, `-Stop` and `-Resume`. `tools/soak-report.ps1` reads the run folder after it.

## 45. The outcome-aware caster and mana inside the fight (M5 item 1; D13-D15, D26, D27; DoD 2, 3)

**The cast policy (`CastAttempt`).** `CastAttempt` (`src/Lifestone.Client/Session/Casting/CastAttempt.cs`) is the 
outcome-aware cast policy of the P5b design, section 2. A caller decides to cast: it hands a `CastRequest` to `Start`, 
then calls `Step(now)` once a frame until the result is no longer `Running`. Each try goes through the unchanged Action 
API, one action per call; the API itself retries nothing. The attempt reads each outcome and, by its `CastRules`, 
either ends or sends the next try: the same level, the level below, or the same spell again after a spacing. Every try 
is logged (category `cast`, one line per try and one per end) and counted. The attempt is engine-free and reusable 
(`Start` resets it), and `Step` allocates nothing while a try is in flight or waiting (`CastAttemptAllocationTests`).

**What the policy never does.** It never changes the combat mode; its callers own it, and their caster swaps. It never 
reads the mana before the first try, because retail's client does not either (`gmSpellcastingUI::Cast`, 11.6096 
0x004C6C40; `ClientMagicSystem::CastSpell`, 0x00568DE0). It reads the mana at each send, to count the mana tries, and 
before a fizzle's retry when the rules check the ceiling.

**The presets.** Each preset is a `CastRules` value. The table names the caller each one reproduces; the rule is the 
one that caller had at 6857174d, checked against its code.

| Preset | Rule | Reproduces |
|---|---|---|
| `BuffLine` | no same-level retry; one level down once (the level below must be in the SpellTable); then a named miss; the mana checked before the retry | `BuffCycle`'s buff line (`CastOutcome`) |
| `Once` | no retry | the upgrade pass; `GearScenario`'s check cast; `HealStations`' checks |
| `Utility(n)` | the same spell, at most n casts while they fizzle; the mana checked before each retry | the mana loop (`TransferTries`, 8); `VitalsKeeper` (`MaxFizzlesInRow`, 3); the pre-trip heal (`PreTripMaxHeals`, 4) |
| `Bolt` | one level down to the next usable level (`Usable`), once per scenario (`LevelsDownSpent`); the mana checked before it | `FightScenario`'s bolt (`DropOneLevel`) |
| `Travel(n)` | n same-level retries (n + 1 casts), sent at once with no mana check | `RecallHome` (`MaxFizzles`, 5); the script legs (`TravelStations.MaxFizzles`, 3) |
| `Timed(s)` | fizzles retried without a count inside s seconds; the mana checked before each retry (fix A, 1-R1: it was not) | `HarmHealScript` (`HarmCapSeconds`, 60) |
| `ManaDance(presses, spacing)` | the same spell pressed again `spacing` after each 0x0401 or fizzle until it lands; at most `presses` presses, each counted | the stamina-to-mana dance (D27): the keeper's and the fight's |
| `.WithManaTries(n, spacing)` | a 0x0401 inside the tries waits `spacing` and is sent again; outside them, `ManaShort` | D15 (`--cast-mana-tries`; 0 is today's ceiling rule) |
| `.WithBusyRetries(3, 1)` | the design's busy rule: a 0x001D waits 1 s and is sent again, three times | every preset above (fix A, 1-R6); the form sets another bound (`WithBusyRetries(0)` ends `Busy` at once) |
| `.WithResistRetries(1)` | the design's resist rule (debuffs) | none today |

`BuffLineD14`, `BoltD14` and `.WithFizzleRule()` carry the owner's fizzle rule (D14, below). Where the code differs 
from the design's tables, the code wins: `Utility(n)` counts casts and `Travel(n)` counts retries; no caller retries 
a resist.

**Busy (fix A, 1-R6).** The owner's DoD 2 says "busy waits and resends". Every preset now carries the design's busy rule 
(`CastRules.MaxBusy` = `DefaultBusyRetries`, 3; `BusyWaitSeconds` = `DefaultBusyWaitSeconds`, 1 s, the keeper's own 
numbers for its kits and potions): a 0x001D is waited out and the same cast sent again, and the fourth busy answer ends 
`Busy`. Each busy answer is counted (`busy=`), and the wait stays inside the attempt's time cap. A preset's name is 
unchanged. Before the fix every preset ended `Busy` on the first, so a live 0x001D was a named miss for the buff line, 
a miss for the bolt and a failed recall.

**D14, the owner's fizzle rule.** The buff line and the bolt now retry a fizzle at the same level twice more, then drop 
one level once, then miss by name. Before, a buff line dropped one level after its first fizzle and missed after its 
second; the bolt dropped one level once per fight (kept across attempts), and each fizzled try was a miss. A fizzled 
try is still a miss and a cast. The miss text of a fizzled line reads "fizzled N times (2 more tries at the same level, 
then one level down), the last at X (no further try)". The mana is checked before each retry; a short retry goes back 
to the mana step and resumes with its spent bounds.

**The rules.** One row per outcome of a try. The bound is the preset's count; "Counted" names the report field.

| Outcome | Rule | Bound | Counted |
|---|---|---|---|
| Confirmed (self enchantment, projectile) | `Landed` | none | tries |
| Sent (UseDone 0, no wire proof) | the request's proof decides: `Landed` (proved), a resist, or `Unproven` | none | `proved` |
| Resisted (seen by the proof) | the same level again | `MaxResists` | `resists` |
| 0x0402 fizzle | the same level; then the level below; then `Fizzled` | `MaxFizzles`, `LevelsDown` | `fizzles` |
| 0x0401 mana | again after `ManaTrySpacingSeconds`; else `ManaShort` | `MaxManaTries` (0 = today's rule) | `mana_refusals` |
| 0x001D busy | again after `BusyWaitSeconds`; else `Busy` | `MaxBusy` | `busy` |
| 0x0400 | `NoComponents` | 0 | |
| 0x0550, 0x042C | `OutOfRange` | 0 | |
| UseDone 0 without the effect | `NoEffect` | 0 | |
| Refused, Cancelled, DryRun, NotImplemented | their own end, never retried | 0 | |
| 10 s timeout | `Timeout`, never resent (the cast may have happened) | 0 | |
| any other code | `ServerError` | 0 | |

`MaxTries` bounds the tries in all. `MaxSeconds` (120 s by default) is the attempt's time cap: no try is sent after it, 
and the attempt ends `TimeCap`. `CeilingBeforeRetry`: before a fizzle's retry, when the mana held is under the retry 
level's ceiling and no mana tries are left, the attempt ends `ManaShort` with nothing sent. `NextLevelIndex` names the 
level to resume at, and `request.Resume(report)` carries the spent bounds. The ceiling is the request's `ManaCeilings`, 
or `GearCasts.ManaCeiling`. `ManaTryFloor` (D15): under the floor, a refusal ends `ManaShort`.

**The ends (`CastEnd`).** `Landed` and `Unproven` count as success (`Succeeded()`). The other ends are `Fizzled`, 
`ManaShort`, `NoComponents`, `OutOfRange`, `NoEffect`, `Busy`, `Resisted`, `Refused`, `Cancelled`, `DryRun`, `Timeout`, 
`TimeCap`, `ServerError` and `NotImplemented`. `Running` is the only state that is not an end.

**The report and the tally.** `CastReport` holds the end, the code and the outcome kind; `LevelIndex`, `SpellId` (the 
level cast last) and `NextLevelIndex`; the counts `Tries`, `Fizzles`, `FizzleRetriesSpent`, `LevelsDownSpent`, 
`ManaTries` (the tries sent under that level's ceiling), `ManaRefusals`, `ManaLanded` (the mana tries that landed, 
confirmed by the server's effect or proved by the chat proof; a Sent cast nothing proves ends `Unproven`, is counted in 
the tally's `Unproven` and is not a landing: fix A, 1-R16), `Busy`, `Resists` and `Proved`; 
and `Seconds` and `Why`. `CastTally.Add` sums reports into attempts, landed, unproven, failed and each count. 
`CastTally.Fields` writes `mana_tries= mana_refusals= mana_landed= busy= resists= proved=`, report-only in their first 
gate (m5-plan.md section 5 item 3). The fizzle fields stay the callers' own: `fizzles=`, `recall_fizzles=` and 
`tie_fizzles=`.

**The source (D13).** An attempt sends as `ActionSource.Automation` unless its request names another source. Before 
every send, the first included, an Automation attempt reads the kill switch; when it is on, the attempt ends 
`Cancelled` with nothing sent. A try in flight cannot be unsent: it finishes, and the attempt ends `Cancelled` instead 
of resending.

**The chat-line proof (`CastChatProof`).** `ICastProof.Check` is asked once, when a try ends Sent. `CastChatProof` 
reads the caster's own Magic lines (chat type 0x07, a System line) that arrived since the send. The forms are ACE's, 
from `WorldObject_Magic.cs` @95ef426d:
- `You cast <spell> on <name>|yourself`, alone or with ", surpassing", ", refreshing" or ", but it is surpassed by ...": lines 409-421 and 443;
- the dispel forms: lines 1410-1422;
- the transfer forms: lines 835 and 843;
- the boost forms: lines 546-554;
- `<name> resists your spell`: line 192, a resist.

The spell name comes from the SpellTable and the target's name from the object table. The proof reads spans and 
allocates nothing (`The_chat_proof_allocates_nothing`).

What it closes: `TODO(m3-cast-other-target-proof)` is closed for every target the object table names. It stays for a 
target with no name or no proof given, which ends `Unproven` and names the TODO. The design's second proof (the 
target's own registry, its section 4.2) is not built.

**The source rule (D13).** Every automated caller's casts, mode changes and drinks are `ActionSource.Automation`:
- the buff cycle (`Session/Mage/BuffCycle.cs`): the buff lines, the upgrade pass, the mana loop's casts, the Mana Potion drink and the mode changes; no `Host` send is left in it;
- the rebuff driver (`RebuffDriver.cs`): its two mode changes (its passes are the buff cycle's);
- the fight (`FightScenario.cs`): the bolt and its three mode changes; `Quit`, `LogOff` (twice) and `QueryHealth` (a read of the target's health, not a cast path) stay `Host`;
- the gear scenario (`GearScenario.cs`): the check cast and its three mode changes; login, entering the world, quitting, log-offs, the wields and unwields, and the tool's spawn stay `Host`;
- the keeper (`VitalsKeeper.cs`), harm-heal (`HarmHealScript.cs`), the recall (`RecallHome.cs`), the pre-trip heal (`DeathScenario.Heal.cs`), and the journey's script legs and heal stations (`Journey/TravelStations.cs`, `Journey/HealStations.cs`): their casts, mode changes, drinks and uses.
- the caster swap (`Fighter/CasterSwap.cs`): its wields, unwields and mode changes (fix B, review 1-R7); a switch armed between two steps leaves the swap where it stood, since the restore it then tries is refused too;
- the gear recharge (`Fighter/GearRecharge.cs`): the mana charge's use and the take-off and wear-again of a silent piece; its appraisals stay `Host` (reads);
- the scenarios' own mode changes and heals: the buff scenario's magic mode before the cycle and its peace after it (`BuffScenario.cs`), the death scenario's peace before the suicide and after a rebuff alone (`DeathScenario.cs`, `DeathScenario.Order.cs`), the journey's peace request (`JourneyScenario.cs`), and the hunt's kit use and potion drink (`HuntScenario.cs`: `UseWithTarget` and `Drink`).

Kept as `Host`, and why: the log-offs, the quit and the login (a kill switch never stops them); the movement sink; 
`SpawnItem` and `Drop` in the refused-kit station (the mod's item verb and the clean-up of its own spawn); the suicide 
tool of the death station and of the journey's recovery (a tool verb, the test itself); the appraisals and health 
queries (reads); and the hunt's own melee loop (attack, cancel, corpse open and close, the weapon swap's moves, its own 
mode changes), which is a scripted scenario outside this item: only its kit use and potion drink moved (fix B). The 
caster swap's sends are `Automation` since fix B (above).

The kill switch and dry run now reach the whole pass, not only its casts, because the Action API applies both to 
`Automation` sends. A queued cast is Cancelled; the next send of a pass is Refused ("the kill switch is on"); an 
attempt ends `Cancelled` with nothing sent. A buff pass fails naming the switch ("the cast was not carried out: ..."), 
and the fight fails the same way instead of missing and walking on. Dry run answers every Automation cast `DryRun` and 
sends nothing. A cast the fight ends Cancelled or DryRun fails the fight (new); a 0x0401 refusal is still a plain miss. 
The Action API's loopback gate (`CheckLoopback`) also applies to Automation sends, so against a server that is not 
loopback these casts and mode changes are refused, as the localhost rule intends.

**The kill switch on a non-Player account.** No host in `src/` sets the kill switch or dry run by a property. 
`PlayerTools` engages the kill switch itself (`ActionApi.Engage`) when the account's access level is above Player, or 
when ACE's first-account Admin auto-promotion is on. Dry run is set only by tests. So on such an account the casts that 
went through as `Host` before (the buff cycle's, the fight's, the gear scenario's, and the keeper's, harm-heal's, 
recall's and journey's, and since fix B the caster swap's, the gear recharge's, the buff and death scenarios' mode 
changes, the journey's peace request and the hunt's heals) are now refused. A refused mode change or heal ends its step 
by name (the buff scenario fails at `mode-magic`; a refused peace is a warning and the run goes on to its log-off, 
which stays `Host`). No test or live run uses such an account.

**Named ends.** The keeper's pass ends `Blocked why=kill-switch` with nothing more sent, its stand-up key and swap-out 
included. The recall ends `recall:kill-switch`, before its mode change or cast, or after a cast in flight has finished 
(the caster swap is not restored, since a restore is a send). The harm-heal script names `why=kill-switch` when its 
cast attempt is cancelled. A script leg that cannot send a cast fails with "cast N was not sent: cancelled (the kill 
switch is on ...)".

**The Mana Conversion model (`ManaConversionModel`).** One model is the source for ACE's mana cost. The scripted caster 
in the tests and the stand-in roll through `RollCost`; the try floor and the fight's mana phase use the exact side. 
ACE's cost is `Creature_Magic.cs:58-100` @95ef426d (`GetManaCost`, behind `CalculateManaUsage` at 41-45); the skill factor is 
`SkillCheck.cs:9-14` @95ef426d (a float, 0.03); the power modifier is `SpellProperties.cs:76` @95ef426d (the larger of the power and 25). 
An untrained caster (Mana Conversion 0) and a spell that ignores Mana Conversion pay the base and draw nothing.

ACE rolls twice. Its rolls are doubles (`ThreadSafeRandom.Next(float, float)` returns one), so the source is a 
`Func<double>`. The draw order is ACE's: the roll and the luck (both always), then the second roll only while the cost 
after the first stage is over 1. The cost is `Math.Round` (to even) of the stage's cost times `1.0f - (chance - roll * 
luck)`, and at least 1. The tests pin the to-even cases (2.5 gives 2, 3.5 gives 4, 4.5 gives 4; 
`The_cost_rounds_to_even_as_Math_Round_does`).

`LandingChance` is exact, not sampled. It is the probability that the cost is at most the mana held, integrated 
analytically over the three uniform draws: for a luck l the first stage gives each cost k over an interval of first 
rolls, the second stage lands with the smaller of its own bound and the mana bound over the luck, and the products are 
constants integrated with logs. It is O(base) arithmetic on the stack, with no allocation 
(`ManaConversionModelAllocationTests`). The one modelling step is that the draws are continuous and uniform; ties at a 
half have measure zero. It was checked three ways: against item 0's table (to that table's rounding, 0.0005), against a 
deterministic Halton integration of `RollCost` over 2^20 points (largest gap 7e-5 on the CDF, 3e-4 on the mean), and 
against a seeded sample of 200,000 draws (within 0.01). A 4-million-draw Monte Carlo, run outside the repository, 
agrees to three places.

Item 0's landing chances (`docs/milestones/m5-plan.md`, section 8, the measures table) against the model, for 
Meditative Trance (base 70, power 300):

| Mana Conversion | Mana held | Item 0 | Model |
|---|---|---|---|
| 311 | 1 | 12% | 12.1% |
| 311 | 5 | 35% | 34.8% |
| 311 | 10 | 53% | 52.7% |
| 311 | 15 | 65% | 65.3% |
| 258 | 5 | 18% | 18.1% |
| 258 | 10 | 37% | 36.8% |
| 258 | 15 | 50% | 50.3% |

Stamina to Mana Self VI (base 60, power 250) at 311 lands 28.4% at 1 mana, 57.8% at 5, 74.9% at 10 and 84.1% at 15. 
Item 0's figures at 220 (17% at 10 mana, 35% at 15) are not pinned by a test.

The exact means against item 0's rounded figures (`ExpectedCost`):

| Spell (base, power) | Mana Conversion | Item 0 | Exact mean |
|---|---|---|---|
| Meditative Trance (70, 300) | 311 | 14.16 | 14.187 |
| Meditative Trance (70, 300) | 258 | 20.63 | 20.664 |
| Stamina to Mana Self VI (60, 250) | 311 | 7.84 | 7.835 |
| Flame Bolt VII (35, 300) | 311 | 7.1 | 7.149 |
| Flame Bolt VI (30, 250) | 311 | 4.0 | 4.054 |

The design's open question (Harm Self V at 166, base 60, power 200) is answered by the model at 24.0, not the 28.3 that 
section 40 estimated.

**The try floor (`TryFloor`, D15).** The try floor is the least mana at which a press lands with the chance (0.25 by 
default, `DefaultTryChance`), and never under the minimum (`DefaultTryMinimum`, 5). The minimum decides the floor at 
311; the chance alone gives 1 to 3 there.

| Spell | Mana Conversion | Floor with no minimum | Floor with the minimum 5 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Flame Bolt VII | 311 | 2 | 5 |
| Flame Bolt VI | 311 | 1 | 5 |
| Meditative Trance | 311 | 3 | 5 |
| Meditative Trance | 258 | 7 | 7 |

**The stand-in's charge and refusal (`StandInWorld.Cast.cs`, `StandInWorld.Actions.cs`).** The stand-in now charges 
mana, which closes `TODO(m3-standin-cast-mana)`. A cast rolls its cost once, before the words (ACE 
`Player_Magic.cs:566-585` @95ef426d). A cost over the mana held is answered UseDone 0x0401 at once, with no words, motion, charge 
or effect. Otherwise the charge is the private vital update 0x02E9 at the gesture's end, just before the effect or the 
fizzle (`Player_Magic.cs:855-858` @95ef426d). A fizzle costs 5 (the stand-in's fizzle has no dice: its power is over 350). The 
cost is the base mana, plus ManaMod for each worn clothing piece or shield that carries an armour-category item 
enchantment on the player, plus ManaMod for a fellowship spell (a fellowship of one). It is rolled by the model with 
the stand-in's own Mana Conversion: skill 16 when the description holds it trained or specialised 
(`SkillRules.TryCurrent`, formula (Focus + Self) / 6), else 0, untrained, which pays the base. The default description 
holds no skill 16. Two named placeholders replace the old one where the stand-in cannot charge: 
`m5-standin-cast-mana-spell` (no spell table) and `m5-standin-cast-mana-vital` (no mana vital in the description). The 
rolls are seeded (`StandInWorld.ManaSeed`), and every stand-in cast now sends 0x02E9 before its effect.

**The mana order (`ManaOrder`, `src/Lifestone.Client/Session/Vitals/ManaOrder.cs`).** `ManaOrder.Decide` is one rule 
for the keeper, the buff cycle and the fight. It reads the caster's mana, stamina, Mana Conversion, the need 
(`ManaNeed`: ceiling and floor), the potions held and the profile (`ManaProfile.Mage` or `Fighter`), and returns a 
`ManaStep` with its reason.

| Step | Condition | Fact behind it |
|---|---|---|
| Ready | no need, or mana at or over the ceiling | ACE never charges more than the base (`Creature_Magic.cs:58-93` @95ef426d) |
| CeilingOrder | `--cast-mana-tries 0` and mana under the ceiling | today's order, run by the caller's own code, unchanged |
| TryReal | floor at or below the mana, under the ceiling, tries not spent | D15. A refused cast is answered 0x0401 and charges nothing, so every try is a fresh roll (`Player_Magic.cs:566-585` @95ef426d). The floor is `ManaOrder.FloorOf`: the model's try floor at 0.25, never under 5 |
| Dance (mage) | under the target; a known Stamina to Mana Self level lands at the mana held (`DanceLevelOf`); the stamina funds it (`Funds`) | D27. The give is 0.875 x stamina at VII and 0.75 at VI, capped at the missing mana (`WorldObject_Magic.cs:697-760` @95ef426d). Item 0: 158 stamina gave 277 mana |
| Potion (mage) | the stamina is too low for a transfer; potions held | the brief: a potion only when the stamina is too low |
| RestStamina (mage) | no potion; rest in peace until `StaminaFor` | item 0: stamina regenerates 1.30 a second at rest and 0.66 in magic stance; mana 0.44 in every stance |
| Wait (mage) | stamina enough, but no press can land; or no Stamina to Mana known and no potion | the caller's bounded rest (keeper) or mana wait (buff cycle, 180 s) |
| Potion (fighter) | under the target; potions held | D40, the order kept |
| Dance (fighter) | no potion; stamina at or over `--vitals-stamina-until` (80%); transfers left in the cap (2) | D40. The fighters' stamina is an attack resource |
| Wait (fighter) | otherwise | D40's rest in peace |

The rules the steps use:
- `Funds`: at the worst roll the mana after a landing is at least the mana kept (the mana less the base, or 0) plus the give, so it reaches the target; or the give is at least twice the base (`WorthwhileGiveBases`), since a landing nets at least the base again.
- `DanceLevelOf`: the highest known level whose press lands at least at the try chance; else the level with the best chance above 0. Item 0 at 311, mana 1: VI lands 28% and the Trance 12%.
- A dance with no landing in all its presses sets the dance aside: for the keeper until its next needs, for the buff cycle for the pass. Either is named in the log.
- `PreemptTransfer` (D27): stamina at or over 0.9 of its maximum and at least 100 mana missing. Stamina regenerates nothing at its maximum. Below 100 missing, the cap scales the drain, and a press (mean cost 14 at 311) buys under half a landing.

The thresholds and their reasons:

| Threshold | Value | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| try chance (`--cast-mana-try-chance`) | 0.25 | the landing chance a try must have at the mana held; it sets the try floor |
| try floor minimum | 5 | the floor is never under 5; at 311 it sets the floor |
| tries (`--cast-mana-tries`) | 8 | a refused cast is free, so each refusal is one more roll |
| spacing (`--cast-mana-try-spacing-s`) | 0.25 s | a person's click rate |
| presses (`--cast-mana-dance-presses`) | 40 | each press is one action; the keeper's pass allows 40 actions |
| fighters' stamina gate | 80% (`--vitals-stamina-until`) | D40 |
| fighters' transfer cap | 2 a pass (`VitalsKeeper.FighterTransferCap`) | D40 |
| fight's dance aside | 60 s (`FightScenario.DanceAsideSeconds`) | Lifestone's own |
| fight's rest cap | 300 s (`FightScenario.RestCapSeconds`) | Lifestone's own: 275 stamina at 1.30 a second is about 210 s |
| potion vital wait | 3 s (`VitalsKeeper.PotionVitalWaitSeconds`) | the potion's vital update is waited for before the next step |

`--cast-mana-tries 0` is the ceiling order exactly: no tries, no dance. It is the old `ManaShort` path, and the bolt's 
`BoltD14` keeps the owner's fizzle rule; the existing tests pin it.

**The keeper.** A station's need is met at `VitalsNeeds.NextCastFloor` (new; 0 means the ceiling). The keeper's own 
casts (Heal Self, Revitalize, Stamina to Mana Self) are sent from their floor with `Utility(3).WithManaTries(n, 
spacing)` and `ManaTryFloor`. Once its tries are spent, a line waits for its ceiling until the next needs. Each dance 
press is one action; a dance is cut to the actions left, and the rest cap still holds. The stations pass their floor 
too (fix B): the harm-heal script's mana rest sets `NextCastFloor` from the harm levels' try floors and its harm casts 
are sent under the ceiling from the floor with `Timed(60).WithManaTries` and `ManaTryFloor` (a level counts as 
affordable from its floor); the cast-facts station funds its three casts to the sum of their floors, waits for that 
sum, and sends a cast under its ceiling from its floor (`Once.WithManaTries`). Both read the keeper's `ManaOptions`; 
with `--cast-mana-tries 0`, or a caster with no Mana Conversion (the fighters), every floor is the ceiling and nothing 
changes.

**The buff cycle.** A buff line is sent from its floor with `BuffLineD14.WithManaTries`, and the upgrade pass with 
`Once.WithManaTries`. A refusal inside the tries is expected. When they are spent, the cycle goes back to the mana step 
to fund the ceiling. The mage never casts Revitalize for mana in this order; a stamina rest goes to NonCombat at once. 
A dance counts as one transfer tried and as one of `ManaCastLimitPerLine`'s casts. The fighters' futile-window rule and 
`PreemptTransfer` (transfer ahead) belong to the ceiling order only.

**The fighters.** For a fighter the order is `VitalsOptions.FighterOrder` (D40, kept): a Mana Potion first, under the 
station's next need; then a Stamina to Mana Self dance only at stamina `--vitals-stamina-until` or more, with no potion 
left, at most two a pass; then a rest in peace.

**The options (group "Mana order", main scene and headless host).** `--cast-mana-tries` (0-40, default 8), 
`--cast-mana-try-chance` (above 0, at most 1, default 0.25), `--cast-mana-try-spacing-s` (0.1-5, default 0.25) and 
`--cast-mana-dance-presses` (1-100, default 40). A bad value gives the error "bad cast option: --cast-mana-...: ...", 
which lands in `VitalsOptions.Errors`, `BuffOptions.Errors` and `FightOptions.Errors`, so the hosts that refuse those 
errors fail the run at once. The headless host lists each distinct problem once (its error list is de-duplicated, fix 
B).

**The fields (report-only).** The keeper's verdict, once it has cast, ends with `mana_tries= mana_refusals= 
mana_landed= busy= resists= proved=`, and where a dance ran with `mana_presses= dances= dances_landed=`. Both come 
before `cast=needs-swap` and `swaps=`, so those stay last. The buff cycle's `pass done:` and `FAILED:` lines end with 
`BuffResult.CastFields`, which adds `mana_presses= dances= dances_landed=` where a dance ran. The buff station's 
verdict (`BuffScenario.VerdictFields`) carries the same fields, summed over the passes (ordered, forced or driver; 
`BuffLegVerdict.CastFieldsOf`), between `cycle_s=` and `misses=` so that the swap, driver and `surpassed=` tails stay 
as they were. The buff leg's line carries them after `cast_scripts_expected=` and before `frame_over_50=` and the 
driver fields. Report-only. The dance's requests (`BuffCycle`, `VitalsKeeper`) carry a `CastChatProof` (fix B), so a 
landed Stamina to Mana Self press, which ACE answers UseDone 0 and the Magic chat line "You cast <spell> on yourself 
and lose N points of stamina and also gain M points of mana" (`WorldObject_Magic.cs` 835 @95ef426d, a form 
`CastChatProof.Classify` already reads), ends `Landed` instead of `Unproven`: it counts in `proved=` and in 
`mana_landed=` as well as in `dances_landed=`. The other casts of both callers still ask no proof. The fight's dance 
does the same (fix A).

**The fight's mana (`FightScenario`, D15, D27).** The bolt is sent from its try floor (`ManaOrder.FloorOf`, the model 
at the caster's Mana Conversion, chance 0.25, never under 5) with the Mana Conversion tries 
(`BoltD14.WithManaTries(--cast-mana-tries, --cast-mana-try-spacing-s)`, with `ManaTryFloor` set to the floor). A 0x0401 
inside the tries costs nothing (ACE `Player_Magic.cs:566-585` @95ef426d) and is the mana's, not a miss. A bolt that ends 
`ManaShort` with its tries spent goes to the mana phase with `TriesSpent`, and the order funds the ceiling next.

The mana phase follows `ManaOrder.Decide` for the mage:
- Ready or TryReal: back to the pick; the bolt is sent.
- Dance: `CastRules.ManaDance(--cast-mana-dance-presses, spacing)` at the level `DanceLevelOf` chose. Every press is counted (`mana_presses=`). A dance that spends every press without a landing is named (a WARN: "the server's costs are not the model's") and set aside for `FightScenario.DanceAsideSeconds` (60 s).
- Potion: a Mana Potion only when the stamina is too low for a transfer. The vital's update is waited for (`VitalsKeeper.PotionVitalWaitSeconds`, 3 s); a lost update or a refused drink sets the potions aside for the fight, named.
- RestStamina: NonCombat (standing, in peace) until the decision's stamina target or `FightScenario.RestCapSeconds` (300 s). A fall in health ends the rest early (a WARN, named) and sets the rest aside for `FightScenario.RestAsideSeconds` (30 s, Lifestone's own: something is hitting, and a rest beside it would end on the next blow and flip the mode each time; fix A, 1-R3). During the aside the order waits in the magic mode ("the rest for stamina is set aside"), where a bolt or a dance goes out as the mana allows; when it ends the order may rest again. Otherwise, after the rest: the magic mode and the dance.
- Wait: "waiting for mana: the bolt needs N (up to C), the player has M; <reason>", logged once per wait. The order decides again when the mana or the stamina moves.

The pre-emptive transfer (D27): before a bolt, when `ManaOrder.PreemptTransfer` holds, the dance comes first. 
`--cast-mana-tries 0` is today's ceiling wait exactly (the test 
`Cast_mana_tries_zero_is_the_ceiling_wait_with_no_tries_and_no_dance` pins it). The dance levels are the book's Stamina 
to Mana Self levels at the buff cycle's cast chance, read at the fight's start and again with the level. The arena-here 
run, which uses the offline stand-in, dances nothing.

**The level and its cap (D10, D26).** `--fight-max-level` (1-7, default 7) caps `ChooseLevel`; both overloads take 
`maxLevel`. BSD Mage at 127 keeps VII. The level-80 mage casts VI by the profile's rule (D26); the rule is the ground's spell plan (M5 item 2, section 52: `FightScenario.PlanCap` reads it for the character's level; D41, `m5-level80-vi` closed). The level is chosen again, and logged once per change ("the level is chosen again: VII to VI ..."), when the 
enchantment registry's array changes (it is replaced on every 0x02C1-0x02C8 change: buffs, debuffs, vitae), or the 
character's level, War Magic's ranks, or Focus or Self changes. The check is allocation-free at each pick; only a 
change re-reads the skill, Mana Conversion and the dance levels. The fizzle's once-per-fight drop (the owner's rule) is 
unchanged; a re-choice may lift the level back after a drop.

**The fight's limits.**
- `--fight-kills 0` is no kill limit; it needs `--fight-minutes` (without it, an error). `FightOptions.MaxKills` (1,000) stays for the death loop and the journeys.
- `--fight-stamina-full` (default 90, 0 is off): the stamina line the fight refills to with Revitalize Self (M5 7 F5, D88; section 56).
- `--fight-minutes` (0-1440, 0 off) ends the fight CLEANLY at the limit ("the fight's N minutes are up"), Succeeded with the kills made. The `bsd-fight` verdict still judges kills of at least 1 and `damage_dealt` above 0.
- `--fight-seconds` stays a failure limit. With minutes set, the failure limit is `FightOptions.FailSeconds`, the larger of the seconds and the minutes times 60.
- The hosts: the headless run's limit is `Program.FightQuitAfterSecondsFor` (3,600 s, raised by a fight limit above the 300 s default). The game's leg uses `FightLegRunner.WholeCheckSecondsFor(options.FailSeconds)`, which stays 750 s by default.
- The journey (fix B, 1-R4): the den-fight station's limit is the larger of `ExpectedSeconds x 3 + 300` (750 s) and `FailSeconds + 300` (`JourneyLimits.StationSeconds`, `FightMarginSeconds`), so `--fight-minutes 30 --fight-kills 0` gives 2,100 s; the factory answers it through `IStationFactory.StationLimitSeconds`. Both whole-run limits add the raise of the fight over its 300 s default for each den fight (`JourneyLimits.WholeExtraSeconds`: `Program.JourneyQuitAfterSeconds(plan, fight)` and `JourneyLegRunner.WholeSecondsFor(plan, fight)`, 1,500 s for 30 minutes). With the default options every journey limit is what it was.
- The killed list (`m5-fight-killed-growth`): a guid leaves it once its object has left the object table (pruned at each pick, with no allocation); a re-created guid is pickable again.

**The fight's fields (report-only).** `VerdictFields` ends with `mana_wait_max_s= mana_wait_s= kills_per_hour= 
kills_per_hour_burst= mana_tries= mana_refusals= mana_landed= busy= resists= proved= mana_presses= dances= 
dances_landed= storeys_visited= basement_entries= returns= slots_standing= legs_recovered= kills_unpaired= slots_expired=`, and the `bsd-fight done` line carries the same (the tour fields are section 52's, report-only). `level=` keeps its place before `range_m=`, which a 
test pins.
- `mana_wait_max_s` is the longest mana wait and `mana_wait_s` the total. A mana wait is the whole pause for mana (fix A, 1-R9): it opens at the bolt's first refused try (0x0401) or at the first bolt not sent for mana, whichever comes first, and closes at the next bolt that lands or is sent with the mana for it (at or over its ceiling, so no refusal is possible). The refused tries, the dance, the potion, the rest in peace for stamina and the plain wait are all inside it; the pre-emptive transfer (D27) is not a mana wait, since it spends stamina before the mana is short and holds no bolt back. One still open at the end counts to the end. `mana_wait_max_s` at 10 or less is judged from item 2's gate on the mage journey's den-fight station line (since M5 item 2 k1; the failure texts are in the README; section 52).
- `kills_per_hour` is the kills over the whole fight time. `kills_per_hour_burst` is the kills over the fight time less the time with no target to pick (from a walk begun after "no tusker is in range on the floor" or "no tusker has a clear line" to the next target picked).
- `mana_tries` to `proved` are the bolts' tallies (`CastTally`, above), and the dances' presses: a dance that was still pressing when `--fight-minutes` ended the fight keeps its presses in them (fix A, 1-R11). `mana_presses` counts the dance's presses, `dances` the dances, and `dances_landed` the dances that landed. The dance's press (and the pre-emptive transfer's) carries the chat proof (`CastChatProof`): a landed Stamina to Mana Self is proved by ACE's own line ("You cast <spell> on yourself and lose N points of stamina and also gain M points of mana", `WorldObject_Magic.cs` 835 @95ef426d, chat type Magic), ends `Landed`, and counts in `proved`, `mana_landed` and `dances_landed`.
- `spells_cast` counts the casts that went through: the tries sent, less those the server refused 0x0401 (nothing was cast; they stay in `mana_refusals`) and less a try still in flight (fix A, 1-R10).
- The kill switch: when it is on, the fight fails at once, named `fight: Blocked why=kill-switch` as the keeper names its own, and sends nothing more as Automation (fix A, 1-R15; before, a Cancelled Drink or mode change left it waiting in the mana phase until its next bolt).

**The measures (lead-run, live).** `DenMeasureLiveTests.DenTour` (M5 item 2 k1: the 30-minute whole-den read, section 52), `DenMeasureLiveTests.FightDance` (Loopback, `Tier=Measure`, `Lane=solo`, account 
`test`; about 40 minutes, limit 3,000 s) runs the top-up, then Magic, spends the mana down to 15 or less with self 
buffs, then the fight with `Kills=0 Minutes=30 MaxLevel=7`. It prints `[measure] FightDance fight_ok= ...` with the 
verdict fields, the dances, presses, landings, refusals, mana gained, stamina spent per landing, the lowest stamina, 
the potions, the rests and the waits. It asserts that it ended ok, ran to the minutes, and danced at least once. 
`DenMeasureLiveTests.FightDanceVi` is the same at `MaxLevel=6` for 15 minutes (about 25 minutes, limit 1,800 s). Run 
one at a time: `dotnet test tests/Lifestone.Loopback.Tests --disable-build-servers --filter 
"FullyQualifiedName~DenMeasureLiveTests.FightDance"`.

**The tests (offline, `tests/Lifestone.Client.Tests`).** The test count baseline is 9,529 
(`tests/Shared/test-count-baseline.json`).

| Class | What it proves |
|---|---|
| `CastAttemptTests` (with `CastAttemptAllocationTests`) | each preset reproduces its caller's rule; every preset family waits out a busy 3 times and ends `Busy` on the fourth, counted (fix A, 1-R6); `Timed` checks the mana before a retry (1-R1); an unproven Sent cast is not `mana_landed` (1-R16); D14; the mana tries and their floor; each end; the kill switch and dry run inside an attempt; the chat proof's table; the tally's fields; `Step` and the proof allocate nothing |
| `CastAdoptionTests` | the callers' sends are Automation; their casts go through their attempts with today's counts (the keeper, harm-heal, recall, the pre-trip heal, the script legs); the kill switch and dry run end each one named. The heal station has no offline rig (`TODO(k4-heal-station-offline)`) |
| `KillSwitchCastTests` | a queued cast, a waiting try, a buff pass after its second cast, a mode change and dry run: each stops with nothing sent after it; releasing the switch lets a new pass run to its end |
| `ManaConversionModelTests` (with `ManaConversionModelAllocationTests`) | item 0's landing chances and means; the distribution; the exact integration against the Halton one; the seeded sample; ACE's draw order; the to-even rounding; ACE's skill chance; the try floor; the landing chance, the floor and a roll allocate nothing |
| `StandInCastManaTests` (in the same file) | an untrained stand-in pays the base; a cost over the mana held is refused with 0x0401 at once, charging nothing, and every press is refused again; a fizzle costs 5, and a player under 5 is refused; a trained stand-in pays the rolled cost; the same description gives the same costs |
| `ManaOrderTests` | the steps and their conditions; `--cast-mana-tries 0` as today's order; the fighters' order and cap; the stamina rule; the pre-emptive transfer; the options and their errors; a decision allocates nothing |
| `FightManaTests` | the bolt from its floor with each refusal counted, the 0.25 s gaps and `spells_cast` (a seed that refuses three tries, fix A 1-R5/1-R10); the mana wait from the first refused try to the landing (1-R9); the dance under the floor with each press and gap counted; the rest set aside after a health fall while a tusker hits, and a bolt during the aside (1-R3); a fight ended mid-dance keeps its presses (1-R11); the kill switch fails the fight at once (1-R15); a potion at low stamina; the rest for stamina, then the dance; the dance before the bolt at 90% stamina with 100 mana missing; `--cast-mana-tries 0` as the ceiling wait; the waits and both rates on the fake clock; `--fight-minutes`, `--fight-kills 0` and the killed list; `--fight-max-level 6` choosing VI; the re-choice after a buff change; the fight options' parse and errors |

The other offline tests changed with the rules (`BuffCycleTests`, `BuffScenarioTests`, `FightScenarioTests`, 
`GearScenarioTests`, `RebuffDriverTests`, `VitalsKeeperTests`, `HeadlessFightOptionsTests`, `StandInCastSequenceTests`, 
`StandInActionsTests`, `StandInFightTests`, and the `ScriptedCaster` test double; fix A added `HarmHealScriptTests`' fizzle at the affordable edge and changed `FcaRecallBusyTests`' busy answer from an end to three waits and resends); they now assert the Automation 
source, the stand-in's 0x02E9 before its effect, and the fight's new fields.

**The live checks (lead-run).** `KillSwitchBuffLiveTests` (Loopback, Account test, Lane solo; `LoopbackFact`; BSD Mage)
arms the kill switch after a forced pass's second confirmed cast. It no longer requires two casts sent (fix B, review
1-R2): it records the clock when the switch is armed, requires that no Automation cast was queued at or after it and
that the pass ended `Failed` naming the kill switch, checks that an Automation mode change is accepted after the switch
is released, and reports `casts_sent=` and `queued_after_switch=` (a count, not judged).
`DenMeasureLiveTests.FightDance` and `FightDanceVi` (above) are the live reads of the fight's mana.

## 47. The wire and the read model: questions, property updates, login notices and six actions (M5 6 w1, w2; D31, D32; DoD 11)

The codecs are `Lifestone.Net` `Messages/Events/ConfirmationEvents.cs` (GE 0x0274 and 0x0276, the `ConfirmationType` enum), `Messages/Events/LoginNoticeEvents.cs` (GE 0x0295, 0x0029, 0x0021), `Messages/World/PropertyUpdateCodecs.cs` (the public and private property updates) and the client actions in `Messages/Actions/`. Their layouts and the ACE file:line of each are in `docs/protocol.md` section 5.5. The reducer is `GameStateReducer.Confirmations.cs` and `GameStateReducer.Properties.cs`; the actions are `IActionApi`, `ActionApi` and `Trackers/RetailTrancheTrackers.cs`, with `ActionKind` appended. Three option-block TODOs are the codec's: `TODO(m5-options-block-gameplayoptions)` (the gameplay bytes also hold the inventory tail, so the writer takes the archive from a parameter), `TODO(m5-options-block-genericqualities)` (never written: ACE never sends it, and ACE's reader reads its float table as strings, a bug in ACE), `TODO(m5-options-block-flags)` (which sections retail's own client sends is not captured; the live step records one).

**The server's questions.** `GameState.PendingConfirmation` is the newest open question (or null) and `GameState.PendingConfirmations` every open one, oldest first; each is a `PendingConfirmation(Type, Context, Text, Time)` with `Kind` as ACE's `ConfirmationType`. GE 0x0274 opens one (`ConfirmationRequested(Type, Context, Text)`). A question is closed by GE 0x0276 with the same type and context (`ConfirmationClosed(..., ServerDone, null)`), by the answer (`Answered`, with the answer), by the world exit (`WorldExit`, from `LeaveWorld`: logoff F653, reset), or by a second request of the same type (`Replaced`, a WARNING).

ACE's rule (@95ef426d `ACE.Server/WorldObjects/Managers/ConfirmationManager.cs`): a dictionary keyed by the type (line 16), so one open question per type; a second of an open type is refused before it is sent (33-51); a 30 s timeout (20, 39-42), after which ACE sends 0x0276 only if the context still matches (57-61); an answer removes the question with no 0x0276 (89-135). ACE notes that retail's fellowship dialog stays open after 0x0276 (98-99); the read model closes it anyway, because a withdrawn question cannot be answered usefully. Retail: `CM_Character::DispatchUI_ConfirmationRequest` 0x006A3BB0, `DispatchUI_ConfirmationDone` 0x006A1C40 and `Event_ConfirmationResponse` 0x006A2030 (11.6096); `CM_UI::Event_ConfirmationRequest` is unknown. A local notice 11, `ConfirmationAnswered` (value the type, text `context:1|0`), records the local close for replays.

**The property updates.** `WorldObject.Locked` (`bool?`) is null until GM 0x02D2 key 3 (ACE `PropertyBool.Locked` = 3, `ACE.Entity/Enum/Properties/PropertyBool.cs:16`; the lanes notes' "key 2" is wrong). The CreateObject does not carry it: the weenie description has no lock field, and the Openable bit is not the lock (`WorldObject_Networking.cs:845-856`: Openable is "not locked" for containers, corpses, chests, hooks and storage, is forced on for a locked chest under `fix_chest_missing_inventory_window`, and is never set for a door). ACE's writers are `Door.cs:220`, `Chest.cs:251` and `Lock.cs:275, 310`. `WorldObject.Properties` (`ObjectProperties`) keeps every public update by kind and key (`TryGetBool`, `TryGetInt64`, `TryGetFloat`, `TryGetString`, `TryGetDataId`, `Count`), with the event `ObjectPropertyChanged(Guid, ObjectPropertyKind, Key)`. The kinds are Bool 0x02D2, Int64 0x02D0, Float 0x02D4, String 0x02D6 (the key comes before the guid on the wire) and DataId 0x02D8. A public update for the player's own guid also lands in the player's qualities. Each object keeps one sequence byte per kind and key; an older update is dropped (`MessageDropped`, `Stale`) by retail's `WTimeStamper::UpdateTS` rule (11.6096 0x006B3CB0), per object. An update for an object that is not in the table is ignored, and the first of each kind is logged (`TODO(m5-6-property-unknown-object)`: whether retail queues it is unknown). `TODO(m5-6-public-update-handler)`: retail's own public-update handlers are not named in hand.

The player's private updates go to `SelfState.Qualities`: 0x02D1 Bool (`PlayerQualities.Afk`, key 110, `AfkProperty`; ACE `Player_Networking.cs:438-443`), 0x02D9 InstanceId (CurrentAttacker, `Player_Combat.cs:207`), 0x02D7 DataId (the MotionTable) and 0x02DB Position (`TryGetPosition(type)`, for example 14 LastOutsideDeath). The events are `QualitiesChanged(Bool | InstanceId | DataId | Position, key)`. Stale ones are dropped by the same timestamper; the four timestamp tags are Lifestone's own (`TODO(m5-6-quality-stamp-tags)`; only their distinctness matters).

**The login notices.** GE 0x0295, 0x0029 and 0x0021 are decoded and ignored by name: one INFO line each, "decoded and ignored (M5 6, D31)", with `count` and `entries`. Later ones are counted only (`GameStateReducer.LoginNoticeCount`). They raise no event and change no state.

**The six actions** (`IActionApi`; the trackers in `RetailTrancheTrackers.cs`).

| Action | Sends | Completes | Fails | Lane, limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RespondToConfirmation(type, context, accepted, source)` | GA 0x0275 | Sent; the question closes locally at once | Refused: not in the world, or no open question of that type and context | Immediate |
| `SetCharacterOptions(o1, o2, source)` (D32) | GA 0x01A1, the block built from the read model (`SetCharacterOptionsTracker.Build`: filters, shortcut rows, one or eight favourite bars, desired components; no timestamp format, no gameplay options) | Sent; then the words applied (local notice 12 `CharacterOptionsSet`), the session overrides dropped, `QualitiesChanged(Options, 0)` | Refused: not in the world, or no description yet | Immediate |
| `RecallLifestone(source)` | GA 0x0063 | Confirmed by the teleport F751 (the detail says whether "<name> is recalling to the lifestone." was seen) | ServerError: WeenieError YoureTooBusy 0x001D, YouHaveMovedTooFar 0x0498, YouHaveBeenInPKBattleTooRecently 0x04CC, ExitTrainingAcademyToUseCommand 0x055D (by name), or "Your spirit has not been attuned to a sanctuary location."; TimedOut 38 s | Magic |
| `RecallMarketplace(source)` | GA 0x028D | as above, with the marketplace line | the same WeenieErrors, plus OlthoiCanOnlyRecallToLifestone 0x0593; TimedOut 24 s (ACE's 14 s plus 10 s) | Magic |
| `Emote(text, source)` | GA 0x01DF | Confirmed by the player's own GM 0x01E0 echo | Refused: empty; ServerError: the gag line; TimedOut 5 s | Chat |
| `SetAfk(afk, message, source)` | GA 0x0010, then 0x000F when away with a message, else 0x000F | Confirmed when 0x02D1 makes `Afk` equal the value asked | TimedOut 5 s | Chat |

ACE's rules behind these: `Player_Location.cs:132-195` (the lifestone: the PK timer, recalls disabled, busy, no sanctuary; the mana halved; peace mode; the recall line; the motion; the delay is the motion table's LifestoneRecall length; moved more than 8 m), `:199-262` (the marketplace: Olthoi, PK, disabled, busy; a fixed 14 s at :243); `Player.cs:822-831` (the emote; `EnqueueBroadcast` sends to the sender first, `WorldObject_Networking.cs:1376-1385`); `Player_Networking.cs:438-452` (AFK; an empty message becomes the default line); `GameActionSetCharacterOptions.cs:45-190` (stores both words and the gameplay options; reads and drops shortcuts, bars, components and the timestamp format). `TODO(m5-6-recall-anim-length)`: the lifestone bound (2 x 14 s + 10 s) is not ACE's delay; it is logged on a timeout. `TODO(m5-6-afk-message-only)`: the Action API sets a message only with the mode, so `/afk msg` also turns AFK on (`docs/retail-ui.md` section 21.2).

Retail senders (11.6096): `Event_TeleToLifestone` 0x006A29B0, `Event_TeleToMarketplace` 0x006A2A40, `Event_CharacterOptionsEvent` 0x006A1EE0, `CM_Communication::Event_Emote` 0x006A4F40, `Event_SetAFKMode` 0x006A4D00, `Event_SetAFKMessage` 0x006A5260. The kill switch and the dry run apply to `ActionSource.Automation` as they do to every action (tested for a recall and for AFK). `IAcSession` gained seven sends with throwing defaults (`SendConfirmationResponse`, `SendSetCharacterOptions`, `SendTeleToLifestone`, `SendTeleToMarketPlace`, `SendEmote`, `SendSetAfkMode`, `SendSetAfkMessage`), implemented by `SessionMachine` and `ReplayAcSession`; the stand-in answers them in section 48.

**The real recordings.** The five recordings under `cache/sessions` still replay to their trailer digests, since the digests were not changed by design. A recording whose final state holds a 0x02D9 value would now differ in `qualities.instanceIds`, because 0x02D9 is applied; none of the five does.

## 48. The stand-in's answers and the play-checks (M5 6 k1; DoD 11)

The stand-in's retail answers are in `Session/StandIn/StandInWorld.Retail6.cs`, a re-expression of the ACE rules listed in `docs/third-party/effects.md` (the file header names them). `StandInWorld.Answers` takes RespondToConfirmation, SetCharacterOptions, RecallLifestone, RecallMarketplace, Emote, SetAfk and Tell. The responder's `LocalSession` implements the seven `IAcSession` sends that had throwing defaults, and `SendTell`. The offline refusal (`ActionApi.OfflineRefusal`) no longer reaches them when the host opted in with `AnswerWorld`; a rig that calls only `AnswerLocally`, with no world part, still refuses them.

- **The server's question.** `StandInServer.AskQuestion(text)`, or a visit's `Confirmations` (`StandInConfirmation(After, Type, Text)`, after the unhide), sends GE 0x0274 with type 7 (Yes_No), context 1, 2, and the text. One question of a type at a time: a second returns 0, as ACE refuses a duplicate type. ACE sends no 0x0276 for an answer (`ConfirmationManager.HandleResponse` 89-135), so the stand-in only records it (`QuestionAnswers`). An unanswered question is closed 30 s later by 0x0276 {type, context} and, for Yes_No, by the chat line "You waited too long to answer the question!" (`EnqueueAbort` 52-84). The text "(stand-in question) Do you want to continue?" is a placeholder.
- **The options.** GA 0x01A1 is accepted: the stand-in stores both words (`StoredOptions`, kept on the responder, so a later visit of the run keeps them, as the database does across logins) and answers nothing. `WithOptions` puts them into the next PlayerDescription (`Options1`, `Options2`). The gameplay archive, shortcuts, bars and components in the block are read and dropped.
- **The spell bars** (`AddSpellFavoriteKept`, `RemoveSpellFavoriteKept`): the same ACE rules as before, now also copied to the responder (`KeptBars`), so the next visit's description carries them. Before, a bar lasted for the visit's own next description only. `TODO(m3-standin-bars-live)` stays: the live state does not change on the send.
- **The recalls.** 0x0063 and 0x028D: busy is WeenieError 0x001D. Otherwise (the lifestone only) the mana halves, a combat mode goes to peace, "<name> is recalling to the lifestone." (or "... marketplace.") is an F7E0 of chat type 0x17 (Recall), F74C carries the recall motion (0x10000153 or 0x10000166), and after the delay the F751 teleport: the lifestone to the sanctuary (else the visit's start, as the death's respawn), the marketplace to a stand-in point 40 m east of the visit's start. The lifestone delay is 3 s, a placeholder (ACE waits the motion table's length: `TODO(m5-6-standin-recall-length)`); the marketplace delay is ACE's own 14 s. `TODO(m5-6-standin-marketplace)`: the marketplace point is a placeholder.
- **Emote, tell, away.** 0x01DF is echoed as 0x01E0 EmoteText to the player (the player's own line is Confirmed). 0x005D is refused as ACE refuses a tell to a player who is not online: WeenieError 0x052B, since the stand-in has no other player online. 0x000F is answered with the private bool 0x02D1 Afk; 0x0010 stores the message (an empty one becomes ACE's default) and answers nothing. `ChatAnswers` reads Emotes, Tells, recalls, Afk and AfkMessage.
- **The key and the chest.** `StandInServer.LockChest(chest)` sends 0x02D2 Locked true (`TODO(m5-6-standin-lock-create)`: ACE carries the lock in the appraisal; the stand-in sends the property update). Using a locked chest gives the transient line "The <name> is locked", the OpenFailDueToLock sound (0x94), then UseDone.
