The stand-in key is weenie 9,000,061 ("Stand-in Key (placeholder)", `StandInServer.KeyWcid`); a visit's `Items` must name it (`StandInServer.WithKey()`; `TODO(m5-6-standin-key)`). UseWithTarget(key, chest) with the right key sends 0x02D2 Locked false, F750 LockSuccess (0x93) on the chest, the chat line "The <chest> has been unlocked.", and UseDone. A key with no Structure has unlimited uses, so no uses line is shown (`ConsumeUnlocker` 35-98). A wrong key gets UseDone 0x04B2, an unlocked chest 0x04AF, and a target that is no stand-in lock 0x04B0.
- **A gotcha.** `IActionApi.UseWithTarget` returns a `HealHandle`, and for a key its outcome reads as the heal tracker settles a UseDone (no key tracker exists). A check judges the key by the chest's `Locked`, not by the handle's kind.

**The play-checks** (`scripts/Session/PlayChecks.Retail6.cs`): twelve new rows, one replaced (`a-ghosted-option-does-not-change`, since the options are no longer ghosted), net +11 to 200 rows; `slice-check -Steps play-checks,shell-frames` gave pass=200 fail=0, godot_errors=0. Each is judged by the state or the screen. The first six run in the first session: `an-option-with-no-client-effect-is-settable-and-both-words-reach-the-stand-in`, `the-servers-question-is-a-yes-no-box-and-no-closes-it-and-sends-0275`, `the-selected-focus-sends-a-tell-to-the-selected-creature`, `a-spell-dragged-onto-a-bar-is-added-through-the-action-api-and-shows-on-the-bar`, `a-magic-mode-number-key-casts-the-spell-in-its-cell`, `a-key-unlocks-a-locked-chest-and-it-reads-unlocked`. The next three run in the second session after the reconnect: `an-option-applied-is-set-again-after-the-next-description`, `a-window-moved-is-in-the-same-place-after-a-second-world-entry` (the toolbar, through the Godot layout store and the reconnect) and `a-spell-dragged-onto-a-bar-stays-after-the-next-description`. The last three are commands: `slash-e-reaches-the-stand-in-and-its-line-shows-in-the-chat`, `slash-afk-msg-reaches-the-stand-in-and-the-state-reads-away` (it types `/afk msg gone`, then `/afk off`) and `slash-lifestone-reaches-the-stand-in-and-the-recall-completes-on-the-teleport`.

Notes for the play-checks: the options page's own "Report Abuse" button lies over the toolbar's options button, so the options row closes the page through the host (`ClosePanelAsync`), not by a press. The Godot user data (`cache/godot/.../UI-<world>-<character>-<w>-<h>.txt`) persists across runs, so the window row moves the radar window, not the toolbar (the shortcut bar rides the toolbar, and the hotbar row's drags missed), and puts it back at the start of the second session; the second-entry row runs first in the second session for that reason. Delete the file if a run died between the two. `GameOptions.Apply` no longer sets its "words last seen" itself, so the model taking the words tells the readers, and the vitals window follows an accepted Apply.

The frames, in the `panels/` folder of a frames run (not in the shell's ten): `e1-server-question.png` (the Yes/No box over the shell; its question text is the stand-in's placeholder, noted on the frame) and `c2-spellcasting-edited.png` (the spellcasting window on tab I after a spell was dragged onto its bar; the spell and the book are the probe book's, the wand is the kit's placeholder).

Open TODOs: `m5-6-standin-marketplace`, `m5-6-standin-recall-length`, `m5-6-standin-lock-create`, `m5-6-standin-key`, `m3-standin-bars-live`, `g6-option-effects`, `g6-confirm-social`, `g6-window-locks`, `g6-layout-smartbox`, `g6-chat-options`, `m5-6-afk-message-only`.

## 49. The two live checks: the keep leg and KeyChestLiveTests (M5 6 k2; D32, D33; DoD 11)

**The keep leg: `--login-check=keep`.** One login shows the character options and the window layout kept from the login before. It is a kind of its own and never part of `--login-check=all` (the bare form stays `all`). It is report-only in its first gate, as a new judge. It runs on the tools test account only, as Lifestone Throwaway, which the check creates through the Action API when the list lacks it and never deletes; `AutomatedAccountRule.TryLoginCheck` refuses any other account. The script is `Session/RetailChecks/KeepAcrossLogins.cs`, a `ScriptLeg` that needs no engine and is tested over the Action API harness (`KeepAcrossLoginsTests`); the Godot side is `scripts/Session/KeepLegRunner.cs`, installed by one line in `LoginCheckRunner.Install`, which is also the `IKeepShell` over the retail UI's game screen. No automation click moves a window: the move is `UiElement.MoveTo`, the call the layout keeper's load and a player's drag end both make (M3 D29).

The stages, which begin every failure message: `login`, `select-1` (creates the character when absent), `entering-1`, `arriving-1`, `shell` (the in-game shell is built and its saved layout read: `GamePlayScreen.LayoutLoaded`), `flip-option` (sends `SetCharacterOptions`, GA 0x01A1, flipping `DisplayTimeStamps` (ACE `CharacterOptions2` 0x40, a chat-display preference that ACE stores and nothing in Lifestone reads), from whatever the first PlayerDescription says, so a leftover of a crashed run is read as the starting value), `move-window` (the first of `<RADA>`, `<VITS>`, `<CHAT>` and `<TBAR>` the shell has, moved 40 px inside the root), `logging-off-1` (the shell writes the layout at `LogOffRequested`; the handle completes with the character list), `select-2`, `entering-2`, `arriving-2`, `description-2` (a new PlayerDescription must arrive after login 2 began, because `SetCharacterOptions` applies the words to the read model at once and the model alone would read "kept" without a server), `shell`, `read`, `put-back` (the starting value is sent again), `logging-off-2`, `quitting`.

Verdict lines: `[logincheck] keep options_kept=<yes|no> layout_kept=<yes|no> option=<name> window=<tag>` (judged by what login 2 reads: the option bit equals the flipped value, and the window is at the place and size login 1 left it, not at its starting place), then `[logincheck] keep done ran=<true|false> held=<true|false> problems=<n>`. The process ends 0 when the script ran to its end; `held` is both yes. The layout file is `user://UI-<world>-<character>-<w>-<h>.txt`, under the Godot user data, so `-IsolateUserData` isolates it, and one process reads the same file for both logins. The time limit is 300 s (the runner logs off and quits at the limit). The lead runs it, with the backend up and item 1 merged:

    pwsh -File tools\godot.ps1 -Run -Offscreen -IsolateUserData -TimeoutSeconds 420 -Resolution 800x600 -GameArgs:'--account=test-tools --login-check=keep'

No smoke step runs it yet (a `login-keep` step would sit beside `login-throwaway` if the gate should carry it). The window pick is a preference list (`<RADA>`, `<VITS>`, `<CHAT>`, `<TBAR>`), not a profile value, so no den fact is involved.

**`KeyChestLiveTests`** (Loopback, `test-tools`, Lifestone Throwaway; run by the lead). Two facts, each printing one verdict line and the bytes it was written to capture, then asserting.

1. **`A_key_unlocks_the_locked_Aluvian_Pathwarden_Chest_and_the_server_says_so`.** The chest is weenie 33609 (the Aluvian Pathwarden Chest), a static instance in the pinned world database, in Holtburg's landblock at cell 0xA9B40022, (99.0, 29.5, 94.0): 27 m from the arrival of `place poi "Holtburg"`, and the same chest M4's chests station is refused by. Its weenie has `PropertyBool.Locked` (key 3) true and LockCode `pathwardenchestkey`. The key is weenie 33608 (the Pathwarden Supply Key; KeyCode `pathwardenchestkey`, Structure 1). Both come from the pinned world SQL, read as a file. The test uses the mod's existing verbs only: `item 33608` gives the key (`SpawnItem`), and `place loc 0xA9B40022 99 31 94` hops to 1.5 m from the chest (the range M4 found works; a longer one made ACE's walk time out at 15 s). The first read of the chest shows `WorldObject.Locked == null`, because its CreateObject does not carry the lock; the test prints that, then probes with a plain use (a locked chest answers with a "locked" refusal; an open answer means an earlier run left it unlocked, and the test closes it and waits up to 150 s for ACE's reset). Then `UseWithTarget(key, chest)`: ACE's `Lock.cs` `LockHelper.Unlock` sets the chest unlocked and broadcasts `GameMessagePublicUpdatePropertyBool(chest, Locked, false)` (GM 0x02D2: the opcode, a sequence byte, the guid, the property 3, the value 0; 17 bytes), then `UnlockerHelper.ConsumeUnlocker` uses the key up (`TryConsumeFromInventoryWithNetworking`). Asserted: a 0x02D2 for the chest with key 3 arrives after the use; `WorldObject.Locked == false`; the key is gone from the pack; the unlocked chest opens. Printed: every 0x02D2 seen, as `t= guid= key= value= bytes=<hex>` (the lead's capture of the real message). Clean-up: the chest is opened and closed once, which makes ACE schedule its reset (`Chest.cs` `Open`, 120 s); the reset closes and relocks it, and unless the server property `fix_chest_missing_inventory_window` is set it broadcasts a second 0x02D2 (value 1). The test waits up to 150 s and prints `relock=seen|not_seen`, which is reported, not asserted. The key is consumed and the chest resets on the server either way. Verdict: `keychest first_read_locked= probe= keys_granted= use= m02d2_for_chest= m02d2_seen= unlocked= key_left= relock= problems=`. It takes about three minutes; the reset wait dominates.
2. **`A_server_question_arrives_and_No_closes_it`.** The cheapest solo path to a GE 0x0274 is the Coordination To Endurance Gem (weenie 23045, an `AttributeTransferDevice`), given by `item 23045` and used from the pack. ACE `AttributeTransferDevice.ActOnUse` checks the requirements (the new character's Coordination 55 is above 10, its Endurance 55 is below 100, and nothing wielded has an attribute requirement) and sends `Confirmation_AlterAttribute`: 0x0274 type 3 with "This action will transfer 10 points from your Coordination to your Endurance." The test prints the F7B0 envelope's bytes, answers No (`RespondToConfirmation`, GA 0x0275), and asserts `PendingConfirmation == null`, a `ConfirmationClosed` with reason Answered and Accepted false, and the gem still in the pack (a No consumes nothing: `Confirmation_AlterAttribute.ProcessConfirmation`). ACE sends nothing for an answer: its 0x0276 is only the 30 s timeout (`ConfirmationManager.EnqueueAbort`), so the close asserted is the client's own (section 47) and no 0x0276 is required; the test prints any 0x0276 that does come. The gem stays in the pack for the next run (reused, not granted again). If the grant comes back `not-creatable`, the other paths are the craft-success dialog (`RecipeManager.cs:339`, type 5, under `UseCraftSuccessDialog`) and an NPC's InqYesNo emote (`EmoteManager.cs:832`, type 7). Verdict: `question text_len= requested= type= answer= closed= requests= closes= problems=`.

Run: `dotnet test tests\Lifestone.Loopback.Tests\Lifestone.Loopback.Tests.csproj --disable-build-servers --filter "FullyQualifiedName~KeyChestLiveTests"` (with the DOTNET variables of CLAUDE.md "Build and test"), or inside the gate: `pwsh -File tools\live-gate.ps1 -Loopback actions` (`KeyChestLiveTests` is in the `actions` area of `tools\lib\GateAreas.psd1`, as `LiveGateRunnerTests` R12 requires).

Open: the gem grant could come back `not-creatable` live (the paths above); the keep leg runs in no smoke step yet.

## 50. The layout file and the options saved (M5 6 u1; D32, D33; DoD 11)

**The options (D32).** All **49** `PlayerOption` toggles are settable and nothing is ghosted. A click changes the box; Apply sends ONE GA 0x01A1 with both words (`GameOptions.Apply`, through `IActionApi.SetCharacterOptions`); the server stores the words and returns them in the next PlayerDescription, so a set option is set in the next login. Reset restores the saved values; Defaults sets every option's default (word 1 0x50C4A54A, word 2 0x00948700; retail `RestoreDefaultValue` 0x00486CF0) and Apply sends them. Changing an option with no client effect logs `TODO(g6-option-effects)` once per option; the panel claims no effect for it. The two options with a client effect (`SideBySideVitals`, `VividTargetingIndicator`; `PlayerOptionDef.Honoured` now means "has a client effect") act at once. The combat window's three boxes are settable the same way (`TODO(m3-combat-options)` is closed). `IActionApi.SetSessionOption` stays for tests only; nothing in `src/Lifestone.Client/RetailUi` or `src/Lifestone.Game` calls it, so M2's D42 (the options last for the session) is retired.

ACE reads some options itself (a grep of `GetCharacterOption` at @95ef426d): allegiance and fellowship request ignoring, fellowship auto-accept and share, loot permits, deception, give, trade, the "allow others to see" six, allegiance logons, the six chat listens, auto-repeat, auto-target, charge attack, lead missile targets, fast missiles, and the cloak and helm display. The rest the server only stores.

**The layout file (D33).** The floating windows' places and sizes, and the chat windows' text-type masks, are kept per world, character and window size in `UI-<world>-<character>-<width>-<height>.txt`. The Godot store (`GodotScreenLayoutStore`, `scripts/RetailUi/`) writes it under `user://`, so `-IsolateUserData` isolates it. The pieces are `IScreenLayoutStore`, `MemoryScreenLayoutStore` (for a host that gives no store), `ScreenLayoutFile` (the name, the retail line format and the parser) and `ScreenLayoutKeeper` (save, load, clamp), all in `RetailUi/Core`. The shell loads the file once the character is known, and saves it at log-off and at Dispose; nothing is written before the read. A saved window off the screen is moved back inside on load; retail's load does not clamp (11.4186 0x004EA8F0). Lifestone's own `LS-CHATMASK` lines hold the chat masks, which start at retail's defaults. `TODO(g4-window-layout-save)` is closed. The check that reads a file back across two logins is the keep leg (section 49).

**The server's Yes/No box (u1).** `ServerConfirmation` shows GE 0x0274 as a Yes/No confirmation and answers it once (`RespondToConfirmation`, GA 0x0275); the details are in `docs/retail-ui.md` section 21.8. `--retail-confirm-slot` restores retail's single slot (a second question dropped while one shows), a glitch the default does not have; it is part of `--retail-ui-glitches`.

Open TODOs: `g6-option-effects`, `g6-confirm-social`, `g6-window-locks`, `g6-layout-smartbox`, `g6-chat-options`, `g6-layout-store` (the host gave no store).

## 51. The UI slices of item 6: the sizes, the spell bars, the keys and the slash commands (M5 6 s1, u2, v1; DoD 11, 12; D34)

The UI detail is in `docs/retail-ui.md`: the play camera's angle (section 21.1: `cam-fov-resize` is closed; the portal tunnel's own lens still uses the old angle, `cam-fov-resize-tunnel`), the slash commands and the talk focus (21.2), the magic, melee and missile keys (22.1 and 23.8: the mode maps, the spell keys and the editable bars; `m4-combat-map-keys` and `m3-spellcast-hotkeys` are closed) and the option boxes (21.8).

The **sizes** (v1, D34): `-AllSizes` on `slice-check.ps1` and `smoke.ps1` runs the shell frames and the play-checks at 800x600, 1280x720 and 1920x1080, each size in its own `<W>x<H>` folder, with the play-checks' verdict per size in `play-checks/<W>x<H>/play-checks.txt`; the retail-ui frames also run at 1920x1080. `-HiDpi` adds the 2560x1440 scale-2 shell run, opt-in and not in the gate. `slice-check -Smoke` implies `-AllSizes`; the item-end offline check is `slice-check -Changed -AllSizes` or `-Smoke`. The frame judge (`login-check`, `login`) stays at 800x600 (M2 D57). Nothing of this runs by default.

Open TODOs in these slices: `g4-chat-channels` (M6), `g4-chat-commands-rest`, `g4-chat-at-commands`, `g4-chat-emote-prefix`, `g4-chat-help`, `g4-chat-focus-row`, `g4-chat-squelch`, `m5-6-afk-message-only`; `m5-spellbar-drag-begin`, `m5-spellbar-refusal-line`, `m5-spell-drag-icon`, `m5-combat-power-key-slider`, `m5-combat-keys-unattached`; `cam-fov-resize-tunnel`, `uicheck-hidpi-split` (the `--retail-ui-checks --all-sizes` run still places the shell at 2560x1440, so `-AllSizes` runs that placement check too).

## 52. The hunting-ground profile and the whole den (M5 item 2; D22, D25, D26, D39-D46; DoD 4, 5)

Item 2 makes a hunting ground data. The den and the two hunt grounds (the belt and the forest) are JSON profiles, and every class that fights, walks, tours or journeys takes a `HuntingGround`; the next dungeon is one more file and its checks (D46). Sections 31, 41 and 45 above are corrected where they now read false, each with a pointer here. The decisions are in `docs/milestones/m5-plan.md` section 7.

**The profile and its catalog (D22, D43; `Session/Grounds/HuntingGround.cs`, `GroundCatalog.cs`, `profiles/`).** A ground is one JSON file under `src/Lifestone.Client/Session/Grounds/profiles/`. The csproj embeds every file there (logical name `Lifestone.Client.Session.Grounds.profiles.<file>`), and `GroundCatalog` reads them once with `System.Text.Json`: `Get(key)` and `All`, where the key is the file name. An unknown key throws `KeyNotFoundException` naming it. A missing, unknown, duplicated or out-of-range field throws `GroundProfileException` naming the file and the field's JSON path. Cell ids, guids, landblocks and item-type masks are hex strings. A storey's cells and the no-go set may hold inclusive ranges (`0x01050100-0x01050121`) within one landblock. `null` is a value only for `portal` and `outdoor`. Every profile has a `sources` object naming the source of each of its 17 sections: landblocks, arrival, walk, storeys, no_go, targets, level_band, portal, home, vendors, spell_plan, components, supplies (M5 item 3, section 53), hold_spots, respawn, tour and outdoor.

The shape is any dungeon's (D46): storeys, one to many, over one or more landblocks; target classes, `target` or `avoid`; several generators, each with its own `delay_s` and `tick_s` (`respawn.generators`); slots that name their generator; the room graph's doorway lean; and the walk's stop rules. Nothing in the records is the den's. At load, a cell of the arrival, a storey, the no-go set, the tour, a hold spot, a generator, a slot or an outdoor cell must lie in the profile's landblocks; a cell is in at most one storey; the spell plan's level ranges do not overlap; delays and ticks are not negative; a dungeon's tour cells are walkable and not no-go; a dungeon has a storey and no outdoor block, and an outdoor ground has an outdoor block; a slot's generator is listed. Since M5 item 3 (section 53), every row of the spell plan has its components at its level, each a stock line of `supplies` (any spell level, D48), else the profile fails at `$.spell_plan[i]` with `TODO(ground-plan-gap)`.

The hunt grounds are profiles too, read through the same catalog: `low-b-belt` and `low-north-forest-mix` (kind `outdoor`; D42). `HuntGround.LowB`, `LowNorthForestMix`, their tables and their creature lists keep their names and types and are views over the profiles (`HuntGround.BeltKey`, `ForestKey`). An outdoor creature has a required `spawn_mass` (0 to 1): the chance that one roll of a spot's generator spawns it, with the cumulative-probability rows multiplied through nested generators and camps, and a creature that is itself a generator counted with its young. `expected_yield_per_spot` is the sum of the masses of the creatures in the profile's own level band (the belt 0.9802 for levels 8 to 20, the forest 0.34236 for 30 to 40; a test pins both to the creatures). The belt's spots are the cells' centres, as before. The forest's spots are its encounter points, the generator's corners (cell x 24, cell y 24; `Landblock.SpawnEncounters`), 17 m from the centres. Its 33 spots run as one loop by landblock, from the spot nearest Holtburg (0x9FB50039) north and back down: 3,191 m from the first cell's centre through the last spot. The hunt's bound is the profile's `cell_cap` (belt 8, forest 33; `HuntScenario.CellLimit`). Before its first walk the hunt logs the expected yield, report-only: the spots to visit, the usable creatures a spot for the band, the chance that no spot holds one. For the archer over the forest that is 0.167 a spot, 33 spots, 5.51 over them, a chance of 0.0024 that none holds one (0.23 for the old eight spots). `HuntingGroundDatTests` (new; M4's `HuntGroundDatTests` keeps the old rows' tests) reads the three profiles from the dats: the den's arrival is standable, every belt and forest cell has the terrain its profile says, every forest spot is dry, and the den's components equal the foci formula of Flame Bolt VI and of Ilservian's Flame in the dats' SpellTable.

**Adding a dungeon (D46; `GroundDraft.cs`, `GroundDraftRunTests`).** The draft builder writes a profile's draft from the dats and the pinned world SQL (v0.9.296), read in streamed passes and never loaded into a database. It drafts the walkable cells and their storeys (every cell the arrival reaches over the doorways at the lean); the generators, with their own delays and ticks; the slots, each a cell and a wcid; the creatures, with names and levels; the exits, by portal instance and by a creature whose use or death emote casts a teleport spell; the entry portals; and the town half: the portal's instance, the nearest outdoor lifestone and the vendors near it, with their wares and prices. Every hand-set value is the string `TODO(hand-set)`, which the catalog refuses at load, naming the field, so a draft never loads by accident. The command is a lead-run tool test (`[Trait("Tier", "Tool")]`, so the default filters leave it out). It writes `cache/grounds/<key>.json` and `<key>.txt` of the running checkout (gitignored, never under `src/`) in about 5 seconds:

```powershell
$env:LIFESTONE_GROUND_DRAFT = '<landblock>,<arrival cell or empty>,<doorway lean>,<key>[,<home landblock>]'
dotnet test tests/Lifestone.Client.Tests --disable-build-servers --filter "FullyQualifiedName~GroundDraftRunTests"
```

1. Find the landblock. The dungeon's entry portal is a weenie whose Destination (`weenie_properties_position`, PositionType 2) lies in it. Pick the doorway lean: 0.3 for the den and Tusker Temple (the den's plaza doorways lean 0.196 in the dats), 0 for a dungeon of upright doorways. Run a few leans and read the walkable count.
2. Run the draft command with the arrival cell, or with the field empty to take the entry portal's destination (several entry portals are named, and the first is taken). Read `cache/grounds/<key>.txt`: the storeys, the floorless and pocket counts, the generators with their delays, ticks and slots, the creatures and their levels, the exits by portal and by emote, the entry portals, the town (the portal's instance, the lifestones, the vendors with their wares and prices) and the notes. Every `TODO(ground-draft-*)` line is a decision to make by hand.
3. Copy `cache/grounds/<key>.json` to `src/Lifestone.Client/Session/Grounds/profiles/<key>.json`. The key is the file name, and the csproj embeds the file.
4. Replace every `TODO(hand-set)`: the name and the town's name; the walk's stop rules (the den's 1.0 m, 0.25 s and 90 s are the defaults); the roles (`target` for what the loop kills, `avoid` for the rest); the avoided classes and the no-go cells the design asks for beyond the exits (a wing, outliers, the cells beside avoided creatures), then the cells those cut off (`GroundDraft.CutOff`; the per-ground check names any left); the tour; the vendors the ground uses, with exactly the wares its supply lines need (the loader refuses a vendor's ware that is no stock or kit line, a ware two vendors sell, and a line whose name differs from the vendor's row; section 53); the portal's front point (`front_cell`, `front_point`: three metres in front of the portal, from its heading); the spell plan; the components; the hold spots (`[]` for none). The arrival, the portal's guid, cell, tie and recall spells, the home's lifestone and the level band come from the draft; check the tie and the home's standing point live (`TODO(m6-ground-live-facts)`).
5. Check the home town. The profile's `home.town` must be the display name of exactly one ACE point of interest (`AcePointsOfInterest`, the list the way out resolves to). `GroundExit` leaves by that name and fails at construction for a ground whose town is not one; `GroundRulesTests.Every_dungeons_home_town_is_one_ace_point_of_interest_the_way_out_resolves` checks every dungeon in the catalog.
6. Rewrite each section's `sources` entry to name what it is now: the draft's for the dats' and the world SQL's parts, the decision or the measure for the hand-set ones.
7. Run `DenRoomGraphTests`, `GroundDraftTownTests` and `GroundRulesTests` (the home town, step 5). They take the new profile from `GroundCatalog.All`: every target slot is reached from the arrival without a no-go cell, every no-go cell is refused, every walkable cell is reached, every storey is walkable, and the portal, home and vendors equal the draft's except the differences the test names. A dungeon of several landblocks fails there by name (`TODO(ground-multi-landblock)`).

The estimate for a dungeon of this kind is about 40 minutes of the person's choices (steps 1, 2 and 4 are most of it). The first dungeon added this way was Tusker Temple (landblock 0x5C4A, the portal "Tusker Temple" at Fort Tethana), added as data only and not played in M5. Its first pass took about two hours: 15 minutes reading, 12 for the arrival, 8 for the leans, about 70 for the hand-set fields (about 40 of them the town's facts, which the town half now drafts), and 20 for the per-ground checks. It is one landblock like the den but has four storeys (z 0, -6, -12, -18), one generator (wcid 7924, 300 s delay, 60 s tick) with 145 slots in the arrival cell, and no avoided class in its walkable cells; its casters are targets. Its only exit is a creature's use emote, which casts the spell "Entrance to Tusker Island" (2936) and is no-go with the two cells it cuts off. At lean 0.3 it has 489 walkable cells, and 486 with the no-go set; its arrival is 0x5C4A02F9 at (160, -130, 0.005), and its 142 hunted slots stand in 100 distinct cells. Its home is Fort Tethana's lifestone (in 0x2780); its portal has the guid 0x72A80000 in cell 0x2A800013. Its checks pass with no code edited under `src/` beyond the profile file. The findings the draft now covers: the arrival is the entry portal's destination and nothing read it before; an outdoor object's row carries 0xLLLL0000 as its cell, so the cell is `x/24 * 8 + y/24 + 1` by whole divisions; a vendor's sell multiplier is the vendor's own (1.8 here, so a Prismatic Taper costs 40, not 30), with the price `max(1, ceil(m x Value - 0.1))`; the level band is the targets' levels, not every slot's; and an exit can be a creature's emote. Still open for a role format: a "kill first" role for casters (`m6-ground-fields`), and the portal's minimum level (its MinLevel, 40 here), which no field takes.

**The ground through the seams (D46; `GroundRooms`, `GroundRules`, `GroundWalk`, `GroundExit`).** `--fight-ground <key>` (`OptionCatalog.Fight.cs`; default `FightOptions.DefaultGround`, `black-spawn-den-aluvian`, the one place code names a ground) selects the ground of the fight, the death and the journeys' den (`JourneyContext.Dungeon`). An unknown key, or a key that is not a dungeon, is refused at once, naming the known keys; the headless host, the game's fight leg, the death leg and the journeys refuse the run or the leg on it. `GroundRooms` replaces `DenRooms`: `CoordOf`, `FloorOf` (the cells the ground's `IsWalkable` accepts), `GraphOf` (a `RoomGraph` at the ground's lean) and `Load`. `GroundRules` (extensions on `HuntingGround`) answers the fight's questions: `TargetWcids()`, `IsTarget(wcid, cell)` (a target role on a walkable cell), `ArrivalDestination()` (a `place loc` request from the profile's arrival, not the Places row) and `ArrivalLandblock()`. The fight's settle check names a cell that is not walkable ("settle: the player settled in cell 0x... which is not a walkable cell of <name>"). `GroundExit` leaves by the ground's home town, as an ACE point of interest by display name; a ground whose town is not a point of interest fails at construction. The walks stop on entering a no-go cell, named by the ground ("the walk entered the no-go cell 0x... of <name>"). M4 D12's "left the floor" rule is retired (D4): a cell no storey holds and no no-go set lists does not stop a walk. `BlackSpawnDen`'s constants and `DenWalk.Tour` are gone; the den's tour is the profile's. `InteriorLocations.BlackSpawnDenAluvian` (the Places row) stays as a teleport destination. A few Holtburg facts (the lifestone and its standing point for bind and recall) stay constants in `Journey/TravelStations.cs`: they are a town's, not a ground's.

**The walks on the runner (D4, D12; `GroundWalk`, `GameFightWalk`, `RunnerLegs`, `LegRecovery`).** The fight's walks (the headless `GroundWalk`, the game's `GameFightWalk`, the journey host's den walk) drive a `NavRunner` over the ground's room graph with the creature layer, the hostile creatures' radii read from their Setups, so the den's legs get the indoor steering (`docs/wayfinding.md`). `RouteDriver` stays for `RoutePlay` and the other scripted walks only. A leg is a point goal at the middle of the next waypoint's room, within the profile's walk stop distance, standing the walk's dwell after the runner arrives, and limited to the walk's leg time. A leg that fails (the runner Blocked after its stuck ladder, Failed by a move of the player that no run caused, or over the leg time) is abandoned, and the walk plans the next waypoint from where the player stands: log lines `m5-fight-walk-recover: leg N to 0x... abandoned (...)` and `recovered: leg N+1 ...` (`m5-fight-walk-recover`, closed). Three failed legs in a row (`LegRecovery.MaxFailedLegsInARow`, Lifestone's own; the profile's walk section has no field for it) end the walk, and the fight reports `fight: the walk failed: ...` (the death, `the walk failed: ...`). An arrival resets the count. A person's movement key (`NavRunState.Interrupted`) is not recovered: the player's keys win and the walk fails. The death's walk goes on through a recovered leg as well. Entering a no-go cell is still a stop at once. The game's fight walk (`GameFightWalk.Tick`) checks the no-go set on every tick that has a body, as `GroundWalk` does, whether or not a leg is active, so a slide or an overshoot between legs that enters one fails the walk at that frame, naming the cell (2-R9). The headless walk answers the clear line from its own physics world (section 31). The seam is `IGroundTour` (`Session/Grounds/IGroundTour.cs`): `Waypoints`, `Next(from, now)`, `LegFailed`, and the defaults `LegArrived`, `Killed` and `Created` (the last two take the creature's guid as a trailing argument, 0 for the death-cell rule alone). Its first implementation, `ProfileTour`, takes the profile's tour in its order, round and round, never offering the player's own cell; `GroundTour` (below) replaces the implementation, not the seam.

**The tour and the respawn schedule (D25, D40; `RespawnSchedule.cs`, `GroundTour.cs`).** The schedule covers any ground's slots, each with its own generator's delay and tick. Every slot stands at the start; the schedule knows only the deaths it is told of (`TODO(ground-slot-dead-at-start)`, logged when the schedule is made: a creature killed before the run reads as standing until it is seen). A kill is paired to its slot by the creature's spawn, not by where it died (2-R1). When a creature of a slot's wcid is created in that slot's cell (`Created`, fed by the fight's `ObjectCreated`), the schedule remembers its guid for that slot: the dead slot the create brings back, else the first standing slot of that wcid and cell that no creature is remembered for. A kill of that guid is that slot's wherever the creature died, and its due time starts at the kill (logged `paired by its spawn`, or `paired by its spawn in 0x...; it died away from the slot's cell`). A guid never seen created in a slot's cell falls back to the cell it died in: the first standing slot of its wcid there that no creature is remembered for (logged `paired by the cell it died in: its guid was not seen created in a slot's cell`), and a slot another creature holds is never taken by the fallback. A kill that neither rule takes is unpaired, named once (`pairs with no standing slot`), and changes nothing. The counters are `Paired` (`PairedBySpawn` plus `PairedByCell`), `Unpaired` and `Expired`; `SlotOfGuid` reads the memory, and a guid created again changes nothing. A slot that `Expire` takes as standing has no creature remembered until one is seen created in its cell. A dead slot is due at its death plus its generator's delay, rounded up to that generator's next tick. The tick's phase is learned per generator from the first create that brings one of its dead slots back inside its window; until a phase is seen, the due time is death plus delay plus one tick, named "phase unknown". A slot more than `RespawnSchedule.UnseenGraceSeconds` (60 s) past its due time with no create seen is taken as standing, logged once (`TODO(ground-respawn-unseen)`), so a missed create cannot pin the tour to one storey.

The tour (D40) walks the profile's storeys in their listed order, down to the last storey with a hunted slot and back up, round and round; a storey with no hunted slot (the den's z -30) is passed over. Its next waypoint is the nearest cell, by the room graph's walk, with a standing hunted slot on the storey the player stands on; when none stands there, the nearest such cell of the next storey in the order; with none standing anywhere, the cell of the earliest due slot, where the tour waits. The timed return (D25): off the arrival's storey, the tour turns back when that storey's earliest due slot comes due within the walk back plus `GroundTour.ReturnMarginSeconds` (60 s, Lifestone's own: one tick, about four kills on the way). On the arrival's storey the tour holds rather than leaves (2-R3) when no hunted slot stands there and a dead slot is due within the walk to it plus `ReturnMarginSeconds`; a slot due in the cell the player stands in is a hold too (`Next` offers that cell, logged once as `tour: holding in 0x... on '...'`). `returns=` counts only the timed returns begun from another storey than the arrival's (`GroundTour.Returns`); a wait or a hold on the arrival's storey is not one. The walk back is the room graph's walk at the run speed the tour measures from its own arrived legs (legs of 20 m or more; `DefaultRunMetresPerSecond`, 10.4 m/s, until one arrives). `--fight-storeys-min N` (1 to 16, default 1) makes the tour leave the storey it stands on after a kill, until N distinct storeys are visited (2-R2, `GroundTour.WantsToLeave`). It leaves only when the next storey in the order has a standing hunted slot it could offer, and it is not heading back for the arrival; with nowhere to go, as on a one-storey ground, it never leaves. The fight's pick then asks in tiers: the next storey's targets, then any other storey's (both on walkable cells, with a clear line). With none in range it walks off the storey (`the tour leaves this storey (--fight-storeys-min) and no tusker off it is in range`) rather than taking the next target on the floor. With `--fight-storeys-min` 2 or more the review frames wait for a storey other than the arrival's (section 31). A waypoint whose leg failed rests for 120 s (`FailedCellRestSeconds`). A lap of all 78 hunted slots of the den is about 23.5 minutes against a slot's 15.5, so every slot is back before a lap ends and the schedule decides only when to turn back (D40). The fight feeds the tour: each kill notice that names the target goes to the tour with the target's wcid, the cell it was last seen in and its guid, and each CreateObject of a non-missile object in the ground's landblocks during the fight goes to `Created`. The pick of targets is unchanged.

**The seed (fix F4, from live gate round 1: `kills=3 ... kills_unpaired=2`).** A creature that already stands in the ground when the fight starts was created before the fight existed, so the schedule never heard its create and its kill fell back to the death-cell rule. `RespawnSchedule.Seed(WorldObjectTable, now)` now offers every living creature of the object table whose wcid is some slot's as a create in the cell it stands in, in guid order: one in a slot's cell is remembered for that slot (the first standing slot of that wcid and cell with no creature of its own), so its kill pairs by its spawn wherever it dies; one in no slot's cell, or with no free slot there, is left to the named fallback; a dead one is skipped. The fight calls `IGroundTour.Seed` in `FightScenario.BeginFight`, just before the stage becomes "fight" (the default does nothing). It logs one line (`respawn: seeded from the creatures already known: ...`), allocates once there and nothing per frame (`RespawnSchedule.Seeded` counts the creatures remembered). Tests: `RespawnScheduleTests.A_seed_remembers_each_creature_standing_in_a_slots_cell_...`, `FightScenarioTests.A_fight_that_starts_with_creatures_already_in_their_slot_cells_pairs_every_kill_of_them_...` and two more.

**Moving with purpose (fix F5, the owner's D47, from live round 2: the game's fight paced about 2 m between two neighbouring rooms, 0x010502A0 and 0x010502A1, for four minutes, and the 30-minute read spent 1,123 of 1,800 s walking with no target).** A slot whose creature died before the schedule began, or out of sight, used to stand for good, so an empty room taught the tour nothing and two such rooms side by side were walked between again and again. Now every waypoint has a purpose, logged at the leg's start (`walking: purpose=<p> (<note>); ...`): `seen` (a living target the object table holds, ranked first), `presumed` (standing by the schedule, not yet seen), `due` (due within the walk plus the margin) or `return` (the timed return). With none, the tour holds where the earliest due slot is in view (one walk there first, purpose `due`), logged with the slot and its due time (`tour: holding in 0x...`), and the walk makes no leg (`RunnerLegs.HoldRecheckSeconds`, 1 s). `RespawnSchedule.Look`: a hunted slot in view (the player's cell plus its visible-cell list from the dat, the cells whose objects ACE sends; `GroundTour.For` reads them from the room graph's cells) for `SeenEmptySettleSeconds` (2 s, Lifestone's own) with no living creature of its own is seen empty, due at the latest at the moment seen plus its delay plus one tick; a create brings it back. `TODO(ground-slot-dead-at-start)` is narrowed to "standing until in view". A cell a leg arrived at is not offered again until one of its slots is due or a target's create is seen in it; an offer cycle of period two or three with no kill and no create between is logged once by name and held (`walk_loops=`). The run speed sample is the runner's own running frames (a pause or a fight inside a leg left out), and the leg line reports that leg alone (`route_m run_s walk_s speed_mps plans replans rungs blocked turns key_changes_per_min creature_replans creatures_max_walk creature_min_m_walk`). The fight's verdict, the station and `bsd-fight done` lines and DenTour's `[measure]` line add `walks_by_purpose=seen:n,presumed:n,due:n,return:n holds= hold_s= slots_seen_empty= walk_loops=`, report-only. Tests: `GroundTourPurposeTests` (the two neighbouring phantom slots, which failed on the code before the fix with a ping-pong; a seen target outranks a presumed slot; the walk then the hold; the loop guard; the per-leg report and the speed sample; theories over both dungeon profiles) and two new `RespawnScheduleTests` facts. **F6 (D47, round 2's aimless first leg):** the headless journey host's `GroundFightWalk` now follows the same rules through `LegPacing` (shared with `RunnerLegs`): a hold makes no leg and arrives after `HoldRecheckSeconds`, and the tour is told the runner's walking seconds (`J4JourneyHostTests.The_hosts_den_fight_walk_makes_no_leg_while_the_tour_holds_and_arrives_after_the_recheck`, `The_hosts_den_fight_walk_tells_the_tour_the_runners_walking_seconds`); and the runner runs through a dungeon's doorways instead of walking near each (docs/wayfinding.md, "Through a doorway at a run"; `IndoorRunThroughDatTests`).

**The level and the fight's fields (D26, D41, D44; `FightScenario.PlanCap`).** The bolt's cap is the ground's spell plan (`HuntingGround.PlanFor`). For the den and Tusker Temple it is Flame Bolt VI for character levels 1 to 99 and VII from 100 (D41, `m5-level80-vi`, closed). `FightScenario.PlanCap(ground, characterLevel, maxLevel)` takes the plan's row for the level, and `--fight-max-level` caps it lower still; each change is logged ("the ground's plan caps Flame Bolt at VI for level 80"). A plan with no row for the level (a PlayerDescription without its level reads 0) leaves `--fight-max-level` alone and is named (`TODO(ground-plan-gap)`); a row of another family than the bolt line is named too (`TODO(ground-plan-family)`). BSD Mage at 127 keeps VII; a level-80 mage casts VI.

The fight's verdict line ends with `storeys_visited= basement_entries= returns= slots_standing= legs_recovered= kills_unpaired= slots_expired=` (D44, and 2-R4, report-only): `storeys_visited` counts the distinct z bands the body stood in during the fight stage; `basement_entries` counts the entries into a no-go cell (0 unless the walk's rule breaks, and each entry is logged); `returns` counts the tour's timed returns; `slots_standing` the hunted slots standing in the tour's schedule at the end. The last two print `n/a` when the walk has no tour (the arena run, a scripted walk). The three of 2-R4 are report-only too: `legs_recovered` is the failed legs the walk abandoned and went on from (`IFightWalk.LegsRecovered`, an `int?`: `GroundWalk`, `GameFightWalk` and the headless journey's den walk count them, and a walk without a count prints `n/a`); `kills_unpaired` is the target kills the schedule paired to no slot (`RespawnSchedule.Unpaired`); `slots_expired` is the dead slots `Expire` took as standing with no create seen (`RespawnSchedule.Expired`, `TODO(ground-respawn-unseen)`). `kills_unpaired` and `slots_expired` print `n/a` with no tour, as `returns` and `slots_standing` do. The journey's den-fight station prints them, and so does the `bsd-fight done` line (after the mana fields).

**The journeys on the ground (k2; `JourneyGrounds`, `TravelStations`).** The journey's dungeon is `JourneyContext.Dungeon`, from `--fight-ground` (the hosts pass `FightOptions.GroundProfile`). `JourneyGrounds.For(StationWhere.Den, height, dungeon)` is that ground's arrival, and `At(...)` is "inside its landblocks"; both refuse the den without a ground. The den-travel's portal facts are the profile's `portal` section (`wcid`, `origin`, `front_cell`, `front_point`). The den's are measured live (wcid 7242, the front cell 0x1B90002C, the stand 3 m in front of the portal); Tusker Temple's are read from the pinned world SQL and are not measured live (`TODO(m6-ground-live-facts)`). A ground with no portal record fails the den-travel's portal path, named (`TODO(m6-ground-fields)`); the recall path needs no record. The mage's den-fight station fights two storeys: `JourneyPlans.MageDenFightArguments` sets `--fight-storeys-min 2` and 3 kills, and the station's verdict carries `storeys_visited>=2 basement_entries=0` (see the checks below). The journey's fight option `TODO(journey-ground-option)` is closed: `--fight-ground` reaches the journey's fight, its death and its room graphs.

**The checks (k1).** The live gate judges `mana_wait_max_s<=10` on the mage journey's den-fight station line (`Get-JourneyVerdict`, which `tools/lib/GateJudge.psm1` takes out for the headless `-Serial` journeys): a value over 10, a field that is absent or `n/a`, or no den-fight station line fails the journey (the texts are `mana_wait_max_s <x> over 10 (mage den-fight)` and `mana_wait_max_s n/a on the den-fight station, not 10 or less (mage)`). Only the mage journey is judged; contention does not excuse it, since a mana wait is not a frame time. The `bsd-fight` smoke line carries the same field, report-only. From item 3's gate (c2b, D44) the live gate also judges `storeys_visited>=2 basement_entries=0` on the mage journey's den-fight station line, beside `mana_wait_max_s<=10` (`Get-JourneyVerdict`; the worst den-fight line counts; contention does not excuse it). The texts are `storeys_visited <x> under 2 (mage den-fight)`, `basement_entries <x> over 0 (mage den-fight)`, `storeys_visited n/a on the den-fight station, not 2 or more (mage)` and `basement_entries n/a on the den-fight station, not 0 (mage)`; a field that is absent or `n/a`, or no den-fight line, fails the mage journey, and the melee and archer journeys are not judged. `returns`, `slots_standing`, `legs_recovered`, `kills_unpaired` and `slots_expired` stay report-only (2-R4). The report-only bracket (`Get-JourneyReportOnly` in `tools/lib/GateJudge.psm1`, `tools/smoke.ps1`'s TourFields, `tools/live-gate.ps1`'s help) now reads `returns slots_standing legs_recovered kills_unpaired slots_expired loot looted kept loot_value` for the den-fight station, with two brackets of their own after it (section 53): the logistics station's `trigger granted places loot sold bought spent notes_sold spent_xp raises`, and the death station's `deaths corpse_recovered route items_back vitae_grants rebuffed relines trip_abort`. The done line's `places` joins `granted` in the first bracket for every journey. `LiveGateRunnerTests` and `SliceCheckAndSmokeTests` pin them. `DenMeasureLiveTests.DenTour` (Loopback, `Tier=Measure`, `Lane=solo`, account `test`; about 35 minutes) is the 30-minute read of the whole den: the fight on the den's profile with the default tour for 30 minutes, which prints `[measure] DenTour` with `kills_per_hour` (against item 0's 68), the tour fields, `legs_recovered`, `kills_unpaired`, `slots_expired` beside `failed_legs`, the mana wait and the kills by five minutes, and writes `cache/m5/0/measures/DenTour.txt`. It asserts only that it measured; it was compiled and not run in the slice. The draft command is `GroundDraftRunTests`, a `Tier=Tool` test that skips without `LIFESTONE_GROUND_DRAFT`; the default filters leave `Tier=Tool` out (README, "Tool trait"). The wayfind scenario's indoor ends are in `docs/wayfinding.md`.

**The TODO names.** Closed by item 2: `wayfind-indoor` (k1), `nav-creatures-indoor` and `nav-creature-radius` (g4, g5), `nav-creature-radius-host` (g5; k1 took the name out of the fallback warning), `m5-fight-walk-recover` (g5), `m5-den-roam-respawn` and `m5-level80-vi` (g6), `m5-den-storeys` (g2, the ramp walk), and `journey-ground-option` (k2). Opened and named in code: `ground-draft-arrival`, `ground-draft-delay`, `ground-draft-tick`, `ground-draft-link-profile`, `ground-draft-link-outside`, `ground-draft-nested`, `ground-draft-scatter`, `ground-draft-portal`, `ground-draft-home`, `ground-draft-vendors`, `ground-draft-vendor-price` (GroundDraft.cs), `ground-multi-landblock`, `ground-slot-dead-at-start`, `ground-respawn-unseen`, `ground-plan-gap`, `ground-plan-family`, `m6-ground-live-facts`, and `wayfind-indoor-default` (k1). `m6-ground-fields` gained the entry gate (the portal's MinLevel), "kill first" roles and exit kinds. `m5-hunt-band-empty` and `m4-hunt-band-empty` are built (D42) and close when the archer runs of the live gate pass.

## 53. The mage's logistics and the death handler, cheat-free (M5 item 3; D38, D48-D52; DoD 6, 7)

Item 3 makes the mage's kit, its trips and its death play, not build. The ground's `supplies` section says what the mage carries and when it goes to town; the death loop is one handler that brings its corpse back; and the mod's grants are removed from play, the build keeping its own (section 5.6 of `docs/milestones/m5-first-dungeon.md`). The decisions are D38 and D48 to D52 in `docs/milestones/m5-plan.md` section 7, the rows are in its section 4.5, and the lead's numbers are in its section 8. Every value of the den is a value of the ground's profile (D46), never a constant.

**The supplies section (D48, D49; `Session/Grounds/HuntingGround.cs`, `GroundCatalog.cs`, `GroundDraft.cs`).** Every profile has a `supplies` object, the seventeenth of its sections (section 52). Its `stock` lines are `wcid`, `name`, `keep`, `trip_under` and `stop_under`, with `keep >= trip_under >= stop_under >= 0`: a trip buys up to `keep`, is due under `trip_under`, and the loop stops under `stop_under`. Its `kit` lines are `wcid`, `name` and `slot` (`worn` or `wielded`); `burden_trip_percent` (1 to 100) is the load line; `purse` is `coin` (pyreals) and `notes` (`wcid`, `name`, `value`, `count`). A line's vendor and price are not fields: they are the vendors' `sells` (`HuntingGround.VendorSelling(wcid)`, `PriceOf(wcid)`), and a line no home vendor sells is carried (`HuntingGround.IsCarried`), so it has no trip, only its stop. At load the catalog checks the section against the rest of the profile: a vendor's ware is a stock or kit line, a ware is sold by one vendor, a line's name agrees with the vendor's row, and every spell plan row has its components as stock lines (`ground-plan-gap`). A draft writes the section as `TODO(hand-set)`, which the catalog refuses (section 52). The outdoor grounds' sections are empty.

**The den's supplies and the kit (D49; `Session/Mage/GearKit.cs`, `Session/Scenarios/MageBuildScenario.cs`; `MageKitTests`).** The den's stock is Prismatic Taper 20631 (keep 1,000, trip under 200, stop under 12; Archmage Cindrue, 30 pyreals), Mana Potion 379 (keep 30, trip under 5, stop under 0; Thelnoth Cort, 230), Copper Scarab 686 (keep 20, trip under 5, stop under 2; the travel spells' foci), and Pyreal Scarab 690 and Platinum Scarab 8897, carried (keep 100, trip and stop under 3, D8). The kit is Faran War Apprentice Robe 6071 (worn) and Wand 2472 (wielded), both from Cindrue's list, with `burden_trip_percent` 90. The purse is 300,000 pyreals: 40,000 coin and two trade notes (250,000 and 10,000, wcid 20630 and 2625). `GearKit.Build(spells, account, heroRing, ground)` builds the mage's kit from these: the wielded vendor wand first, the worn vendor robe, the four foci (`GearKind.Focus`, 15268 to 15271), then each stock line to its keep. The foci formula does not depend on the account, which is now only checked. `GearKit.CastBurns` is the set of components the casts burn; a component that is not a stock line, or whose stop line is under one cast's count, fails the build `ground-plan-gap`. `GearKit.KitPlan.For` adds the purse (coin in pyreal stacks of at most 25,000, and the notes) and checks the load against the burden line; `WieldShortfalls` names a robe the mage cannot wear as `TODO(m6-tethana-supplies)`. The build grants the kit once. At the fight's start a missing focus is the stop `m5-mage-foci`, and a carried line under its stop is `m5-platinum-stock` or `m5-carried-stock`, named with the home town. ACE destroys the coin a death drops; the trade notes are bonded in the pinned world SQL, and ACE never drops a bonded item at a death, so they stay in the pack (D49).

**The kit wired (w1; `Session/Scenarios/Journey/BuildStations.cs`, `Lifestone.Headless/Program.cs`).** The mage build is made in one place, `BuildVerifyStation.MageBuildOf(table, classes, log, ground, spells, account, endSession, freshCharacter)`, which passes `GearKit.KitPlan.For`. The headless `--scenario=mage-build` and the journey's build-verify both use it, so a fresh BSD Mage ends its build holding the ground's kit. The mage's wand is the kit's wielded line (`GearKit.WandItem`, Wand 2472), never weenie 8552: the cast-facts station finds it with `CastFactsRun.EquippedWand`, the buff leg by the wand wcid the loaded kit sets, and the keeper's "caster in the pack" read by ItemType Caster (`cast=needs-swap`). The buff cycle has a level floor: a level whose foci components the pack does not hold is skipped and logged by name; the level below it that the pack can pay for is cast, with the line reported down (miss token `down`); a line with no payable level is `no-level`. A pack with no focus (the fighters', the older kit) has no floor. The fight, death and journey legs build the kit of their `--fight-ground`.

**The loot step (D38, off by default; `Session/Mage/MageLootRules.cs`, `Session/Fighter/CorpseLooter.cs`, `Session/Scenarios/FightScenario.cs`, `Options/OptionCatalog.Fight.cs`).** `--fight-loot=on|off` (default off: the owner's D38 has the step built whole and switched off, so turning it on later is a switch) runs after a kill. The fight keeps the creature's name and last position; at its next Choose it finds `Corpse of <name>` within 6 m of that position (5 s to appear), walks to it when it lies beyond 2 m through the travel seam (`TravelPurpose.Corpse`, logged `purpose=loot`, D47; a foot travel only, `TODO(m5-loot-no-walk)`), opens it, waits 3 s for the items, appraises any item whose description sent no Value (section 38), plans with `MageLootRules` as the per-corpse `MageLootPlan`, and takes the plan with the shared `CorpseLooter`. `MageLootRules` keeps pyreals first and always, then ranks the rest by value per burden, each kept while its ratio reaches `MinValuePerBurden` (default 1.0, `TODO(m5-loot-floor-measure)`), within the load line and the free main-pack slots (`SlotsKeptFree`, default 0). A value found neither in the description nor by appraisal counts as 0 and is left (`TODO(m5-loot-value-unknown)`). A corpse named for the player is the death handler's and is never looted here. The thresholds are `FightOptions.LootThresholds`, not command-line options (D46). The last kill waits for the loot (`FightScenario.LootBusy`). The verdict ends `loot=on|off looted= kept= loot_value=` (report-only); off reads `loot=off looted=0 kept=0 loot_value=0`. `LootRules` is no longer sealed (`Decide` and `Describe` are virtual) so that `MageLootPlan` can feed `CorpseLooter`; the fighters' rule is unchanged. Tests: `MageLootRulesTests`, and `FightScenarioTests` with looting on and off.

**The counters and the experience (D48; `Session/Mage/LogisticsCounters.cs`, `Session/Mage/XpSpendRule.cs`).** `LogisticsCounters(ground)` reads only the ground's `supplies` and its vendors' `sells`. `Check(GameState)` returns a `LogisticsVerdict`, `None`, `Trip`, `Stop` or `Unread`, with a named cause (`StockLow`, `StockStop`, `Burden`, `PackUnread`, `ItemsUnsettled`, `CapacityUnread`). A line's count is the stacks of its wcid in the main and side packs, equipped items excluded. A line under its `trip_under` is a trip unless no vendor of the ground sells it, which leaves only its stop. A line under its `stop_under` is a stop, and a stop outranks a stock trip, which outranks the burden trip. The load passes while `burden * 100` is not over `capacity * burden_trip_percent`. `Unread` means the counts are not yet the truth: the caller asks again rather than stopping at login. `XpSpendRule` spends the unassigned experience: it raises the stat that `MageSpendingPlanner.Compute` names first, one rank for exactly that rank's price, through ACE's raise actions (`RaiseSkill`, `RaiseAttribute`, `RaiseVital`) as `Automation`, one in flight at a time. When the pool does not cover the next rank the run ends `PoolCoversNothing`, and it never skips to a cheaper stat. A refused raise ends the run `Refused` with ACE's line. The bounds are 60 raises and 120 s of the session clock (Lifestone's own, not retail's); hitting the cap is `RaiseCap`, not a failure. The fields are `spent_xp=` and `raises=`. The keeper's fizzle and Mana Conversion tries are cut to the pass's remaining actions (`MaxActionsPerPass` 40); a line whose Mana Conversion tries were all refused is sent once more at its ceiling (`BuffCycle.CeilingAttemptsPerLine` 1), and a second refusal fails the cycle by name. Closed: `m5-keeper-tries-cap`, `m5-keeper-tries-rig` and `m5-buffcycle-ceiling-refusal`. Tests: `LogisticsCountersTests`, `XpSpendRuleTests`, and three keeper facts.

**The resupply station (D48, D49; `Session/Scenarios/Journey/ResupplyStation.cs`, `ResupplyPlanner`, `ResupplyOptions`).** From the home lifestone, in peace, `ResupplyStation` buys every stock line under its keep that a home vendor sells (`keep` less what is held), grouped by vendor and walked in the shortest order (`ResupplyPlanner.Order`). A carried line is never bought. Each walk is a `TravelPurpose.Vendor` request logged `walking: purpose=vendor:<name>` (D47). The vendor is met by the profile's guid in the object table, and his live cell and point are logged beside the profile's (`vendor: <name> live ..., profile ..., off by <m> m`; `m6-ground-live-facts`). The stop is at the vendor's use radius from where he stands: his live cell when it is not the profile's, else the profile's stop cell. `m5-station-where-home`: `StationWhere` has no value for a ground's home, so the run checks the lifestone's landblock itself. The vendor's price is checked against the profile's. When the coin is short, a held trade note is sold first, the smallest that covers the shortfall or else the largest, at 1.0 of its value; a purse still too thin buys what it pays and logs `purse: short by <n> pyreals for <line>`. With looting on, the kept items go to the loot buyer, the ground's vendor whose buy mask takes the most of them. `IsSellable` is read from an appraisal's bool 69 when one carries it, else ACE's class list decides (`m4-vendor-issellable-wire`). Refusals are named by kind (`refused: <kind>: ...`: coin, pack, stock, unsellable, open, other); the full text of ACE's refusal lines is not yet read (`m5-resupply-refusal-text`). The kept list is the fight's (`FightScenario.LootKeptGuids`, closed). A bought scroll is not read (`m6-scroll-read`). The scripted vendors are `ScriptedShop` in the tests' `VendorRig.cs`. Tests: `ResupplyStationTests` over both dungeon profiles (the buy list, the thresholds, the refusals, a purse too thin, a vendor not found).

**The death handler (D51; `Session/Mage/DeathHandler.cs`, `Session/Scenarios/DeathScenario.Corpse.cs`, `DeathScenario.Order.cs`, `DeathScenario.LieDown.cs`, `Session/Scenarios/DeathOptions.cs`).** After the respawn at the lifestone the handler runs one order for every option set (logged `handler: the order (D51)`): the rebuff of what the mage still holds (the composed fight's buff stage alone, no walk); a pre-trip heal when the health is under `--death-recover-min-health` (a cast skipped for mana resumes when the mana regenerates, `m4-death-trip-heal-resume`, closed); the corpse trip by its route, a plan value logged at the trip's start (`route=recall|runner`); inside the ground the seam's Corpse runner walks to the corpse (`purpose=corpse`, D47); the loot is taken all; the wand and the kit's armour are wielded or worn again from the pack; the item lines are re-cast; the fight resumes with no place. The route is Primary Portal Recall, cast by the handler itself (`RecallHome`), where the mage holds the ground's tie; otherwise the runner goes from the lifestone to the ground's portal and through it, which is not built (`TODO(m6-first-tie-walk)`). The steps are the stages `handler-<step>` (`DeathScenario.HandlerStagePrefix`; the hold is `handler-trip-heal`), each logged once on entry. A death at any step, by any creature (an avoided one too, `m5-avoid-creature-reach`), restarts the handler for the newer corpse, and the older one is remembered and named (`m5-death-older-corpse`). A corpse past its rot reads `corpse_recovered=rotted`, one not in the table within 15 s of the arrival `not-found`, and the fight resumes either way. A host whose seam is still the place travel makes no corpse leg (`corpse_recovered=no`, `m5-death-corpse-runner`).

**The trip's aborts (D51, d4).** Each abort is the handler's, named in `trip_abort=` and tested on both dungeon profiles. The health line (`--death-recover-abort-health PCT`, default 40): when the share falls under it on the way, at the corpse or at its open, the trip holds where it stands and heals as the pre-trip heal does (Heal Self VII, up to 4 casts and a minute of regeneration, mana waited for) up to the larger of that line and `--death-recover-min-health`, then goes on. Only when the heals cannot bring the share back is the corpse given up (`trip_abort=health-under-<N>`). It holds rather than turns back, because nothing may be placed back on foot (D51) and a turn at low health would fight at that health anyway. `--death-recover-abort-seconds S` (default 90) counts from the arrival in the ground, a hold included (`trip_abort=trip-over-<N>s`). A refused or unanswered open (15 s) is `open-refused`, and a corpse that appears late within its window is found (`trip_abort=none`). Each abort keeps what was taken and resumes the fight. The death's line ends `deaths= corpse_recovered= route= items_back= vitae_grants=0 rebuffed= relines=` (report-only, `DeathScenario.VerdictFields` with `HandlerFields`), and the handler's last log line adds `trip_abort= trip_heals=`. Tests: `DeathHandlerTests` (both dungeon profiles over the scripted ACE), `DeathScenarioTests.Order` and `DeathScenarioTests.Corpse`.

**The rearm, the front-room stop and the level floor (F4 to F6, D53, and the live gate's fixes; `Session/Mage/DeathHandler.cs`, `Session/Mage/GearKit.cs`, `Session/Mage/LevelFloor.cs`, `Session/Scenarios/Journey/ResupplyStation.cs`).** ACE drops a level-127 character's 6 to 8 most valuable unbonded items at its death, the wielded caster among them (@95ef426d `Player_Death.cs` 488-505, 650-672), and the magic mode needs a wielded caster (`Player_Combat.cs:872-880`), so a mage respawned without its wand cannot cast the recall to its corpse. After the respawn the handler rearms (D53): it wields the pack's caster (`GearInventory.PackCaster`; a fresh character's Training Wand counts), else buys the ground's kit wand at the home vendor that sells it (the resupply's kit trip, `ResupplyOptions.Kit`, `ResupplyPlanner.KitBuyList`), else fails by name; a host with no journey context cannot shop and fails at that step (`TODO(m5-death-rearm-standalone)`). The order is the respawn, the rearm, the rebuff, the corpse trip, the items back, the item lines and the fight, and the death line carries `rearm=none|pack|bought|failed`. When the items come back, another caster in the wand's slot goes to the pack before the kit's wand is wielded (the gate's round 3: ACE refused the kit's wand while the Training Wand held the slot). The build's tie wields the pack's caster before it asks for the magic mode. `rebuffed=` reads `no` while an item line's piece lies on the corpse; those lines are re-cast after the items come back (`relines=`); the M5 plan's section 9 row `m5-death-rebuffed-item-lines` asks item 4 to judge the rebuff together with the relines. A vendor whose stop cell the room graph cannot reach has a `stop_point` in a room it can (F4: Archmage Cindrue's entry hall 0xA9B40117 for her shop 0xA9B4011B), and the shop's open lets ACE's move-to walk the rest (`Player_Use.cs:205` `CreateMoveToChain`). `LevelFloor` (F6) is the one rule every automated caster shares: while the pack holds the school's focus, a level whose foci-formula components (its scarab and the Prismatic Tapers) the pack lacks is skipped and logged once. `BuffCycle`, `GearCasts.Pick` and `PickFizzle`, the cast-facts station (`worn=` is the kit's worn lines) and, after the gate's round 3, the harm-heal script's Harm Self levels read it, so no cast is sent that ACE refuses with 0x0400 for a scarab the kit does not carry. A check with no payable level reads `fizzle=n/a:unpayable`. At item 4's gate (round 1, G1) the keeper's and the buff cycle's mana, heal and revitalize picks read the floor too, so a level whose scarab the pack lacks is skipped (a WARN once), and with none payable the dance ends named and the mana regenerates. The replayer runs the client-side cull after each frame as the live tick does, with no cells (`TODO(m5-object-cull-cells)`).

**The grants removed (d1 to d3, c1).** The death loop no longer sends the vitae `xp` grant, the regear by the item verb or the decoy top-up (the stages `vitae`, `regear` and `unbuff-decoys` are gone). `--death-stages` with `vitae` or `regear` is refused by name, and `--death-decoys` is refused whatever its value (it stays in the option catalog as a retired entry, as `--smoke-statics` does). The decoys are retired: `DecoyKit`, `GearKit.ForDeathLoop`, `GearKit.WithDecoys` and `GearKind.Decoy` are gone with their tests (`DecoyKitTests` is deleted; `DeathDecoyOptionsTests` proves the refusal). A death drops the mage's own gear, as ACE's rule has it, and the corpse trip brings it back. `DeathDropModel` stays as the prediction of what a corpse holds; `gear_left=` counts the kit lines the trip did not take (`DeathDropModel.NeededOf`, `IsNeeded`). `m4-decoy-bonded-flag` and `m4-decoy-value-unknown` are closed by removal. The journeys' gear station and the death's gear stage run `GearScenario.NoGrant`, which counts the pack, fails a missing wand, armour piece or focus by name (`gear: <item> is not in the pack and play grants nothing (M5 DoD 6)`) and sends no `item` request. Every mod verb after the build passes through `PlayerTools.Verbs` and is counted by `ModVerbCounter` (`item`, `xp`, `place`, `corpses rot`, `spell learn`); `granted=` (every verb but `place`, M5 3 F2) and `places=` on the done lines are its counts, report-only until item 4's gate. `GrantsCountedTests` closes `k4-heal-station-offline` (the heal station runs on an offline rig with no mod verb).

**The places (D50; `Session/Scenarios/MageBuildScenario.cs`, `Session/Scenarios/Journey/JourneyTravel.cs`, `Session/Scenarios/Journey/JourneyPlans.cs`).** The build ties the ground's portal. After the kit grant the session-ending build runs `PortalTieLeg` (the stages `tie-place`, `tie-find`, `tie-cast`, `tie-link` and `tie-home`): one place to the portal's front point, the Primary Portal Tie cast through the cast policy as a host action, ACE's "linked with the portal" text or its refusal named, and one place back to the ground's lifestone. This is build time, the owner's one cheat layer (D11, section 5.6). `MageBuildScenario.TieIntent` is `none` for an embedded build or a build with no kit plan, and `skipped` (`TODO(m6-ground-fields)`) for a ground with no portal record. The mage plan's budget is `JourneyPlans.MageTeleportBudget` = 0, so the journey after the build makes no place; the hero mage keeps 1 and never spends it (`m5-hero-budget`). The tie is one state per ground (`JourneyFacts.PortalTieOf` and `SetPortalTie`, with `PortalTie` Unknown, Tied or Untied): a fresh character is tied after the build, but a journey that cannot read the link tries the recall, and ACE's 0x04A3 sets it untied. `JourneyGrounds.DungeonOf(cell, journeyGround)` names the journey's ground for a cell. The travel seam sends the mage to a dungeon by the ground's Primary Portal Recall unless the tie is untied; untied fails `travel:ground:untied` (`TODO(m6-first-tie-walk)`). `m6-fighter-home` (a fighter ground of another town) is not built. Tests: `P1PlacesTests` over both dungeon profiles.

**The logistics station (D52; `Session/Scenarios/Journey/LogisticsStation.cs`, `JourneyPlans.cs`, `StationKinds`).** The mage plan's `logistics` station runs after the den fight (one of the 22 station kinds; expected 150 s, `JourneyPlans.MageLogisticsSeconds`; the mage's plan is 1,315 s in all: den fight 215, logistics 150, death 280, recall home 30). It reads `LogisticsCounters.Check` of the journey's ground, waiting up to 10 s for `Unread`. A stop fails the station by name even when forced (`logistics: stop: <line>: <n> held, under its stop line <m>`). In the gate the trip is forced (`JourneyPlans.TripForced`, `trigger=forced`, D52): three kills cannot bring a stock line under its line, so the counters' own triggers are proved offline (`LogisticsCountersTests`) and live by item 4's autopilot lane. Unforced, a trip is `trigger=stock:<name>` or `burden`, and otherwise the station is skipped (`skipped=no-trip-due`). The trip is Lifestone Recall home (the travel seam's Home), the resupply (`ResupplyRun`, stepped inside the station), the experience (`XpSpendRule`, up to `LogisticsStation.MaxXpCalls` = 3 calls while a call ends `RaiseCap`), and Primary Portal Recall back when the trip began in the ground. A failed resupply is named (`resupply: <why>`), and the experience and the recall back still happen; a failed recall home ends the station. It needs peace and 20 % mana. Its fields, report-only, are `trigger= granted= places= loot= sold= bought= spent= notes_sold= spent_xp= raises=`, then `skipped=` or `why=` where they apply. `granted=` and `places=` are the verb counter's deltas over the station (0 expected). Test: `LogisticsStationTests` over both dungeon profiles. Open: `m5-logistics-xptable` (the station fails named when the session has no XpTable and the journey has no dats).

**The gate's checks (c2b; `tools/lib/GateJudge.psm1`, `tools/smoke.ps1`, `tools/live-gate.ps1`).** From this gate the live gate judges `storeys_visited>=2 basement_entries=0` on the mage journey's den-fight station line (section 52). The logistics station's, the death station's and the done line's `places=` fields are report-only: `Get-JourneyVerdict` returns them as `LogisticsFields`, `DeathFields` and `Places`, and smoke prints `report-only logistics:`, `report-only death:` and `report-only done: places=`. The death station's new fields (`deaths=`, `corpse_recovered=`, `vitae_grants=`, `rebuffed=`) are report-only too. `granted=0 places=0` is judged from item 4's gate (section 5 item 3 of the spec). The mage journey's limit grows for the logistics station and the death's new order: smoke and the gate's table go from 2,200 to 2,500 s, and the mage's expected seconds from 600 to 800 s, pinned by `SliceCheckAndSmokeTests`.

**The lead's measures (run in the gate's round 3).** `DenMeasureLiveTests.DenLoot` (`m5-den-loot-value`, about 35 minutes) is the den's fight with looting on for 30 minutes. It prints the kills, `looted=`, `kept=`, `loot_value=`, `payout_est=` (the items the loot buyer's mask takes, at the pinned buy rate of 0.9, `TODO(m5-loot-buyprice-wire)`), `per_kill=` and `per_hour=`. `DenMeasureLiveTests.ComponentLossVi` (`m5-component-loss-vi`, about 10 minutes) casts Flame Bolt VI for 60 casts with the four foci in the pack and counts the Prismatic Tapers and Pyreal Scarabs before and after (`tapers_per_cast=`, beside VII's 0.43). Both write `cache/m5/3/measures/<name>.txt` through `EmitFor` (`TODO(m5-measure-dir)`), and assert that they measured. `ComponentLossVi` read 0.8 tapers and 0.017 scarabs a cast at VI. `DenLoot` read 211 kills an hour and about 318,000 pyreals an hour of sellable loot, but looted only 8 of 95 corpses (`m5-loot-no-walk`), and it failed its own 30-minute assertion when the mage died at 27 minutes. The details are in `docs/milestones/m5-plan.md` section 8.

**The TODO names.** Opened by item 3 and named in code: `m5-loot-no-walk`, `m5-loot-floor-measure`, `m5-loot-value-unknown`, `m5-loot-buyprice-wire`, `m5-measure-dir`, `m5-den-loot-value` and `m5-component-loss-vi` (the two measures close when the lead has run them), `m5-mage-foci`, `m5-platinum-stock`, `m5-carried-stock`, `m5-hero-ring-diamond`, `m5-hero-budget`, `m5-resupply-refusal-text`, `m5-station-where-home`, `m5-logistics-xptable`, `m5-avoid-creature-reach`, `m5-death-older-corpse`, `m5-death-corpse-runner`, `m6-tethana-supplies`, `m6-first-tie-walk`, `m6-fighter-home` and `m6-scroll-read`. Closed: `m5-resupply-kept-list` (c2a), `m5-keeper-tries-cap`, `m5-keeper-tries-rig` and `m5-buffcycle-ceiling-refusal` (m4), `m5-death-handler-fields` and `m5-death-m4-order` (d2), `m4-death-trip-heal-resume` (d1), `m4-lie-down-controller` (d4), `m4-decoy-bonded-flag` and `m4-decoy-value-unknown` (by removal, d3), `journey-portal-per-ground` (p1) and `k4-heal-station-offline` (c1). `m5-d2-levelxp` (the lead, after the w1 merge: `DeathScenario.LevelXp` removed) is closed too. Opened by the fixes and the gate: `m5-death-rearm-standalone` and `m5-object-cull-cells` (and the plan's row `m5-death-rebuffed-item-lines`). Still open: `m4-death-walkless-wait` (the death-wait half, in `DeathScenario.cs`).

## 54. The outer loop and the dial (M5 item 4; D55-D59; DoD 8, 9)

Item 4 makes the mage's day run by itself. The autopilot walks the journey's own stations (buff, travel, fight, trip, death) in cycles, saves its counts and budget, reconnects after a drop, stops by its rules, and gives the controls to the person by `/stop` typed in the chat and takes them back by `/auto` (since M5 item 7, D145; the last paragraphs of this section). The decisions are D55 to D59 in `docs/milestones/m5-plan.md` section 7, the rows are in its section 4.6, and the lead's numbers are in its section 8. Every value of the den stays a value of the ground's profile (D46). The autopilot's own bounds (the watchdogs, the reconnect's waits, the upkeep's 5 s check) are Lifestone's own and are named as such. The autopilot lane and its judges (c1) are built by the item's last slice.

**The state machine (D57; `Session/Autopilot/AutopilotScenario.cs`).** One state machine runs over the journey's own stations, made by the journey's `IStationFactory` with one `JourneyContext`. Its states are known state, buff, travel, fight, upkeep, trip, death and stopped. The places are home, ground, elsewhere and dead. The world decides the known state, never the checkpoint. At home the autopilot goes to buff. On the ground it goes to the fight, or to buff first when no enchantment is up. Elsewhere it casts the recall home once and classifies again; a second time elsewhere stops `failed:known-state:elsewhere`. Dead, it takes the death arrow. It waits up to 60 s for presence. The specs it asks the catalog for are `buff` (open to Here, so the mage buffs where it stands, at its 60 per cent mana), `den-travel`, `recall-home`, `den-fight` with `open-ended`, `logistics` with `trip=counters` or `trip=forced`, and `death` with `no-fight`. Before each station it checks its stop rules, runs the keeper's pass (`JourneyScenario.VitalsTimeoutSeconds`) and asks for peace when the station needs it (`JourneyScenario.PeaceSeconds`). A miss in `StationPreconditions` stops the run by name. The autopilot never logs in or off; its host does. Its seams are `StateChanged` (an `AutopilotTransition` after each arrow, which the checkpoint and the dial use), `RequestStop(why)`, `Abandon(why)` and `Seed` (both used by the dial, below), `Totals`, `CycleTally`, `Cycle`, `Cycles`, `State`, `StopName`, `Ok` and `CurrentRun`.

**The fight's external stop, its triggers and the upkeep (D57; `AutopilotScenario.cs`, `Session/Scenarios/FightScenario.cs`, `FightOptions.cs`).** `FightScenario` implements `IStoppableFight` (`Kills`, `StopReason`, `RequestStop(FightStopReason)`). `FightStopReason` is an open set of names. `LogisticsTrip`, `LogisticsStop`, `StopRule`, `ForcedTrip` and `TestDeath` are built in; the upkeep adds `buff-margin` (`AutopilotScenario.BuffMargin`) and `health-floor` (`AutopilotScenario.HealthFloor`). The first reason stands, and a blank name throws. The stop is honoured at the fight's clean `--fight-minutes` point, after the kill notices reach the tour and with no loot step in flight: `EndFight` stops the casts and the dance, abandons the walk, asks for peace and succeeds. The fight's line ends ` stop=<name>` only when a stop was asked. Each frame the autopilot checks, in this order: a stop rule or a request gives `stop-rule`; the scripted death (once, at the first fight with at least one kill) gives `test-death`; the forced trip (once a run, when the cycle's kills and the fight's kills reach N) gives `forced-trip`. Every second the logistics counters (`LogisticsCounters.Check`) give `logistics-stop` or `logistics-trip`. Every 5 s (`UpkeepCheckSeconds`, the last check in order) it reads the upkeep. Health under `HealthFloorPercent` (50, Lifestone's own; the fight has no heal of its own) gives `health-floor`, and the upkeep is the keeper's pass in place up to the fight's 80 per cent (`JourneyPlans.FightHealthPercent`), with no peace need. A keeper that cannot restore it stops `failed:upkeep:vitals`. A buff line under its margin gives `buff-margin`, and the upkeep is the journey's buff station, run in place, with the re-buff driver's own check (`RebuffDue` with `RebuffMarginSeconds`, and the wait for the armour memory's seed). The margin is the host's own: the play host and the leg pass `--buff-rebuff-margin` and the other buff settings to the autopilot as `AutopilotOptions.Buff = BuffOptions.FromArguments(...)` (4-R4). After any upkeep the fight resumes. An unknown reason name is logged as `TODO(m5-autopilot-reason)` and goes back to the fight.

**Routing and cycles (`AutopilotScenario.cs`).** After the fight a stop rule goes to stopped (ok=yes); `test-death` goes to the death station; `forced-trip` goes to logistics with `trip=forced`; `logistics-trip` goes to logistics by its counters; `logistics-stop` stops `stop=logistics-stop:<line>` (ok=no); an upkeep reason goes to upkeep and back to the fight; a fight with no reason fails `failed:fight:ended-unasked`. A logistics run that reports `skipped=` makes no trip and goes back to the fight. A station failure is no longer a stop. It takes one recovery of the cycle's budget (`--autopilot-watchdog-recoveries`), logged `autopilot recovery after <state>:<why>`, and the autopilot goes to the recall home and the known state; past the budget it stops `failed:<state>:<why>`. Stopped by name at once, with no recovery, are the counters' stop (a failure text holding `logistics: stop`), a kit stop (a failure text holding `kit`), the factory failing, the known state's own stops (not present, elsewhere twice), the session closed by the person, a failed reconnect and the counters' stop in the fight (4-R6). A cycle ends at each return to town, that is at the end of a trip or of a death, and writes its `autopilot cycle` line then. A stop writes the cycle in progress with `state=stopped`, which is not counted in `cycles=`, and then the done line.

**The trip, forced and unforced (D52; `Session/Scenarios/Journey/LogisticsStation.cs`, `JourneyStations.cs`).** The trip is the logistics station the journey already has. Unforced, it runs when the counters say `trigger=stock:<name>` or `trigger=burden`; otherwise the station is skipped (`skipped=no-trip-due`). Forced, `--autopilot-trip-after-kills N` (0 to 100,000) fires once a run, counted from the cycle's start. A forced trip still fails by name when a stop line is held. The `logistics` spec takes `trip=counters` or `trip=forced`, and any other value is refused by name (`ArgumentException`). A hand-back switches the once-a-run triggers off (`AutopilotCarry.Remaining`); a restart from the checkpoint re-arms them. A trip that is cut (a drop, a watchdog trip, a station failure or an abandon) keeps its sold and bought, which fold into the cycle, and the logistics station runs again from the known state: it is forced again only if the forced trip had made no sale, purchase or spending yet, else it runs by its counters. A resumed station that finds nothing due still counts as the trip when the cut one had done work (4-R2).

**The death, scripted and unscripted (D51, D53, D56; `AutopilotScenario.cs`, `Session/Scenarios/DeathScenario.cs`, `Session/Mage/DeathHandler.cs`).** The scripted death, `--autopilot-test-death[=on|off]`, kills the mage once, at the first fight with a kill, through the M4 undress and lie-down (M4 D37).
