The death station runs with `no-fight`, so the autopilot returns to its own fight. The station gets the journey context, so the rearm shops (D53); `TODO(m5-death-rearm-standalone)` stays only in the standalone death leg. A death the autopilot did not script is also handled, in any state, or in a known state with health 0. The autopilot keeps a death tally from its start, offers it to the journey facts (`JourneyFacts.ObservedDeath`) and begins the journey's death station with `no-fight` and no vitals pass before it (`UnscriptedDeathSpec`). The scenario takes it after the respawn, at stage `respawn`, with no walk, no undress and no lie-down; the handler's order after that is unchanged. A second death during the handler restarts it. The known limit: a known state that finds the mage at health 0 with no death seen enters the station with an empty tally, and its respawn wait fails by name after 60 s if no arrival comes. A death station that is cut (a drop, a watchdog trip, a station failure or an abandon) is entered again from the known state with its tally kept: the tally is offered through `Facts.ObservedDeath` until the station ends (the corpse recovered, or named lost, and the death counted); a stop with a death still pending counts it, and a scripted death that had not happened when the station was cut is scripted again. `_unscripted` and `Facts.ObservedDeath` are cleared at every cut (4-R2, 4-R15). D56 settles the rebuff: `rebuffed=yes` when every buff line is up once the item lines are re-cast, because an item line's piece lies on the corpse until the items come back. The value before the trip is kept as `rebuffed_pretrip=`, and `stage_rebuffed=` stays the respawn's measure. The death line's fields (`deaths=`, `corpse_recovered=`, `vitae_grants=`, `rebuffed=`, `rebuffed_pretrip=`, `relines=`, `rearm=` and the rest) are report-only in the first gate. The code half of `m5-death-rebuffed-item-lines` is closed; its judge is the lane's (c1).

**The stop rules and the stop names (`Session/Autopilot/AutopilotOptions.cs`, `Options/OptionCatalog.Autopilot.cs`).** `--autopilot-minutes M` (0 to 10,080) is a budget of minutes on the session clock, which a checkpoint carries over every start (`minutesUsed`). `--autopilot-level L` (0 to 275) stops the run when the character reaches level L. A stop request from `/stop`, the panel's button or a host ends the run at the next clean point. The `stop=` field takes `minutes`, `level`, `request:<why>`, `logistics-stop:<line>`, `watchdog:<state>`, `session-closed`, `reconnect-failed`, `failed:session-dropped` and `failed:<state>:<why>` (the factory, vitals, peace, `precondition:<x>`, or the station's own failure). A death the autopilot did not script is no longer a stop; it is handled as above. **The unattended end (M5 item 7 s1).** A run that `--autopilot-start` started ends by itself when it reaches Done with a stop of its own (`stop=minutes`, `stop=level`, or any stop with ok=no, such as `watchdog`, `failed`, `reconnect-failed` or `logistics-stop`). It logs one decision line after the done line: `autopilot: the run was started by --autopilot-start and ended by itself (stop=... ok=...); the game logs off and quits with exit code N`. It then logs the character off (Lifestone's own wait, 30 s, after which it quits anyway) and quits the game with exit code 0 for ok=yes and 1 otherwise. A host fault or a failed kit load also quits the game, with exit code 1. A person's own stop request (`stop=request:...`) logs `... ended on a stop request (stop=request:key); the game stays open` and keeps the game open. An interrupt pauses the run (Interrupted never reaches Done), so nothing ends; after a hand-back the run is still the unattended one and ends as above. A run a person started with `/auto` or the panel's button never quits the game.

**The watchdogs (`Session/Autopilot/AutopilotWatchdogs.cs`).** Each state has a bound in seconds of the session clock. A state's clock starts when its station runs, after the keeper's vitals pass (its own bound, 420 s) and the peace (15 s); the known state counts from its entry. The bounds are known state 90, buff 300, travel 150, upkeep 300, trip 900 and death 900. The fight's is counted from its last kill: the ground's longest respawn plus the tick plus 600 s (`AutopilotWatchdogs.FightMarginSeconds`, Lifestone's own), so the den (a 960 s respawn) is 1,560 s and the Tusker Temple (360 s) is 960 s; a ground that lists no generator keeps 900 s (4-R7). Stopped has none. `--autopilot-watchdog-scale F` (0.01 to 100, default 1) scales them. A state over its bound is logged `watchdog: <state> over <n> s (bound, cycle, recovery k of N)` and recovered by the recall home and then the known state, except a death station, which goes straight to the known state (a dead mage casts nothing). At most `--autopilot-watchdog-recoveries N` (0 to 20, default 2) recoveries happen a cycle, reset at each cycle's end. Past the budget, the next trip stops `stop=watchdog:<state>` with ok=no (a station failure past it stops `failed:<state>:<why>`). The clock restarts after a reconnect, and the recoveries are kept.

**The checkpoint and its stores (D57; `Session/Autopilot/AutopilotCheckpoint.cs`).** The checkpoint is one JSON file, camelCase, schema 1: the schema, the character with its guid (`characterGuid`), the run's tag (`runTag`, from `AutopilotOptions.RunTag`, which a host sets and no command-line option does), the ground, the state, the cycle and the cycles, the totals of the ended cycles, the cycle in progress (`cycleTally`), the minutes used and their target, the level target, the last decision and its session time, the stop, and `completed`. It holds the counts and the budget, never the position. A start reads it after the in-world check, but the world decides the state, and the stop rules of the run hold (a difference from the file is logged). The file is written on every state change (inside the state setter, before the `StateChanged` hook), on exit (a run that has not stopped writes `lastDecision` as `exit:`), at the stop (with `stop` and `completed`), at an abandon (with `abandoned: <why>` as the last decision), and quietly every 60 s inside a state (`CheckpointRefreshSeconds`). So a kill in the middle of a fight loses at most a minute of budget and the kills since, and the fight's kills so far are in `cycleTally`. A failed write is named (`checkpoint: write failed (<store>): ...`), and the next state change tries again. The forced trip and the scripted death are not resumed; they are re-armed. A torn, unreadable, foreign-character or newer-schema file is named (`checkpoint: set aside: <why>; moved to <where>`), renamed aside (`.aside`, then `.aside2`, never deleted), and the run starts fresh. A file of another character (a re-created one: `set aside: another character (guid 0x... in the file, 0x... now ...)`) or of another run (`set aside: another run (tag ... in the file, ... now)`) is set aside the same way (4-R1); a guid of 0 (a file of an older build) matches. A run that ended by its stop rule or a request is finished, and the next start is fresh over it with a named line. A run that ended by a failure (a watchdog, a failed reconnect, a station) is resumable on purpose, so the budget and the totals carry on. The known limit: a fight in progress at a kill contributes its kills, not its experience and mana fields. `IAutopilotCheckpointStore` has `Describe`, `Read`, `Write` and `SetAside`, and `MemoryCheckpointStore` serves the tests. Each host supplies a file store that writes a temp file beside the target and then moves it over the target in one step. The play host's `UserDataCheckpointStore` writes `user://autopilot/<character>.json` (for the mage, `bsd-mage.json`; `-IsolateUserData` isolates it). The headless `AutopilotFileCheckpointStore` writes into `--autopilot-checkpoint-dir DIR`, by default an `autopilot` folder beside the run's recording (`OutputPaths` refuses `src/` and `tests/`). There are two copies on purpose: the architecture tests keep file I/O out of the client. A hand-back's store is `ColdReadOnce`: it reads nothing and writes through, so the file the interrupt wrote is not counted on top of the carried totals. The checkpoint's log lines name the file alone, never an absolute path: the Godot leg logs the file name (`Path.GetFileName`), the headless host the path relative to its output folder (4-R11).

**The reconnect and `m5-reconnect-auto` (`Session/Autopilot/IAutopilotSession.cs`, `AutopilotScenario.cs`).** The session is checked in every state. When its phase is no longer in the world (a failure, a boot, the 20 s silence watchdog, a reset), the station in progress is cut, a fight's kills so far still count, a death or a trip cut this way is entered again from the known state (see the death and the trip above; 4-R2), and the reconnect begins. `LoggingOff`, `Closing` and `Closed` are the person's own, so the run stops `session-closed` with ok=yes. With no `IAutopilotSession` the run stops `failed:session-dropped`. Each try waits (`--autopilot-reconnect-waits S,S,...`: up to ten entries of 0 to 600 s; default 5, 15, 30, 60 and 120 s, 230 s in all), then calls `IAutopilotSession.Reconnect()` (the session's reconnect and a fresh login), then steps the login until it succeeds, fails or 150 s pass (`ReconnectLoginSeconds`). A try that ends outside the world fails. Each step is logged (`reconnect: the session left the world ... try 1 of 5 in 5 s`, `reconnect: try n of N after S s (session phase ...)`, `... failed: <why>`, `... try n+1 of N in S s`, `... try n of N ok ...`). A success adds one to `reconnects` on the cycle and its later lines, and the state is classified from the world again. The autopilot's own try is named (`autopilot: its own reconnect try N begins ...`), and its offline double goes through `GameStateReducer.Reset` and a relogin that delivers the player, its description, the pack and the vendors again (4-R14). The last failure stops `reconnect-failed` with ok=no. A stop request or the stop rule waits for the world to come back. `m5-reconnect-auto` is closed with the 20 s silence watchdog kept, and the reason is in the `IAutopilotSession` doc comment. `SessionTimeouts.Silence` (D18) is what turns a dead link into the failed phase in 20 s instead of retail's 140 s (`--retail-net`), and a reconnect makes a new network session with its own timer, so the watchdog cannot break it. ACE drops a silent client after 60 s (`NetworkSession.cs:336-338` at @95ef426d, `DefaultSessionTimeout`), so the first tries (5 and 15 s) can meet the old session still held, and the later waits outlast it. `SessionTimeouts.cs` is unchanged. Two things are not exercised offline: the offline stand-in's own reconnect (the host's), and whether the disconnected screen returns to the game screen after the person's reconnect (`m5-autopilot-ui-reconnect`, 4-R8's live half). The screen's Reconnect button drives the same failed phase (D43), but a press of it during the autopilot's run is a hand-over, not a second login (below). The live reconnect check is c1's.

**The hosts (`Lifestone.Headless/Program.cs`, `Client/Session/Hosting/HeadlessAutopilotScenario`, `scripts/Session/AutopilotLegRunner.cs`, `scripts/Session/AutopilotHost.cs`).** Three hosts drive the same logic. The headless `--scenario=autopilot` plays BSD Mage's plan on the `test` account only (another account is an error that names no value); `--journey` may only say `mage`, and the `--autopilot-*` options belong to this scenario alone. Its limits are lifted: with no `--autopilot-minutes` nothing limits the run; with minutes the login gets 300 s, then the minutes, then a 1,200 s safe point, so a trip of 600 s or a death of 280 s finishes first. `--quit-after` still wins. The done line's ok is the exit code, read after the log off, and the autopilot's bare lines go to stdout before the summary. The game's `--login-check=autopilot` (`LoginCheckKind.Autopilot`, never part of `all` or the bare form, the `test` account only) steps the same dial logic at process priority 100 over the journey's parts. It prints `[logincheck] autopilot ...` and `autopilot leg done ok= seconds= cycles= stop= frames_saved= frames_not_taken= failure=`, and exits 0 only when the done line says ok=yes and nothing else failed. Its limit is 60 s, then 300 s, then the minutes times 60, then 1,200 s; with no minutes it has none. Its review frames are report-only, `<stem>-autopilot-<state>-c<cycle>.png`, two seconds after each state begins, one per state per cycle. The play host's `AutopilotHost` is a thin node around `AutopilotHostLogic`, as the re-buff driver's host is around its logic. Its `ProcessPriority` is 60, after the session host (-900), play mode (-800), and the retail UI and the re-buff driver (50). `PlayMode.Install` installs it inside its auto-enter branch, beside the re-buff driver, and only when the dats exist. A first press reads the spell table and the den's rooms on workers (`KitLoads`, `JourneyLegRunner.StartLoads`); a later press is immediate. The factory is `AutopilotKit.Create`. Only BSD Mage plays; a press on another character is a log line. A non-OOM exception in the node is reported once, the logic is disposed and the dial stays off for the run. The re-buff driver and the autopilot's buff station no longer both cast on the one mage: with `--buff-auto on` the driver stands down while the dial owns the buffs (4-R4, below). `AutopilotHost.Unattended.cs` (the partial that holds the start and the end) came with the item 7 s1 changes: `AutopilotHostLogic` gained `Start` and `Toggle` with a `startedBy` argument, `UnattendedEnd` (an `AutopilotUnattendedEnd` with `ExitCode` and `Decision`), `CloseVitaePage` and `VitaeCloses`.

**The dial (D59 and D20; `Session/Autopilot/AutopilotHostLogic.cs`, `Session/Contracts/AutopilotStatus.cs`, `RetailUi/Lifestone/LifestoneHotkeys.cs`, `ToolsPanel.cs`, `AutomationInspector.cs`).** `AutopilotHostLogic` owns one scenario at a time, made by a factory, and is in one of six modes: Off, Running, Stopping, Interrupted, Halted or Done. Since M5 7 D145 the dial is two chat commands, `/stop` and `/auto` (the last paragraphs of this section); `Toggle` is what `/auto` and the panel's button call, and `/stop` is `StopHere`. Off or Done starts the autopilot (from `/auto`, a resume with the reason `resume`); Running asks a clean stop (`RequestStop("key")`, the token D59 chose, kept); Interrupted or Halted hands back. The option `--autopilot-start[=on|off]` (default off; the play host only, registered in `OptionCatalog.Autopilot.cs` and parsed by `AutopilotHostOptions.StartAtLogin`) starts the dial once, as `/auto` does, when the character is in the world and named. The host's own rule applies: BSD Mage only, and any other character gets the one existing line. It logs `autopilot: started by --autopilot-start` and never starts again by itself: after a stop, Done or a hand-off, `/auto` and the panel's button decide. D59 and D20 made Num Lock the dial's key; D145 retired it. `LifestoneHotkeys` keeps the key only to answer with a hint line in the chat ("Lifestone: Num Lock no longer starts or stops the autopilot; type /auto or /stop in the chat."; `LifestoneHotkeysTests`), and nothing starts or stops because of it. D59's keypad finding stands for the movement keys: retail binds the keypad (scan codes 0x48, 0x4A to 0x4E, 0x50, 0x52, 0x53, Enter 0x9C and Divide 0xB5) only in input map 5, the camera's, and in none of the player's movement map, and Lifestone translates a host key by its physical code (`GodotKeyCodes.ToDik`), so Num Lock's state cannot change what a keypad key is (`KeypadNumLockTests` and a play-check row). While the logic runs, the person's movement keys do nothing (`HumanKeyLock`, below), and `/stop` is the person taking the controls: the logic cancels the queued automation and releases the automation's held keys (`MoveIntents.StandDownAutomation`), counts `interrupted=` in the cycle tally, abandons the run with the token `chat-stop` (the fight in flight is folded into the cycle, so its kills count; the checkpoint is written with `abandoned: chat-stop`; no done line), disposes the run, and stops stepping it (4-R9). The hand-back is a fresh scenario built from `AutopilotCarry`, which holds the cycles, the totals, the cycle in progress with its interrupted and resumed counts, the seconds used (`ElapsedSeconds`, the scenario's `SecondsUsed`) and the once-a-run flags; its `Seed` (the cycle, the cycles ended, the seconds used, the pending death and the pending trip) starts the new scenario, and `AutopilotKit.Create` no longer makes an interim `Cycles + 1` seed, so the play host and the leg agree (4-R9). It reads the state the world shows (a walk the person broke off cannot resume), counts `resumed=`, and runs on the minutes left: with a seed the minutes budget is the run's total, so `--autopilot-minutes 480` with a restart that resumed 240 minutes and a hand-back at 250 minutes leaves 230 (4-R5). A hand-back while a movement key is still held (`AutopilotHostLogic.HumanKeyHeld`, read from the physical key state of the movement map) is deferred: the dial logs `hand-back: waiting for the movement key to be released` once and retries each frame (4-R16). The dial remembers the foreign reconnect count at each start and hand-back (`AutopilotHostLogic.ForeignReconnects`, which leaves out the autopilot's own tries); if the person's reconnect (the disconnected screen's button) moves it under a running dial, the dial yields at once: an interrupt (`interrupted=` +1), logged `autopilot: the person reconnected: handing over`, and no try of its own (4-R8). While the autopilot owns the buffs (Running or Stopping, the host not faulted), the re-buff driver starts no pass and logs `auto-buff: standing down: the autopilot owns the buffs` once; an interrupt, a halt or Done gives them back (4-R4). The switch itself is not changed. The kill switch: the Action API cancels queued automation and refuses new actions. The loop's own sends are `Automation` as well, so the switch withdraws them too: the fight's loot (its open, identify and close), the resupply's vendor sends, the death handler's rearm, corpse and wear sends, and `CorpseLooter`'s pick-up (4-R3, F2). A dry run answers those sends `DryRun`, and a non-loopback server refuses them, as it does every automated send. A `/stop` withdraws the queued automation (`m5-autopilot-interrupt-queue`, closed by D145). The logic checks the switch first thing in `Tick`, so the loop is not stepped on that tick (Halted) and the run is abandoned (`Abandon("halt")`); it releases the held keys, and sends the host's own `CancelAttack` when a sequence is active, because ACE repeats a swing until it is cancelled. Resume needs the switch off and `/auto`. The hunt's attack, missile, cancel, corpse, weapon-swap and combat-mode sends are `ActionSource.Automation` (`m5-hunt-host-sends`, closed); a cancel goes as Host while the switch is on, and the hunt stops at once with `Blocked why=kill-switch`. The panel's button is `tools.json` element 0x7F000128, beside Add XP, its label follows the dial's mode, one word each to fit its 82 px: "Autopilot" when the dial is Off or Done, "Stop" when Running, "Stopping" when Stopping, and "Resume" when Interrupted or Halted (`ToolsPanel.AutopilotButtonText`, Lifestone's own literals). It is a placeholder, since retail never had an autopilot button; since D145 it types the same commands, `/stop` while the autopilot runs and `/auto` otherwise. `ToolsPanel.ToggleAutopilot` is wired by the host; unwired, the button writes a status line and a chat line and warns `TODO(m5-autopilot-key-unwired)`. The inspector (`inspector.json` element 0x7F000209, `AutomationInspector.AutopilotId`) gains a three-line text that shows the state, the last decision and the next trigger from `AutopilotStatus`: the nearest of the stop rules, the forced trip by the kills so far, and the stock line nearest its trip. `FormatAutopilot` is engine-free and tested. The contracts live in `Session.Contracts`, the namespace the retail UI architecture test keeps the retail UI to.

**The scripted hand-off (D58; since D145 typed, not pressed; `scripts/Session/AutopilotHost.cs`, `AutopilotTestHandOff`).** The gate cannot have the lead at the keys, so `--autopilot-test-interrupt-at S` and `--autopilot-test-handback-after S` (Main scene; 0 to 86,400 s; a hand-back needs an interrupt) script the person's own input. The interrupt counts S from the first Fight state of the dial's run (`AutopilotInterruptArm`); with `--autopilot-test-death`, it counts from the first Fight state of the cycle after the scripted death's (`AutopilotHostLogic.TestDeathCycle`), so it lands mid-fight (D58, 4-R10). `--autopilot-test-handback-after` is unchanged and counts from the key. At S it types `/stop` into the main chat's entry and sends it, the person's own path through the client's command table (nothing calls the logic directly); `--autopilot-test-handback-after` seconds later it types `/auto` the same way. The log reads `test-interrupt: typed /stop in the <state> state` (the scenario's stage, e.g. `fight:den-fight`) and `test-handback: typed /auto`, with a start line naming both times. A game screen with no chat window to type into logs an error and types nothing (`TODO(m5-autopilot-test-chat)`). An interrupt with no hand-back warns, and the leg then ends only at its time limit. The lead's own hand-off was done by the owner, live, in the long run (D145, below).

**The soak tallies (`Session/Autopilot`, `SoakLineFormat.cs`, `SoakLine.cs`; `m5-soak-tallies`).** `SoakTallies.Read` takes the autopilot as the soak line's source. `kills=` and `deaths=` are the ended cycles plus the cycle in progress plus the fight in progress, and `cycles=` is the ended cycles; with no autopilot they read n/a. The read allocates nothing, which a test pins. The Godot half binds the running scenario in `AutopilotLegRunner`. Closed on both halves. The `--soak-line-s` help text still names the TODO (pinned by `SoakOptionTests`), for the item-end pass to trim.

**The gate's own rows (c2; `tools/live-gate.ps1`, `tools/smoke.ps1`, `tools/lib/GateJudge.psm1`, `tools/lib/GateHost.psm1`).** `-Steps <names>` (the parameter variable is `$OnlySteps`) runs only the named steps of the plan, in a later round. The names are `build`, `recreate-<mage|melee|archer>`, `loopback-<account>`, a loopback class name, `journey-<name>` (with `-solo` for its solo phase), `frame-judge`, `live-phase-b`, `throwaway-frames`, `leak-scan`, `soak` (which implies `-Soak`) and `headless-journey-<name>` in `-Serial`. Only the named steps run: there is no build (`--no-build`), no re-create unless it is named, and the backend starts only when a named step needs it. A name not in the plan throws with the list of valid names. The round's folder is `<Tag>-r<n>` (the first free from 2, or `-Round <n>`) under the worktree's and the main checkout's `cache/gate`. `-KeepBackend` leaves the backend up after the gate; the start line and the finally say so, and the done line reads `backend=kept-up|stopped|not-started`. A backend that failed to start is still stopped. The display (`GateHost.psm1`): the preflight reads `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=display_active`, and a display that is off is woken by a 1-pixel mouse move and back. The gate then holds the display on for its run: a hidden child calls `SetThreadExecutionState` (`ES_CONTINUOUS | ES_DISPLAY_REQUIRED | ES_SYSTEM_REQUIRED`, 0x80000003), and the hold ends in the gate's finally or when the gate's process is gone. `-NoDisplayHold` skips it. Both the start line and the done line carry `display=<Before[->After]> hold=on|failed|off (<why>)`. No setting, registry key, task or service is changed. A Godot process that is not the gate's own (not a descendant of the gate's process) is named before each solo Godot step (the frame judge, a journey's solo step, the throwaway's frames, and smoke's solo wait) with its pid, start time and game arguments, secrets hidden. The step waits `-ForeignGodotSeconds` (default 120; 0 fails at once), and a process still up after that fails the row `foreign-godot: pid N (started T) still up after S s; the step was not started`. Closed: `m5-gate-step-rerun`, `m5-gate-stops-backend`, `m5-gate-slow-machine` and `m5-gate-foreign-godot`. `GateHost.psm1` is not yet in `tools/lib/StepPaths.psd1`'s no-Godot list; c1 adds it.

**The first live round's fixes (G1 and the lead; `Session/Mage/LevelFloor.cs`, `Session/Mage/DeathHandler.cs`, `tools/smoke.ps1`).** Every stamina-to-mana, heal and revitalize level pick of the buff cycle, the keeper and the harm-heal script reads `LevelFloor` (the rule of section 53: a level whose scarab the pack lacks is skipped, a WARN once, and with none payable the dance ends named and the mana regenerates). The death handler's corpse recall waits up to `DeathHandler.RecallManaSeconds` (180 s) for its mana. `smoke.ps1` imports `GateHost` after `GateJudge`, since GateJudge's `-Force` import had removed it.

**The cycle and done lines (report-only; `Session/Autopilot/AutopilotScenario.cs`).** Each cycle writes `autopilot cycle n= state=death|trip|stopped why=<name:count,...>|none kills= xp= deaths= trips= sold= bought= spent_xp= mana_wait_max_s= fizzles= mana_tries= mana_landed= storeys_visited= basement_entries= granted= places= interrupted= resumed= reconnects= s=`. The run ends with `autopilot done ok=yes|no cycles= stop= <the same fields as totals> s=`. The sources are these. Kills, xp (`xp_gained`), `mana_wait_max_s` (the maximum), the mana tries and landings, `storeys_visited` (the maximum) and `basement_entries` come from the fight station. `sold`, `bought` and `spent_xp` come from the logistics station. `deaths` comes from the death station (1 when absent). `granted` and `places` are the session's verb counter over the cycle. `fizzles` is the fight's `fizzles=` (a3). `interrupted` and `resumed` come from the cycle tally (h2), and `reconnects` from the reconnect (a2). `why=` names every trigger the cycle asked: `stop-rule`, `test-death`, `forced-trip`, `logistics-trip`, `logistics-stop`, `buff-margin`, `health-floor` and `death-unscripted`; a cycle that ends by a stop request reads `why=stop-rule:1`. The fields are report-only in the first gate and judged from the next, as spec section 5.5 says. The autopilot lane and its judges (c1) are built by the item's last slice.

**The vitae page at a death (M5 item 7 s1).** While the dial runs, the death station's exit (any transition out of Death) closes the vitae page (panel 15) if it shows, and keeps looking for 10 s (`AutopilotHostLogic.VitaeGraceSeconds`, Lifestone's own) in case the vitae message is late. It logs `autopilot: closed the vitae page after the death` once a death. A page a person opened outside a death is left alone. The close is the shell's `SetPanelVisibility(15, false)`, wired by the host in `WireShell`. The page itself (`GameVitaePage`, `RecvNotice_VitaeChanged`, 11.6096 0x004A7AA0) is kept as it is.

**The TODO names.** Opened in item 4 and still open, all loud guards rather than gaps in the feature: `m5-autopilot-fight-stop` (a den-fight scenario that takes no external stop; never with `FightScenario`), `m5-autopilot-reason` (an unknown fight stop reason), `m5-autopilot-key-unwired` (the panel's button in a host that does not wire the dial), and `m5-autopilot-test-key` (the control drill's Move Forward press with no key Godot can press). `m5-death-rearm-standalone` stays open for the standalone death leg. Closed in item 4: `m5-autopilot-upkeep`, `m5-autopilot-fizzles` and `m5-autopilot-death-unscripted` (a3); `m5-autopilot-cycle-seed` (wired at the h3 merge through a3's `AutopilotSeed`); `m5-autopilot-checkpoint` and `m5-autopilot-reconnect` (h3); `m5-reconnect-auto` (a2); `m5-hunt-host-sends` (h2); `m5-logistics-step-off` (a3); `m5-soak-tallies` (h1); the four gate names (c2); and the code half of `m5-death-rebuffed-item-lines` (a3; its judge is c1's). Still open after the fixes, named here for the item's end: `m5-autopilot-interrupt-queue` (an interrupt does not cancel the autopilot's queued actions; only the kill switch withdraws them, and a per-source cancel on the Action API would close it; 4-R3's second half), `m5-autopilot-ui-reconnect` (whether the disconnected screen returns to the game after the person's reconnect; 4-R8's live half), `m5-autopilot-start` (the panel button's text does not follow the dial, and the play host has no unattended start; 4-R17, item 7), `m5-death-tally-resume` (the death tally's restart zeroes its count) and `m5-autopilot-vitae-panel` (the vitae panel stays open over the review frames after a death). Closed by the fixes: the `--buff-auto on` double cast (4-R4: the driver stands down while the dial owns the buffs) and the buff margin's default (the host's buff settings reach the autopilot through `AutopilotOptions.Buff`). Since item 7: `m5-autopilot-interrupt-queue` is closed by D145 (a `/stop` cancels the queue), `m5-autopilot-start` and `m5-autopilot-vitae-panel` by item 7 s1; the rest stay as `docs/milestones/m5-plan.md` section 9 places them.
**The dial since D145: `/stop` and `/auto` (M5 item 7 P7m; the owner's D145, `docs/milestones/m5-plan.md` section 7; `Session/Contracts/HumanKeyLock.cs`, `Session/Autopilot/AutopilotHostLogic.cs`, `RetailUi/Chat/ChatCommands.cs`).** The two commands are client commands: the chat handles them and sends nothing to the server (`ChatCommandsTests`; ACE has no such commands). Num Lock and D98's hand-off by a movement key are retired. *The lock.* While the autopilot runs, `HumanKeyLock` (one on each session's `MoveIntents`) makes the whole movement map do nothing (forward, back, the turns, the strafes, jump, the run key and the emote actions): each press is logged, and the chat shows "The autopilot has the controls: type /stop to take over." at most once every 5 s. The camera, the mouse, the chat, the windows and inspection are other input paths and stay free. A key held when the autopilot started is let go, and the release of a swallowed press is swallowed too (`MovementKeyLockTests`). */stop.* While the autopilot runs or stops, `StopHere` stops him where he stands, with no recall and no log off: the run in flight is dropped (its fight's kills folded in; no done line), every queued automation action is cancelled, every held key is released, a swing in progress is called off, and the movement keys are unlocked; `interrupted=` counts. A check a frame later reads that nothing is held and the keys are unlocked, and names anything it had to release again. In any other mode it answers that nothing runs. Any stop the bot makes on its own (a stop rule, an error, `stop=failed`, a give-up) unlocks the keys the same way, with a chat line giving the reason: a stopped bot never locks the person out. */auto.* From the person's stop (or from off or done) it starts the audit, then resumes. The audit (`AutopilotScenario.FullAudit.cs`, `StartupAudit`; P7n) reads every state a fight needs from the live state, never from what the autopilot last did: the actions the stopped run left, alive or dead, portal space, the place and how far it is from the den's arrival, the hostiles and an attacker, an open vendor, corpse or trade, the kit and whether a trip is due, the packs, the worn set, the caster held and whether it is the gear plan's pick, the stance, each buff line with its time left and the Spirit Drinker aura, the vitals, the load, and the loot queue. Each gap gets its named fix through the recovery table (`RecoveryTable`, P7f, D141 part 4: no caster held wields the best, no free slot drops an empty stone beyond the keep, too little mana runs the keeper, busy waits 2 s; one retry each), checked after the act; one log line a check (`autopilot audit <check>: <finding>; fix=<fix>`), a summary line, and one plain line in the chat window (`ChatNotice`). With a hostile on the mage the slow fixes wait and the fight fights back first (D88). Each damage spell's hit line now names the caster and the Spirit Drinker aura (`autopilot hit: ...`). A reconnect's audit runs the same way (the reason `reconnect`). *The proof.* The control drill (`--autopilot-control-drill=DIR`, `tools/soak.ps1 -ControlDrill`; the play host with `--autopilot-start`; `ControlDrill`) drives it through the game's own input: W held mid-fight does nothing and the notice shows; /stop mid-fight stops him in place and W then moves him; from a bad state (walked off the spot, peace, the caster moved into a side pack) /auto puts each right, says so and resumes; on a town trip /stop mid-walk and /auto after it (steps a to d3, each a `control drill: step=... verdict=` line and a frame in DIR). It passed on 2026-10-30 (`docs/milestones/m5-plan.md` section 8), and the owner took the controls once in the long run, /stop at 21:10:26 mid-fight and /auto at 21:17:35 (`interrupted=1 resumed=1`). Open: `m6-trade-window` (the client keeps no trade window, so the audit's trade check says so), `m6-resume-loot-queue` (the stopped fight's loot queue is not carried over), `m5-audit-*`, and the commands' robustness (`m5-7-chat-controls-robust`, whose tag in the code is `m6-chat-controls-robust`; the owner: more robust commands next milestone).

## 55. A solid world: the server objects' physics, the creatures' transitions, doors and the lifestone landing (M5 item 5; D45, D70-D73; DoD 10)

Item 5 makes the world solid: server objects (lifestones, doors, NPCs, creatures, other players) collide with the avatars as in retail, so two creatures do not stand on one spot and a door stops a walker. Before it the client gave server objects no collision (docs/physics.md section 10, "Other objects"). The decisions are D45 and D70 to D73 in `docs/milestones/m5-plan.md` section 7, the rows are in its section 4.7, and the lead's numbers are in its section 8.

**The host (D70, D73; `src/Lifestone.Client/Physics/ServerObjectPhysicsHost.cs`, `ObjectTableFeed.cs`).** The host keeps one `PhysicsObj` per server object whose Setup has collision (a physics BSP, a cylinder-sphere or a sphere), in the player's `PhysicsWorld`. The game feeds it from the avatar set (`AvatarSet.ServerObjects`: create, move, state, parent, pick up, delete, and each body's position after its step); headless, `ObjectTableFeed` feeds it from the session's object table and skips the self object. A create enters with retail's sliding `enter_world` (`CPhysicsObj::enter_world`, 11.6096 0x00516C70) against everything but the player, and the player's body then takes the same sliding placement (`OfflineWalker.ReplaceAfterObjectsEntered`, each frame): the player is moved out of the object, not the object out of the player (D73). A server move of an object already in the world is `ForceIntoCell` (0x00516160). A create that fits nowhere is forced in at the server's position and counted (`TODO(m5-create-forced)`). An object in a landblock not yet attached waits in the lost cell; when the attach's placement fails it is forced in at the server's position (`TODO(m5-reenter-forced)`). Hidden turns collision off (`set_hidden`); Ethereal goes through `SetEthereal` and its overlap check, retried by each host tick. The weenie takes IsCreature from the ItemType and IsPlayer from BF_PLAYER; the avatar create now carries its ObjectDescriptionFlags, so `TODO(m5-object-description-flags)` fires only for a feed that gives none. IsPK, IsPKLite and IsImpenetrable stay false (`TODO(m5-object-pk-status)`).

**The transitions (D45, D71, D72; `PhysicsObj.TryTransitionTo`, `src/Lifestone.Client/Avatars/AvatarBody.cs`).** A stepped avatar's transition runs retail's `CPhysicsObj::transition` (11.6096 0x005138C0) through its host object and takes the result with `SetPositionInternal(transit)` (0x00515E30): cell, frame, contact, collisions and shadows, indoors and out. A body with no valid position only turns. Its contact is the server's Grounded flag, carried into the transition so a grounded creature steps down onto the floor. Retail takes the transition's contact for a remote object (`CPhysicsObj::MoveOrTeleport`, 11.6096 0x00516E30), but that needs the gravity D24 leaves out, so the carried flag is a named deviation (`TODO(m5-remote-contact)`). Projectiles keep the path without collision (`TODO(m5-missile-transitions)`; retail runs a missile's transition with PathClipped). A teleport, a far position or a blip is retail's sliding `SetPositionSimple` (11.6096 0x00516DB0) through the host, so a creature is not placed inside another object or the player.

**Cost (D45; the 50 ms frame rule).** Twenty tuskers around a standing player, ten walking across one another and ten stuck to the player by swings, 600 frames at 60 Hz: transitions 0.67 to 0.83 ms a frame on average, 1.9 to 10.3 ms at most (first frames and contention). Sixty humans walking into one pile on flat ground: the whole advance 2.1 ms a frame on average, transitions at most 20 ms in the pile's worst frame. A standing or walking crowd allocates nothing per advance once warm (`ServerObjectTransitionAllocationTests`); a crowd entering cells it has not met grows their shadow lists, as any moving object does. Two tuskers stuck to a player from 2.2 m apart now end 2.03 m apart (without transitions 1.27 m, overlapping). A refused blip costs 0.025 ms, and a blocked creature is refused every frame: retail's `InterpolationManager::UseTime` (11.6096 0x00556B40) asks again each frame and nothing rate-limits it, so a den crowd costs about 0.025 ms per blocked creature a frame. Kept and named. `--server-object-transitions=off` (`OptionCatalog.Avatars`) is a diagnostics switch: avatars take the new frame with no collision transition, as under D24; it is not a glitch flag.

**Indoors (5-R4; the den, landblock 0x0105).** A tusker through a portal (0x01050375 to 0x01050382) ends in the new cell with its host object there, and no transition is blocked. Down a 31-degree ramp (0x010502DA to 0x01050262, 1.42 m) it ends on the floor at z -5.989, 6 mm off the walker's, with its contact set from the transition and no hover. Two tuskers stuck to a player in 0x01050381 end 2.11 m apart; without transitions 0.00 m. No fix was forced indoors.

**Doors (d1, d2, F2; `src/Lifestone.Client/Movement/Wayfinding/DoorCrossings.cs`, `NavRunner.cs`).** A closed door on a route is a stop, not a wall. The runner stops 1.3 m short of the sill (`DoorStandOffMetres`), Uses the door (ACE's `Door.Open` sets Ethereal at once, and `IsBusy` ignores a Use: ACE `Door.cs:90` and `:111-130` @95ef426d), waits and walks on. It retries the Use once at 2 s (`DoorRetrySeconds`); a door still shut at 5 s (`DoorOpenSeconds`) fails the leg `door-closed-timeout` (Lifestone's own numbers, not retail's). Each door step is logged `purpose=door`. `door_opened=yes|no` counts only a door the walk itself Used (its `SessionDoorFeed.Use` ledger) that then gained Ethereal while the watch was live; ACE opening a door, a door found open and the vendor's own Use never count (F2, 5-R2). The hosts build the doors' extents once from the dats (`DoorCrossings.SetupExtents`: the Setup's selection sphere, not the 1 m fallback); the fight walks and the death leg's walk do the same (`TODO(door-extent-host)`, closed by d2 and F2). The door rules are in docs/wayfinding.md, "Doors on the routes". A door the plan did not know is found on the way. The runner looks for the doors of the rest of its path again whenever the door feed's set of doors changes (a door enters the object table), whenever the path source's tiles or room graph change (a doorway becomes placeable), and at the first stuck rung before any recovery move. A closed door found that way becomes an ordinary door step (the same stand-off and Use) and is logged once as 'the route crosses a door (found after the plan)'. The planner's routes and the door Use itself are unchanged. Cause (gate round, resupply leg to the mage trainer's tower): the first plan was made 150 m away, before the door was in the object table, so the leg pressed on the shut door for four rungs (about 20 s) until the stuck replan found it.

**The lifestone landing (l1, D73; `LifestonePlacementTests`, `StepOffLifestone`).** The lifestone (Setup 0x020002EE) collides as one sphere, centre z 1.504, radius 1.504. A recall's arrival is a sliding placement in retail's order, so the body ends 2.22 m north of the stone's centre, where ACE's where-am-I check puts it, to the centimetre. A lifestone created after the teleport, with the body already at its centre, used to hold the body stuck; the same sliding placement now moves the body out (`TODO(m5-create-overlap)` names the case; it is logged as "a server object appeared on the body" headless and "on the player" in the game). The recall-home station's step-off walks only when the body is inside the stone's collision (`stepped_off=yes`, the named fallback); a clear body expects `stepped_off=no`. The body comes from the host's `HuntFollow`; in the game `GodotJourneyRunnerHost.HuntFollow` returns null, so the server's position stands in (`TODO(m5-godot-body-position)`). Other towns use the host's stone object, and a host without one fails named (`TODO(m5-lifestone-point-other-towns)`: walked live only at Holtburg).

**The planner judges the fixed world (F3, the live gate's round 1).** Once the host put server objects in the player's world, the route checker's probes met them: the ground probe stepping down at a closed door's sill landed on the door's physics BSP ("no walkable ground", Renald's and Cindrue's walks), and the placement probe met creatures' and NPCs' cylinders (the Archer's seek, the mage's den walks). `RouteConfirmer` and `RouteLibrary` now probe with `PhysicsObj.FixedWorldOnly` (`ObjectInfo.FixedWorldOnly`, `PhysicsWorld.TryFixedGroundZ`): terrain, cells, buildings and landblock statics only, since the runner meets server objects while it walks (a closed door as a door step, creatures through the creature layer, anything else through the stuck ladder). This is Lifestone's planner, not retail; the player's, the avatars' and the server objects' own transitions are unchanged. `ServerObjectRouteTests` walks the whole chain offline: from the lifestone through Renald's closed door (one Use, the door Ethereal 0.6 s later, 58.3 m) and past an NPC and a tusker on the path.

**The blips and retail's rate (F4, F5, F7, D74; the live gate's round 2).** The jump detector's line names what held a creature back before a blip (`short_transits= blocked_by=<player|creature:0x..|object:0x..|env:0x..|step|none> behind_m=`), `AvatarStats.BlipCauses` counts blips by cause, and a debug line (`TODO(m5-creature-held-back)`, rate-limited) reports a creature held ten frames. Round 2's den fight logged 60 RootJumps in 75 s, mostly `placed=Blip blocked_by=creature`: retail's `InterpolationManager::adjust_offset` (11.6096 0x00556950) counts `frame_counter` once per object update behind `CPhysics::UseTime`'s 30 Hz gate (0x0050A420), while Lifestone steps avatars every drawn frame, so the 5-frame progress check ran eight times too often. It now counts a frame per 1/30 s of quantum (`UpdateInterval`, D74); the drawn step stays per frame. Round 3: 62 in 108 s, 36 of the 40 logged `blocked_by=creature` (named `m5-den-creature-blips`). F5 found F4's "refused every frame" blips came from a test scene inside a Holtburg building's hull, where retail's placement fits nothing; on open ground a blip lands or slides beside what holds its spot.

**A server MoveTo the server abandons (F6, the live gate's round 2).** ACE ends a use's walk-to after 15 s (`Player_Move.cs` 16, 119-124 @95ef426d) and sends UseDone 0 but nothing that ends the client's MoveTo, and retail's `MoveToManager` counts `fail_progress_count` but reads it nowhere, so the client held the MoveTo and the runner yielded 570 s. The player controller now cancels a server MoveTo still running after 15 s (`cancel_moveto`, 0x00510CC0, ActionCancelled), logged as `TODO(m5-moveto-stuck)` with the stall's fields, and the runner's yield ends with it, named.

**Open after item 5:** `TODO(m5-remote-contact)`, `TODO(m5-missile-transitions)`, `TODO(m5-create-forced)`, `TODO(m5-reenter-forced)`, `TODO(m5-object-pk-status)`, `TODO(m5-godot-body-position)`, `TODO(m5-lifestone-point-other-towns)`, `TODO(door-locked-unknown)`, `TODO(door-locked-behind)`, `TODO(door-locked-replan)`, and the statics half of `TODO(nav-sight-no-physics)`. Tests: `ServerObjectPhysicsTests`, `ServerObjectTransitionTests`, `ServerObjectIndoorTests`, `ObjectTableFeedTests`, `DoorHostTests`, `LifestonePlacementTests`, `J3bStationTests`, `AvatarJumpWatchTests`.

## 56. The level-80 mage and the fight's toolbox (M5 item 7; D86-D90; DoD 13)

**The traditional level-80 build (D87).** The soak's BSD Mage is built at level 80 by `--scenario=mage-build --fresh-character --mage-level=80`. `--mage-level=L` takes 50 to 126 and defaults to 126. Below 126 the build is the traditional mage (`MageTemplate.BuildFor`); the 126 build is unchanged, so the live gate still re-creates its mage at 126. The traditional mage has no Melee Defense, so it soaks the damage. War Magic and Life Magic are specialised at creation (28 and 20: 48 of the heritage's 52 skill credits, 4 spare). Creature Enchantment, Item Enchantment and Mana Conversion are trained afterwards: 22 of the 32 credits that level 80 gives (4 spare and 28 from the levels), 10 left. Level 32 is the first level that pays for all three later skills. Melee Defense's experience share goes to the other stats, and the spend of the experience follows the dance shares (`MageShares.Dance`).

The kit wears a Faran Robe with Hood (wcid 5851, Value 50, burden 200, 68 pyreals from Ecutha the Tailor, now a profile vendor) and Leather Gauntlets (wcid 56, armour level 130, burden 270, 1,485 from Shopkeeper Renald the Elder, already a profile vendor). The wand (wcid 2472) is unchanged. The robe covers the head as well as the body (its ValidLocations 0x7F01 adds the head to the old robe's coverage), and the gauntlets cover the hands. No hooded robe is named for the War school, so the hood won over the school. Holtburg sells no cloth gloves, and the Leather Gauntlets are the lightest on offer. Neither piece has a wield requirement. The old robe's two item spells (ids 1451 and 635) are lost. The buff cycle now enchants the gauntlets too, one more piece per cycle. The robe and the gloves cost 1,553 against 1,512 for the old robe. The kit's worn lines go from one to two (`gear worn=2 kit=12/12`, was `worn=1 kit=11/11`), and the whole kit's load goes from 9,980 to 10,250 of 19,650 (52%, under `burden_trip_percent` 90).

The supplies for a day at VI (F3, F10): Pyreal Scarabs keep 400 (four stacks, about 348 a day), Diamond Scarabs keep 6 (carried, since Holtburg sells none; trip under 2, stop under 2), Prismatic Tapers keep 1,000 bought on trips (a trip about every 1.2 hours), and the purse is 550,000 (five Trade Note 50,000, about 26 hours at 20,700 pyreals an hour). The kit's load is about 10,678 of 15,750, under the 90% line of 14,175. The D87 row's earlier figures (300 scarabs, a 400,000 purse) are superseded by these.

**The vitals in the fight (D88, F5, F7, F15).** The rule is that the mana may run down to nearly empty and the stamina is kept near full, so mana costs time rather than stock. The bolt runs the mana down to its try floor. Then Stamina to Mana Self is pressed until a Mana Conversion roll lands (no long rest and no mana potion while stamina is there). Then Revitalize Self refills the stamina to `--fight-stamina-full` (default 90; 0 is off), and then the bolts come again. Between kills a low stamina is refilled first.

`ManaOrder.Decide` takes a third input, the Revitalize Self levels the caller may cast and the pack pays for (`RevitalizeLevel`), and two new steps, `Revitalize` and `StaminaPotion`. With the share set, whenever the mana is at or over the bolt's target and the stamina is under the share, the order returns the highest level whose base mana the mana pays with the bolt's target still in hand (`RevitalizeLevelOf`). A Stamina Potion comes only when no level is payable at all. The keeper and the buff cycle never set the share, so their order is unchanged. The levels are filtered by the level floor (`LevelFloor`, the same rule as the buff cycle's): a level whose foci-formula components the pack lacks is passed over and named once ("Revitalize Self: level VII needs ...; skipped"), and the Stamina to Mana Self levels go through the same floor. At level 80 with Pyreal Scarabs the refill is Revitalize Self VI and the transfer VI; at 126 with Platinum it is VII. The levels are read at the fight's start, when the level's signature changes, and after every transfer or refill cast. They are never read while the fight waits, which allocates nothing.

Between kills (`BoltNeedsMana`), when the stamina is under the share and the mana pays one Revitalize plus the bolt's target, the mana phase is entered for a refill. It opens no mana wait, since the bolt is not short of mana. Each refill cast is one log line, `mana: revitalize: <spell> at stamina x of y, mana m of n`. The cast goes through `CastAttempt` as Automation with `CastRules.Utility(3)`, and its chat proof is the line ACE writes ("You cast <spell> and restore N points of your Stamina"). A cast that does not land, or six casts in a row that leave the stamina under the share, set the refill aside for 30 s (`FightRefill.AsideSeconds`). While it is aside the order is asked for no refill at all, so a potion does not stand in for a spell that merely failed. With the stamina at or over its floor and no level landing the target with the mage's funds, the mage still presses the transfer (no Mana Potion, no rest in peace). A dance whose presses were all refused or fizzled is pressed again at once while the stamina is over the floor (log: "so the presses go on"), instead of being set aside for 60 s. The old fallbacks (potion, rest) remain under the floor and with `--fight-stamina-full 0`.

The floors (`ManaOrder.ManaFloorOf`, `StaminaFloorOf`; `FightScenario.ComputeFloors`, logged as "mana: D88 floors: ..."): the mana floor is the bolt's own try floor plus the least mana at which a Stamina to Mana press lands at least a quarter of the time, plus the same for one Revitalize Self press, each never under 5. The stamina floor is the least stamina at which a landed transfer gives at least the transfer's base plus the Revitalize's base. For a level-80 mage (Mana Conversion 258, stamina 275; Stamina to Mana Self VI base 60 power 250, Revitalize Self VI base 70 power 250, Flame Bolt VI base 30 power 250, from the dat's spell table) the mana floor is 15 (5 + 5 + 5) and the stamina floor 174 of 275 (a landing gives 130 = 60 + 70). A landing takes 1.5 presses at 15 mana, 2.8 at 5 and 10.2 at 1 (`RefillFloorsDatTests`).

**The drain when health runs low (D88's health rule, F7).** With no Melee Defense the mage takes the damage, so the fight holds two health lines, both options and Lifestone's own numbers: `--fight-drain-under` (default 50, the health share under which the rule starts) and `--fight-drain-until` (default 75, the safe line at which the bolts begin again; never under the first). Between them the rule holds, a hysteresis. Under the first line, with a living target in line and range, the fight casts Drain Health Other instead of the bolt. With no target in reach it casts Heal Self at the highest level the pack's scarabs pay (the keeper's heal). Before F7 the fight did nothing about its own health: it bolted whatever it was, the vitals keeper healed between fights (VitalsKeeper, VitalsMeans), and HarmHealScript is only the gate's test script. The upkeep line sits under it: the drain starts under 50%, and the upkeep stops the fight under 30% (`AutopilotOptions.HealthFloorPercent`: 50 with the drain rule off, `--fight-drain-under=0`; otherwise 60% of `--fight-drain-under` and at most 30, `AutopilotScenario.HealthFloorWithDrainPercent`).

Drain Health Other is a Life Magic transfer: ids 1237 to 1242 (I to VI, powers 38, 88, 138, 188, 238, 288, base mana 10 to 60), 2328 (VII, Vitality Siphon, power 325, base mana 60), and 4643 (the incantation, power 400, base mana 68; it has no transfer row in the pinned world SQL, so the picker does not offer it, `TODO(m5-drain-viii-row)`). The level-80 pack pays VI with the Pyreal Scarab and the level-126 pack pays VII with the Platinum Scarab (`LevelFloor` kept). The line is one of `ArenaProfile.Extras` (`ProfileRole.LifeDrain`), so the build learns it with the others. ACE's rule (`WorldObject_Magic.cs` @95ef426d, `HandleCastSpell_Transfer` 697-760): the target loses round(current health x proportion), cut to the transfer cap (721-727); the caster gains round(taken x (1 - loss)) (732), cut to its missing health, and itself cut to the cap (737-749). The rows from the pinned world SQL v0.9.296 (proportion, loss, cap) are I 0.25, -1, 60; II 0.25, -0.6, 80; III 0.25, -0.33, 100; IV 0.25, 0, 100; V 0.3, 0, 120; VI 0.4, 0.25, 150; VII 0.5, 0.5, 180. On a 400-health tusker VI heals 112, VII 90 and V 120, so the highest level is not the best one. The resist is `MagicDefenseCheck` (104-113), the 0.03 logistic of the caster's Life Magic against the target's Magic Defense. The chat proof is "You gain N points of health due to casting <spell> on <name>" (853).

The level is chosen at the moment of casting by `DrainPicker` (`Session/Vitals`, engine-free and allocation-free, so the harm-heal and the death handler can read it later). Every input is read live: the mage's Life Magic (`GearCasts.TryCurrentSkill`, buffs up), its Mana Conversion (`ManaOrder.TryManaConversion`), its health and mana, the target's health (the last health update, timed against the ground profile's max_health) and the distance. The score is the chance times the heal if it lands, cut to the health missing so that overshoot earns nothing, per second of casting. Of the levels within 10% of the best, the cheapest in mana is taken. When even the best expected heal is under 3% of the mage's maximum health, the answer is no drain and the bolt goes (a nearly dead tusker). The chance is the cast chance (`GearCasts.Chance`, 0.07 factor) times the target's pass chance (0.03 logistic). The targets' measures are ground profile values: `black-spawn-den-aluvian.json` gives the Tusker Slave max_health 400 and magic_defence 200, and the Tusker Guard 260 and 225 (the pinned world SQL's `weenie_properties_attribute_2nd` type 1 current_Level and `weenie_properties_skill` type 15 init_Level, without ACE's formula share for the skill: `TODO(m5-drain-magic-defence-formula)`). A target the profile gives none for is assumed large with an even resist, named once (`TODO(m5-drain-target-stats)`). Left named: `TODO(m5-drain-resistance-mods)` (the drain and boost resistance mods are taken as 1) and `TODO(m5-drain-cast-time)` (the cast time is taken as equal across the line). Each pick is one log line, `health: drain pick: <spell>, expected heal, chance, mana, why`, and each drain one line, `health: drain: <spell> on 0x<guid> at health x of y`. The verdict gains `drains= heals= health_min=` (report-only).

The mana floor holds a drain's cost too. Each offered level has the bolt's kind of floor, `min(ceiling, ManaOrder.FloorOf(ceiling, power, ceiling, Mana Conversion read now) + the D88 reserve for one Stamina to Mana press and one Revitalize Self press)`, the ceiling being the level's base mana (VI 60, VII 60; Heal Self VI 70). A level is offered only when the mana held is at or over its floor. With none paid, the fight casts the bolt it would have cast, since its own order has already funded it. The stamina order of D88 runs first (the transfer, then the Revitalize Self refill, then the fight): the drain is a step of `Choose` after `BoltNeedsMana`. Safety: a cast that did not land (not a resist), or 8 casts in a row that did not end the low state, set the rule aside for 30 s, named.

**Self-defence (F4, F15).** A living creature that attacks the player becomes the fight's target at once, before the profile's targets, whatever the profile says of it (avoided, no-go, outside the level band). It stays the target until it is dead, gone from the object table, or has not attacked for `--fight-defend-forget-s` (default 20 s; 0 is off; 0 to 600, Lifestone's own bound). The fight hears the attack on the session bus: the defender and evasion notices (GameEvent 0x01B2 and 0x01B4, the `CombatEvent`s `DefenseHit` and `DefenseEvaded`) and the Magic-typed chat lines a creature's spell writes to the player (ACE @95ef426d `SpellProjectile.cs` 810-845, `WorldObject_Magic.cs` 199 and 1429). Every notice names the attacker and none gives its guid. ACE's CurrentAttacker private update carries the guid and is not decoded (`TODO(m3-autotarget-attacker-id)`), so the attacker is the nearest living creature of that name in the object table (`NearestLivingCreatureNamed`). A name with none is logged once as `TODO(m5-defend-attacker-unseen)`. The pick asks `DefenceStep` first: the nearest attacker in cast range with a clear line is the target, wherever it stands, a no-go cell included. With none, the nearest attacker decides. In a no-go cell the fight holds, since nothing is walked into a no-go cell (logged once: "stands in the no-go cell ... holding"). Otherwise it walks, and the walk phase drops that walk the frame an attacker is in range with a clear line. The walk has no leg toward a given creature, so it is the tour's next leg (`TODO(m5-defend-walk-to)`). The hold has its own bound (F15): `--fight-defend-hold-s` (default 30; 0 to 600; 0 is no bound). After it, the attacker is dropped for the profile's targets, logged once, and not fought back at while it stays in the table. Heal Self comes before a hold and before a walk. Each attacker is logged once: "self-defence: <name> 0x<guid> attacked (<how>); fighting it before the targets". Its kill counts in `kills=` and `target_kills=`, reaches the respawn schedule (`GroundTour.Killed`), and the verdict gains `defended=<n>` after `target_kills=` (report-only). Not covered: the loot step runs before the pick, so an attacker is fought after the corpse in hand is done; a bolt in flight and the mana wait finish first. This closes the first rehearsal's finding, in which a Virindi attacked the mage and the mage kept to the tuskers.

**The one live picker (D90, F10).** The fight's toolbox is one picker, `FightPicker` (engine-free and allocation-free per decision), fed by a snapshot of the fight at that moment (`FightScenario.Picker.cs`: the mage's vitals and skills, the target's health and range, the scarabs in the pack). At each decision with a target picked and the bolt's mana order passed, `Choose` calls `PickerDecide`. D88's drain-health floor comes first (F7's `DrainPicker` picks the level). Then the picker scores the bolt; F9's best war option (`WarSpellPicker.Choose`, scaled by its seconds a kill over the bolt's); Drain Health Other VI or VII as an attack; and every debuff of `FightDebuffs` at the plan cap's level (VI at 80), each in expected all-in seconds to finish the target. The least wins, and a tie keeps the bolt. A debuff's or a drain's score is the bolt's minus its `FightDebuffs` net seconds. The profile's `opener` names the debuffs a target allows (the hint) and stays the default when the picker cannot score (no target max_health, no bolt row, or a plan cap under VI: `TODO(f10-debuff-levels)`). F8's `TryBeginOpener` casts the vulnerability the picker chooses. Two measured rates feed the score (`FightRates`), both exponentially weighted means with weight 0.2. The dance's seconds a mana point is seeded at 0.09; then each landed dance adds its seconds plus the Revitalize Self seconds since the last dance, over the net mana. The heal's seconds a health point is seeded at each decision from Drain Health Other VI's yield on the target at the live Life Magic (cast seconds plus its mana at the dance's rate, over the expected heal); then each landed drain or heal adds its seconds plus its mana at the dance's rate, over the health gained. The incoming damage rate is the tally's damage taken over a 20 s time constant, fight-wide (`TODO(f10-incoming-per-attacker)`: the notices name the attacker and the tally sums them).

The log writes one line when the pick changes, never per repeated cast: `pick: <line> <level> on <name> 0x<guid> | all-in <s> s vs bolt <s> s | hit <p>% | mana <m> | why <reason>`. Each debuff that does not win gets one line once: `pick: <spell> never wins: <ACE reason>` for Imperil, Exhaustion, Vulnerability and Defenselessness, and `pick: <spell> does not win here: it saves x s and costs y s (net z s) ...` for the rest. The attribute debuffs carry `TODO(f10-target-attributes)`, since no source gives the fight their attributes. The stamina drain's line reads `pick: Drain Stamina Other VI on <name> 0x<guid> | stamina a a second vs Revitalize Self b | hit p% | mana m | why the stamina floor: the drain refills faster`. The verdict's `picks=` counts the casts per spell the picker chose, as `picks=Flame_Bolt_VI:40,Fire_Vulnerability_Other_VI:12,Drain_Health_Other_VI:3` (spaces as underscores, apostrophes dropped). It sits after `openers=`, in place of F9's shape counts, and is report-only in its first gate.

**The opener, the war spells, the debuffs and the stamina drain (D89, D90; F8, F9, F10, F11).** A target's optional `opener` (in `ArenaProfile.Extras`) names the debuff the fight casts first. The den's Tusker Slave opens with Fire Vulnerability Other, and a Tusker Guard gets bolts only (the owner's choice). ACE multiplies a war bolt's damage by the fire vulnerability (SpellProjectile.cs:526-557, EnchantmentManager.cs:925-939 at 95ef426d): x2.5 at VI (1108) and x2.85 at VII (2170). Imperil does nothing to a bolt. The opener's level is the highest the ground's plan cap, the spellbook, the cast chance and the pack allow (VI at 80, VII at 126 with Platinum). A resisted or fizzled cast is pressed again up to `--fight-opener-tries` (default 3, 0 to 10, Lifestone's own; 0 is off). A target under half its health skips it. The opener burns the same scarab and tapers a Flame Bolt of its level does, about one cast a Slave and up to three on resists. The verdict field `openers=sent:n,landed:n,resisted:n,skipped:n` is report-only in its first gate. The opener's ids are 21, 1104-1108, 2170 (Inferno's Gift) and 4481.

The war spell is chosen live by `WarSpellPicker` (`Session/Mage/WarSpellPicker.cs`; the glue is `FightScenario.WarPick.cs`). For each option it counts the casts the cast target needs at the option's mean hit, each cast costing its gesture, 0.4 s of overhead and 0.09 s a mana point of the dance (registered estimates the rehearsal measures), over the kills the casts make. The least seconds a kill wins, and a tie keeps the bolt. One `war pick:` line is logged when the pick changes, and `picks=` counts the choices. At level 80 the data keeps Flame Bolt VI for a lone full-health Slave or Guard. For a Slave the all-in seconds a kill are 10.1 for the bolt, 14.8 for the streak, 13.1 for the volley, 22.7 for the blast, 10.0 for the arc (with no way to test its lob) and 25.8 for the ring. The picks that pay are two. A streak pays for a target one streak finishes (a hit of about 62 against the hit points left, at any range in reach). Cassius' Ring of Fire pays for three or more tuskers within 3 m of the mage: 9.4 s a kill against 10.1 on three Slaves, 7.1 on four, and 6.3 against 6.8 on three Guards. It never pays for two (13.8 against 10.1). The ring is built in, and its scarabs are carried since F10 (a Diamond Scarab line, keep 6, trip and stop under 2; about one lost every 450 casts). It needs 80 mana in hand. Volleys and blasts never win on the den's numbers. Arcs are left out: a lob does not track a moving target (WorldObject_Magic.cs:1728), and no arc-line sweep exists (`TODO(m5-war-pick-arc-line)`). Walls (Slithering Flames, 1841: 10 projectiles in a line, 80 mana, about 63 a projectile) pay only on tuskers standing in a line in front of the mage. The spell is in no profile line and is not learned, so the walls stay numbers only. The war line's mean hits and mana at War Magic 361 and Mana Conversion 258 are: bolt 146.5 and 6.59 mana, streak 62.2 and 13.10, volley and blast 59.0 a projectile and 7.83, ring 75.5 a projectile and 10.88. The damage rows are ACE world SQL v0.9.296 (table `spell`) and the projectile speeds of the projectile weenies (bolt 15 m/s, streak 45, arc 40, ring 2); no code is ported.

The debuffs are picked by the same scale, and most never win. The data per debuff is for a level-80 mage against a Tusker Slave (Light Weapons 287, Magic Defense 219, health 400, body damage 60), with illustrative rates: the target hitting for 25 a second, a heal of 0.04 s a health point, and the dance at 0.09 s a mana point. The net seconds at VI, where positive pays: Weakness Other -5.5 at 400 health (it cuts the damage taken by 14%, so it pays only in a fight longer than about 20 s, +13.6 at 4000 health). Clumsiness Other -6.8. Light Weapon Ineptitude Other -6.7. Slowness Other -6.2 (the swing takes 0.94 s instead of 0.8 s; `TODO(f11-swing-seconds)`: the swing length is not in the data read, so a 1.2 s stand-in is used and a debuff reports unscored at 0). Bafflement Other, which is the Focus debuff, -6.8. Feeblemind Other, which is the Self debuff, -6.8. Frailty Other -6.0 (a Slave at full health loses 17 of its 400). Magic Yield Other -6.6. Harm Other -4.7 (a third of a bolt's damage for 15.6 mana, against 6.6 for the bolt). Drain Stamina Other -2.5 at 0.05 s a stamina point. Imperil Other, Vulnerability Other (Melee Defense), Defenselessness Other (Missile Defense) and Exhaustion Other have no value at all: ACE's reasons are constant strings in `FightDebuffs`. Imperil lowers an armour level that a war bolt does not read; Vulnerability and Defenselessness act on the target's defence against melee and missiles, which a mage does not roll against; Exhaustion stops only a monster's stamina regeneration. Fire Vulnerability Other pays +3.65 on a 400-health Slave (+3.87 at VII): 10.46 s of fight over a Slave, more the more health is left (1.3 s at 100, 5.2 at 200, 10.5 at 400, 41.8 at 1600). It does not pay on a target a bolt kills anyway, and it breaks even near 260 health. Drain Health Other pays +4.0 (VII +4.2), but only while the mage is hurt: at full health ACE scales the cast to nothing. The picker's verdicts follow: a full-health Slave gets the vulnerability and then bolts (a landed vulnerability makes the next evaluation "already on"); a Guard gets bolts, since its entry names no opener; a mage at 60%, over D88's floor, casts Drain Health Other VI as an attack against a healthy Slave; and the rest never win. The corrected names are these: ACE's Bafflement is the Focus debuff and its Feeblemind the Self debuff, Vulnerability is Melee Defense, Defenselessness is Missile Defense, and Magic Yield is Magic Defense. A debuff's cast is paid under the target's blows, so a debuff that cuts the target's damage needs a long fight, while one that changes the mage's own damage (the vulnerability) does not.

Drain Stamina Other is the refill beside Revitalize Self (F10, F15). When D88's order says Revitalize Self, the target is alive, and it is in the drain's range and in line, the fight casts Drain Stamina Other at it instead, when `FightPicker.StaminaDrainWins`. That is so when the drain's expected stamina over its all-in seconds (the cast, the mana at the dance's rate, and the blows taken while casting at the heal's rate) beats Revitalize Self's (VI: 80 a cast). It is never an attack (F11: -2.5 s). The target's stamina is not in the profile, so it is taken as enough for the cap to bind (`TODO(f10-target-stamina)`). A cast that did not land (not a resist) sets the drain aside for 30 s. The yield is measured (F15): the stamina after the landing minus the stamina before, against the gain expected if it lands. Under `StaminaDrainMinYieldShare` (0.25) the drain is set aside for that target and Revitalize Self takes over; above it, the share scales the drain's score. The line is in `ArenaProfile.Extras` (I to VI 1249 to 1254, VII 2330 Vigor Siphon, the incantation 4645; SpellsToLearn 277 to 285).

**The buffs at 80 (F12).** The cycle casts 24 lines at Level VI (power 250, 2,700 s, which is 45 minutes): the focus and willpower lines, the creature, war, item, life and mana masteries, the four attributes (Strength, Endurance, Coordination and Quickness, which raise burden, health and stamina, and Melee Defense), Arcane Enlightenment, Magic Resistance (Magic Defense), Invulnerability (Melee Defense, which the mage does not train), Impregnability (Missile Defense), Armor Self (armour level +200), Bludgeon Protection (bludgeoning damage taken x0.4), the Regeneration, Rejuvenation and Mana Renewal lines (x1.85), Impenetrability and Bludgeon Bane on the worn armour, Hermetic Link (x1.6) and Defender (+0.15). The order is focus, willpower, the creature mastery, the other masteries, then the rest in profile order, then the upgrade pass. The buffed skills after a cycle are War 364, Life 326, Creature 325 and Item 325, and a cycle costs 1,640 base mana (live: 26 lines in 56 s). Level VII is not cast. It needs a skill of 332 for the owner's 0.9 cast chance, and Creature, Item and Life sit at 325 to 326. The tuskers do bludgeoning damage and carry no spell book, so no element line is added. The recast timing is unchanged. The upkeep checks every 5 s (`UpkeepDue`, `--buff-rebuff-margin-s`, default 300 s); a line lapses only if the time from crossing the margin to its recast exceeds 300 s. The headroom is about 150 s at the model's numbers. The residual risk is named: the health floor is checked before the buff in each 5 s check, so a health crisis lasting longer than that headroom would delay the buff.

**The autopilot's bind and the journey's level (F14).** A fresh mage at level 80 is not bound to a lifestone, so its first recall is refused with 0x049E (YouMustLinkToLifestoneToRecall; ACE `WorldObject_Magic.cs` 1087-1093, `WeenieError.cs` 658). The client cannot read the bind beforehand: ACE's player description carries only the last outside death position, not the Sanctuary or the linked lifestone. So the autopilot reacts to the refusal. A failed `recall-home` whose text carries 0x049E runs the bind station once (log: "autopilot: no lifestone bind: binding at <home town> first"), re-arms the recall-once flag, and classifies the state again. The bind walks from where the mage stands to the profile's home lifestone (`HuntingGround.Home`; any ground, no den constant), and it sets the linked lifestone that Lifestone Recall reads (Lifestone Tie, 2644; `WorldObject_Magic.cs` 966-976). A second refusal stops by name, `failed:travel:no-lifestone-bind`, because a recall cannot cure it. The journey takes `--mage-level` (50 to 126, default 126): `--scenario=journey --journey=mage --mage-level=80` verifies the level-80 traditional build (`MageTemplate.BuildFor`). Another journey refuses the option. This closes `TODO(m5-7-journey-mage-level)`, and the gate's journey at 126 is unchanged.

**The review's fixes (F15).** The fight review's findings were fixed as follows. (1) A kill notice that names the target is held as a pending kill. The target is marked killed, and the tour, the defence count and the loot are told, only when the object table shows it dead (health 0, Dead motion) or gone. A notice the table does not confirm within `FightScenario.KillNoticeSeconds` (2 s) was a neighbour's of the same name: it is logged once ("that creature lives ... it stays the target") and the target stays. The named creature is kept out of the pick while the notice is pending. `target_kills=` still counts by name, as a report-only count. (2) The upkeep's health stop is set as in the vitals paragraph above. (3) `DrainFirst`: with the drain rule on, a drain level known, health wanted by the rule and a target picked, the drain is cast before the stamina refill, and the pre-emptive transfer and the refill branches wait. (4) The stamina drain's yield is measured, as above. (5) Heal Self comes before a Hold and before a Walk of the self-defence, and the hold has its bound. (6) Nits: a Fire Vulnerability pick whose opener is settled re-decides without debuffs, so the war picks are weighed without a direct fall to the bolt; `EndFight` stops and tallies a pending opener and a pending stamina drain; and the dance (Stamina to Mana) and the Revitalize refill respect the plan cap (`FightRefill.Fill(..., maxLevel)`, `FillDanceLevels(..., maxLevel)`, `LevelCapFor`). The cap binds every level the fight casts, so a level-80 mage casts VI whatever scarabs it carries, and D88's "the highest the pack's scarabs pay" is read under the cap. Heal Self and the drain-health levels are unchanged. Still open from the review: the drain does not count toward the refill's in-a-row bound, and a vital update that lags the landing line would read as a zero yield (harmless, since Revitalize runs).

**Buff lines never lapse in a batch (D126 a).** Soak round 12 lost 12 to 15 lines together twice: at 09:43 UTC (about 30 s, 09:43:05 to 09:43:18) and in the 10:28 trip. The lines had been cast together and so came due together. At 09:43 the fight was in a two-minute walk (a 48 m leg that the runner replanned around creatures to 491 m), so no decision point came, and the 30 s hard floor never ran. At 10:27 the sell trip left with the lines due 23 to 48 s later. The fixes are below (`FightScenario.Refresh.cs`, `AutopilotScenario.cs`, `BuffCycle.cs`).

- **The batch.** Every pick sorts the lines by their time left. The k-th soonest line (counting from 1) is due when it has less left than the threshold plus k × 1.5 s (`BatchLineSeconds`; round 12's hard floor recast 13 lines in 19 s). So a batch starts early enough for its last line. At a lull and before a trip, every line due within 60 s (`BatchGapSeconds`) of the last line taken joins the batch. A batch once started is finished, because its end is kept on the clock. The log line carries `(batch: the lines under N s go together)`.
- **The walk.** In the Walk phase, a line under the hard floor (as a batch) drops the walk. The check runs once a second, and the next decision recasts the line (`buff refresh: the walk is dropped: ...`, `WalksDroppedForBuffs`).
- **The trip.** Before the stop for a trip (the counters' trip or the forced trip), the autopilot asks the fight (`IBuffRefreshFight.RequestLeaveRefresh`) to recast every line due within the trip's expected length plus the lookahead margin. The stop then waits for that, for 120 s at most (`buff refresh: leave: ...`). The trip's length is an exponentially weighted mean (weight 0.3) of the trips made, from the trip station's start to its end. It is seeded at 120 s, because round 12's trips took 97, 121 and 119 s. A stop asked before the fight's first pick ends the fight at once (F30) and runs no refresh.
- **The start's pass.** A start's or restart's buff station (`AutopilotScenario.BuffSpec`, `JourneyStations.StartPassArgument`) uses one buff cycle as its margin: `--buff-start-margin-s`, 2,700 s on the autopilot, which is VI's duration. So it recasts every line, and no line left from before falls due on its own in the first fight. The upkeep's station (`UpkeepSpec`) keeps the re-buff margin. Outside the autopilot the option defaults to the re-buff margin, as before.

The known-state after a trip still goes straight to the fight when an enchantment is up (`TODO(m4-journey-buffed)` is unchanged). The trip's refresh covers the lines due inside the trip, and the fight's hard floor covers any line that lapsed.

**The experience planner raises only what this mage gains from (D126 b).** In round 12 the spending rule bought Loyalty 120 → 121 (421,961 XP, 08:12 UTC) and Run 120 → 121 (421,961, 10:29), 843,922 in all. It bought no Salvaging. The level-80 build itself holds 120 ranks (5,470,950 XP) in each of the three. The relay's figure of about 1.6 M is not borne out by round 12's raise lines. Each skill was checked against ACE (ACEmulator/ACE@95ef426d, `Source/ACE.Server`):

- **Loyalty is dropped.** It is read only by the allegiance's pass-up: `AllegianceManager.DoPassXP`, `Managers/AllegianceManager.cs:255-265`, through `Creature_Skills.cs:72` `GetCurrentLoyalty`, an `IPlayer` wrapper "used by the AllegianceManager to handle passup". The pass-up returns at once when the vassal has no patron (`:256-257`). A solo mage is sworn to no one, so Loyalty earns nothing.
- **Salvaging is dropped.** It is read only by the salvage operation (`Player_Crafting.cs:142` `HandleSalvaging`; `:220` the value factor; `:313-327` the amount), and item 7 runs no salvaging (tinkering is later).
- **Run is capped at 81 ranks.** ACE runs a player at `MovementSystem.GetRunRate(burden, Run, 1)` (`Monster_Navigation.cs:346-368`, `Physics/Animation/MovementSystem.cs:20-28`): (loadMod × Run / (Run + 200) × 11 + 4) / 4. The load modifier is 1 under a full burden (`Physics/Common/EncumbranceSystem.cs:32-40`), and the mage carries about half. The client's `Lifestone.Data.Motion.MovementSystem.GetRunRate` is the same formula.
  - The mage's Run is 238 at 121 ranks ("Your base Run skill is now 238!"), plus Quickness Self VI's 35, so 273. The rate is 2.59, and one more point is worth 0.095% of the walking time.
  - Round 12 walked at most 27% of its 4.57 hours: the runner's 274 runs took 3,888 s, and ACE's MoveTo to a corpse took at most 608 s over 677 opens. The kills earned about 5.7 M XP an hour (7,053,567 in 73.6 minutes and 5,695,297 in 59.7 minutes).
  - So one more point brings about 1,465 XP an hour. The 121st rank cost 421,961, which is about 288 hours to pay back.
  - The rule (`RunPayback`, `MageSpendingPlanner.cs`) buys a rank of Run while it pays for itself within one soak (12 hours, D85's window). On the dat's trained list that is 81 ranks, whose last step costs 21,837 for about 1,840 an hour. At rank 120 the payback is 286 hours, at 100 it is 57 hours, and at 50 it is 1.4 hours.

`MageShares.Dance` (the soak mage's shares, every level under 126) sets Loyalty and Salvaging to weight 0 and caps Run. A weight-0 stat is left out of the plan, and the spending rule never raises a stat that is not in the plan. The capped Run moves first in the pool, so what its cap leaves rolls on to the stats after it. At level 80 the plan moves from Run 119 to 81 ranks, with no Loyalty or Salvaging. The freed experience goes to the rest of the pool (Strength 106 → 115, and Endurance and Max Stamina by their weight), and the remainder stays under 1%. The soak mage already holds 121 ranks of Run and 121 of Loyalty, so it raises none of the three again. The level-126 D19 split is unchanged, because it is the owner's decision. Pinned by `MageSpendingD126Tests` and `MageLevelPlanDatTests`.

**The mute check from the run's start (D126 c).** `tools\play.ps1` judges an automated run silent by the game's start-up line `audio service installed ... muted=true`. With `--log-copies=file` it reads that line from the isolated logs folder. A long run rolls `lifestone.log` (`--log-max-mb=16`), so after round 12 the line was in `lifestone.1.log`, and a clean run exited with code 3. The check now reads the start of every `lifestone*.log` written since the game was started (`Get-PlayRunLogHead` in `tools\lib\Play.psm1`), oldest first (the highest N). The previous run's copy, which the game's start makes as `lifestone.1.log` with its old write time, is left out.
