**The packs, one job each (D121; `Session/Mage/PackLayout.cs`, `PackSorter.cs`; `PackLayoutTests`).** The main pack holds the loot to sell, the coins and the trade notes, nothing else; the four foci keep their side slots; the three side packs (two Packs and a Sack, 24 items each: ACE weenies 136 and 166, ItemsCapacity 24) take one job each. `PackRules` gives an item its job: money and a note the main pack; a spell component (ItemType 0x1000 or a class of the ground's `components`) the components pack; a stock line that is no component (the Mana Potion), a mana stone (ItemType 0x80000), a healing kit, a spare of a worn or wielded kit line, or a piece the self-equip took off (`PackMemory.Rotation`) the kit pack; a find the keep-aside rule kept (`SaleMemory.KeptAside`) the review pack; everything else the main pack. Gear already in the kit pack and an owner's find already in the review pack stay. `PackLayout.Assign` chooses the packs by a rule, logged: a pack that already holds a job's items keeps that job, and the jobs left go to the packs left in their slot order, so the empty packs of 2026-10-29 become components, kit and review in that order and keep those jobs once filled. Every station that moves an item follows the layout. The loot step's plan (`MageLootRules.Plan(..., layout, packRules)`) counts each kept item against the free slots of its job's pack and falls back to the main pack when that pack is full; the shared looter picks it up into that pack (`LootRules.DestinationOf`, `CorpseLooter`). The death's take-back is `LayoutTakeAll`. The resupply sorts the packs at the start of the visit and again after the buys, since ACE creates a bought ware in the main pack. The sale reads the main pack only (`ShopLeg.PackItems`), so D99's drop of the cheapest comes from the main pack only. The start-up audit's packs check runs `PackSorter` once per audit (`autopilot audit packs: ...`, `packs=<moved>/<planned>` in the summary). The trip's slot trigger still reads the main pack (`LogisticsCounters`, `MageLootRules.FreeSlots`). The lines are `packs: layout (<why>): components='Pack' 0x... (side slot 1); ...; packs main=<free>/<cap> components=<free>/24 kit=... review=...` and one `packs: move '<name>' 0x<guid> main -> <job> pack 0x<pack> (<why>): done` per move. The soak line ends with `free_components= free_kit= free_review= self_equips= stone_refills= stone_captures=`.

**The shoes (D122; `SelfEquipJudge.PickFootwear`).** The mage wears footwear from its own items only while the feet are free. ACE refuses any clothing whose ClothingPriority overlaps a worn piece's (`WieldedLocationIsAvailable` through `GetEquippedClothingArmor`, Creature_Equipment.cs 50-58 and 93-101; "You must remove your X to wield Y", Player_Inventory.cs 1801-1809). The Faran Robe with Hood is worn at 0x7F01, which holds FootWear 0x100, and its ClothingPriority 0x17F00 includes Feet 0x10000, so today the step logs `gear: shoes: feet covered by Faran Robe with Hood ...` and wears nothing. D129 (the owner): the mage keeps the robe and wears no shoes, as retail. The pick stays dormant and acts only if the feet are ever free (then the best armour level among the pieces whose requirements are met, then the lightest). No shoe is ever bought (`TODO(m5-shoes-vendor)` is closed), and looted footwear is loot like anything else.

**The self-equip and the items' mana (D125; `Session/Mage/SelfEquip.cs`, `ItemManaKeeper.cs`, `Scenarios/Journey/ResupplyStation.Gear.cs`; `SelfEquipTests`).** A better caster has a higher war damage factor on the plan's element. The factor is the caster's ElementalDamageMod (float 152), but only when its DamageType (int 45) is the spell's element (WorldObject_Weapon.cs 382-401; Flame Bolt's `e_Type` is 16, fire). On a tie the higher ManaConversionMod (float 144) wins, then more useful cantrips. A wearable, jewellery piece or trinket is worn when it casts a useful cantrip that no worn piece casts and a slot is free (coverage for clothing and armour, the location for jewellery, a ring or a bracelet on the free side). The useful cantrips are the ground's keep-aside list. Both kinds must meet the wield requirements (int 158-160 and 270-272; Skill, RawSkill, Level and Training are read) and the activation requirements (Arcane Lore against ItemDifficulty 109, UseRequiresLevel 369, ItemSkillLevelLimit 115). Any other find goes to the review pack and is never sold. The loot step wears a free-slot wearable at once. A caster swap waits for the home visit's gear phase, in peace, and the old caster goes to the kit pack as rotation gear. The gear phase also keeps the worn items' mana. A charged stone is used on the player when a worn piece is under half its maximum or has under two hours left at its ManaRate (ManaStone.cs 119-190: rationed over every equipped item that needs mana; the stone is destroyed on its ManaStoneDestroyChance, 0.2 for the Greater Mana Stone, else left empty). While fewer than two charged stones are held and something worn uses mana, an empty stone may be used on a junk magic item of the main pack (weighed against its sale since S2b, below). That drains `round(efficiency x mana)` into the stone (2.0 for the Greater Mana Stone) and destroys the item (ManaStone.cs 84-117); a Retained item is refused. The lines are `gear: swap in|wear|review '<name>' 0x<guid>: <why with the numbers>` and `gear mana: refill|capture: ...`. A worn item's "<item> cast <spell> on you" and "Your <item> is low on Mana." reach the log through the chat tap (category `chat`, `type=7`, ACE ChatMessageType.Magic). After a death, and at every start-up audit, the worn set goes back on (S2d, closes `TODO(m5-self-equip-death-rewear)`; `Session/Mage/WornRestorer.cs`, `WornRestorerTests`). While the mage lives, the autopilot takes a snapshot of every worn piece that is no caster, by object id and location, every 5 s (never at 0 health or after a death is seen), and writes it into its checkpoint (`worn`). `WornRestorer` then wears, one action at a time: (1) the remembered pieces at their locations; (2) any clothing or armour held whose coverage no worn piece overlaps, by best armour level, then lightest (with no memory, as at the login of 2026-10-29 after the gate's scripted death, this is how the Shirt and the Wide Breeches went back on); (3) the cantrip wearables; (4) the one best caster held, the review and kit packs included. Each wear and refusal is logged as `gear: restore: ...`; the audit's summary gains `worn_restored=<n>`, and the death handler logs `handler: worn_restored=<n>`.

**A worn item's mana, the drain against the sale, the kit's stones and the requirements (S2b, the owner's addition to D125; `Session/Mage/WornManaWatch.cs`, `ItemManaKeeper.cs`, `SelfEquip.cs`; `ItemManaTests`, `ItemManaDatTests`).** ACE burns a worn item whose spells are on by `-ManaRate x 5` on the player's 5 s heartbeat (Player_Tick.cs 110-116 and 624-660; WorldObject_Tick.cs 44-56). Setting ItemCurMana sends nothing (WorldObject_Properties.cs 2163-2167), so the client learns the mana only from an appraisal, or as a fraction from a one-shot QueryItemMana (0x0263 and 0x0264, Player.cs 440-455, WorldObject.cs 621-637; the client has no verb for it, `TODO(m5-query-item-mana)`). The only lines are "Your X is low on Mana." under 120 s left (Player_Tick.cs 662-679) and "Your X is out of Mana." at 0 (681-699), both F7E0 lines of ChatMessageType.Magic (7); the item's spells drop 2 s later.

The autopilot's `WornManaWatch` therefore re-appraises each worn magic item every 300 s and predicts the mana between reads at its rate. Each read logs `gear mana: read '<name>' 0x<guid> <cur>/<max> (<pct>%), burns <rate>/s while its spells are on: <h> h left; predicted <p> from the read <s> s ago`. The two server lines log `gear mana: ALARM: ...`, and "out" sets the prediction to 0. Round 12's Compass casts Major War Magic Aptitude (spell 2534, base mana 10 in the dat). ACE therefore gives it a ManaRate of -1/120 (`CalculateManaRate`, LootGenerationFactory_Magic.cs 105-112), a point every 2 minutes, and an ItemMaxMana of 120 to 200 at workmanship mod 1 (`RollItemMaxMana`, 67-100: jewellery takes 12 to 20 times the base). That is 4 to 6.7 hours from full to empty, and half after 2 to 3.3 hours. The examine window's "Mana: N / M." is the appraisal's number when the window opened (`GameExaminePanel`, as retail's `Appraisal_ShowMagicInfo`); it changes only when the item is appraised again.

The capture is weighed against the sale. The captured mana is `round(efficiency x ItemCurMana)` (ManaStone.cs 108). The efficiency is PropertyFloat 87, an assessment property, else the world SQL's table: Minor 0.1, Lesser 0.25, Mana Stone 0.6, Moderate 1.2, Greater 2, Major 3. The use's tracker reads ACE's capture line, "The Mana Stone drains N points of mana from the X.\nThe X is destroyed." (ManaStone.cs 110), as the success it is (S2c: the rehearsal of 2026-10-29 read it as "nothing happened"); its N is the efficiency times the appraised mana (2.0 x 2,934 = 5,868). The caster swap is one pick per visit, the best of every caster held with the wielded one included (`SelfEquipJudge.BestCaster`), and a caster off the plan's element never wins a tie (S2c: the rehearsal wielded an Electric Sceptre on its mana conversion and never sent the better Fire Sceptre's swap, because only the first swap of the item-by-item loop was sent). The captured mana is valued at 1.8 pyreals a point (the Titan Mana Charge's Value 9,000 for 5,000 points) and set against D96's payout at the best buyer; the item is drained only when its mana is worth more. Each item weighed logs `gear mana: weigh '<name>' 0x<guid>: <m> mana -> <c> in the stone, worth <w> pyreals at 1.8 a point, against a payout of <p>: drain|sell`.

Round 12 left 38 mana stones under the floors (22 Moderate, 12 Mana Stone, 3 Lesser, 1 Minor). The loot rule now keeps a stone, empty or charged, whatever the floors say, while the kit holds fewer than `ItemManaKeeper.StoneTarget` (4: two charged and two to capture with). Such a stone gets the verdict `KitStone` (the decision line says ", for the kit's mana stones") and goes to the kit pack. The excess goes by the value rules, and a stone of the target is never offered to Archmage Cindrue, whose mask buys them.

The activation requirements are read in ACE's order (WorldObject_Use.cs 191-324): Arcane Lore, the item's skill limit (the armour loot names Melee or Missile Defense, LootGenerationFactory_Magic.cs 177-203), UseRequiresSkill, the specialised limits, the level, the attribute and vital limits, then the heritage. Each refusal names retail's line: 0x028B 0x04C9 "Your X is too low to use that item's magic.", 0x04CB, 0x04C6, or the transient "You are not high enough level to use that!". A wield refusal is 0x00A0 SkillTooLow 0x0468 or LevelTooLow 0x0420 (Player_Inventory.cs 1655-1661 and 2126-2229), for which retail prints no line (`WeenieErrorText.RetailShows`).

**Trade notes and coin that stack (D136 point 3, D133; `Scenarios/Journey/ResupplyStation.cs`, `Fighter/StackMerge.cs`; `ResupplyStationTests.Notes.cs`).** Every trade note weenie stacks to 250 (wcids 2621-2627 and 20628-20630, MaxStackSize PropertyInt 11 = 250, StackSize 1, ACE-World-Database v0.9.296) and the coin (273) to 25,000. ACE never joins a new stack to a held one. A buy makes one new stack of up to MaxStackSize a row (`Vendor.cs` 392-424 `ItemProfileToWorldObjects`) and adds it with `TryCreateInInventoryWithNetworking` (`Player_Commerce.cs` 59-102, `Player_Inventory.cs` 90-118, `Container.cs` 499-575, no merge). A sale's payout comes as new coin stacks (`Player_Commerce.cs` 194, 211-218, 302ff `CreatePayoutCoinStacks`). Joining stacks is the player's own GameAction 0x0054 (retail `CM_Inventory::Event_StackableMerge`, 11.6096 0x006ACDD0; ACE `HandleActionStackableMerge`, `Player_Inventory.cs` 2849ff), the `MergeStack` verb the corpse looter already sends for looted coin. Round 12's six single notes were, by this, separate buys (one note a trip), each its own object. After the coin-to-notes buy, the trip now merges the notes into the main pack's stack with the purchase merge, `StackMerge.TryPlanFor(..., mainPackTarget: true)`: only a stack in the main pack itself is a target (D121), so a stack ACE put in a side pack comes home. It logs `resupply: merging a bought Trade Note (250,000) stack 0x<new> into 0x<held>: x<n>` and then `purse: Trade Note (250,000) held in 1 stack(s): 0x<held> x<total> (main pack)`. The coin's stacks are merged the same way after that buy and after the loot sale (`resupply: merging a held Pyreal stack ...`). One buy may take as many notes as the coin pays, up to 250 for each free main-pack slot (`ItemsToReceive.cs` 78-110 asks one slot a stack). A row whose 0062 names no MaxStackSize counts one per slot, as before. Changing a note when the coin is short: ACE sells the whole object it is handed (`Player_Commerce.cs` 199-205 and 232-298; `Vendor.cs` 586-609 pays the stack's whole Value). So a note from a stack of several is first split off into the main pack with GameAction 0x0055 (`SplitToContainer`; ACE `HandleActionStackableSplitToContainer`, `Player_Inventory.cs` 2243ff), logged `purse: split one <note> 0x<one> from its stack 0x<stack> (x<n>) to change it`. That one note is sold; with no free slot it is refused as `pack`. D99's sale reads the main pack and never offers a note, so it is unchanged. `TODO(m5-note-merge)` is closed.

**The purse's follow-up (soak round 13; `Scenarios/Journey/PurseNotes.cs`, `Fighter/StackMerge.cs`).** Two faults in round 13's trip (19:59-20:01 UTC). The trip bought no note and gave no reason: it held 150,021 against the 40,000 floor and wanted a whole 287,500. The coin merge ping-ponged 36 times: the old plan took a full 25,000 stack as the source into a partial one and back.

Now every trip converts the coin above the floor, greedily from the largest note, into the notes of the purse's `min_note` and up (`PurseNotes.Greedy`). For the den `min_note` is 25,000, one coin stack, and it is also the default. A smaller note saves no pack slot and costs 15%, so the 10,000 and smaller stay coin. ACE has no 25,000 note, so the den buys the 250,000, 200,000, 150,000, 100,000 and 50,000. Round 13's 150,021 now buys one 50,000 and keeps 92,521 in coin: four coin stacks and at most five note stacks. The vendor of the purse's largest note is asked for all ten notes, and those under `min_note` are left out. Archmage Cindrue (wcid 2304) lists them all: her CreateList, ACE-World-Database v0.9.296 `weenie_properties_create_list` rows 21060-21069, destination 4. Thelnoth (715) lists only the 100 and the 500 (rows 12597-12598), and Monyra (716) only the 100 to the 1,000 (rows 65467-65469). Each note is priced as ACE does, `max(1, ceil(1.15 x Value - 0.1))` (`Vendor.cs` 577-585): 287,500 down to 115. At the vendor her 0062 decides: a note she does not list is skipped and logged. The coin left is under the smallest offered note's price (57,500 for the den). Each denomination is one buy, merged into its stack before the next.

Every trip logs the decision. For a buy: `purse: coin <c>, floor <f>, above <a>, notes of 25000 and up (a smaller note saves no pack slot and costs 15%): buy <n> x <note> at <price>, ...; <left> stays coin (under 57500, the Trade Note (50,000)'s price)`. Otherwise `... no notes: <why>` (no vendor sells a purse note; no note is of `min_note` or more; the coin above the floor is under the smallest such note's price; the main pack has no free slot). After the buys come one `purse: bought ...` line for each denomination and `purse: done at <vendor>: the coin <c>, <a> above the floor <f>`.

The coin merge (`StackMerge.TryPlanFor`) takes only partial stacks, smallest into the largest other one. A full stack is never a source or a target (`TryPlan` refuses a full source too). Every merge empties its source or fills its target, so the merges end with at most one partial stack. Round 13's three cases, read back from the log, take 2, 1 and 2 merges where the old plan took 12 each (36 in the trip). Tests: `ResupplyStationTests.Notes.cs`. The scripted vendor that lists a trade note lists all ten (`ScriptedShop.Shop.AddTradeNotes`), so the den's forced trip now walks to Cindrue a second time, for the notes.

Nothing in this section was run live or in a loopback: the rehearsal, the soak and the item-end checks are the lead's.
